# Theme — HBM / Memory Super-Cycle

_Wiki · generated 2026-06-19 · cross-company theme · sources: equity calls (`../_equity_calls/Semis`), briefing roll-ups (`../_briefings/by-ticker`), earnings transcripts, company pages. Company pages: [../00_INDEX.md](../00_INDEX.md) · themes index: [00_THEMES.md](00_THEMES.md)._
## 🆕 Recent signals (Aug 27, /run-inbox — **THE DE-SPEC FINALLY GETS ARITHMETIC: ~1.63x MORE OUTPUT FROM THE SAME DIE POOL, AND HBM REVENUE AT 81.5-98% OF PLAN RATHER THAN FALLING WITH THE BIT COUNT**) — 🔴🔴 **THE 21h RELAY GAVE THIS PAGE THE RECONCILING *MECHANISM* ([[SKHYNIX]] ON WHICH CUSTOMERS, JEFFERIES ON ALLOCATION). THE PRIMARIES ADD THE *NUMBERS* — AND THEY SAY THE MARKET'S FIRST-ORDER "HALF THE BITS = HALF THE REVENUE" REACTION IS WRONG BY 10-25 POINTS.**

**Sources: DAMNANG — "What Does Rubin Ultra's 8-Hi HBM Mean?" (2026-08-10) and "HBM Density Peak: Why AI Value Is Moving From Memory to Connectivity" (2026-08-20)** ⚠️⚠️ **INDEPENDENT SUBSTACK AUTHOR, NOT A BROKER — no rating, no PT, no model. The author states the 8-Hi configuration is *"UNCONFIRMED information based on industry checks"* and the revenue figures are *"scenario SENSITIVITIES, not company guidance or shipment forecasts."*** Plus **Jefferies "2026 Semiconductor Conference Recap" (the FULL note), 2026-08-27**. [8-Hi](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/What_Does_Rubin_Ultras_8-Hi_HBM_Mean__-_Damnangs_Substack.html) · [Density Peak](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/HBM_Density_Peak__Why_AI_Value_Is_Moving_From_Memory_to_Connectivity.html) · [Jefferies](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_Semiconductor_Conference_Recap.html)

- 🔴🔴 **THE SENSITIVITY, AND IT IS THE FIRST QUANTIFICATION OF THE DE-SPEC ON THIS PAGE:** dropping 12-Hi → 8-Hi lifts effective output by **up to 1.5x on stacking time** (if stacking time scales with layer count) **× 1.084x on stack yield** (98% per-core-die yield, four fewer layers) = **~1.63x effective output ceiling**. If [[NVDA]] absorbs all of it into additional GPU shipments, **total HBM revenue lands at ~81.5% of plan with 8-Hi priced at 50% of the planned HBM4E, and ~98% at 60%.** The naive per-bit calculation (price scales with bits, 10% per-GB discount → 45% of 4E price × 1.63x) gives **~73%** — and the author argues it undershoots because *"the actual bit count falls by half, but the **CORE DIE COUNT is 8 versus 12, about two thirds, and the BASE DIE goes in the same** whether it is 8-Hi or 12-Hi."* ⚠️ **He also notes the process has fixed time unrelated to layer count, so *"the real number may be LOWER"* than 1.63x. Treat 1.63x as a ceiling.**
- 🔴 **THE FALSIFIABLE BAR, WHICH IS WHAT MAKES THIS USEFUL RATHER THAN JUST OPTIMISTIC: *"**if 12-Hi HBM4E could not actually meet more than 81.5% of the required volume, this despec can actually result in HIGHER revenue.**"*** ➤ **That is a testable condition, and this page already carries the evidence that it holds: SK Hynix said on 08-26/27 that supply is tight *"even over the long term"*, and the UBS Korea Summit put the DRAM demand-fulfilment ratio at only **60%** with undersupply *"getting WORSE in 2027."* Under those conditions the de-spec is revenue-ACCRETIVE, not dilutive.**
- ✅ **AND A BROKER WITH A MODEL REACHED THE SAME CONCLUSION INDEPENDENTLY, SEVENTEEN DAYS LATER: Jefferies — *"**HBM De-Spec a Supply Response, Not a Demand Signal**"* — with NVDA reportedly evaluating **8-Hi HBM4E, 12-Hi HBM4 and 8-Hi HBM4** against the original 12-Hi HBM4E baseline. *"We do NOT expect broad-based HBM de-spec and view selected moves from 12-Hi to 8-Hi primarily as a means of **STRETCHING A CONSTRAINED DIE POOL and INCREASING ACCELERATOR SHIPMENTS**… it does not undermine structural bit growth and, **if anything, REINFORCES that demand is running ahead of available supply.**"* ➤ **Two independent sources, one with a model and one without, same conclusion. That is the strongest cross-source agreement on this page's central question.**
- 🔴 **THE CAUSE IS A QUALIFICATION FAILURE, NOT A DEMAND FAILURE — AND IT IMPLICATES ALL THREE SUPPLIERS EQUALLY:** NVDA has been demanding **~16Gbps pin speed on HBM4E, far above spec**, and *"the HBM4E samples submitted so far by **the three memory makers** are reportedly having a HARDER TIME THAN EXPECTED PASSING QUALIFICATION at that spec"* — echoing the 12Gbps HBM4 episode. With HBM4 already holding **10-11Gbps against an 8Gbps JEDEC baseline**, proven HBM4 plus a modest bandwidth bump beat forcing Rubin Ultra (**2H 2027 launch**) onto 4E. ⚠️⚠️ **A LABELLING TRAP THIS PAGE SHOULD ADOPT: the author notes the newly requested HBM4 pin speed *"comes close to the 15 to 16Gbps it had demanded on the earlier HBM4E"*, so *"on pin speed alone, the newly demanded HBM4 could just as well be called HBM4E class."* He calls it HBM4 on core-die density (3GB) and qualification path. **The HBM4 / HBM4E label is NOT a clean bandwidth distinction and must not be used as one in any bit or ASP model on this page.**
- 🔴 **THE COMPETITIVE AXIS SHIFTS, WHICH IS THE STRATEGIC CONSEQUENCE AND IS NET-NEW: *"In the regime where **12-Hi and 16-Hi stack height was itself the AXIS OF DIFFERENTIATION**, high-stack qualification and stacking technology carried a **LARGE PREMIUM**. **If the life of 8-Hi extends, YIELD, CAPACITY, SUPPLY STABILITY and BASE DIE FUNCTIONALITY carry more weight.**"*** ➤ **A durable 8-Hi node moves competition away from exactly the dimension on which [[SKHYNIX]] built its lead and toward the dimensions where [[SAMSUNG]] and [[MU]] are closest. It simultaneously rewards SK Hynix's custom-base-die and CXL investments — the author reads *"SK hynix expanding custom base die and a CXL-based memory tier together"* as sitting against precisely this change. Directional, not a share forecast.**
- ⚠️ **CONTEXT THAT SHOULD DAMPEN EVERY FUTURE DE-SPEC HEADLINE ON THIS PAGE: Rubin Ultra's capacity has been cut at EVERY revision — *"from the **4-die 1,024GB announced at GTC 2025**, through the 12-Hi reduction and the 2-die transition, down to **today's 192GB**"* — with Vera Rubin's SOCAMM loadout also reported cut roughly in half. *"Looked at the other way, this may be evidence of just how SERIOUS the memory supply constraint is — serious enough that the CHIP DESIGNER HAS TO KEEP CHANGING THE SPEC."*** ➤ **Repeated de-spec is a shortage indicator, not a demand indicator. This page has reacted to each one as news; the pattern is the news.**
- 🔴 **AND THE REASON THE CAPACITY LOSS DOESN'T COST PERFORMANCE — WHICH IS ALSO WHY THIS IS AN NVDA-ONLY MOVE:** local HBM stays the fastest tier (**Rubin local HBM ~21-22TB/s vs NVLink 6 at ~3.6TB/s GPU-to-GPU per GPU**), so *"another GPU's HBM does NOT replace local HBM. **What changes is HOW MUCH of the weights and KV cache must sit in ONE GPU's HBM.**"* NVL576 binds **eight 72-GPU racks into one NVLink domain** over optical rack-to-rack links, extending peer-HBM reach from 72 to **576 GPUs**; software then hides it — **Wide Expert Parallelism** (send the token to the GPU holding the expert), **DWDP** (prefetch peer weights concurrently with compute), **Helix** (shard long-context KV), **Dynamo** (demote cold KV to CPU/storage). *"This is a move that would be **HARD FOR ANYONE BUT NVIDIA**, which has built technical vertical integration spanning **chip design, optics, and software**."* ⚠️ **NVDA's public material confirms the 8-rack/576-GPU domain and the direct optical rack-to-rack link but does NOT state whether the packaging is NPO or CPO — SemiAnalysis reads it as NPO. Do not attribute the topology to NVIDIA.** ➤ **The bit demand does not vanish; it MOVES — to peer HBM, CXL tiers and eventually optically-shared memory. For a supplier that is a MIX SHIFT, not a demand cut. See [cxl-memory-fabric](cxl-memory-fabric.md).**

## 🆕 Recent signals (Aug 27, /wiki-ingest — **THE DE-SPEC DEBATE FINALLY GETS ITS RECONCILING MECHANISM, FROM THE SECOND SUPPLIER, ON THE RECORD — AND NVIDIA ANSWERS THE WHOLE ARGUMENT WITH A NEW MEMORY STANDARD OF ITS OWN**) — 🔴🔴🔴 **THIS PAGE HAS CARRIED "NO DE-SPEC AT THE HIGH END" (SAMSUNG IR, 08-25) AGAINST NAMED, SIGHTED HIGH-END CUTS (SEMIANALYSIS/JEFFERIES ON RUBIN ULTRA; MIZUHO ON [[GOOG]] TPUv8 8-Hi) AS AN UNRESOLVED CONTRADICTION FOR THREE DAYS. [[SKHYNIX]] JUST SUPPLIED THE MISSING DISTINCTION THAT MAKES BOTH SIDES TRUE, AND JEFFERIES SUPPLIED THE ALLOCATION MECHANIC BEHIND IT.**

### 🔴🔴 THE RESOLUTION — DE-SPEC IS A **SUPPLY-ALLOCATION INSTRUMENT AIMED DOWN THE CUSTOMER TIER STACK**, NOT A DEMAND SIGNAL

**① [[SKHYNIX]] on the record (BofA-hosted virtual meeting, 2026-08-26/27), and it is the cleanest segmentation any supplier has given:**
- *"**de-spec is mainly occurring in PCs/smartphones and LOW-END GPU/ASIC**, but this should reflect **memory chip shortages rather than demand cuts**"*
- *"**high-end applications (INCLUDING NEXT-GEN GPUs) will continue to use high memory content (NO DE-SPEC)**"*
- Plus: *"limited wafer capacity expansion (for both DRAM/NAND) **even with new shell fab construction** (as new cleanroom space remains tight)"*; *"memory supply likely to remain tight **even over the long term**"*; *"**ASPs likely more stable (no longer highly volatile) with growing LTAs**"*; no comment on China NAND fab expansion or Solidigm stake sales, *"given nothing has been decided"*; reiteration of the **50%+ payout ratio including the W40tn buyback (Aug-Nov 2026)** (BofA · **Simon Woo**, *"Simon's tech check (27 Aug)"*, 2026-08-26).

**② Jefferies supplies the mechanic that makes ① compatible with the sighted Rubin-Ultra checks — and it is the most useful sentence on this page today:** *"Reducing memory content would have a huge impact to the chip and rack performance for Rubin Ultra, so it is **NOT THE ONLY OPTION. MEMORY VENDORS MAY BRING THE DE-SPEC PRESSURE TO SECOND OR THIRD TIER CUSTOMERS INSTEAD.**"* (Jefferies · **Conor O'Mara** Taiwan checks, in Global SEMIS Daily, 2026-08-26.)

➤ **PUT ①+② TOGETHER AND THE THREE-WAY CONTRADICTION THIS PAGE HAS BEEN CARRYING COLLAPSES INTO ONE COHERENT STATEMENT: de-spec is real, it is a rationing tool, and the flagship SKU is the LAST place it lands — the tiers below it are the first. Samsung IR's "no de-spec for high-end GPUs" and SemiAnalysis's "Rubin Ultra de-spec evaluation" were never the same claim: one is about where the cut has LANDED, the other about where it is being MODELLED.** ⚠️ **The one datapoint this does NOT absorb is Mizuho's already-logged *"[[GOOG]] has also scaled down TPUv8 HBM3e to 8Hi"* — a high-end accelerator that has ALREADY taken the cut. If that check is right, the "high end is protected" line is true for NVDA's flagship and false for the ASIC flagship, which is a materially different world for [[AVGO]] and [[SKHYNIX]]'s ASIC-facing mix than for NVDA's.**

**③ The candidate-configuration list widened (same Jefferies checks):** *"Nvidia and memory vendors are working on **different de-spec scenarios, including 4e 8hi, 4 12/8hi and others**… Although it is not yet decided, NVDA and memory vendors have to make the decision **very fast, maybe in the next 1-1.5 months**, because when changing memory spec a lot of components and rack design need to be changed as well."* And the conclusion Jefferies will commit to: *"**one thing we can confirm is HBM supply will still be in SERIOUS SHORTAGE in 2027.**"* ⚠️ *The note also renders the candidate as "192GB of HBM8 8 hi" — **"HBM8" is a typo for HBM4**; the 08-25 primary on this page has 192GB HBM4 8-Hi. Do not propagate HBM8.*

**④ A third-party press leg pointing the same way, and it names THERMAL rather than cost as the driver:** *"**Samsung, SK Hynix to Increase Share of 8-High HBM4 Shipments, Signaling Changes in Nvidia's Supply Chain** … Samsung Electronics and SK hynix are expected to increase the share of 8-high products in their shipments of sixth-generation high-bandwidth memory (HBM4) to Nvidia. The main driver is **Nvidia's procurement strategy of securing a broader range of HBM options to address THERMAL-MANAGEMENT concerns**"* (Zdnet Korea, relayed by @jukan05, corpus, 2026-08-26, ~75k views). ➤ **This converges with the KYEC check already on [[NVDA]]'s page attributing the Rubin end-of-3Q push-out to *THERMAL DESIGN changes* — two independent sources, different parts of the chain, same cause. If the driver is thermal rather than cost, de-spec is a physics constraint that HBM pricing cannot relieve, and it does not reverse when supply loosens.**

**⑤ And the demand-neutrality argument, restated by UBS the same week with its own procurement estimate moving the OTHER way:** *"HBM de-specing was a talking point, and we continue to expect **VR300 to adopt HBM4 8-Hi**. Yet, recall that back then/earlier this month we **slightly INCREASED our Nvidia HBM procurement estimate for 2027**. **De-specing effectively MANAGES SUPPLY PER AI GPU SiP considering supply constraints, but DOES NOT LOWER DEMAND**"* (UBS · **Nicolas Gaudois** et al., *"Key takeaways from UBS Korea Summit 2026"*, 2026-08-27).

**⑥ The quantified version of ⑤, which is the sharpest sizing this page carries — ⚠️ but it is a RELAY of a note not held here.** @QQ_Timmy (corpus, 2026-08-26) relays a **Mizuho** note: *"Memory remains the key bottleneck, but there is **significant DRAM de-spec risk. GPU/ASIC OEMs may limit future HBM to 4-8Hi, making 12Hi less attractive; 16/20Hi and HYBRID BONDING remain HIGHLY UNCERTAIN for subsequent generations.** Supply-chain and **MU CEO** checks show **Google has already de-spec'd TPUv8's HBM3e to 8Hi**… The HBM 'trade ratio' constraint weakens, which will increase planar DDR5/LPDDR5 supply. **Lower DRAM/GPU content could allow GPU/ASIC shipments to increase by ~20-30%**, thereby boosting overall DRAM bit demand for training, inference and KVCache, **and improving GPU/ASIC margins.** Impact on WFE is uncertain, but Terafab and multiple greenfield fabs provide offsetting upside. Reduced memory pressure will drive increased GPU/ASIC shipments, which benefits **copper interconnect, NPO**…"* ⚠️⚠️ **ATTRIBUTION GUARD: this is a Twitter relay, in Chinese, of a Mizuho note that was NOT received in Outlook and is NOT held on disk. The TPUv8 leg independently corroborates the Mizuho line already logged on this page 08-25, which raises confidence in the relay — but the +20-30% shipment figure and the "16/20Hi highly uncertain" line are single-sourced through a relay and should be treated as such until the primary is obtained.** ➤ **If the +20-30% units figure survives, de-spec is BIT-NEUTRAL-TO-POSITIVE and MARGIN-POSITIVE for the accelerator vendors — which is exactly why both Samsung and SK Hynix can call it bullish without contradicting themselves.**

### 🔴🔴 NVIDIA'S OWN ANSWER TO THE ENTIRE ARGUMENT: **NVHBM** — A NEW CUSTOM HBM STANDARD, WITH [[AMZN]] AS FIRST ADOPTER

**NVDA extended NVLink Fusion with NVIDIA NVHBM (announced with the print, 2026-08-26):**
- *"Traditional HBM architectures place the **memory controller on the XPU die**, consuming valuable silicon area that could otherwise be dedicated to compute. **NVHBM, built on the same technology that NVIDIA will use for future GPUs, INTEGRATES NVIDIA'S CUSTOM MEMORY CONTROLLER INTO THE HBM BASE DIE.**"*
- **Claimed versus standard HBM4E: up to 30% greater memory bandwidth · 15% lower HBM power consumption · up to 25% more free area on the XPU compute die.**
- *"NVIDIA is establishing a **STANDARD NVHBM implementation, available from MULTIPLE memory providers**. This **reduces the engineering effort required to integrate and qualify memory across multiple suppliers** — giving NVLink Fusion customers a **faster path for bringing custom AI chips to market**."* To be *"validated and offered by leading memory partners."*
- 🔴 **[[AMZN]]'s Annapurna Labs is the FIRST to work on NVHBM and will support NVLink Fusion with next-generation Trainium **starting with Trainium4** — *"which would allow Amazon chips and NVIDIA GPUs to work together with common rack-scale architecture."***
(NVIDIA blog *"NVIDIA NVLink Fusion Expands With NVHBM Custom High-Bandwidth Memory"*, relayed with source link by Jefferies · Conor O'Mara, 2026-08-26; corroborated by @nvidianewsroom, corpus.)

➤ **THREE CONSEQUENCES FOR THIS THEME, and they do not all point the same way:**
1. **It is a FOURTH de-spec channel — efficiency per GB rather than GB per package.** Jefferies asked exactly that and hedged its own answer: *"Could this be part of the despec narrative that we keep hearing about if it can make more efficient use of memory?"* … *"investors first of all hadn't seen the announcement and then were asking whether this is negative for HBM. **I think it's just a sign of the times of memory being so tight and Nvidia is innovating to make its memory more efficient.**"* ⚠️ **30% more bandwidth per package is, mechanically, a way to hold system bandwidth while cutting stacks. That is de-spec by another name — and unlike a stack-height cut it does not degrade the rack.**
2. **It moves NVDA's controller IP INSIDE the memory vendors' base die and makes NVDA the standard-setter for third-party XPUs.** Bullish base-die logic content and advanced packaging; **it also gives the memory vendors a multi-supplier standard to qualify against, which is the opposite of the bespoke-per-customer engineering that has been supporting their pricing power.**
3. **Three of the four largest players in the chain are now moving logic into — or eliminating — the interposer/base-die boundary in the same week:** NVDA's **NVHBM** (controller into the base die), [[SAMSUNG]]'s **cHBM → zHBM** (logic into the base die, then HBM stacked directly on the XPU with no 2.5D interposer), and [[SKHYNIX]]'s **I-HBM** (see below). **This is now the theme's central architectural direction, not a set of one-off vendor claims.**

### 🔴 [[SKHYNIX]]'S OWN HYBRID-BONDING PRESS RELEASE — WHICH ARBITRATES THE BACK-END DEBATE THIS WIKI LOGGED ON 08-25, AND IT SPLITS THE DECISION

SK hynix published on its use of hybrid bonding for HBM, NAND and 3D DRAM (SK hynix press release, relayed by Jefferies · Conor O'Mara, 2026-08-26):
- **Timing, which is the whole point: *"While existing packaging approaches remain viable UP TO HBM4, structural limitations are likely to emerge from HBM4E ONWARD… In the HBM segment, the full-scale adoption of hybrid bonding is most likely to occur in **HBM4E OR HBM5, where stack heights are projected to exceed 20 LAYERS.**"***
- **Cost and readiness gates: *"manufacturing costs that are **two to three times higher** than those of flip-chip packaging, the maturity of process technology, and the pace of equipment development. There is also a **significant gap in equipment capabilities between domestic and overseas suppliers.**"***
- **The transition is PHASED, and TCB is being maximised first: *"just as wire bonding was replaced by flip-chip bonding in the past, TCB is expected to be increasingly replaced by hybrid bonding… **At present, a FLUX-FREE variant of TCB is being adopted**, and further attempts are expected to directly connect copper to copper using TCB-based processes. **When no viable alternatives remain, the industry will shift toward hybrid bonding.**"***
- **The performance case for eventually doing it: hybrid bonding vs conventional TCB — **speed ~11.9x, heat dissipation >100x, integration density 15x**. And *"the elimination of bumps and underfill reduces chip height and thermal resistance… provid[ing] a foundation for moving beyond 2.5D architectures toward FULLY 3D HBM packaging,"* with research ongoing on **3D HBM without TSV at all**.
- **Plus SK hynix's thermal answer, the counterpart to Samsung's Heat Path Block: **I-HBM** — *"embeds a high-thermal-conductivity, electrically insulating cooling material within the HBM D2D PHY area, close to the hotspot, creating a dedicated heat path that can **further reduce thermal resistance by more than 30%**,"* to support **HBM5** alongside proprietary **Advanced MR-MUF**. SK hynix is **already using advanced MR-MUF for 16-Hi HBM3e** and is *"looking to hybrid bonding as a key path BEYOND 16-Hi HBM."* Its **2.5D HBM roadmap features [[INTC]] EMIB alongside CoWoS-L, CoWoS-R and CoWoS-S** (Hot Chips 2026, via Wccftech / ServeTheHome, relayed by Jefferies, 2026-08-26).

➤ **HOW THIS SCORES THE 08-25 BACK-END DEBATE (MS desk: *"if you don't scale to 16-high, you don't need hybrid bonding"* → bearish [[BESI]]/CAMT/ONTO/[[TER]]; BofA: *"migration to new architectures INCREASES WFE intensity"* → bullish semicaps):**
- **MS's desk wins the TIMING half.** Hynix itself says HBM4 does not need hybrid bonding and that fluxless TCB comes first — so the hybrid-bonding revenue is pushed out to HBM4E/HBM5.
- **BofA wins the DIRECTION half, and Hynix is explicit about it.** The transition is characterised as inevitable (*"when no viable alternatives remain"*), and the intervening steps — fluxless TCB, Cu-Cu via TCB, MR-MUF, I-HBM, EMIB in the 2.5D roadmap — are themselves tool-and-materials intensive.
- ⚠️ **AND THE BEAR PREMISE IS PARTLY FALSIFIED ON ITS OWN TERMS: SK hynix is ALREADY shipping 16-Hi HBM3e on advanced MR-MUF. "You don't scale to 16-high" is not a forecast about the future; it is already wrong about the present.** The live question is 20+ layers, not 16.
- ⚠️ **Against all of that, the Mizuho relay in ⑥ above says **16/20Hi and hybrid bonding are *"highly uncertain for subsequent generations."* Hynix's own roadmap and a broker's checks now point in opposite directions on the single variable that decides the back-end capex cycle. Logged unresolved.** See [semicap-wfe.md](semicap-wfe.md), [[BESI]], [[ADVANTEST]], [[TER]].

### THE SUPPLY-SIDE NUMBERS THAT ARRIVED WITH ALL THIS — AND THE 2028 DISAGREEMENT IS NOW THREE-WAY

**UBS Korea Summit 2026 (Gaudois / Yoon / Yoo / Abrams, 2026-08-27) — the hardest new S/D datapoint on the page:**
- 🔴 **"The current DRAM DEMAND FULFILMENT RATIO is 60%, with unmet demand spilling over to 2027. It is in turn now captured for many customers through LTAs."** ➤ **A 40% unfilled order book is the single best explanation on this page for why de-spec, LTAs and price increases are all happening at once.**
- **"Memory undersupply will get WORSE in 2027 — whilst this is UBS' view, it also is what BOTH Samsung and SK Hynix expect."** *"We expect memory pricing will continue to increase, even with LTA mechanisms, throughout 2027 and beyond."*
- **Capex timing, which is the semicap read: SK Hynix **Y1 starts receiving equipment in February 2027**, with two clean rooms likely seeing capacity adds in 2027 (in sequence); **[[SAMSUNG]]'s P5-1 receives equipment from July 2027 onwards.**
- **Shareholder returns, and the policy change matters more than the number: SK Hynix's '25-27 policy now states **50% of FCF return is a FLOOR, versus a CEILING previously**, and it *"will come to markets when needed to support the share price through share buybacks."* Assuming 50% FCF returns for both names in 2027-28, UBS gets **Won378tn combined in 2027 and Won433tn in 2028 — 14% and 16% of combined market cap.** ⚠️ *Sanity flag: W378tn as a 50%-of-FCF payout implies ~W756tn of combined FCF, which is far above BofA's ~W170bn-scale Hynix FCF figures below. UBS shows no bridge; the percentages-of-market-cap are the usable part, the absolute won figure is not reconcilable from the material read here.*
- **Pecking order: Memory > MLCC > Semiscap > PCB; stock order [[SAMSUNG]] (Key Call Buy) > [[SKHYNIX]] > SEMCO > Eugene Tech > ISU Petasys > Simmtech > Hansol Chem.** ⚠️ *Note UBS ranks **Samsung ABOVE SK Hynix** — an explicitly stated house preference, not a derived CY-sum artefact.*

**BofA on [[SKHYNIX]] (Simon Woo, 2026-08-26) — and its 2028 is the bearish outlier:**
- **DRAM ASSUMPTIONS: ASPs **+25%/+9% q/q in 3Q/4Q26**; 2027 full-year average **+25% y/y**; then **FALLING 8% IN 2028.** **80%+ OP margin sustainable even in late-2027 and 2028.**
- **Wafer capacity: running capacity **~700k wpm in 2028** (even with the new Yongin fab) versus **~500k as of 2025** — a low-teens % CAGR — *"due to HBM (3-4x larger wafer capacity needed vs conventional DDR5)."* **NAND wafer capacity CAGR 2025-28 is even lower at sub-10%**, with new capacity mostly at Korea's Cheongju fab rather than China's Dalian.
- **Returns: ~W30tn of dividends for 2026 results plus the W40tn buyback ≈ a **40:60 dividend-to-buyback mix**; residual payable FCF **W15tn = 50% × W170tn FCF less W30tn dividend and W40tn buyback**, payable April 2027 after the March 2027 AGM. **2027 FCF has potential to exceed W200tn → total shareholder returns >W100tn in 2027.** Even in 2028, OP and EPS *"should remain well above the 2026 upcycle level… implying a P/E of just 4x."*

⚠️⚠️ **THE 2028 QUESTION IS NOW A THREE-WAY SPLIT AND THIS PAGE IS NOT SMOOTHING IT:**
| View | 2028 memory pricing | Source |
|---|---|---|
| **Bearish** | Hynix **DRAM ASP −8% y/y**; earnings *"moderating slightly"* | BofA · Simon Woo (2026-08-26) |
| **Balanced-by-then** | memory reaches **S/D balance by the END OF CY27** — *"sooner than most DRAM bulls anticipate"* | [[NVDA]] management (2026-08-26), flagged by Vital Knowledge |
| **Still tightening** | pricing *"will continue to increase… throughout 2027 **and beyond**"*; relief *"only starting in C2H:28"* | UBS · Gaudois (2026-08-27); UBS · Arcuri (2026-08-27) |

➤ **The buyer and the two sellers now disagree about 2028 in a way they did not about 2027. That gap is where the memory trade's terminal value sits, and it is the single most valuable thing to resolve on this page.**

### CHINA MEMORY — THREE INCOMPATIBLE READS ON CXMT'S TECHNICAL POSITION, PUBLISHED WITHIN 24 HOURS

| Read | CXMT's position | Source |
|---|---|---|
| **~2 generations behind, but HBM3E in volume next year** | *"process trails Samsung and Micron by about two generations"*, yet **"CXMT can produce 3-4mn units of HBM3E stacks in 2027 despite low yields, sufficient to support ~0.8-1.0mn units of China AI GPU shipment"** | MS · **Charlie Chan**, *"China DRAM Going Mainstream; Initiate CXMT at OW"* (2026-08-26) |
| **~2 generations behind, and only at HBM2E risk / HBM3 sampling** | brought **High-k metal gate into volume production on its G4 (16nm-class) LPDDR5X** — *"tech previously exclusive to Samsung, SK Hynix, Micron"* — but **"HBM2E risk production, HBM3 sampling"** | Korean media citing **TechInsights**, via Rothschild & Co Redburn (2026-08-27) |
| **Essentially at parity on DDR5 speed** | *"CXMT DDR5 closes in on SK Hynix at 8,000 MT/s, **benchmark gap falls below 1%**"* | DIGITIMES (2026-08-27) |

⚠️ **"HBM3 sampling now" and "3-4mn HBM3E stacks in 2027" are not reconcilable on any normal qualification timeline. Both are logged; neither is adopted as the wiki's number.** A speed benchmark being within 1% is also not the same claim as being two nodes behind — **bit cost, yield and power at that speed are the missing variables, and none of the three sources supplies them.**

**The rest of the China-memory package (all 2026-08-26/27):**
- **MS initiates CXMT at OW, PT Rmb88** (residual-income model, **18.5x 2027e P/E vs global peers' 4.4x**). **11% global DRAM bit-shipment share in 2026 (MSe)**; backed by the *"Big Fund"* with **Alibaba, Tencent and Xiaomi as strategic investors**; **~100kwpm added annually to reach 800kwpm by 2031** assuming DRAM industry margins stay near 80%; **140% 2025-28e revenue CAGR**. **⚠️ "ASML DUV lithography tools may be the main equipment bottleneck under export controls"** while *"domestic Chinese equipment vendors continue improving and are replacing US suppliers across several process steps."*
- **On global pricing MS is explicit that CXMT is NOT the 2027 risk: *"we see NO PRICING IMPACT BEFORE 2028, when CXMT should have enough capacity for large-scale shipments."*** But quality is already accepted: *"**Apple reportedly tested CXMT memory products** for devices sold in China, and we have seen **US PC brands (incl. HP) adopt CXMT DRAM**, suggesting commercially acceptable quality."*
- **CXMT LTAs, reported but company-unconfirmed: a 5-year agreement with ByteDance worth **>US$7bn**, and a long-term supply agreement with Tencent worth **>Rmb20bn** (Reuters, via MS · Chan, 2026-08-26). 2Q26 preview: MS projects 1H26 revenue **~Rmb136bn** and net profit **~Rmb65bn** against company guidance of Rmb110-120bn / Rmb50-57bn; **3Q26 revenue +35% q/q and +590% y/y with GM rising to 77%**, on **3Q26 DRAM contract prices tracking +15% q/q.**
- **MS's structural frame (Shawn Kim, *"Tech Bytes: China Memory — Reshaping Global Competition"*, 2026-08-26): DRAM leadership went US (70s) → Japan (80s) → Korea (90s, dominant since 1998) → *"may now be challenged by China in the coming decade."* **CXMT likely to surpass #3 Micron by 2028**; **YMTC adding ~100kwpm per year and likely global #2 in NAND by 2028**, with **YMTC localisation penetration near 60% for new fabs.** The warning for incumbents: *"over time, their expansion could **erode the supply discipline that has underpinned incumbent returns**"* and *"the biggest negative initially should come from **higher-volume markets with lower barriers to entry**."*
- ⚠️ **YMTC'S OWN AMBITION IS MORE AGGRESSIVE THAN MS'S ESTIMATE: *"the FT reports that YMTC aims to overtake Samsung AND SK Hynix to become the market share LEADER in NAND by the END OF 2027"*** — versus MS's *"global #2 by 2028."* YMTC has **filed for an IPO to raise Rmb33bn**, reporting **Q1 operating revenue RMB47bn and net profit RMB33bn, *"double the level of all of 2025"*** per its prospectus, at an **IPO valuation range of RMB200-300bn** (FT, relayed by Jefferies · O'Mara, 2026-08-26). ➤ **Company ambition vs broker estimate; both logged, neither adopted.**
- 🔴 **AND THE ROUTE AROUND THE EXPORT CONTROLS IS NOW DESCRIBED: *"YMTC and CXMT are seeking to increase the share of their memory supply shipped OUTSIDE China. YMTC is reportedly selling NAND chips to independent third-party module makers, which then assemble them into finished enterprise SSDs for customers such as **U.S. NEOCLOUD PROVIDERS**. This indirect route is intended to avoid sensitivities surrounding the provenance…"*** (DIGITIMES, relayed by @jukan05, corpus, 2026-08-25, ~77k views). ➤ **If Chinese NAND is already reaching US neoclouds through module-maker intermediation, the enterprise-SSD pricing power on [[SNDK]] / [[WDC]] / [[STX]] / [[KIOXIA]] has a supply leak that none of those names' guidance reflects. See [china-export.md](china-export.md).**
- **Redburn's read on the same set: *"DRAM capacity remains tight with **[[SAMSUNG]] raising Q3 prices up to 20%**. A qualification of new entrants like CXMT provides second-sourcing to ease supply-demand tension. CXMT's investments likely lean upon local China semicaps **Naura, AMEC, Piotech**."*

### DOWNSTREAM: THE FIRST CLEAN P&L EVIDENCE THAT MEMFLATION HAS REACHED THE END MARKET

**HPQ's F3Q26 (reported 2026-08-26; HPQ has no wiki page — logged here for the read-through):** **revenue $15.7bn, an 8% beat, driven by Personal Systems +18% y/y — but *"this growth was ENTIRELY PRICE-LED, with PC UNIT VOLUMES DECLINING 16%."* Price hikes *"failed to offset rising memory costs,"* compressing overall operating margins to 6.5%. Management expects continued PC margin pressure through Q4 on rising commodity costs and *"the EXHAUSTION OF LOW-COST INVENTORY,"* with margin recovery pushed to FY27.** Stock **−9/−10%** (Rothschild & Co Redburn; MS · Erik Woodring, PT to $19 from $17, 2026-08-26/27).
➤ **This is the cleanest confirmation available that (a) the memory sellers' pricing power is real and being collected, and (b) [[SKHYNIX]]'s "de-spec is mainly occurring in PCs/smartphones" is visible in the units: −16% volume with price-led revenue is exactly what rationing plus pass-through looks like at the OEM. It is also the first hard evidence of demand destruction in the chain, which is the mechanism any 2028 downcycle would run through.** See [[GLW]], [[DELL]], [[HPE]].

### NAND — A GREENFIELD IS CONFIRMED, BUT THE WFE BENEFIT IS 2029-2030, NOT 2028

**[[KIOXIA]] plans a THIRD fab at its Iwate (Kitakami) site with partner [[SNDK]], for high-density 3D NAND for AI data centres. Investment expected at >1tn yen (~$6.3bn). Kioxia had been evaluating expansion since June; this firms it up. 🔴 PRODUCTION IS NOT EXPECTED UNTIL 2029-2030. Iwate will focus on higher-end datacentre NAND, with Yokkaichi remaining for consumer/smartphone.** Kioxia stock **+5%**. Redburn's read: *"NAND supply-demand tightness is expected to continue through 2026-28, which is driving stronger pricing for longer. Bit supply growth and investments near-term remain in **UPGRADING EXISTING CAPACITY to taller layer counts**. New greenfield NAND capacity additions is a positive read-x to WFE growth **BEYOND 2028**, most notably for **[[LRCX]] (Buy)** given its high NAND exposure as 3D layer counts rise; a more modest read to **[[AMAT]] (Neutral)**"* (Rothschild & Co Redburn, 2026-08-27).
➤ ⚠️ **THIS QUALIFIES THE "NAND GREENFIELD RESTART" THAT MS COUNTED AS A MATERIALISED BULL-CASE ELEMENT IN ITS 08-25 WFE RAISE. The fab is real; the tool orders land after 2028. That timing matters directly for MS's $254bn 2028 WFE number and for the *"[[LRCX]]/[[AMAT]]/[[KLAC]] need 2028"* framing. See [semicap-wfe.md](semicap-wfe.md).**

### 🔴 WHY [[SAMSUNG]] STRUCTURALLY CANNOT MATCH SK HYNIX ON BUYBACKS — A LEGAL CONSTRAINT, NOT A CHOICE

Jefferies' Shawn Park, asked directly why Samsung cannot do a bigger buyback (via Jefferies · O'Mara, 2026-08-26):
- The founding family controls Samsung Electronics through **Samsung C&T**, which controls **Samsung Life** via a **19.3%** stake.
- In Samsung Electronics: **Samsung Life owns 8.6%, Samsung C&T 5.1%, Samsung F&M 1.5%.**
- **Samsung Life + Samsung F&M together exceed 10%, and Korean law prohibits financial subsidiaries owning more than 10% of an industrial subsidiary.**
- ➤ **Therefore a buyback-AND-CANCELLATION would mechanically raise Life's and F&M's percentage stakes and force them to SELL Samsung Electronics. Hence *"speculation dividend payout will be the main tool to lift return than direct buyback & cancellation."***
- ⚠️ **The workaround being speculated is directly relevant to this wiki's standing Samsung share-class trap: *"Some speculate Samsung Elec may just do buyback & cancellation more focused on **Samsung Elec PREF (005935 KS)** given no impact on Sam Life & F&M stakes in Samsung Elec Ord."***
- **Market already voting for the indirect route: Samsung proxies outperformed on 08-26 — Samsung C&T **+5.7%**, Samsung Life **+10.4%**, Samsung F&M **+5.7%** — *"as street sees indirect method as the way to go for SEC to raise shareholders' return given complicated circular holdings."***
➤ **This is a hard structural ceiling on the Samsung-vs-Hynix return comparison that no amount of FCF changes, and it sits directly under UBS's "Samsung > SK Hynix" preference above. See [[SAMSUNG]], [[SKHYNIX]].**

### 🔴 AND A CROSS-READ FROM AN UNEXPECTED PLACE: [[SNPS]]'S KOREA REVENUE +61% Y/Y ON HBM

**Synopsys F3Q26 (reported 2026-08-26): *"Korea was a notable outperformer growing **61% y/y** in 3Q, driven by **HBM engagements and [[SAMSUNG]]**"*; IP strength *"supported by improving titles like **LPDDR6** and **die-to-die** for AI/HPC designs"* (Rothschild & Co Redburn, 2026-08-27). ➤ **An independent, EDA-side confirmation that HBM/base-die DESIGN activity in Korea is accelerating — which is what the cHBM / zHBM / I-HBM / NVHBM roadmaps above would predict, and a leading indicator that runs ahead of any wafer or capex series.**

## 🆕 Recent signals (Aug 26, /run-inbox — **THE BIGGEST BUYER OF HBM IN THE WORLD JUST CUT ITS OWN GROSS-MARGIN GUIDE BECAUSE OF MEMORY PRICES, AND SAID THEY ARE STILL GOING UP**) — 🔴🔴🔴 **EVERY DEBATE ON THIS PAGE HAS BEEN A SUPPLY-SIDE ARGUMENT BETWEEN SELLERS AND ANALYSTS. TODAY THE DEMAND SIDE SPOKE, IN THE ONE FORM THAT CANNOT BE TALKED DOWN: [[NVDA]] TOOK A ~300-400bp GROSS-MARGIN HIT ON ITS OWN P&L AND NAMED MEMORY AS THE CAUSE.**

- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE QUOTE, FROM NVIDIA'S CFO ON THE Q2 FY27 EARNINGS CALL: *"Many of you have expressed concerns regarding our gross margins as component costs have risen significantly. As you are already aware, **we are experiencing EXTREME PRICING CONDITIONS IN MEMORY. The magnitude of the price increase has EXCEEDED OUR PRIOR EXPECTATIONS and are headed even higher into next year. As a result, we are resetting expectations today.**"*** (Colette Kress, NVIDIA Q2 FY27 earnings call, 2026-08-26.) **The reset itself is the evidence: FQ3 GM guided to 74.0% ±50bp, a FQ4 TROUGH OF 71-72%, and an FY28 settle of 72-73% — against 75.0% actual in FQ2 and a buy-side bogey of 74.8% for the FQ3 guide.** ➜ **This is the single strongest demand-side confirmation of the memory up-cycle available anywhere on this wiki, and it is worth more than any supplier's guide because it is the BUYER paying the price and booking the damage. Suppliers have an incentive to talk the cycle up; the customer taking a 400bp margin hit does not.**
- 🔴 **AND IT IS A FORWARD STATEMENT, NOT A BACKWARD ONE. Two clauses matter more than the level: (1) *"has EXCEEDED OUR PRIOR EXPECTATIONS"* — NVIDIA's own memory-cost forecast has already been wrong, to the upside, at least once; and (2) *"are headed even higher INTO NEXT YEAR"* — the buyer expects 2027 pricing above 2026.** ➜ **Place that against the widest open disagreement on this page — UBS's [[SAMSUNG]] blended HBM ASP **+90% y/y in '27E** versus JPM's industry HBM ASP **+42% y/y** (logged Aug 24). NVIDIA's language does not adjudicate between +42% and +90%, but it does rule out the LOW end of the range and it puts the largest counterparty on the record expecting further increases. The 48pp gap stays open; the direction does not.**
- 🔴 **THE COST IS BEING PASSED ON, BUT WITH A LAG, AND THE LAG IS NOW DATED: Kress says the FY28 recovery to 72-73% happens *"as EXECUTED PRICE INCREASES take effect in Q1"* [FQ1 FY28].** ⚠️⚠️ **THIS IS A DIRECT TEST OF THE MOST QUOTED BOM-SIDE CEILING ON THIS PAGE — the 07-30 BTG-hosted expert call with Hongkyo Jung (ex-SK Hynix), who argued 2027 HBM prices would rise only *"between 20 to 30%"* against the modelled doubling, *"because NVIDIA, THEY KEEP LIKE 75% THEIR PROFIT FOR [THE] OVERALL GPU SYSTEM. So they do not increase the BOM, the component cost, the HBM cost very much."* ➜ THAT ARGUMENT IS NOW PARTLY FALSIFIED. NVIDIA did NOT hold the line at 75% by refusing to pay: it paid, took the margin hit down to a guided 71-72% trough, and is raising ITS OWN prices to recover. The customer-margin wall the expert described did not stop the repricing — it deferred it into the customer's price list. What survives of his argument is that NVDA will not sit at 71-72%: the pass-through is coming.**
- ✅ **NVIDIA'S OWN FRAMING, RECORDED BECAUSE IT IS THE BULL CASE FOR THIS THEME STATED BY THE VICTIM: *"unlike a component that simply raises our costs with no offset benefit, **tighter memory supply is a SYMPTOM OF THE SAME DEMAND SURGE that's driving our own growth**."* Kress adds NVDA has *"long standing, deep relationships with all three major memory suppliers, and we're working closely with them to **further increase the capacity**."*** ➜ **Read with the same call's supply commentary: NVIDIA guided FY28 revenue to +~70% and called it *"a supply constrained outlook"* against customer forecasts pointing to ~100% growth, and when asked by Jim Schneider (GS) to RANK the constraints — power/shell vs DRAM vs wafer vs foundry — Jensen Huang declined: *"our entire supply chain is challenged and it's everybody. Everybody is really running flat out."* ⚠️ **No company endorsement for a single named bottleneck. Memory is one of several, not the identified one.**
- 🔴 **THE MEMORY-SPECIFIC SUPPLY-CHAIN ITEM FROM THE SAME DAY: NVIDIA *"announced a MULTIYEAR TECHNOLOGY PARTNERSHIP WITH [[SKHYNIX]] to advance next-generation memory for the global AI factory buildout"*** (NVIDIA press release, 2026-08-26). ⚠️ **No dollar value, no volume, no node, no exclusivity language disclosed. It sits alongside — and does not supersede — the NVDA/SK Group $500bn+ initiative logged on 2026-07-27, and it is NOT a supply agreement on its face.**
- ⚠️ **ONE ITEM THAT CUTS THE OTHER WAY AND IS EASY TO MISS: NVIDIA's inventory rose to **$32bn** (from $21.4bn at Jan-26) *"as we prepared for the Vera Rubin launch."* A ~$10bn inventory build at the largest HBM consumer is, mechanically, memory that has already been bought and is sitting in NVIDIA's working capital rather than in a supplier's order book. It is a pull-forward, not incremental demand, and it is the kind of thing that makes a supplier's next quarter look softer than the end-market.**

### 🔴🔴 OPEN ITEM — THE DE-SPEC NOW HAS **THREE** DATED POSITIONS FROM 2026-08-25, AND THEY ARE NOT THE SAME CLAIM. DO NOT COLLAPSE THEM.

| # | Position | Source, 2026-08-25 | What it actually says |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **RELAYED DENIAL** | [[SAMSUNG]] IR → BofA-hosted virtual group meeting (BofA · Simon Woo) | *"DRAM supply remains tight but **still no HBM de-spec for high-end GPUs**"* — a flat negative, second-hand, one clause long. |
| 2 | **ACTIVE THIRD-PARTY EVALUATION, WITH A DEADLINE** | Jefferies (Conor O'Mara); TrendForce; SemiAnalysis via Mizuho; UBS | Multiple live configurations (**4e 8hi / 4 12hi / 4 8hi**), *"nothing has been decided"*, decision forced **"in the next 1-1.5 months."** UBS goes further and treats it as done: *"we believe **Nvidia has moved to HBM4 8-Hi from HBM4E 12-Hi for VR/R300**."* |
| 3 | 🔴 **PRIMARY, SUPPLY-DRIVEN EXPLANATION** | **[[SAMSUNG]] IR, BTG-hosted call (THIS BLOCK)** | *"We are also aware that a lot of key investors are reportedly **considering lowering HBM stack layer, or lowering the commodity density**… **The reason why they are considering to do so is mainly because of SUPPLY CONSTRAINT, not a big technology issue or other issues, other than the demand issue.**"* |

➤ **Position 3 is neither a denial nor a bear datapoint. It is "it is happening, and here is the mechanism, and the mechanism is bullish." The company's own worked example: *"as for the HBM side… if they insist on same stacking layer like twelve or sixteen layer, they may not get enough HBM volume because of the shipments."*** ➤ **Positions 1 and 3 are BOTH Samsung IR on the same date. The most likely reconciliation is that they answer different questions — "are YOU de-speccing your product" (no) vs "are CUSTOMERS lowering stack height / commodity density" (yes, on supply) — but that is an INFERENCE, not something either source states. LOGGED AS OPEN. Per this wiki's standing rule the PRIMARY (BTG call, full Q&A, verbatim) outranks the RELAY (one clause inside a desk note) where the two are in tension — but the relay is not deleted, because it is the only version in which the company uses the word "no".**

### THE NUMBERS THE PRIMARY ADDS, AND THEY ARE THE MOST IMPORTANT ONES ON THE PAGE

- 🔴 **DEMAND FULFILMENT — the company's own fill rate, across the whole book and then for HBM alone.** *"if you look at each supply and demand dynamic and demand fulfillment rates for all products, especially commodity, even HBM, **the demand booking rate still remained very low, about SIXTY TO SEVENTY PERCENT**. On top of that, **the demand that cannot be met this year due to supply constraints are being PUSHED INTO NEXT YEAR**."* And separately, on HBM specifically: *"**HBM… we are seeing about only FIFTY TO SIXTY PERCENT demand fulfillment rate.**"* ✅ **Independently corroborated the same day by UBS on [[SKHYNIX]] (below): "Overall DRAM demand fulfilment rate is likely close to 60-70%, and with unmet demand spilling over to 2027, industry undersupply should worsen."** ➤ **Two of the three DRAM makers now put the same 60-70% number on the record within 24 hours, and BOTH attach the same spill-forward mechanic. This is the strongest version of the "it's supply, not demand" leg this page has carried, and it comes from the producers rather than from channel checks. It also corroborates the Aug-11 "60% of big-tech demand" and Aug-4 PM "fill rates are 60-70%" marks already here — the number has now held for three weeks across four independent tellings.**
- 🔴 **THE DURATION CLAIM, and it now runs PAST 2028.** *"overall supply and demand dynamic is expected to be **TIGHTER IN TWENTY SEVEN, EVEN TWENTY EIGHT**. If a lot of investors are concerned about oversupply coming from the new capacity addition and build out, we believe that **very tight supply and demand condition is expected to CONTINUE AFTER TWENTY EIGHT**."* ⚠️ **Directly opposite in sign to the TrendForce sufficiency series this page carries from 08-17 (DRAM +4.0% sufficiency in 2028) and to the JPM 2028 down-leg (DRAM −8% / NAND −14%, 08-06). Producer vs third-party forecaster. Both stay; neither is retired.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE HBM MARGIN INVERSION, AND IT IS THE BEST NEW EVIDENCE ON THE OPEN 2027 HBM ASP CONTRADICTION.** Asked directly whether HBM is margin-accretive to DRAM: *"**at this point, HBM margin level is WAY LOWER than the commodity DRAM.** The main reason is **the HBM contract for this year was committed at the END OF LAST YEAR.** However… this year we have seen very significant price increases in commodity. It is the main reason why HBM margin level is very lower than commodity. **However, for next year's contract… we are quite confident that we can raise the prices much higher. It should be AT LEAST A SIMILAR LEVEL OF THE COMMODITY DRAM.** If we successfully finish the contract with the major players for next year's volume, **the margin gap between commodity DRAM and HBM will be NARROWED SIGNIFICANTLY next year.**"* Pressed on the basis: *"we haven't finished the contract, but clearly compared to D[RAM] level, we strongly believe that we will see much higher pricing."*
  ➤ ⚠️⚠️ **HOW THIS BEARS ON UBS +90% (SAMSUNG BLENDED) vs JPM +42% (INDUSTRY $/Gb) — AND IT DOES *NOT* RESOLVE IT.** The mechanism management describes — a **2026 HBM contract struck in late 2025 at pre-inflation prices, being re-cut for 2027 against a commodity DRAM price that has since exploded** — is a **LOW-BASE argument**, and a depressed base is exactly what would let a single supplier's blended ASP outrun an industry $/Gb series. It is therefore **directionally supportive of the higher number, and it identifies [[SAMSUNG]] as the name with the depressed 2026 base.** **But management gives no percentage, no $/Gb, and states the contract is unsigned.** ➤ **THE CONTRADICTION STAYS OPEN. What changes is that it is now partly a BASE-YEAR question rather than only a mix question — and a base year is a checkable fact, which makes the 48pp gap more tractable than it was on 08-24. Re-routed to reconciliation with that framing. ⚠️ Always state the basis inline: JPM +42% is INDUSTRY $/Gb; UBS +90% is SAMSUNG BLENDED across its own generation mix. Never net them.**
- 🔴 **THE LTA SPLIT, WITH THE INTENT STATED OUTRIGHT — 60-70% CONTRACTED, 30-40% DELIBERATELY HELD BACK TO CAPTURE PRICE.** *"**We are decided, as we will intentionally allocate sixty to seventy percent toward the LTA. The reason we left thirty to forty percent of the capacity is to address the mobile, PC, and non-LTA customers.** Still, of course, the LTA is a safety net to reduce the risk. **But in order to take more opportunities, especially more price uptrend, we inevitably have to leave some capacity to address this market.**"* ➤ **This is the missing half of the 08-25 BofA relay's "LTAs target 60-70% of total wafer capacity." The relay gave the number; the primary gives the REASON — the uncontracted third is a deliberate long position on spot. Read alongside the 08-02 [[SAMSUNG]] LTA asymmetry already here (−5% floor vs +10-20% open) it says the same thing twice: Samsung has structured itself to be LONG the upside, not to smooth it.**
- **HBM VOLUME AND MIX, from the company.** **HBM sales expected to be MORE THAN 3x q/q into Q3, with HBM4 OVER 60% OF THE HBM MIX IN 2H26** (put to management by the analyst and not contested). Growth: *"we haven't changed our estimate still for the next few years. We still believe that **at least a fifty to sixty percent growth rate** is expected to continue."* Wafer intensity: *"much larger chip size, H[BM]4 and 4E will drive much higher blended ASP and the wafer consumption. So… **we have to actually allocate more wafer capacity for HBM.** Also… **regardless of the profitability, we also committed that we will allocate more capacity to HBM**"* — the stated reason being *"in order to have very deep and broad relationship with leading AI companies like [[NVDA]], [[AMD]], [[AVGO]], AWS. We had to be the major supplier for HBM."* ➤ **"Regardless of the profitability" is a strategic-allocation statement, not an economic one, and it sits oddly against the margin-repricing confidence two bullets up. Both are management's words on the same call.**
- ⚠️ **AN IN-DOCUMENT INCONSISTENCY ON SAMSUNG'S OWN 2027 HBM SHARE TARGET — RECORDED, NOT RESOLVED.** First HBM answer: *"in second half of this year's HBM4, we believe that we already recover our market share to about **30% to 40%**. And even next year our HBM market share is expected to return to **the mid thirty to high thirty percent**, which is similar to the commodity leader market share."* Later answer, on HBM4E leadership, same speaker: *"next year we strongly believe that we can secure **at least mid to high twenty percent of market share. Which should be definitely similar to the overall market.**"* ➤ **Mid-to-high-30s and mid-to-high-20s cannot both be "similar to our commodity DRAM share." Either an ASR error or two different denominators (bits vs revenue, or HBM4-only vs all-HBM). ⚠️ DO NOT PICK ONE — this page already carries two houses putting [[SAMSUNG]] #1 in HBM in 2027 (08-18), and the choice between ~25% and ~37% materially changes whether that holds. Flagged for reconciliation.**
- **HBM4 CONFIGURATION, from the maker:** *"we will apply **one C** [1c DRAM core die] and sometimes **four** [nm logic] for base [die]… compared to our competitors, we will apply more advanced technologies."* ✅ Consistent with the ISSCC 2026 Samsung HBM4 paper in the historical backfill below.
- 🔴 **"3D HBM" — HBM STACKED DIRECTLY ON THE XPU, AND IT HAS ALREADY BEEN SHOWN TO CUSTOMERS.** *"One of the options is the **3D HBM**. It is the 3D stacked type. On top of the logic die, like the GPU… we are also trying to **stack the HBM on top of the GPU and CPU**. By doing so, we can expect much higher bandwidth and lower power consumption by reducing the interconnection between GPU and HBM. **Recently, we just provided this concept to our customer, and some leading GPU and AI players are quite interested in this solution**."* ✅ **This is the same object [[SAMSUNG]] presented at Hot Chips as "zHBM" (already on this page from the 08-25 21h run) — but the primary adds that it has been TAKEN TO CUSTOMERS and drawn interest, which the Hot Chips coverage did not carry. Packaging read-through on [cowos-packaging.md](cowos-packaging.md).**
- 🔴 **THE COMMODITY→FOUNDRY ARGUMENT, made explicitly by the producer.** *"if you look at current HBM and customized HBM products, it's quite obvious that **customers cannot replace the vendors if they started the design**… this kind of business, the framework and the types are **quite similar to the foundry**. If they start a design and place an order, definitely they cannot change the vendor… we are also expecting the memory industry will have much less [volatility] and low volatile capital discipline."* ➤ **The structural bull case in the seller's own words. Weigh it as such.**
- **DOWNSIDE PLAYBOOK — "SHARE FIRST", AND AN EQUIPMENT LTA AS THE HEDGE.** Asked what Samsung can do if demand shocks: *"currently we are having the **share first strategy**. It means on top of actual demand, we… **prepared the INFRASTRUCTURE ahead of bringing in the equipment**. It means that if we have more creative spaces [cleanroom], we can adequately and flexibly adjust to market dynamics… we also recently made a **long term agreement with [[AMAT]] and [other equipment] companies. We think that this [L]TA would act as a SAFETY NET** for us [to] respond to this market changes."* ➤ 🔴 **This is the MECHANISM behind the "capex is going into shell fab / infrastructure rather than equipment" line the 21h run took from the BofA relay — it is not caution about demand, it is an OPTION on demand: build the shell, defer the tool, and pre-contract the tool vendor so the option can be exercised fast. Full treatment on [semicap-wfe.md](semicap-wfe.md), where the [[AMAT]] LTA is the new item.**
- **NAND — TLC, NOT QLC, AND THE REASON IS KV CACHE.** Q3 NAND bit growth guided high-single-digit q/q after low-single-digit in Q2. *"most of our new production growth is coming from the **Xi'an fab**… process migration to [V]9 and [V]10. At this point in Korea, we don't have enough spaces to allocate the capacity toward [NAND]. So in Korea we are mostly allocating the premium spaces toward the D[RAM]."* On technology: *"**we are only working on TLC SSD, not QLC**… the **KV cache** needs mean that a lot of key GPU and hyperscalers are requiring high spec SSD products in order to **offload the data workload from the training from the HBM to commodity**… **not only [[NVDA]], but also ASIC and data center customers are placing very high volume orders for TLC SSD**. So we are also actually allocating more wafer capacity… to produce more TLC based SSD."* He does not dismiss QLC (*"we know the QLC market is also expected to be much greater"*) but says TLC momentum is *"much stronger"* near term. ⚠️ **This CUTS AGAINST the "TLC vs QLC is segmentation, not a race" framing this page took from the Solidigm co-CEO on 08-20 — the largest NAND maker says it is simply not working on QLC. Both stay.** China/tool-access half on [china-export.md](china-export.md).
- **FOUNDRY, for the read-through only** (name-level detail belongs on [../SAMSUNG.md](../SAMSUNG.md)): advanced node **>50% of foundry contribution this year**; **AI/HPC >30% of foundry vs high-teens last year**; **2nm HVM for Tesla next year**; 4nm NPUs; *"we also secured multiple hyperscalers orders on two nanometers"*; an **MOU with [[AVGO]]**; additional capacity under consideration on volume/backlog. And — new for [optical-cpo.md](optical-cpo.md) — **silicon photonics named as a MATURE-NODE growth driver: *"one of the examples is silicon photonics… we can also utilize our legacy node to address this silicon photonics market."***
- **CXMT, from the incumbent:** *"we are fully aware and do not dismiss this challenging competitor… [but] memory competitiveness cannot be evaluated on the capacity alone… especially high-density server products and HBM['s]. We still believe that there is a **huge gap between the existing vendor and Chinese vendor**. However… **Chinese competitors [are] the biggest threat to existing vendors**."* Response: *"we will double down on our R&D."* ✅ Same shape as [[MU]] management's read (Mizuho, 08-09, below): serious, but one-to-two generations behind and EUV-gated.
- **DEMAND, unprompted and flat:** *"if you look at the demand side, **we haven't seen any kind of slowdown signal so far**."*

### THE SECOND PRIMARY OF THE SAME DAY — UBS ON [[SKHYNIX]], AND IT SUPPLIES THE $/Gb ANCHOR THE ASP DEBATE HAS BEEN MISSING

**Source: UBS · Nicolas Gaudois, Jimmy Yoon, Luke Yoo — "First Read: SK Hynix — Key Takeaways from UBS Korea Summit 2026", 2026-08-25. Buy, 12m PT W3,000,000 (px W1,678,000 on 25-Aug); ADR PT US$210. Valuation basis: 4.32x NTM P/BV on a long-term ROE forecast of 49.2% and CoE 11.4%.** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/UBS_000660KS_First_Read_SK_Hynix_Key_Takeaways_from_UBS_Korea_Summi.html)

- 🔴🔴 **A LIKE-FOR-LIKE $/Gb SPREAD BETWEEN THE TWO KOREANS — THE FIRST TIME THIS PAGE HAS HAD ONE.** *"company DRAM ASPs, which were lower than peers' in 2Q26 (**UBSe $1.47 per Gb vs Samsung's $1.55**), **should close the gap into 2H26**."* ➤ **A ~5% [[SKHYNIX]]-vs-[[SAMSUNG]] DRAM ASP discount in 2Q26, on UBS's own estimates, closing in 2H26. Use this as the basis anchor whenever the +90%/+42% argument is re-run: it is UBS's own $/Gb work, so it can be placed against JPM's $/Gb industry series ($1.9 / $2.8 / $3.4 for 26/27/28E) without a units conversion.**
- 🔴 **UBS TREATS THE NVDA DE-SPEC AS DONE, AND SAYS IT IS BIT-NEUTRAL.** *"Regarding HBM, **de-specing is viewed as possible considering supply constraints. We believe Nvidia has moved to HBM4 8-Hi from HBM4E 12-Hi for VR/R300. This is NOT AFFECTING OVERALL DEMAND (more SiPs, less content per SiP).** Overall HBM demand is expected to be very strong in 2027. **HBM could remain undersupplied into 2030 or over.**"* ➤ **"More SiPs, less content per SiP" is the same offset SemiAnalysis's three-step decomposition (08-24, above) says CANNOT hold, because two of the three steps are design/generation decisions rather than availability ones. Same fact set, opposite conclusion, still open. UBS's "undersupplied into 2030 or over" is the longest-dated shortage call on this page.**
- **LTA MECHANICS — VOLUME ONLY, NO PRICE.** *"we maintain our view that **over 50% of bits will be LTA-covered by year-end. The main focus is on VOLUME VISIBILITY WITH NO PRICING FLOOR/CEILING.** Priority was given to negotiations with hyperscale customers, but large OEMs should also be included."* ✅ **Independently confirms the [[SAMSUNG]] IR line the 21h run flagged — "memory ASP remains negotiable on a quarterly basis even under LTAs." Two of three makers now say the LTA contracts VOLUME and leaves PRICE open. ➤ This page's "LTAs smoothen the cyclicality" framing should henceforth be treated as HALF TRUE: volume smooths, price does not — which is also why the 08-02 Samsung floor/ceiling asymmetry and the 08-15 [[MU]]-sold-its-ceiling / [[SNDK]]-kept-it split are the more informative marks.**
- **CAPEX — THE 2027 NUMBER, AND IT IS THE DRAM LEG OF THE MS WFE RAISE.** **UBSe [[SKHYNIX]] 2027 capex W61tn, +30% y/y, "and stay elevated for some time." Yongin 1 starts taking in equipment FEBRUARY 2027, second cleanroom likely later in the year, first phase DRAM-focused.** Process roadmap: *"SK Hynix may continue **6F² til 0a nm** (we believe still TBC vs. 4F²), then move to **4F² for several nodes til 3D DRAM**."* NAND: *"capacity could be added in **Dalian on floating gate** process tech, which over time may move to **240L from 144/192L**. **M17 (NAND) is expected to start MP in 1H29.**"* ✅ **The 6F²-until-0a-then-4F² sequencing corroborates ASM International management's 08-20 "4F² slipping… some of it in 2028 but not broad-based" on [semicap-wfe.md](semicap-wfe.md) — from the customer side rather than the tool side. And the Dalian line is the SKH half of the DIGITIMES China-NAND item on [china-export.md](china-export.md).**
- **SHAREHOLDER RETURNS:** the **W40tn buyback is part of the overall >50% FCF return**; the change from *"up to 50%"* to *"more than 50%"* **does imply 50% is the FLOOR for 2025-2027**; SK Hynix **may support the stock price with additional buybacks within the policy, announced as such**; more detail at the 3Q26 results call; ADR liquidity to be increased over time subject to regulatory approval.
- **UBS vs consensus, for the reconciliation:** EPS (W, diluted) **12/26E 395,021 vs cons 353,128 (+11.9%); 12/27E 598,302 vs 463,642 (+29.0%); 12/28E 735,840 vs 493,869 (+49.0%).** EBIT margin **78.4% / 82.1% / 79.7%** for 26/27/28E; equity FCF yield **15.0% / 25.1% / 29.2%**. **Named near-term catalyst: ~18 September 2026 — "monthly smartphone sell-through numbers, pricing negotiation finalization, NT supply chain color on GPU demand, further updates on shareholder returns."**

### THE THIRD LEG — SEMIANALYSIS'S DE-SPEC VIEW, RELAYED BY MIZUHO, AND IT EXTENDS THE CUT TO **FEYNMAN**

⚠️ **PUBLISHER CORRECTION — this file circulates as a SemiAnalysis note. It is not one.** It is a **Mizuho Securities USA industry report (Vijay Rakesh, David Liu, Jacob Grandstaff, Rafal Tworkowski), 2026-08-24**, written up from a **dinner Mizuho hosted with SemiAnalysis President Doug O'Laughlin**. **Attribute as *Mizuho relaying SemiAnalysis*; the price targets and the shipment-uplift estimate are Mizuho's own, not SA's.** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/SemiAnalysis__Memory_De-Spec_WFE_800V_Uncertainty_with_Cop.html)

- 🔴🔴 **THE NEW SKU IN THE DE-SPEC STORY IS *FEYNMAN*, NOT RUBIN ULTRA — AND THE MAGNITUDE IS 50%.** *"Memory key bottleneck, while aggregate DRAM demand higher, **potentially significant Feynman de-specing (50%)**, and **16-20Hi HBM / hybrid bonding UNCERTAIN for future gens**, could impact WFE."* Expanded: *"**SA noted GPU/ASIC OEMs could CAP FUTURE HBM CONTENT AT 4-8Hi with 12Hi less appealing**, and 16Hi/20Hi and hybrid bonding uncertain for future DRAM gen."* ➤ **This page has been arguing about ONE SKU's configuration. This says the CEILING ITSELF moves down a generation later — a roadmap claim, not a procurement claim, and a far more consequential one.** ⚠️⚠️ **IT ALSO CONTRADICTS MIZUHO'S OWN 08-16 AI SERVER CALL ALREADY ON THIS PAGE (Feynman 768GB/GPU HBM4e/16-Hi). SAME HOUSE, SAME LEAD ANALYST, EIGHT DAYS APART, OPPOSITE DIRECTION. Logged as an open internal inconsistency in Mizuho's own work — do not net them, and do not quietly retire the 08-16 mark.**
- 🔴 **A SECOND NAMED PROGRAM ALREADY CUT, from Mizuho's own checks rather than from SA: "Our checks indicate **[[GOOG]] has also scaled down TPUv8 HBM3e to 8Hi.**"** ➤ **The de-spec is now sighted on an ASIC as well as on a merchant GPU. Read with [custom-asic-tpu.md](custom-asic-tpu.md).**
- 🔴 **THE OFFSET, QUANTIFIED FOR THE FIRST TIME.** *"SA noted a diminished HBM **'trade ratio 3:1 to 5:1 DRAM'** constraint increases planar DDR5/LPDDR5 supply; **we believe the lower DRAM/GPU content could allow ~20-30% MORE GPU/ASIC SHIPMENTS**, driving 'higher aggregate DRAM bit demand for Training/Inference, KVCache' and **better GPU/ASIC margins**."* ➤ **This is the numeric version of UBS's "more SiPs, less content per SiP", and the swing factor for whether the de-spec is bit-neutral.** ⚠️ **It is MIZUHO'S estimate, not SA's, and nothing on this page yet tests it. Note also that the trade-ratio direction is the OPPOSITE of BMO's (3:1 → 3.5:1-4:1 with next-gen HBM, 08-20) and of [[MU]] management's own 3:1-at-HBM3E → ~4:1-by-HBM4E (08-15): those describe the ratio RISING with stack height; this describes it FALLING because stack height is being capped. Same variable, opposite sign, because the underlying assumption about stack height differs. Do not average.**
- **PT ACTIONS, all attributed to multiple compression rather than estimate cuts:** **[[MU]] $1,375 → $1,300** (~5x F27E P/B from ~5.3x), **[[SNDK]] $1,900 → $1,875** (~9x F27E EPS), **[[AMAT]] $650 → $590**, **MKSI $360 → $330**, **[[LRCX]] $370 → $365**. *"We still see memory tight, and aggregate DRAM memory and KVCache NAND demand higher."* **[[SNDK]] specifics: GM staying >80% with its NBM agreements; EPS growing 5x F26-F28E; "potential for SNDK to buy back 25-30% of the company with aggregate $30-50B of FCF 2027-28E."** WFE half on [semicap-wfe.md](semicap-wfe.md); 800V and optics halves on [800v-dc-power.md](800v-dc-power.md) and [optical-cpo.md](optical-cpo.md).

### AND THE MICRON MANAGEMENT LEG — MIZUHO'S 08-09 INVESTOR MEETINGS, WHICH THIS PAGE HAD NOT PICKED UP

**Source: Mizuho · Vijay Rakesh — "[[MU]]: Takeaways — Supply Tight into 2028E, 80%+ GMs Sustainable; Thoughts on SCAs, CXMT, HBF", 2026-08-09. Investor meetings hosted with MU CEO Sanjay Mehrotra and Samir Patodia (Sr. Director, IR). Outperform, PT $1,375 (px $877.57) — ⚠️ superseded to $1,300 on 08-24 above.** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Takeaways_-_Supply_Tight_into_2028E_80_GMs_Sustainable_T.html)

- 🔴 **THE SCA STRUCTURE, SIZED — AND IT COMPLETES A THREE-MAKER PATTERN.** *"MU's **~16 SCAs** cover broad end-markets including Cloud/Consumer/Automotive/Industrial"*; SCAs cover **>50% of bits through C30E** and *"can sustain **80%+ margins** with premium for new products"*; and the important half — *"**~60% (non-SCA and floating-px SCA) bits exposed to PRICING UPSIDE**."* ➤ **Third maker, same structure. [[SAMSUNG]] 30-40% of capacity deliberately open (above); [[SKHYNIX]] "over 50%" LTA-covered at year-end, i.e. up to half open (UBS, above); [[MU]] ~60% of bits exposed to price. The industry is NOT hedging its price — it is hedging its volume, and it is doing so simultaneously. That is a materially more bullish reading of rising contract coverage than "LTAs cap the upside."**
- **CAPEX / SUPPLY TIMING: F27E capex ~$50B, up 67% y/y, "focused on construction of front-end capacity"; C27E WFE "could potentially focus more on building out FAB SHELLS for greenfield in US, TW, SG, and JP"; "MU's first greenfield bits will still be SMALL in 2028E, following line/customer qualifications."** ✅ **Exactly the shells-before-tools pattern [[SAMSUNG]] IR describes above. Two of three makers on the record with it, which is a real constraint on how fast a 2028 down-leg can physically arrive — and a direct cross-check on the DRAM leg of MS's WFE raise ([semicap-wfe.md](semicap-wfe.md)).**
- 🔴 **HBF vs HBM, WITH BOTH SIDES PRICED:** *"[[GOOG]]/Tenstorrent partnering with [[SNDK]]/Hynix on HBF showing **3Tbps bandwidth (vs HBM ~1.6-2Tbps)**, potentially **HBF ~$1/GB, vs HBM4e at ~$30/GB**, still HBF challenges with **endurance/latency, R/W errors/write speed**, versus HBM unparalleled R/W speeds / NO errors. But AI tiered memory will see HBF demand with context lengths / KVcache demand."* ⚠️⚠️ **A ~30x $/GB gap is the widest substitution spread this page carries, and it must be handled like the eSSD-vs-HDD 17-20x gap already here: a 30x price gap does NOT imply substitution, it implies TIERING. Do not net HBF $/GB against HBM $/GB inside any TAM.** **MU also *"still sees CXL memory pooling 2-3 YEARS AWAY"*** — read against [cxl-memory-fabric.md](cxl-memory-fabric.md).
- **DE-SPEC — MU's own read, and it is Position 3 again:** *"concerns around AI GPU/Accelerator customers de-specing — such as **NVDA VR 50% lower LPDDR5** and potentially lower HBM — which we see as **more of a RESPONSE TO SHIPPING MORE AI trg/inference GPU/ASIC to satisfy tight compute**, while balancing memory supply/content."* Headline verdict: **"investor concerns around de-specing overblown."**
- **CXMT, MU's read:** *"serious player, but lags technology with **no EUV**"* — (1) focused on domestic China handset/PC OEMs, (2) *"still lags by **1-2 generations on HBM3** while industry at 4/4e"*, (3) *"**lacks KEY EUV capability** for advanced DRAM/HBM."* (Mizuho-HK separately covers CXMT at Buy, PT CNY70.)
- **Mizuho MU model, for the reconciliation:** FY26E EPS **$73.12**; FY27E **$139.55 vs street $152.74 (−8.6%)**; FY28E **$172.59 vs street $165.54 (+4.3%)**; FY27E GM **86.4%**, FY28E **83.0%**; HBM growing **169% y/y in F26E**. Upside case **$1,750** (~7x upside F27E P/B); downside case **$1,050** (~4x). ⚠️ **The note labels the $1,050 case "our bull case PT" inside its own Bear Case paragraph — an internal typo; it is the downside case. Flagged so the number is not mis-copied.**

## Historical backfill (Mar-Jun 2026, DAMNANG Substack archive) — ⚠️ **DATED CONTEXT ONLY. NOTHING HERE SUPERSEDES ANY CURRENT MARK ABOVE.**

⚠️ **DAMNANG (Substack) — an INDEPENDENT KOREAN SEMICONDUCTOR SUBSTACK, NOT A BROKER: no rating, no price target and no formal estimate anywhere in the corpus. Source identity, launch date, subscriber base and the DAMNANG / Damnang2 / "The Optical Edge" handle collapse are recorded ONCE, on [optical-cpo.md](optical-cpo.md).** **His 2026-08-20 "HBM Density Peak" was already ingested here on 2026-08-21 and is deliberately NOT repeated.** **The value of this block is that it dates how an informed independent voice framed each of this page's live debates two to five months before the current sources arrived — and, where the debate has since moved, whether he was right.**

- 🔴 **THE DE-SPEC ARGUMENT WAS MADE — AND ITS MECHANISM CORRECTLY IDENTIFIED — TWO AND A HALF MONTHS EARLY, ON A DIFFERENT COMPONENT.** **DAMNANG (Substack) — "Vera Rubin's DRAM Cut in Half: Is the Memory Cycle Really Over?", 2026-06-08.** On the 06-04 SemiAnalysis report that NVIDIA was lowering the **SOCAMM** standard build: *"the standard build, which had been expected to center on **192GB modules, shifts to 96GB modules, and CPU-side memory per rack drops from roughly 54TB to 28TB, cut in half**."* The arithmetic he shows: **8 SOCAMM modules per Vera CPU × 36 Vera CPUs per rack** → 192GB ⇒ ~54TB (the official reference spec), 96GB ⇒ ~28TB. **The stated cause was supply and cost — LPDDR5X supply not keeping up — and the choice was "to get racks installed first rather than delaying rack shipments over memory, leaving the high-capacity build as a CUSTOM OPTION."** Rack cost falls **~$7.6M → ~$6.8M**, and TCO per GPU with it. His framing at the time: *"**54TB is the number that says how far it can go, and 28TB is the report of how it actually gets installed within that constraint**"*, and *"what the market priced in and what the SemiAnalysis report actually said were not the same thing."* ✅✅ **This is the SAME "supply-driven de-spec is not demand destruction" argument [[SAMSUNG]] IR, [[MU]] management, UBS and Mizuho all made on 2026-08-24/25 — recorded independently on 2026-06-08, on LPDDR5X rather than HBM. And Mizuho's 08-09 "NVDA VR 50% lower LPDDR5" above is the SAME EVENT, which retro-confirms the June account. ➤ The current de-spec debate is the second instance of a pattern this corpus already documented once.** ⚠️ *"NVIDIA has announced nothing"* was true then, and the official reference spec still listed up to 54TB.
- **THE HBM4 TECHNOLOGY CLAIM THE SAMSUNG IR CALL RESTATED FIVE MONTHS LATER.** **DAMNANG (Substack) — "How Samsung Overtakes SK hynix in HBM4: An ISSCC 2026 Paper Review", 2026-03-20**, a review — written with the author's permission — of Samsung's ISSCC 2026 paper *"A 36GB 3.3TB/s HBM4 DRAM with Per-Channel TSV RDQS Auto Calibration and Fully-Programmable MBIST."* **Samsung HBM4 as presented: 12-layer stack, 36GB, 2,048 I/O pins, 3.3TB/s (+260% vs HBM3E), max 13Gb/s per pin — against a JEDEC JESD270-4 ceiling of 6.4Gbps/pin and 2TB/s total, i.e. >2.0x the standard.** The two-sided generational jump: **base die moved off a DRAM process onto Samsung Foundry 4nm FinFET (VDDQ 1.1V → 0.75V, −32%), and the core die SKIPPED 1b entirely, going 1a → 1c.** ✅ **Management confirmed exactly this configuration on 2026-08-25 ("one C… and sometimes four for base"). A five-month-old paper review since validated by the company itself — and the pin-speed headroom (13Gb/s demonstrated vs 6.4Gb/s standard) is the same headroom the Jalapeño teardown observes on the other side of the trade (~10Gbps HBM4 vs NVDA's 9.6Gbps, [custom-asic-tpu.md](custom-asic-tpu.md)).**
- **WHY HBM CROWDS OUT COMMODITY DRAM — the wafer-intensity number this page uses implicitly.** **DAMNANG (Substack) — "Why HBM Is So Hard to Make", 2026-03-20:** *"On a bit-for-bit basis, HBM consumes roughly **two to three times the wafer area of conventional DRAM**… When fabs allocate capacity to HBM, DDR5, LPDDR and GDDR7 all get squeezed."* ⚠️ **His own caveat: "an industry estimate based on die size and process overhead, not a precise published figure."** Also: **a 12-layer HBM needs dies thinned to ~50µm, 16-layer to ~30µm** ("less than half the thickness of a human hair"), at which point wafers **crack and bow**; **a 12-layer stack contains millions of TSVs and one bad connection kills the die**; and the forward-looking line — **"this base die can carry customer-specific logic. That means [[NVDA]]'s HBM4 and [[AMD]]'s HBM4 will be PHYSICALLY DIFFERENT PRODUCTS. Memory is transitioning from a commodity component to a semi-custom one."** ✅ **Same conclusion [[SAMSUNG]] IR reached on 08-25 ("quite similar to the foundry… they cannot change the vendor") — five months earlier, and derived from the process side rather than the commercial side.**
- **THE "NO LONGER CYCLICAL" THESIS, SOURCED TO AN [[SKHYNIX]] EXECUTIVE.** **DAMNANG (Substack) — "Memory is no longer a cyclical industry", 2026-03-25**, written up from a ~20-minute seminar by **SK hynix's North America regional president at the Silicon Valley K-ASIC Seminar on 2026-03-24**, plus a short 1-on-1 afterwards. The argument: the three makers no longer answer strong demand with aggressive capacity because *"they've all paid for it"*; priority has shifted from share-through-volume to **profitability, capital efficiency and customer mix**; and long-term supply agreements are *"a signal that memory companies are managing supply far more strategically than before… a mechanism for reducing uncertainty and stabilizing supply chains, not just a sales vehicle."* ⚠️⚠️ **The author's OWN disclaimer is the right way to weight it: "Given that the underlying perspective comes from someone inside the memory industry, the content may lean bullish on memory."** ➤ **Five months on, [[SAMSUNG]] IR made the identical argument on 08-25 in almost the same words ("much less and low volatile capital discipline"). Two producers, five months apart, one thesis — which is evidence about the INDUSTRY'S NARRATIVE, not independent confirmation of it. File it as such.**
- **HBF, DISSECTED BEFORE IT BECAME A TAM LINE.** **DAMNANG (Substack) — "HBF's Limits and Reality: Between Promise and Structural Obstacles (H³ Paper Review)", 2026-03-18**, reviewing the SK hynix H³ paper. The promise: **stack NAND with TSVs like HBM for ~16x HBM capacity (~3TB) at equivalent bandwidth (~8TB/s), NAND at ~1/5 the cost of HBM**; SK hynix and SanDisk targeting **sampling in late 2026**. The objections, written by a test/DFT engineer: the architecture needs a **40MB SRAM Latency Hiding Buffer** plus DRAM and complex controllers to mask NAND's **~20µs** access latency (one-to-two orders of magnitude worse than HBM's ~100ns), *"which undermines the initial promise of 'cheap NAND'"*; the performance claims rest on a **read-only, deterministic, prefetchable workload assumption** that is *"very limited in actual LLM inference scenarios"*; and **yield, thermal management and reliability validation are "higher than expected."** Sizing context he used: **Llama 3.1 405B ≈ 405GB in FP8 against 192GB of HBM on a B200; its KV cache alone reaches ~540GB at 1M tokens and ~5.4TB at 10M.** ➤ **Set against Mizuho's 08-09 relay above (3Tbps vs 1.6-2Tbps; ~$1/GB vs ~$30/GB; "endurance/latency, R/W errors/write speed"): the objections are IDENTICAL five months apart and none has been retired. HBF's bandwidth/price case has strengthened; its latency/endurance case has not moved at all.**
- **HYBRID BONDING — the technology whose fate the 16-Hi question now decides.** **DAMNANG (Substack) — "Hybrid Bonding, Fully Dissected", 2026-03-28**, written from direct interviews with engineers at [[SAMSUNG]], [[SKHYNIX]] and [[MU]]. The mechanism: hybrid bonding removes the solder bump entirely (bare copper pads in an SiO₂/SiCN dielectric; the dielectrics bond first at low temperature, then an anneal drives Cu-Cu), taking **bump pitch from ~40µm to below 10µm and ultimately sub-micron**, and collapsing the inter-die gap. The constraint he identified: *"That's fine when you're stacking four dies. **When you need to stack 12, 16, or eventually 20, those accumulated gaps become a hard constraint on total package height.**"* ➤ 🔴 **That sentence is the whole of today's back-end debate, written five months early: hybrid bonding's economics are a FUNCTION OF STACK HEIGHT. If SA/Mizuho are right that the industry caps at 4-8Hi, the technology's pull-in date moves out — which is precisely the transmission mechanism the MS desk flagged into [[BESI]]/CAMT/ONTO/[[TER]] on 08-25 (see [semicap-wfe.md](semicap-wfe.md)). The de-spec question and the hybrid-bonding question are the same question.**
- **THE PRICING-POWER FRAME, and one hard BOM number.** **DAMNANG (Substack) — "Will the Memory Stock Rally Keep Going? Scenario Analysis Beyond the HBM War", 2026-05-11.** The framing: *"What determines the next round of memory stock performance is **not how much HBM gets sold, but at what price**."* Marks as of May: **[[SKHYNIX]] 72% operating margin in a single quarter; [[MU]] locking 3-to-5-year supply agreements including its FIRST 5-YEAR SCA (disclosed on the March 2026 call); and [[SAMSUNG]] had a quarter where CONVENTIONAL DRAM PROFITABILITY EXCEEDED HBM.** ⚠️ **That last one is the May-dated ancestor of the 08-25 Samsung IR line "HBM margin level is way lower than the commodity DRAM" — the inversion was visible three and a half months before management confirmed it and explained the cause. It is the single strongest piece of corroboration in this backfill.** Third-party BOM anchor he cites: **Epoch AI's external model putting an [[NVDA]] B200/B300 unit cost at ~$6,400.** ⚠️ **A third-party estimate, not a disclosure — and it is a UNIT COST, not a price. Do not chain it against the $78-80K Rubin ASP series already on this page.** Demand context used at the time: **Big Tech 4 2026 capex ~$725bn, +~77% y/y** (⚠️ a May-dated projection, superseded by the current marks on [hyperscaler-capex.md](hyperscaler-capex.md)).
- **CXMT, dated April, for the TRAJECTORY rather than the level.** **DAMNANG (Substack) — "Is CXMT a Threat or an Illusion? DDR5, HBM, 3D DRAM, and the Equipment Ecosystem", 2026-04-11.** **Wafer output ~40,000 wpm in 2020 → ~720,000 wafers per QUARTER by end-2025, across three 12-inch fabs.** Process: **pushed to its G4 node on DUV multi-patterning alone; TechInsights puts G4 feature size at ~16nm, a ~20% cell-size reduction vs G3 — a level the Big 3 were in volume production at around 2018-19, i.e. a five-to-six-year gap.** Product: **DDR5 already shipping inside Lenovo laptops; LPDDR5X ~30% of the Chinese smartphone market.** He flags **3D DRAM as the place "the entire competitive landscape gets redrawn,"** citing separate conversations with SK hynix's Americas head and a current ASML employee, and Seoul National University's Prof. Hwang Cheol-seong on **CXMT potentially reaching 11nm-class DRAM without EUV.** ⚠️ **His Shanghai IPO target valuation of ~$42bn is SUPERSEDED — CXMT has since listed; see [china-export.md](china-export.md) for the live marks. The durable content is the capacity trajectory, the DUV-only process claim and the 3D-DRAM warning.**
- **AND A DATED REMINDER OF WHAT A MEMORY DRAWDOWN LOOKS LIKE INSIDE THIS CYCLE.** **DAMNANG (Substack) — "Is SanDisk Done?", 2026-03-26**, written into a **[[SNDK]] fall from $772 to $623 — ~20% in a week, ~$20bn of market cap, in a stock that had risen 25x in a year.** The catalyst stack he decomposed: WD's secondary of its residual SanDisk stake (~5.82m shares at $545, closed 19 Feb); a **20 March lock-up expiry** freeing >2m insider shares; **[[MU]]'s capex commentary** spreading NAND-supply fear; a **$1B [[SNDK]] equity investment in Taiwan's Nanya**; and **Google's "TurboQuant" announcement** triggering an *"AI needs less memory"* selloff across the whole complex. ⚠️ *"BofA, in the middle of this selloff, raised its price target from $850 to $900. The market and the analysts are looking in opposite directions."* ➤ **Filed because the March "AI needs less memory" panic and the August de-spec panic are the SAME TRADE with a different headline — and the March one fully retraced (his own 2026-05-22 scorecard marks SNDK +145.2% from the 03-26 piece). That is a base rate, not a forecast.**
