Any 2027 model extrapolating the Q3 y/y print is extrapolating the comp.**

**ARCHITECTURE — the roadmap beyond HBM4E is now on the record (Hot Chips 2026, 2026-08-25).** [[SAMSUNG]] laid out **standard HBM → custom HBM (cHBM) → zHBM**: cHBM puts **advanced logic on the base die** and swaps the HBM PHY for a compact **D2D** interface (with a **Heat Path Block over 50% of the PHY area cutting peak temperature >35%**); **zHBM stacks HBM vertically on the XPU, eliminating the 2.5D interposer and the SerDes — claimed 70% higher power efficiency, 230% greater DRAM bandwidth vs a standard HBM4e stack, and 8.3% more power freed for the XPU.** [[SKHYNIX]]'s own session was **packaging-led**, comparing the mechanical/thermal stresses that **CoWoS-S, CoWoS-L and [[INTC]] EMIB** impose on HBM stacks and interposers — and separately **SK hynix CONFIRMED it will use Intel EMIB for next-gen HBM, "mainly due to the continued shortage of TSMC's CoWoS capacity"** (@dnystedt, corpus, 2026-08-25). **BofA's synthesis: "memory (not compute) is now the key constraint and enabler of AI systems… core HBM demand intact, extends with 3D DRAM ('zHBM'); HBF / logic-on-DRAM also TAM adders long-term; migration to new architectures INCREASES WFE INTENSITY"** (BofA / Vivek Arya, 2026-08-25). ⚠️ **All vendor claims, no third-party benchmarks, no MP dates.**

**SUPPLY RESPONSE — China keeps getting louder.** **YMTC filed a $4.9bn Shanghai IPO: Q1 revenue $7.0bn (above all of 2024), core NAND revenue nearly 5x y/y, GROSS MARGIN 35% → 77%** (WSJ via JPM, 2026-08-25). **[[SAMSUNG]] and [[SKHYNIX]] are stepping up equipment spending at their CHINA NAND plants through 2027** (DIGITIMES, 2026-08-25 — ⚠️ *headline + lede only*). **CXMT is sold out and has the pricing power to refuse [[AAPL]]:** *"Apple reportedly negotiated supply prices for mobile DRAM including LPDDR5X with CXMT… but was REJECTED for a price reduction. Instead CXMT insisted on prices HIGHER OR SIMILAR to those offered by Samsung or SK Hynix"* — because Huawei/Xiaomi have pre-secured volume through high-priced long-term contracts (Korean trade press via Jefferies, 2026-08-25). **CXMT is also named as the key LPDDR6 supplier for Xiaomi's XRING O3, "the world's first flagship mobile SoC to support LPDDR6"** (@jukan05 / Cailian Press, corpus, 2026-08-25).

SUSQUEHANNA'S CHANNEL PRICING RUNS *ABOVE ITS OWN PUBLISHED ESTIMATES* ON BOTH DRAM AND NAND. AND MICRON PUT THE NUMBER ON WHY HBM EATS THE INDUSTRY'S WAFERS: HBM3E IS ~3x THE SILICON OF DDR5 FOR THE SAME BITS *AND THAT RATIO IS GETTING WORSE*.**

- 🔴🔴 **THE SPEC-CUT ATTRIBUTION, WHICH RESOLVES THE BIGGEST OPEN QUESTION ON THIS PAGE. Supplier side (JPM · Jay Kwon w/ Sangsik Lee, Neelay Kamath, Mio Shikanai, "Key Takeaways from JPM Asia Tech Tour (Seoul Part): Memory Semiconductors", 2026-08-23 — [[SAMSUNG]], [[SKHYNIX]], [[KIOXIA]] and Nanya hosted Aug 20-21, 50+ investors):** *"The dominant message from DRAM makers was that the 12-Hi to 8-Hi HBM spec adjustment or SOCAMM content downgrade was driven by INSUFFICIENT SUPPLY (availability issue) rather than the intent to de-spec (willingness to take performance degradation).   **Customer side, independently, the next day (JPM · Harlan Sur, [[NVDA]] F2Q27 preview, 2026-08-24):** **Rubin has two SKU variations at 288GB *or* 192GB HBM4; Rubin Ultra moves from 16-Hi HBM4E to 8-Hi/12-Hi HBM4E; and the Vera CPU's SOCAMM content is cut 50% to 768GB from 1.5TB** — *"driven almost exclusively by supply tightness, where in order to fulfill compute volume commitments to customers, NVDA has had to scale back memory capacity due to a shortage of available supply."* 🔴 **Two independent legs, supplier and customer, same conclusion. This retires the demand-destruction reading and converts the spec cut into a supply datapoint.** SKU mix outcome remains *"not entirely evident."*
- 🔴 **PRICING, RUNNING ABOVE THE HOUSE'S OWN NUMBERS (SIG · Mehdi Hosseini w/ Bastien Faucon-Morin, "Technology Hardware — Memory Update: Intra-Quarter Pricing Update", 2026-08-24).** **DRAM: 3Q26 contract +50% q/q on average, range 30-70% (materially wider than 2Q26)**, against a **2Q26 actual blended +45% q/q**; **blended DDR contract +~50% q/q** with **DDR4 demand "surprisingly strong"** on legacy CPUs; **≥70% of bits now shipping into hyperscale/enterprise**, more than offsetting softer consumer; **4Q26 blended DRAM ASP ≥+20% q/q, "above the assumptions embedded in our published estimates."** **NAND: 3Q26 contract +60% q/q vs the ~35% embedded in SIG's published estimates**, 2Q26 actual **+65%**; **contract eSSD ASPs ~+70% q/q, "significantly outpacing price gains reflected in the spot market"**; **4Q26 NAND ≥+25% q/q, also above published.** Ratings: **Samsung / [[MU]] / [[SNDK]] Positive; SKHYNIX Neutral; STX / WDC / HPE Neutral; SMCI Negative; DELL Positive.**
- **SIG's rebuttal of the channel-inventory bear, verbatim:** *"hyperscaler demand remains robust, while elevated DOI at channel and module houses largely reflect the higher-cost inventory embedded in their balance sheets rather than weakening end demand… they are likely to maximize pricing leverage later in the quarter."* And the OEM pass-through: *"enterprise demand appears to be strengthening, with customers demonstrating a greater willingness to pay premiums through OEM channels. We expect this trend to be reflected in upcoming earnings reports by the likes of [[DELL]], [[HPE]], NetApp, and Pure Storage."*
- 🔴 **HBM's WAFER TAX, QUANTIFIED BY THE VENDOR (Hot Chips 2026, speaker Ragu, Micron HBM Design Architecture — via TMT Breakout 2026-08-23, viksnewsletter 2026-08-24, jasonschips 2026-08-24; ⚠️ conference-coverage newsletters, NOT broker research):** **"MU has previously quantified HBM3E at roughly 3x the silicon of DDR5 for the same bits. Ragu said that ratio is not improving and is likely getting worse as bandwidth increases."** **Memory can be ~90% of the silicon in a high-end AI package** — four 12-high stacks ⇒ **memory silicon ≈ 8x GPU silicon** — and **one failing DRAM die can affect the entire package.** Reliability is already visible in production: **META's published Llama 3 work attributed ~17% of unintended training interruptions to HBM.** **16-high looks achievable; beyond that "less clear"** (JEDEC has discussed 20-high) with the binding constraints being **TSV density, power density, thermals, mechanical integrity and yield — not signalling distance.** Bandwidth arithmetic: **HBM3E 128 banks/die → HBM4 256; ~1,000 → ~2,000 I/O on roughly the same shoreline; HBM1 128 GB/s & 1GB → HBM4 >2.8 TB/s & >24GB per cube.** Packaging shifting **from micro-bumps and thermal-compression bonding toward fusion and hybrid bonding at single-digit-micron pitches.** Forward: HBM likely becomes **workload-specific**, with processor and memory co-optimised.
- **The independent supply-side framing (Jim Handy, Objective Analysis, same conference):** **HBM yields roughly one-third the gigabytes per wafer of standard DDR**; DRAM suppliers went **more than a decade without major capacity additions** because shrinks sufficed; **new fabs take ~2 years even on aggressive timelines.** His anti-Jevons line, which is the cleanest rebuttal to the efficiency bear: *"If an algorithm improves efficiency by 6x, the result may be 6x more output rather than lower capex."* Spot *"risen roughly 7x from prior levels"* ⚠️ with his own caveat that *"spot is an imperfect industry indicator."*
- 🔴 **SAMSUNG'S "ZHBM" IS THE MOST AGGRESSIVE ARCHITECTURAL CLAIM MADE AT THE CONFERENCE (speaker: Sanghan, Samsung Memory Business):** a three-stage roadmap turning the HBM base die from a communications layer into a customised AI platform, culminating in **stacking HBM directly on the XPU and ELIMINATING the 2.5D interposer.** Targets **~0.5 pJ/bit, ~70% lower total DRAM power vs HBM5, >2.3x higher DRAM bandwidth**; **absolute DRAM power could fall >100W**, freeing it for GPU compute; **moving the memory controller into HBM could reclaim 5-10% of XPU area.** ⚠️ **Samsung's own stated constraint: thermal limits currently point to roughly FOUR-high stacks, not 12- or 16-high.**
- **LTA coverage, disclosed side by side for the first time (JPM · Kwon, 2026-08-23):** **[[SAMSUNG]] max ~70%** (highest in the complex, duration up to 5 years) · **[[SKHYNIX]] >50%** (up to 5 years) · **[[KIOXIA]] 50% of '27-28 volume** (contracts to FY28, FY29/30 in discussion) · **Nanya 50%+ but <70%** (majority sub-1-year today). *"Memory makers unanimously guided that the contract terms were favorable to the memory suppliers with sizable prepayment terms and stronger binding to both parties."* JPM's view: *"LTAs serve a greater value in reducing the industry's cyclicality and would pave the path to a valuation re-rate."* Also: **LTA discussions are now happening between memory suppliers and EQUIPMENT vendors** — read-through to themes/semicap-wfe.
- 🔴 **HBM PRICING, AND A STRATEGIC DIVERGENCE BETWEEN THE TWO KOREANS THAT IS NEW TO THIS PAGE:** *"Investors found SEC's HBM pricing and wallet share commentary more aggressive vs SKH prioritizing strategic partnerships and presenting a rather balanced approach."* Both have **2027E HBM ASP negotiations "under review (at the later part of the process)"**, weighing (a) market conditions incl. the non-HBM DRAM margin gap, (b) next-gen HBM cost structure, (c) HBM performance. **JPM sees UPSIDE RISK to its industry HBM ASP growth assumption of +43% y/y** and calls better pricing a source of EPS upward revision.
- **Demand-mix facts worth carrying:** **agentic-AI-led CPU demand is driving server DRAM content growth to 30%+ versus a historical 15-20%**; **server-grade DRAM and NAND carry a 30-40% ASP premium over consumer**; **spot rebounded +9% (DRAM) and +15% (NAND) off the July bottom**; Kioxia maintains **~20% q/q blended ASP growth for CY3Q26**; **Nanya's new AI-application memory goes from a single-digit % sales mix in 2027E to as high as double-digit % in 2028E at a profitability profile similar to mainstream DRAM.** **[[MU]]'s CEO: Micron can meet only "about 50% to about two-thirds" of key customers' demand relative to its supply in many cases**, and separately *"sees 'no end' to when memory supply will catch up with demand"* (Kalshi via @dnystedt, 2026-08-23).
- ⚠️ **HBF IS BEING TALKED DOWN BY THE SUPPLIERS THEMSELVES, three weeks after the FMS announcements:** *"we sensed a relative conservatism citing the lack of interest from customers and a clear value proposition… many view that the customers are mainly viewing the TLC-based solution to be the primary option in the foreseeable future. - **Supply timing, dated:** **new Korean NAND cleanroom space arrives no earlier than 2029E — one or two phases of Samsung's Pyungtaek Fab 5 versus SK hynix's M17.** Near-term bit growth is **migration-driven for 12-18 months**, and **higher wafer allocation to HBM is itself a supply constraint via the wafer-to-die penalty.** ⚠️ **The NAND bear is investor-side, not supplier-side:** *"investors expressed greater concerns on NAND… turning conservative on 2027 S-D citing YMTC capacity expansion and consumer NAND degradation"*, with JPM's counter being that **eSSD demand fully offsets consumer weakness.**
- **Two new entrants to the memory-supply question:** **[[SPCX]]'s Terafab is hiring Memory Design AND Memory Integration Engineers** — UBS: *"this confirms Terafab's willingness to kick-start its internal memory manufacturing efforts"*, but *"we are unclear where Terafab would source memory IP and see limited incentives for existing memory suppliers to license their IP"* (UBS · Timothy Arcuri, 2026-08-24). And **GS initiated CXMT at Buy (12M TP Rmb129)** as the *"China DRAM leader"* (GS · Allen Chang, 2026-08-23), against Nanya's argument that **CXMT "has now been present in the market for well over a decade without materially disrupting global S/D, in large part because AI has been absorbing the very supply that would otherwise serve consumer end-markets."**

## Recent signals (Aug 20, /wiki-ingest) — 🔴🔴 **THE CAPITAL-RETURN WAVE GETS SIZED AGAINST FCF AND THE ANSWER IS THAT IT IS *NOT* THE END OF IT: ~W45trn OF [[SKHYNIX]] BUDGET IS STILL UNSPENT THIS YEAR AND [[SAMSUNG]] IS BEING QUOTED AT KRW100-150trn. MEANWHILE SPOT KEEPS RUNNING AHEAD OF CONTRACT, CHINA CUSTOMS PUTS MEMORY IMPORT ASP UP 100%+ y/y, AND A CHINESE SUPPLIER TAKES THE #3 NAND SLOT FROM [[KIOXIA]].**

- 🔴🔴 **THE CAPITAL-RETURN ARITHMETIC, AND THE POINT IS THE *RESIDUAL*.** BofA (Simon Woo, 2026-08-20) expects **~W170trn of SK Hynix FCF in 2026**; at the newly raised **"50%+ of FCF"** payout that is a **~W85trn budget**, so **after the W40trn buyback ~W45trn remains for this year alone**. If returns stay buyback-centric, dividends can be **sub-50% of the budget — ~W30trn, or ~W40k per share.** Against a ~W1,200trn market cap, BofA's conclusion is that memory names **"can easily offer high-single-digit% shareholder returns."** **The buyback execution window is dated: 20 Aug – 19 Nov 2026.**
- 🔴 **THE INDUSTRY-STRUCTURE READ, WHICH IS THE REASON THIS BELONGS ON A THEME PAGE AND NOT JUST A COMPANY PAGE: BofA reads the buyback as "extra cash spend for shareholders INSTEAD OF IRRATIONALLY INCREASING CAPEX" — i.e. a supply-discipline signal for the whole complex.** Supporting points: buybacks also secure the ownership structure / enable future ADR issuance, and **2026 cash dividends can be 5-10x the long-term historical average even AFTER the W40trn buyback.** BofA expects the same ~50%-of-FCF ratio at Samsung. ➜ **This is the cleanest statement yet of the bull case that the 2026-27 memory cycle is being managed for cash rather than for share — the falsifier is any capex raise at the 3Q calls.**
- ⚠️ **[[SAMSUNG]]'s number is being quoted in a RANGE and the two figures do not agree: Jefferies (Jeff Kim) estimates a special dividend of at least KRW100trn within the year — "if a larger portion… is paid in cash, the resulting dividend yield could stand out, potentially allowing the shares to settle above KRW300,000" at "just 4x P/E" — while the Korea Economic Daily puts the total programme at KRW150trn (~US$107.7bn)** (both 2026-08-20). **Carry the range; the August board meeting is the resolution event.** 🔴 **BofA supplies the structural difference from Hynix, and it is mechanical: Samsung's mix should be DIVIDEND-centric (~60:40 dividend:buyback) because of (1) the "10% rule" forcing Samsung Life / Samsung Fire & Marine to sell down excess SEC stake and (2) >4.0mn individual holders plus the Lee family's direct stake — whereas SK Square is NOT subject to the 10% rule.** ➜ **That is the mechanism behind the MS Sales claim this theme already carries that the Hynix programme is more EPS-accretive than Samsung's.**
- 🔴🔴 **SPOT IS STILL RUNNING AHEAD OF CONTRACT — AND AHEAD OF BofA'S OWN FORECASTS.** DRAM spot has strengthened **for more than two months despite low season**, with most chips (16Gb DDR5, 8Gb DDR4) up LSD% again in the week to 08-20, implying **~+30% q/q**. But the **August contract price for commodity DDR5 is only up low-to-mid-20% q/q** — a rising September price is what would take 3Q ASP above +30%. **NAND spot +20% QTD**, above BofA's own sub-20% forecast. **LTA-based 3Q prices span a very wide 0-30%, with the majority "close to 20% or higher."** BofA sees **no downside risk to its 3Q blended assumptions: DRAM +23% q/q, NAND +15%.** ➜ **The spot-over-contract gap this theme has tracked all month is still open, and the LTA range (0-30%) is the single widest dispersion on the page — it is where the 2H26 earnings surprise lives.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE INDEPENDENT ASP EVIDENCE, AND IT IS THE BEST THIRD-PARTY CONFIRMATION AVAILABLE: China's July semiconductor imports rose +71% y/y in USD on only +9% in UNITS, and MEMORY-ONLY imports rose +200% y/y — "far exceeded logic" — implying memory import ASP up 100%+ y/y** (BofA, 2026-08-20). ➜ **Customs data is not a survey and not a broker estimate. A 100%+ y/y ASP move corroborated by an independent statistical series is the strongest single datapoint on this page for the pricing leg of the cycle.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE STRUCTURAL NEGATIVE OF THE WEEK, AND IT IS A RANKING CHANGE: YMTC SURPASSED [[KIOXIA]] IN NAND BIT SHIPMENTS IN 2Q26 TO BECOME THE WORLD'S THIRD-LARGEST NAND SUPPLIER, and is now pursuing an A-share listing that "would provide YMTC with a sustainable funding channel for capacity expansion"** (TrendForce, 2026-08-20; framed alongside CXMT's listing as "China's clear ambition to capture a larger share of the NAND market"). ➜ **Against every supply-discipline argument above, this is the counterweight: a Chinese supplier has taken the #3 bit-share slot and is about to get permanent public-equity funding for capacity. So far the bits appear ABSORBED rather than price-destructive (see the China import ASP +100%+ above) — but "absorbed at rising prices" and "absorbed at any price" are different regimes, and the listing is what removes YMTC's funding constraint.**
- 🔴 **THE SUPPLY-SIDE CORROBORATION FROM THE TOOL VENDOR, WHICH IS NEW AND SPECIFIC: [[AMAT]]'s Head of IR told JPM that "the VAST MAJORITY of the 10 new fab projects it saw at customers over the most recent quarter were DRAM-related," with packaging additions also meaningful** (JPM · Sur, 2026-08-20). ➜ **DRAM — not logic — is now the marginal driver of new fab starts. Read two ways, and both matter: it is the demand confirmation for the memory capex cycle ([[semicap-wfe]]), AND it is the leading indicator for the 2028 supply that TrendForce's +4.0% DRAM sufficiency call on this page depends on.** AMAT also flagged **NAND-to-DRAM conversions and the repurposing of former packaging facilities into chip manufacturing** as sources of output growth without greenfield capex — ⚠️ **capacity that does not show up as a new fab announcement is exactly the kind that surprises supply models.**
- ⚠️ **Unverified but material if true: "$SKHY unveils CPO roadmap?? Apparently they're doing something with photonic interposer that links to memor[y]"** (@aleabitoreddit, 2026-08-20). ➜ **NOT ADOPTED — logged as a lead. A memory-side photonic-interposer roadmap would connect this theme directly to [[optical-cpo]] and to the "route around the interposer" architectures already on this page.**
- **Labour cost, newly settled:** SK hynix **reached a tentative union deal — 6.3% salary increase plus a significant change in how performance bonuses are apportioned** (Jefferies desk, 2026-08-20). ➜ **Relevant because Hynix bonuses are 100% CASH (vs Samsung partly in shares), which is precisely what makes the Hynix buyback fully EPS-accretive.**

## Recent signals (Aug 18, /run-inbox — THREE MEMORY PRIMARIES) — 🔴🔴 **TWO HOUSES NOW PUT [[SAMSUNG]] #1 IN HBM IN 2027, ENDING THE [[SKHYNIX]] LEADERSHIP THIS THEME HAS CARRIED FOR TWO YEARS. THE HBM-vs-CONVENTIONAL PRICING ASYMMETRY GETS ITS MECHANISM. AND THE WHOLE COMPLEX'S 2027 NUMBERS GET ONE AFFORDABILITY TEST: US$1.61 TRILLION OF MEMORY INDUSTRY REVENUE.**

_(Sources: **UBS · Nicolas Gaudois**, "Key Call: Samsung Electronics — Implying ROE below pre-AI levels", Key Call Buy, PT KRW535,000, 2026-08-07 — [link](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260807_UBS_005930-KS_Key_Call-_Samsung_Electronics_-Implying_ROE_below_pre_AI.html) · **KB Securities · Jeff Kim + Changmin Lee** (distributed by Jefferies — 🔴 the house is KB, not Jefferies), ">10x Increase in Shareholder Return Expected", Buy, TP KRW600,000, 2026-08-10 — [link](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260809_Jefferies_005930-KS_005930_KS-_-10x_Increase_in_Shareholder_Return_Expected.html) · **UBS · Timothy Arcuri**, "[[MU]]: On The Road With Mgmt", Buy, PT $1,625, 2026-08-17 — [link](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260818_UBS_MU_Micron_Technology_Inc_-On_The_Road_With_Mgmt-_Climbing_T.html) · **Bernstein · Mark Newman + Mark Li**, "New Memory LTAs Part 3 — Bull vs Bear Debate", 2026-07-20 (dated, swept late) — [link](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260720_Bernstein_SNDK_Global_Memory-_New_Memory_LTAs_Part_3_Bull_vs_Bear_Deb_1.html))_

- 🔴🔴 **THE LEADERSHIP CALL: UBS forecasts 2027 HBM BIT SUPPLY SHARE at [[SAMSUNG]] 40% / [[SKHYNIX]] 38% / [[MU]] 22% — Samsung surpassing SK Hynix. KB Securities independently has Samsung's HBM4 share specifically at 44%, "the largest."** Two houses, two different share metrics, same conclusion. ⚠️ **BUT CHECK THE MECHANISM BEFORE ADOPTING: UBS gets Samsung to 40% while CUTTING Samsung's own 2027 HBM bits to 22bn Gb (+95% y/y) from 24bn prior. A share gain delivered alongside a volume cut is partly a denominator effect — it implies UBS cut the rest of the industry harder.** ➤ **The falsifiable version is therefore "does SK Hynix ship LESS than currently modelled in 2027," not "does Samsung ship more." That is checkable against SK Hynix's own capacity disclosures and is the single cleanest test this theme can run in the next two quarters.**
- 🔴 **THE HBM-vs-CONVENTIONAL ASYMMETRY, WITH ITS MECHANISM, FROM BERNSTEIN A MONTH EARLIER — AND IT PREDICTED WHAT MICRON SAID THIS WEEK: 2026 HBM prices are LOCKED by contract; 2027 contracts were still under negotiation. Bernstein assumes HBM up 2.0–2.5× 2026→2027, against CONVENTIONAL DRAM already up ~4× and possibly 5×. "It is remarkable that price has gone up so much in conventional, but not in HBM. So it is MUCH LESS PROFITABLE TO MAKE HBM THAN REGULAR DRAM RIGHT NOW, so price HAS to move."** ➤ **Micron's management then told UBS on 08-17 that it intends "to drive HBM gross margins higher, reflecting HBM's higher value and the fact that existing HBM contracts were signed a long time ago, with pricing effectively lagging the increases in DDR pricing." The outside-in prediction and the inside-out confirmation match exactly. This is now the best-supported pricing claim on this page: HBM 2027 repricing is a CATCH-UP to conventional, not an independent AI-demand event — and it caps how bullish HBM ASPs can get, since the ceiling is what conventional already achieved.** Corroborating on magnitude: **UBS now models 2027 HBM ASPs +90% y/y (raised from +68%)**, i.e. inside Bernstein's 2.0–2.5× band at the low end.
- ⚠️🔴 **THE AFFORDABILITY TEST, AND IT IS THE NUMBER TO CARRY INTO EVERY HYPERSCALER-CAPEX CROSS-CHECK: UBS names its main risk as neither supply nor demand but PRICE — "memory industry revenues are approaching US$1.61 TRILLION in 2027E."** ➤ **Set that against the $5.8tn of TOTAL hyperscaler AI capex over FY2025-FY2030 that Goldman's credit desk carries ([themes/hyperscaler-capex](hyperscaler-capex.md)). If memory alone runs $1.6tn in a single year, memory is claiming a share of the AI bill that no capex model on this wiki currently reflects. Either memory revenue is too high, or the compute/networking/power lines are being crowded out. This is the most important unreconciled number in the run.**
- **DEMAND ACCELERATES INTO 2027 ON UBS's NUMBERS: DRAM bit end-consumption +40% y/y in 2027E vs +21% in 2026E; NAND +26% vs +23% — driven by agentic AI pulling DDR5/LPDDR5 for conventional servers and AI-server CPU head nodes, plus NAND for KV-cache and storage. Supply cannot follow: "almost all of the incremental wafer capacity addition for DRAM is going to HBM, and NO NEW CAPACITY EX-CHINA IS BEING ADDED TO NAND." HBM absorbs 500k/690k wpm of front-end DRAM capacity by end-2026/2027 = 24%/30% of industry capacity.**
- **THE SUPPLY ARRIVAL DATE, FROM MICRON DIRECTLY, AND THREE SOURCES CONVERGE ON IT: MU's C2027 additions are ID1 (~80k wpm) and Tongluo BROWNFIELD (~40k wpm) in mid-C2027 plus the Singapore HBM back-end ramp; the MEANINGFUL net additions land in C2028 — Tongluo GREENFIELD (~40k wpm, mostly EUV) and ID2 (~80k wpm) by C2Q28, plus Hiroshima Fab 15.** Bernstein's Asia team independently puts China's swing-factor date at **2028** ("definitely not this year, and definitely not in 2027"), and UBS's own MU model turns revenue down in FY29. ➤ **C2028 is where this cycle gets decided, on three independent routes. 2027 supply is effectively already set — which means 2027 is a PRICE debate, not a volume debate.**
- **CHINA, QUANTIFIED FROM THE PROSPECTUS: CXMT's 1Q26 IPO filing puts China at 8% of global DRAM, roughly doubling to ~16% in 3-4 years — only ~8pts of incremental supply against an imbalance Bernstein puts at 20-30%. "Samsung even already mentioned 30% to 50% in a recent webinar. And the IR refused to offer detail when we pressed them for details."** ⚠️ **A Samsung-sourced 30-50% undersupply figure, relayed second-hand and disowned under questioning — wider than any number on this page and logged as attributed, not adopted.**
- **STRUCTURAL LTA DIFFERENCE WORTH CARRYING INTO THE NEXT DOWNTURN: Bernstein describes [[MU]] and [[SNDK]] as "the most proactive in pursuing agreements backed by financial guarantees, while ASIA-BASED SUPPLIERS have taken a MORE SELECTIVE approach." And the protection is BACK-END WEIGHTED — guarantees bind harder as remaining obligations decline, so downside protection "can improve materially in the later years of the agreements (when it is needed most)" and is WEAKEST against a near-term shock.** ➤ **Two consequences: (1) the Korean names have less of the earnings floor the US names bought, which is a downside-SHAPE difference rather than a quality judgement; (2) anyone modelling LTAs as protection against a 2026-27 air pocket is using them backwards.**

## Recent signals (Aug 18, /wiki-ingest) — 🔴🔴 **A CUSTOMER-LEVEL 2028 HBM DEMAND SPLIT PUTS [[AVGO]] AHEAD OF [[NVDA]] AND TOTAL BITS NEARLY DOUBLING — AND TWO SEPARATE ARCHITECTURES NOW EXIST WHOSE STATED PURPOSE IS TO ROUTE AROUND THE INTERPOSER, NOT AROUND THE MEMORY**

**Sources this block: @jukan05 (FinTwit corpus, relaying **Meritz Securities**, 2026-08-18) · @zephyr_z9 (corpus, 2026-08-18) · BEP Research · Ben Pouladian with Rick Xie, "The Tax Dodge: Qualcomm's 133 TB/s Isn't What You Think" (independent Substack, 2026-08-18) · @ParadisLabs (corpus, 2026-08-18) · UBS · Timothy Arcuri, "Micron — On The Road With Mgmt" (2026-08-17) · Morgan Stanley · Daniel Yen / Charlie Chan, "PULSE: GigaDevice — 2H DRAM Procurement Spending Steps Up from 1H" (2026-08-18) · DIGITIMES headlines (2026-08-18).**

- 🔴🔴 **THE DEMAND SPLIT, BY CUSTOMER, FOR 2028 — and it re-ranks the buyer list: Meritz's planned 2028 HBM demand is [[AVGO]] 35–40bn Gb · [[NVDA]] 30bn Gb · [[GOOG]] 20bn Gb · [[AMD]] 10bn Gb.** *"What stands out is **Broadcom potentially surpassing NVIDIA in HBM demand**, as well as **Google massively ramping up its own HBM procurement**."* Alongside it: **"HBM demand will nearly double from 6.5EB-7EB in 2027 to 12.5EB in 2028"** (@zephyr_z9). ⚠️ **Broker estimate relayed via corpus; no methodology, no Gb→unit bridge, and "planned" demand is not shipped demand.** 🔴 **WHY THIS IS THE MOST USEFUL THING IN THE BLOCK: it is the *volume-offset* leg of the open Rubin-Ultra de-spec contradiction this theme carries. Total HBM bits can nearly double even if content per NVDA GPU is flat-to-down — because the marginal 2028 buyer is a CUSTOM-ASIC program, not a merchant GPU. That reconciles BofA/Arya's "volume offset" framing with the FundaAI/Mizuho content-per-GPU cut WITHOUT either side being wrong, and it makes the falsifier concrete: watch AVGO's and Google's own HBM procurement, not NVDA's spec sheet.** Corroborating from the supplier side: **"SK hynix's No. 2 customer closes in on Nvidia in 1H26 sales"** (DIGITIMES headline, 08-18 — ⚠️ **the customer is NOT named in what was read, body not read; this is the single highest-value follow-up on the theme**).
- 🔴🔴 **TWO ARCHITECTURES NOW EXIST WHOSE EXPLICIT PURPOSE IS TO DELETE THE INTERPOSER, AND THE CORRECT READ IS BEARISH THE PACKAGING PREMIUM, NOT BEARISH MEMORY.** BEP Research's conclusion, verbatim: **"HBC is bearish the large-interposer premium and neutral-to-bullish the memory oligopoly. Those are not the same trade, and the market is pricing them as one."** The layer under attack is *"the large interposer, CoWoS capacity, and the **30%-plus HBM4 manufacturing premium**"* — *"arriving exactly as the premium is at its highest."* **[[QCOM]]'s "High Bandwidth Compute" (HBC)** places the accelerator die directly BENEATH a DRAM stack, connected vertically by TSVs, claiming multiple stacks deploy with standard packaging and no HBM silicon interposer (**AI200 ships this year WITHOUT HBC; HBC debuts with AI250 in 2027**). **[[NVDA]]'s Vera is NOT itself a dodge** — it is a CPU and the Rubin GPUs beside it still run HBM4 — **but "the largest accelerator vendor in the world has qualified DATACENTER LPDDR as a real memory tier, with multi-vendor sourcing, at rack scale. The question NVIDIA left open is whether that capacity becomes addressable KV cache. If it does, the tier stops being one company's bet."** ⚠️ **The offset the piece itself insists on: the dodge is partial by construction — HBC "still needs direct DRAM-to-logic bonding and 3D integration, which RELOCATES packaging complexity into the vertical stack rather than deleting it", and still consumes wafer thinning, bonding, known-good-die infrastructure and advanced test. "The tax moves. It does not disappear."**
- **THE SPEC ANCHORS, USEFUL FOR ANY CHALLENGER COMPARISON, AND THEY ARE THE FIRST SIDE-BY-SIDE THIS THEME HAS CARRIED: "[[MU]] currently specifies MORE THAN 2.8 TB/s for HBM4 and [[SAMSUNG]] UP TO 3.6 TB/s for HBM4E"** — and BEP's discipline on the challenger's own headline: **QCOM's "133 TB/s" is *effective* memory bandwidth, i.e. "workload bandwidth consumed INSIDE HBC, not raw bandwidth exposed to the main accelerator"**, with the 6x-vs-HBM figure normalised at **card** level and the 200x-vs-SRAM figure at **rack** level — *"different boundaries, so the two numbers were never a common benchmark and cannot be quoted as one."* **The thermal ceiling that governs all near-memory compute: prior die-stacked research used an 85°C DRAM threshold, modelled refresh DOUBLING for each additional 10°C, and found allowable logic power from ~8.5W under passive cooling to ~55W under active cooling.** **Why every prior processing-in-memory attempt died (Samsung HBM-PIM ~2.5x system performance and >60% lower energy; SK Hynix AiMX on H100s with vLLM in 2025; UPMEM's programmable DRAM modules): "It was ownership and software" — none reached a named hyperscale deployment.**
- 🔴 **THE FLASH TIER STOPS BEING A CONCEPT: NVDA's Vera Rubin ships "CMX", a flash layer between HBM and network storage for evicted KV cache — and NAND spot is already moving.** *"NAND prices are surging again thanks to [[NVDA]]'s Vera Rubin expansion. The **spot price is up ~5% from last week, which is an 11.6% increase in three weeks (DRAMeXchange)**."* **Supplier split: [[SAMSUNG]] main and [[MU]] secondary are mass producing Vera Rubin eSSDs; [[SKHYNIX]] is NAND-qualified but no volume production seen yet (while leading HBM4).** Much of NVDA's NAND is pre-secured via LTAs, so pressure lands on spot. **The corpus conclusion: "the best torque is the NAND pure plays with [[KIOXIA]] and [[SNDK]]."** 🔴 **Hold this against [[SNDK]]'s High Bandwidth Flash pitch, because they are the same use case at different maturities and BEP pre-specifies the falsifier: "HBF is moving from a concept into a standards and sampling story, and NAND ENDURANCE IS NOT SOLVED, so the honest question is not whether flash can deliver more bandwidth. It is WHICH AI BYTES actually belong on NAND. Model weights are read-mostly and plausible. KV CACHE could be the far larger demand pool, but it is a WRITE STREAM, and the write stream carries endurance, placement, and reload costs the slide decks do not price."** **i.e. the CMX tier IS the KV-cache-on-flash case, so it is both the proof of demand and the endurance test.** ⚠️ Corpus post; "CMX" not verified against an NVDA document this run.
- 🔴 **THE SUPPLY ROADMAP GETS FAB-LEVEL DETAIL FROM MICRON MANAGEMENT, AND THE RAMP IS BACK-WEIGHTED TO C2028.** From UBS's Singapore NDR with MU management: **C2027 brings ID1 (~80k-wpm) and Tongluo brownfield (~40k-wpm) online MID-C2027 — "effectively representing the largest incremental piece of DRAM capacity additions during the year" — plus the Singapore HBM back-end packaging facility beginning to ramp. The meaningful net additions land in C2028: Tongluo greenfield (~40k-wpm, MOSTLY HOUSING EUV) and ID2 (~80k-wpm) online by C2Q28, with the Hiroshima Fab 15 expansion also online "at some point in C2028."** 🔴 **This is the supply-side companion to the demand split above: on Meritz's numbers demand nearly doubles into 2028 in the same year MU's largest capacity additions land — which is exactly the setup TrendForce's dated oversupply call (DRAM sufficiency +4.0% in 2028, carried in the Aug-17 block) is built on. The two are not reconciled and should not be.**
- **CHINA: THE DDR4 PROCUREMENT STEP-UP IS QUANTIFIED THROUGH A LISTED PROXY.** MS on GigaDevice (OW, PT Rmb750): **2Q revenue Rmb7.4bn, +76% q/q and +229% y/y**, 1% above MS; **2Q EPS Rmb7.7, +269% q/q, +1399% y/y**. **DDR4 revenue roughly DOUBLED q/q; SLC NAND revenue MORE THAN doubled q/q**; NOR flash and MCU achieved significant 1H y/y growth. 🔴 **The CXMT datapoint: "GigaDevice booked a Rmb2.0bn related-party transaction with CXMT in 1H26, vs. the full-year procurement budget of Rmb5.7bn. The company still expects to fulfill the full-year procurement budget, which would imply 85% H/H GROWTH IN 2H, supported by continued DDR4 price hikes. GigaDevice also expects continuously increasing capacity support over the next few years from CXMT."** The company is *"considering some similar [LTA] arrangements with its core customers"*, and expects memory and MCU pricing *"to continue improving moderately"* with costs also rising gradually, so gross margin *"would gradually reach a relatively steady state."* **i.e. Chinese legacy-DRAM pricing is still rising into 2H26 and CXMT's capacity is being contracted forward — a floor under the legacy complex and a competitive-supply clock at the same time.**
- **KOREA: THE MARGINAL MEMORY WAFER STAYS IN KOREA.** The South Korean government **denied** a local report that semis would be the first project under its **$350bn US-investment commitment**; **"investment in US memory fabs is seen as impractical, as [[SAMSUNG]] and [[SKHYNIX]] recently committed to spend $800bn in South Korea's southern Honam region."** **$150bn is allocated to shipbuilding; the remaining $200bn is undecided, with a gas-fired combined-cycle plant in Texas the likely first project** (Rothschild & Co Redburn, 08-18). ⚠️ **The constraint on the Korean plan: "South Korea's Gwangju chip hub faces looming water crunch… Samsung Electronics and SK Hynix are preparing to invest in FOUR MEMORY FABS in southwestern South Korea, but alongside power infrastructure, the semiconductor industry's uninterrupted demand for [water]…"** (DIGITIMES, 08-18, headline + lede only). **Also headline-only: "Samsung faces tougher HBM4E test after HBM4 comeback" (DIGITIMES, 08-18) — an HBM4 comeback conceded, HBM4E framed as the harder qualification.** **Positioning: HF net exposure to US Semis has recovered to June pre-unwind levels while MEMORY exposure remains at depressed July levels — "there remains further net buying to do, particularly in US Memory" (UBS PB desk, 08-18).**

THE RELAY DISAGREES WITH IT. AND MORGAN STANLEY HANDS THE BULLS A SECOND, INDEPENDENT DEMAND ARGUMENT: ENTERPRISES ARE **ACCELERATING** PURCHASES BECAUSE OF MEMORY INFLATION, NOT DEFERRING THEM.**

**Sources this block: FUNDA, "Research | Takeaways from the Future of Memory and Storage Conference 2026" (2026-08-11, independent Substack, written from FMS) · Mizuho · Vijay Rakesh, "AI Server Call: 2027 VR with HBM and NPO/CPO, Adv. Packaging EMIB-T/CoPoS Ramps" (2026-08-16) · Morgan Stanley · Erik Woodring (+ Howard Kao), "Global IT Hardware: AI + Chipflation: Two Tailwinds, One Trade… For Now" (2026-08-10, 98pp).**

**1. 🔴🔴 THE BEAR POLE, WITH DATES AND DECIMALS — AND IT IS THE CLEANEST STATEMENT OF THE DOWN-LEG THIS PAGE HAS (TrendForce, presented at FMS 2026, relayed by FUNDA, 2026-08-11).** *"**DRAM**: TrendForce argues that the supply/demand gap **bottoms out and begins to narrow in 2027**, with the **sufficiency ratio turning positive at 4.0% in 2028**. The shift reflects cumulative capacity expansions catching up with demand. **NAND**: TrendForce argues the sufficiency ratio **turns positive at 0.4% in 2027 and reaches 2% oversupply in 2028**."*
- ⚠️🔴 **TRENDFORCE vs TRENDFORCE — THE 2027 DRAM INFLECTION HAS FLIPPED SIGN IN TWELVE DAYS, AND BOTH VERSIONS ARE NOW ON THIS PAGE. NEITHER IS ADOPTED.** **07-30 (already here, "2027 forks" bullet):** DRAM sufficiency gap *"~-1% to -2% in 2026, **widening in 2027**"*. **08-11 (this block, via FUNDA from the FMS stage):** the gap *"**bottoms out and begins to narrow in 2027**"*, positive by 2028. ➜ **Same house, opposite direction on the single most important date in this theme. Two procedural facts matter when weighing them: (i) the newer version is a THIRD-PARTY RELAY of a conference slide, not a TrendForce publication — the numbers are FUNDA's transcription; (ii) the NAND leg did NOT flip — TrendForce said on 07-30 that NAND *"sufficiency turns positive and the market loosens in 2H27 with prices at risk of correction,"* and the FMS version is the same call with numbers attached. So TrendForce has been internally CONSISTENT on NAND and internally CONTRADICTORY on DRAM. Weight accordingly: the NAND number is the more credible of the two, and it is the one that lands on [[SNDK]], [[KIOXIA]] and [[STX]].**
- ⚠️ **AND IT COLLIDES WITH THE OTHER SUFFICIENCY SERIES ON THIS PAGE. Logged side by side, not averaged.** **UBS (Gaudois, "Memory Semis Monthly — July '26", 2026-07-03):** DRAM *"undersupplied until **at least 2Q28**"*; 2027 bit demand **+36.2%** vs supply **+19.3%**, sufficiency **−13.6%** (raw end-demand) vs **−8.1%** in 2026E, *"unprecedented over the last 30 years"* — with the basis note already on this page that UBS's inventory-ADJUSTED 2027 figure improves to **−1.2%** on a −67.2bn Gb drawdown. **NAND (UBS Fig 41): −1.0% / −9.3% / −0.7% for 25/26/27E**, and the house units put 2027 NAND at **+2.6%** vs UBS's −1.2%. ➜ **So on DRAM 2028 the two houses are on opposite sides of zero (TrendForce +4.0% vs UBS still short into at least 2Q28). On NAND 2027 they are much closer than the headlines suggest — TrendForce +0.4%, UBS −0.7%, house +2.6% — i.e. everyone now has NAND arriving at roughly balance in 2027, and the argument is about which side of the line and what happens in 2028. 🔴 UNIT/BASIS GUARD, because this page has been burned before: sufficiency ratios are quoted here on THREE different bases (raw end-demand, inventory-adjusted, and house units). TrendForce's +4.0%/+0.4%/2% figures come with NO stated basis. Do not net them against UBS's numbers or against the house series without one.**

**2. 🔴 THE BULL POLE, AND IT IS THE RELAY ITSELF DISSENTING FROM THE SLIDE IT JUST PRESENTED — "STRUCTURAL NORMALIZATION," NOT A PEAK (FUNDA, 2026-08-11).** *"**Contrary to TrendForce's view, we believe the recent flattening of memory ASP growth does not point to future oversupply. It is a process of structural normalization**,"* driven by two named mechanisms:
- **(i) Price sensitivity in consumer electronics, and it is segment-asymmetric:** *"Consumer end-devices like **smartphones are highly price-sensitive and have limited ability to absorb further memory price hikes**. By contrast, **PC OEMs have a higher tolerance** for memory price increases due to a **higher baseline ASP and inelastic demand in education and commercial segments**."* ➜ **A refinement of the "2027 consumer rationing" work already on this page (MS/Shawn Kim, 07-27: PCs −15% / −58mn units, smartphones −12% / ~134mn units). FUNDA's mechanism runs the other way round on relative severity — MS models a LARGER percentage shortfall in PCs than smartphones, while FUNDA argues PC OEMs are the MORE tolerant buyer. Both can hold (allocation shortfall ≠ willingness to pay), but anyone using this page to size consumer downside should notice that the elasticity ranking is contested.**
- **(ii) The LTA mechanic, and it is the explanation for the flattening that the bears are reading as a peak:** *"CSPs are actively signing LTAs, with **locked-in prices typically lower than short-term contract prices**. As the **LTA ratio increases, overall ASP growth naturally decelerates**; however, memory vendors also gain protection via **upfront payments and penalty mechanisms**."* ➜ **🔴 THIS IS THE MOST USEFUL LINE IN THE NOTE, because it makes decelerating blended ASP a MIX artefact rather than a pricing-power signal — and it is directly consistent with the two contract structures this page documented on 08-15: [[MU]]'s SCAs carry a CQ2-2026 CEILING on the first 16 deals, and [[SNDK]]'s NBMs a ~80%-GM FLOOR with an uncontracted third floating. A book that mixes ceiling-capped and floor-protected volume with spot prints a slowing blended ASP even while marginal prices rise. Anyone reading blended ASP deceleration as evidence of the down-leg needs to strip the LTA share out first.**
- **FUNDA's conclusion, dated and explicit:** *"We expect **memory prices to hold at elevated levels through 2027**, supported by higher LTA proportions and a mix shifting further toward data centers, **rather than peaking and collapsing**."* ⚠️ **Independent Substack research shop — no rating, no PT, unaudited. Carried as one of the two poles of a live debate, NOT as a house view. ➜ THE DEBATE IS DELIBERATELY LEFT OPEN: TrendForce has a dated sufficiency flip; FUNDA has a dated price plateau; they cannot both be right about 2028.**

**3. THE SUPPLY-SIDE EVIDENCE FUNDA STACKS BEHIND ITS OWN VIEW, and the equipment leg is the one this page did not have.**
- 🔴 **"The semiconductor equipment shortage persists (a point we first made in our LTA report on 1 April 2026), which constrains the overall pace of capacity expansion."** ➜ **NET-NEW MECHANISM. Every bull argument on this page rests on "supply cannot respond," and the reasons carried so far have been capex intent, cleanroom lead times, EUV scarcity and HBM's wafer-per-bit penalty. Tool AVAILABILITY as an independent binding constraint is a different and harder claim — it means capex dollars do not convert to wafers on the announced schedule regardless of intent. Read directly against the $69bn / $96bn of 2027-28 DRAM WFE already on this page (Bernstein, 08-11) and against [[AMAT]]/[[LRCX]]/[[ASML]] on [semicap-wfe](semicap-wfe.md): if the equipment layer is itself rationed, a WFE number is an ORDER number, not a capacity number.**
- **Consumer is still short and is DOWNGRADING SPECS, which is the demand-destruction-vs-deferral test:** *"Smartphone and PC experts we spoke with noted that consumer electronics are **still short of memory and have been forced to downgrade hardware specifications**. Memory vendors recognize that pushing prices too far would destroy consumer demand, so they are **steering capacity toward data centers, optimizing mix while holding ASPs high**."*
- **And the procurement posture, in one phrase:** *"**CSPs are currently running a 'buy all available capacity' strategy** for memory procurement."* ➜ **The behavioural counterpart to the 40-60% fill-rate band below. "Buy all available" is what makes a fill rate meaningful — it says the denominator is not padded double-ordering but a standing bid for everything produced.**
- **The AI demand bridge FUNDA puts under 2027, with units:** *"we project **2027 x86 server CPU shipments to grow >40% YoY and XPU shipments >60% YoY**, and both need large volumes of high-spec memory."* ⚠️ **FUNDA projections, logged not adopted — but note they are the first SERVER-UNIT growth marks on this page to sit beside the bit-demand series, which is the missing link between accelerator counts and DRAM bits.**
- 🔴 **AND THE DE-SPEC RE-READ, WHICH IS THE MOST QUOTABLE LINE IN THE NOTE AND LANDS ON THIS PAGE'S LONGEST-RUNNING DEBATE:** *"**Recent specification downgrades by Nvidia and others are aimed squarely at maximizing GPU shipments within a limited memory supply**."* ➜ **This is the SUPPLY-FAILURE reading of the de-spec, not the demand-weakness reading — the same interpretation this page adopted on 08-06 ("the de-spec is re-explained as a supply failure, 60-70% fill") and on 08-07 ("it comes with MORE units, not fewer"), now stated by an independent shop from the conference floor. ⚠️ It does NOT settle the content-per-GPU dispute in §5 below; it settles the MOTIVE. Bits per accelerator can fall while total bits rise — that has been the reconciliation all along, and this is a third independent statement of it.**

**4. THE FILL RATES CONVERGE — TWO SUPPLIERS, IN THEIR OWN WORDS, IN THE SAME WEEK, AND THEY LAND IN THE SAME 40-60% BAND AS EVERY CHANNEL CHECK ON THIS PAGE.**
- 🔴 **[[KIOXIA]] (met by FUNDA at FMS):** *"sounded confident about demand strength continuing into 2027, telling us it **can fulfill only 40%-50% of demand** and expects the **unmet portion to roll into next year**."* ⚠️ **Basis caveat stated by FUNDA itself: *"we take that fulfillment figure to refer to US data center demand for SSDs"* — not a whole-company number.**
- 🔴 **[[MU]] management (Sumit Sadana, EVP & CBO, KeyBanc Technology Leadership Forum, 2026-08-10, Bloomberg FINAL TRANSCRIPT on disk):** FUNDA's cross-read is *"consistent with Micron's comment yesterday that it cannot meet more than half of data center demand"* — and the primary confirms it verbatim: *"it's most acute in the data center, where quite often **we are not able to meet any more than half of the demand our customers have**."* The same passage ranks the constraint: customers say *"the number one constraint they have is **not power or real-estate or datacenter capacity or logic wafers** — the number one constraint they have is **DRAM**,"* described as *"a common theme across our customers."*
- ➜ **THE TRIANGULATION, WHICH IS WHAT MAKES THIS SECTION WORTH MORE THAN EITHER QUOTE ALONE:** MU **≤~50%** (management) · Kioxia **40-50%** (management, US DC SSD) · Jefferies/Jeff Kim **"only 60% of big techs' memory demand is being met"** (08-11, already on this page) · **60-70%** fill rates in the 08-04 allocation-round block · a supplier at **"60% of big-tech demand"** in the 08-06 de-spec re-read. **Five independent estimates, all inside 40-60%, two of them now from the sellers themselves rather than from channel checks. 🔴 And note the second-order point in the MU quote: memory is ranked ABOVE POWER in the hyperscaler bottleneck stack — which inverts the ordering most AI-infrastructure work starts from and is worth carrying to [ai-datacenter-power](ai-datacenter-power.md).**

**5. THE HIERARCHY GETS LABELS AND THREE NEW TIERS — AND THE HBF WAFER MECHANIC GETS ITS FIRST (UNQUANTIFIED) STATEMENT.**
- **The taxonomy this theme has been missing:** the conference framing was *"the need to reduce dependence on DRAM and HBM, and to look to solutions that address every part of the memory and storage hierarchy (**now often referred to by Nvidia's G1 to G4 labels**)"*, with FUNDA's own diagram adding **CXL pooling at G2.5 and Nvidia CMX at G3.5**. ➜ **Useful because it gives this wiki a shared coordinate system for the tiering argument: HBM at the top, CXL-pooled DRAM at G2.5, NVDA CMX (GPU→DPU→eSSD) at G3.5, high-IOPS/eSSD below. Adopt the labels for cross-page consistency; the tier BOUNDARIES remain contested.**
- **[[SKHYNIX]] proposed THREE new tiers at FMS — 3D stacked DRAM, HBF, and CXL Pooled Memory.** ➜ **Two of the three sit outside HBM (one NAND-based, one commodity-DRAM-based): the #1 HBM vendor is productising the tiers that exist *because* HBM is too expensive. Only HBF carries a date (2027 samples). Filed in full to [SKHYNIX](../SKHYNIX.md) and [cxl-memory-fabric](cxl-memory-fabric.md).**
- 🔴 **HBF's specs and the wafer-diversion mechanic:** *"Bandwidth and capacity both come in above HBM: **three to four HBF stacks put 1.5-2TB on one package, enough for HBF to hold model weights rather than being confined to KV cache**"*; and *"**If HBF ramps, it may consume NAND wafers at premium pricing the same way HBM did inside DRAM. Every wafer diverted to HBF then tightens commodity flash supply.**"* Also: **the SNDK/SK Hynix announcement confirmed [[GOOG]]'s interest, while investors noted [[NVDA]]'s ABSENCE from the HBF presentations and panels, "which suggests Nvidia prefers an architecture built on high-IOPS SSDs"**; **2027 is the target for HBF samples**. ➜ **CHRONOLOGY WORTH RECORDING: this states the supply-absorption mechanism on 08-11, three days BEFORE Bernstein quantified it at 3-4x wafer capacity per exabyte (08-14). Bernstein owns the number, FUNDA owns the priority. ⚠️ And one same-house drift: "bandwidth AND capacity above HBM" here vs FUNDA's own post-Investor-Day "read bandwidth ON PAR with HBM, 8-16x the density" (08-14) — the parity version governs. Full HBF debate on [SNDK](../SNDK.md).**

**6. ⚠️🔴 THE PER-GPU HBM CONTENT LADDER FROM THE MIZUHO *PRIMARY* — AND IT COLLIDES WITH EVERY OTHER MARK ON THIS PAGE. FOUR NUMBERS NOW SIT ON VR-ULTRA AND NONE AGREE.** (Mizuho · Vijay Rakesh, 2026-08-16; **MU Outperform PT $1,375, SNDK Outperform ▼PT $1,900 from $2,200**.)
- **The ladder as published:** **HBM content/GPU flat at 288GB (HBM4) from GB300/BW300 to VR200** · **VR-Ultra to 256GB HBM4e with HBM4e pricing up 70-100%** · **Feynman at 768GB/GPU on HBM4e/16Hi**, *"potentially constrained as **LTAs/SCAs/NBMs determine pricing**"* · **HBM density up 3.5x for the NVL576 architectures (Oberon x4, Taycan) vs NVL72**, flagged *"positive MU"*.
- 🔴 **THE COLLISION TABLE ON VR-ULTRA / RUBIN-ULTRA CONTENT, ALL FOUR MARKS DATED AND ATTRIBUTED, DELIBERATELY NOT AVERAGED: Mizuho **256GB** HBM4e (primary, 08-16) · FundaAI **192GB** HBM4 8-Hi on a "Rubin Ultra Lite" NVL576 (OCP APAC, 08-17) · Fubon **~384GB** on the surviving two-compute-die part with the four-die ">1,024GB/chip" part CANCELLED (08-03) · UBS/JPM's de-spec sizing at **384GB → 192GB (12-Hi → 8-Hi)** (08-07/08-10).** ➜ **Note the structure of the disagreement: it is not noise, it is SKU ambiguity. "Rubin Ultra" now refers to at least two different parts (a 2-die and a cancelled 4-die), on at least two different rack architectures (NVL72 vs NVL576), at two stack heights (8-Hi vs 12-Hi/16-Hi). Any per-GPU content number on this page must name the SKU and the rack, or it is unusable.**
- ⚠️ **AND A RELAY-vs-PRIMARY CORRECTION THAT MATTERS FOR THIS PAGE'S 08-17 BLOCK: the TMTB relay of this same Mizuho note (carried this morning) reads "HBM content stays roughly FLAT from GB300 to VR200 before **stepping up materially** with VR-Ultra." The primary says VR-Ultra goes **DOWN** to 256GB from 288GB, and it is the PRICE that steps up (+70-100% on HBM4e). The relay folded a price claim into a content claim and inverted the sign on capacity. Primary governs; the relay text is retained on [MU](../MU.md).**
- ⚠️ **UNRECONCILED INSIDE THE NOTE, FLAGGED NOT NETTED: a 3.5x system-level density step for NVL576 sits alongside a −~11% per-GPU capacity step, with no bridge shown (GPU/package count per system, package-vs-die basis, GB vs GB-per-area). DO NOT MULTIPLY THEM. What is safe: the "positive MU" claim runs through SYSTEM density and PRICE, not through gigabytes per accelerator.**
- ⚠️ **Calibrate against the standing content marks here: BofA has HBM content/accelerator 187GB → 464GB CY25-30 (~20% CAGR) with Vera Rubin at 288GB HBM4/accelerator (AI 2030, 05-13). Mizuho's 288GB VR200 AGREES with BofA on VR200 — the whole dispute is the generation AFTER, and Mizuho's 768GB Feynman is well above BofA's 464GB CY30 exit. So the two houses are not far apart on the shape (content growth resumes) and far apart on the timing (Mizuho: not until Feynman).**
- 🔴 **The clause with the widest implication, and it is a PRICE-CAPTURE clause, not a price clause:** Feynman HBM *"potentially constrained as LTAs/SCAs/NBMs determine pricing."* ➜ **Test the +70-100% HBM4e price call against the CONTRACT STRUCTURES this page documented on 08-15, because they are opposite in sign: [[MU]]'s first 16 SCAs carry a CQ2-2026 price CEILING (so capped volume cannot capture the melt-up), while [[SNDK]]'s NBMs are a FLOOR with the uncontracted third floating freely. ⚠️ Mizuho writes "LTAs/SCAs/NBMs" as one category and does not distinguish them. It is not one category. An industry HBM4e price move of +70-100% therefore does NOT flow through to the two biggest contract books in the same proportion — which is the single most important asymmetry on this page and is preserved here rather than netted.**

**7. 🔴 THE DEMAND-SIDE MIRROR IMAGE, AND IT IS A SECOND *INDEPENDENT* ARGUMENT AGAINST THE TRENDFORCE OVERSUPPLY CALL — MORGAN STANLEY REVERSES ITS OWN VIEW: ENTERPRISES ARE **ACCELERATING** HARDWARE PURCHASES *BECAUSE* OF MEMORY INFLATION.** (Morgan Stanley · Erik Woodring (+ Howard Kao), "Global IT Hardware: AI + Chipflation: Two Tailwinds, One Trade… For Now", **2026-08-10**, 98pp. ⚠️ **An IT-HARDWARE note, taken here for the memory-demand read only; the OEM ratings/PTs belong on the hardware pages.**)
- **The pivot, and the reversal is explicit:** enterprises now treat memory **'Chipflation' as a multi-year STRUCTURAL headwind** and are therefore **accelerating rather than deferring purchases** — MS's phrase is **"Fear of Missing Procurement"** — to lock pricing and limit shortage risk. **MS states this is a reversal of its own house view from last November, when it expected component inflation to stymie the hardware recovery.**
- **The survey, with the trend comparison that makes it usable (AlphaWise, n=60 US+EU CIOs):** **2027 CIO server AND storage revenue growth expectations at all-time survey highs — server +4.9% vs a 1.4% trailing-8-year average, storage +4.8% vs 3.0%** (i.e. **180-340bp above trend**). **70-75% of CIOs are pulling forward 2026 PC/Storage/Server spend; 20-30% are also pulling forward 2027 spend**, most acutely in Servers and PCs. **60% expect to increase on-prem compute/storage for AI workloads, but >70% of AI inferencing is still expected to happen in the public cloud and to grow over the next 3 years.**
- 🔴 **WHY THIS BELONGS ON *THIS* PAGE AND NOT ONLY ON THE HARDWARE PAGES: it is a second, independent, SURVEY-BASED argument that 2027-28 memory demand holds — sourced from the BUYERS rather than from the sellers, and therefore not vulnerable to the "suppliers talk their book" objection that applies to every fill-rate quote in §4. Set the two demand arguments against TrendForce's sufficiency flip: TrendForce's 2028 oversupply requires demand to behave normally while cumulative capacity lands. FUNDA argues supply cannot land on schedule (equipment shortage); MS argues demand is being PULLED FORWARD into exactly those years. Both would have to be wrong for +4.0% DRAM sufficiency in 2028 to print.**
- ⚠️ **BUT CARRY MS'S OWN COUNTERWEIGHT, BECAUSE IT IS THE HONEST HALF OF THE NOTE AND IT CUTS AT THIS THEME TOO:** MS moved US IT Hardware from **Cautious to In-Line** (not to Attractive), noting hardware stocks are **+100% since the start of 2025**, the US aggregate trades at **25x P/E — nearly double the prior peak multiple** — and even ex-AI-winners traditional enterprise hardware is at **20x NTM P/E, 20%+ above its historical peak**. Its verdict: *"probably closer to the end than the beginning of the upcycle,"* with the trigger to turn cautious again being **peaking estimate-revision breadth** (already **60%** for the group). ➜ **The read across to memory equities is direct and uncomfortable: pull-forward is demand you have already been paid for. A survey showing 70-75% of CIOs pulling 2026 spend forward and 20-30% pulling 2027 forward is bullish for 2026-27 volumes and, mechanically, a HEADWIND to 2028 — the same year TrendForce has sufficiency turning positive. 🔴 Held as a genuine tension rather than resolved: the strongest bull argument for 2027 is also a bear argument for 2028, and it is MS's own framework that says so.**
- **Also logged, since it bears on the affordability risk this page tracks:** MS has median **CY26 EPS 8% above consensus and CY27 EPS 11% above** across the US+Asia OEM universe (~5% above Street revenue, 9-12% above Street EPS), and expects the rollover in **PCs 2027, Servers 2027/2028, Storage 2028 or beyond** — *"Storage over Servers over PCs."* ➜ **The expected-rollover sequence is a demand-side echo of the DRAM-more-defended-than-NAND structure already on this page, arrived at independently and from the buyer side.**

## Recent signals (Aug 17, /wiki-ingest) — 🔴 **[[SAMSUNG]] MOVES *BACK-END FLOOR SPACE*, NOT WAFER STARTS: PACKAGING/TEST TO VIETNAM AND AN R&D FAB TO FOUNDRY, FOR A REPORTED 170K → 250K HBM WPM BY YEAR-END — WHILE THE RUBIN ULTRA DE-SPEC SURFACES AT A THIRD VENUE AND BofA APPLIES [[SNDK]]'S FRAMEWORK TO [[MU]] TO GET ">$230 EPS"**

**1. The Samsung capacity story is a BACK-END story, and that is why it matters.** Two reports, same objective. **(a) Front end:** "Samsung is considering changing the plan for its **NRD K2 fab from an R&D facility to FOUNDRY** to free capacity for **2nm base dies for HBM chips for custom HBM and HBM5**, as the timeline for this plant is 2H28"; **HBM5 base dies will use Samsung's GAA-based 2nm process, with the generation expected to run ~50% faster than HBM4E** (ZDNet Korea / ETNews via Jefferies · Conor O'Mara, 2026-08-17). **(b) Back end:** Samsung is "weighing the relocation of general-purpose DRAM and NAND flash **packaging and testing** operations from **Cheonan and Onyang to VIETNAM**… **This would increase HBM WPM from 170K to 250K by the end of 2026**" (Dealsite / Digitimes via Jefferies, 2026-08-17).
- **The physical reason it must be a relocation, not an expansion — the most transferable fact in the note:** a conventional DRAM/NAND package runs **~700 process steps**; an HBM stack requires **~19 additional materials-engineering steps on entirely different equipment** — TSV formation (etch + copper fill), wafer thinning to **~30 µm**, precision die stacking, and thermal compression bonding of 12-16 dies. **"None of the equipment for any of these steps can also perform conventional DRAM or NAND packaging operations."** Samsung has confirmed **Cheonan is at full HBM stacking capacity across C1/C2/C3**; **Onyang broke ground on an eight-floor HBM backend building in early 2026** but those lines also cannot share floor space with commodity packaging.
- ⚠️ **Sourcing discipline: all four are Korean/Taiwanese trade outlets relayed through a sales desk, the verbs are "considering"/"weighing", and the 170K→250K WPM figure is the reporter's. If it lands it is ~+47% of Samsung HBM back-end inside ~4 months — which is the supply-side answer to the HBM4/HBM5 share debate, and a reason to hold the 2027 tightness case loosely.**

**2. The Rubin Ultra HBM de-spec surfaces at a third venue.** FundaAI, from the **OCP APAC Summit in Taipei**: **"A possible Rubin Ultra HBM de-spec came up repeatedly in our team's conversations. The version being discussed moves Rubin Ultra NVL72 on HBM4E 12-Hi at 384GB to Rubin Ultra 'Lite' NVL576 on HBM4 8-Hi at 192GB"** (FundaAI, 2026-08-17). Mizuho independently has **"HBM content roughly FLAT from GB300 to VR200 before stepping up materially with VR-Ultra"** (via TMT Breakout, 2026-08-17). 🔴 **Log the direct contradiction with the bull side the same day: BofA's Vivek Arya says "recent lower-memory Rubin Ultra discussions are OVERSTATED given the HBM4e and HBM5 roadmaps already point towards 500GB-1TB configurations and industry capacity migrates towards 12-Hi and 16-Hi stacks. Even in a lower-stack scenario, higher GPU volumes would likely offset any HBM content reductions."** ➜ **Arya's is a VOLUME-OFFSET argument, not a denial of the de-spec — the two positions are compatible on content-per-GPU and incompatible on total HBM bits. That distinction is the whole trade.**

**3. BofA transplants the [[SNDK]] long-term model onto [[MU]] and gets a re-rating case.** "Applying SNDK-like assumptions — **~15% sales CAGR, ~80% gross margin and 30%+ FCF CAGR** — BofA sees **MU EPS potentially reaching $230+ by FY30, versus prior-cycle peak EPS around $160-170**, which could support a **12-15× P/E**" (BofA · Arya, reiterate Buy PT $1,550, 2026-08-17), plus **$80bn+ TTM FCF supporting ~10% annual buybacks once CHIPS restrictions expire 2026-12-09**. ⚠️ **This page should flag the method, not just the number: an ~80% gross margin is SNDK's own un-earned long-term model (logged here on Aug 13 as the largest consensus gap on the wiki), and it is now being applied to a second company as if it were an industry template. Also unaddressed by BofA: the house finding that consensus CY27 MU GM of 86.0% sits ~415bp ABOVE the 81.86% MU actually printed in CQ2-2026 — the quarter that caps most contracted volume.** SNDK's own capital-allocation follow-through: **an additional $14bn buyback authorisation, taking the remaining authorisation to $15.5bn**, with the CFO saying buybacks are preferred over dividends (@TheTranscript_, 2026-08-16); **LTAs already cover 2/3 of 2028 output and minimum contracted revenue reaches $93bn**.

**4. Legacy memory is the tightest part of the complex, and it is tightening because the majors are LEAVING.** MS (Daniel Yen / Charlie Chan, "Old Memory: No LTAs Is Not Necessarily Bad", 2026-08-16): **DDR4 +50% pricing in 3Q26 and another double-digit hike in 4Q26**, with demand "broadening… from consumer-related applications as well" and **"global mainstream vendors continu[ing] to phase out the DDR4 supply"**; **SLC NAND is MS's highest-conviction call at 50%+ price increases in BOTH 3Q26 and 4Q26**, with tightness "extending into 2027, suggesting the current cycle could prove longer than investors expect" and MLC→SLC migration leaving upside into 1H27; **NOR another round of hikes likely in 4Q26 into 1H27**. Estimate raises went to Winbond (+17/30/34% 26-28E EPS), Macronix (+139/144/147%), GigaDevice (+108/49/48%, PT cut to Rmb750 from Rmb888 on post-CXMT-IPO derating) and PSMC. 🔴 **The cost of all this, from the demand side: "demand destruction from higher memory prices continues to remain a concern in mainstream categories like smartphones, as is the impact in particular of a structural shortage of LEGACY memory for many categories such as industrial, automotive and telecoms"** (DB · Robert Sanders, "Semis skimmed — Pricing and lead time tracker: Aug 2026", >1,500 SKUs scraped weekly through the distributor channel, 2026-08-17). First hard consumer evidence: **Japanese refurbished iPhone sales MORE THAN DOUBLED after [[AAPL]]'s July price increases** (Nikkei via Jefferies, 2026-08-17).

**5. The politics of the marginal bit.** **Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick has told [[AAPL]] directly that the administration opposes its use of Chinese memory, even as Apple tests CXMT and YMTC parts for China-market devices; [[MU]] lobbied for the block and senators from memory-manufacturing states joined; Apple COO Sabih Khan says the shortage means the company must "look at all options"** (WSJ, 2026-08-16/17). ⚠️ **The second-order effect ran the other way: CXMT became China's most valuable listed company on the same report, which UBS called a "key catalyst"** (@zerohedge, 2026-08-17). **MU +4%, [[SNDK]] +9%, [[KIOXIA]] +15% in Tokyo, CXMT +9% on 08-17.**

**6. Tiering, not substitution — the architecture argument.** BNP's Flash Memory Summit takeaway: **"no single memory technology can economically handle every inference workload, creating room for CXL, HBF, LPDDR and HBM to coexist."** **CXL memory-expansion products from [[MRVL]]/[[ALAB]] ramp 1H27, true memory POOLING later in 2027 as PCIe 6 CPUs (AMD Venice, Intel Diamond Rapids) reach volume. HBF is "complementary rather than cannibalistic to HBM, effectively acting like a very fast, high-capacity SSD for KV-cache/inference workloads."** BNP also flags **DRAM tightness positively via customizable 96GB/192GB SOCAMM2 configurations on Vera Rubin**, and names **[[CRDO]]'s "Weaver" memory gearbox** as another architecture aimed at flattening the memory hierarchy (BNP via TMT Breakout, 2026-08-17). Un-modelled demand line worth tracking: **"persistent/long-horizon agent memory — SanDisk didn't even include it in their TAM estimates… probably around 500-600EB by 2030"** (@zephyr_z9, 2026-08-16). ⚠️ Single-handle X estimate, no methodology. **BofA's new Frontier AI tracker has DRAM prices +8% MoM against B200 GPU rental −2% MoM** (2026-08-17).

## 🆕 Recent signals (Aug 15, /run-inbox) — 🔴🔴 **THE TWO BIGGEST LTA BOOKS IN MEMORY TURN OUT TO BE PRICED THE OPPOSITE WAY ROUND: [[MU]] SOLD ITS CEILING, [[SNDK]] KEPT IT. AND [[MU]] PUTS A NUMBER ON HBM CANNIBALISATION — 3:1 AT HBM3E, ~4:1 BY HBM4E.**

- 🔴🔴 **THE MOST IMPORTANT STRUCTURAL DISTINCTION THIS THEME HAS DRAWN ALL MONTH, AND IT COMES FROM MANAGEMENT ON BOTH SIDES. The market has been treating "memory LTAs" as one trade — downside protected, upside retained. The two disclosures put side by side say that is only true for one of them.**
  - **[[MU]] — the SCAs are a price BAND with a CEILING.** *"most of the volume… is going to have a **price band**. And so there is a **ceiling price. The ceiling price for the SCAs that we announced at the time of earnings of 16 SCAs, our CQ2 pricing**"*; later SCAs ceiling at market-on-signing; the floor is set to give GM *"well-above any prior peak in the cycles of the industry"* (**Sumit Sadana, EVP & CBO, KeyBanc Technology Leadership Forum, 2026-08-10**, Bloomberg FINAL TRANSCRIPT). ⚠️ ASR: "flow pricing" = **floor** pricing.
  - **[[SNDK]] — the NBMs are a FLOOR with the upside left open.** Floor pricing *"itself supports ~80% GM"* while *"the **remaining uncommitted capacity floats with prevailing market prices, which preserves earnings leverage in an upcycle**"* (SanDisk Investor Day, 2026-08-13; framing corroborated by SIG, BofA, JPM and FUNDA).
  - ➜ **So on a further NAND/DRAM melt-up, SanDisk participates on its uncontracted third and Micron largely does not, on the 16 SCAs already signed. CONTRACT VINTAGE becomes an earnings-power variable for Micron: deals struck at the CQ2-2026 ceiling are capped lower than deals struck later. This is a mechanical, disclosed brake on 2027 upside that this theme was not carrying, and it argues the two names should NOT be traded as one memory-LTA expression.**
  - **The floor levels differ too, and in the same direction:** UBS's semicap analyst estimates *"I don't know that Micron would say 80% [at floor]… they did say at floor pricing they would be **considerably higher than prior peak, and prior peak was in the low 60s. So it's probably going to be 70, 75 at least**"* (UBS · Timothy Arcuri, sector-sales callback, 2026-08-14). **SNDK ~80% at floor vs MU ~70-75% — an analyst estimate, explicitly pre-verification.**
- 🔴 **NET-NEW AND QUANTIFIED — THE HBM/DDR TRADE RATIO, WHICH IS THE SUPPLY MECHANISM UNDER THE WHOLE DRAM SHORTAGE:** *"if we produce **100 bits of HBM, we have to reduce 300 bits of DDR supply** because there is that **3:1 trade ratio between HBM 3E and DDR**. You go to **HBM4 and HBM4E**, by the time you get to 4E, that trade ratio has **worsened to closer to 4:1**"* (Sadana, 2026-08-10). ➜ **This is the cleanest available answer to "why is conventional DRAM still tight while wafer starts rise": every HBM bit destroys 3-4 commodity bits, and the destruction rate RISES with each HBM generation. It implies the HBM4E ramp is mechanically more deflationary to available DDR supply than HBM3E's was — a supply headwind that arrives on a known schedule.** Reads onto [[MU]], [[SKHYNIX]], [[SAMSUNG]].
- 🔴 **HBF FLIPS FROM A DEMAND OPTION TO A SUPPLY SINK, AND THAT CHANGES WHO IT PAYS OFF FOR.** Bernstein: HBF *"would also require **3x to 4x the wafer capacity (in our estimates) per Exabyte** due to the additional space required on-chip. This could **significantly eat up wafer supply and lead to shortages continuing far longer than even the bulls expect**"* — roughly **4x the die count and wafer area** versus a conventional ~128GB die, raising HBF's *"share of effective industry wafer capacity relative to the bits delivered, perpetuating supply tightness and supporting pricing"* (Bernstein · Mark C. Newman / April Li / Phoebe Sun, "Why Sandisk investor day makes us more bullish on SNDK," 2026-08-14). ➜ **Every prior HBF mention on this wiki framed it as SNDK-specific optionality. On Bernstein's construction HBF succeeding tightens the entire NAND market — the first HBF argument that pays off for [[MU]] and [[SKHYNIX]] holders too.**
- **⚠️ THE COUNTER-VOICE, AND IT IS THE ONLY SKEPTIC IN THE POST-INVESTOR-DAY SET:** *"I skew a little bit **skeptical on HBF**… SanDisk has been talking about this for, I don't know, **two years** now… **I don't see a lot of evidence that it's really taking off**"* (UBS · Arcuri, 2026-08-14). ➜ **Worth weighting because an HBF ramp would appear in NAND WFE orders before it appeared in SNDK revenue, and equipment is what he covers.**
- ✅ **CONFIRMED FROM MANAGEMENT (previously broker-only): MU's 16 SCAs carry $22bn of cash and cash-like commitments, of which $18bn is cash on balance sheet** — matching Bernstein's $18bn deposits + $4bn letters of credit. Terms are take-or-pay with *"**no contractual outs**"*, volumes committed **by year, by customer**. ⚠️ **But asked directly why these won't break like the COVID-era LTAs, Sadana's answer is a customer-mindset argument, NOT an enforcement mechanism — so this theme's standing skeptic position ("never tested through a real downturn") survives intact.** Duration extended: *"**2027 calendar year will be even tighter**."*
- **On the [[SNDK]] side, the NBM book gets its first REALISED test rather than another contractual term:** *"**2 of the early customers came back to renew and increase the NBMs**,"* and the guarantee **coverage ratio rises ~2x over two years** into the outer years (Bernstein, 2026-08-14). **Company primary confirms coverage at 50% of FY27 supply → ~67% in FY28** — note that **Citi's "50% in FY26, ~66% in FY27" is a one-year shift and is not adopted**.
- **Supply-side primary, now on disk:** SanDisk's own capacity slide — wafer capacity **peaked ~1,830 kwpm; ~500 kwpm idled at the ~70%-utilisation trough; ~560 kwpm RETIRED; the rest now ~100% utilised**. ➜ **The widely-quoted "industry capacity ~30% below prior peak" is a RETIREMENT, not idling. Retired capacity does not come back on a price signal, which is the strongest available argument that this cycle's supply response is slower than prior cycles.** (SanDisk Investor Day 2026 deck, 98pp.)

## Recent signals (Aug 13) — 🔴🔴 **[[SNDK]]'S INVESTOR DAY PUTS A THREE-YEAR ~80% GROSS MARGIN ON THE RECORD FOR NAND, AND THE CONSENSUS GAP IT IMPLIES IS THE LARGEST ON THIS WIKI**

**The FY28–30 model: revenue growth mid-to-high teens % y/y (SIG reads a 15–20% CAGR) · gross margin ~80% average · operating margin 75% · FCF margin ~50% · 100% of excess FCF returned, with $15.5bn of buyback authorisation remaining. Stock +15–16% on a day the rest of the AI complex sold off.**

- **🔴 WHY IT MATTERS TO THIS THEME RATHER THAN JUST TO SNDK: it is a claim that NAND cyclicality has been structurally broken, and it comes with a mechanism.** The **New Business Model (NBM)** book: **8 customers · $93.9bn total contract value · $91.1bn RPO · $16.5bn of financial guarantees** (mostly held by or provided through third-party financial institutions, ~$2.5bn in cash) · **~50% of FY27 bits and ~2/3 of FY28 bits already committed** · **3 of the 8 are US hyperscalers** · **>4 years of average visibility**. Management: *"**even at the lowest price, at any point in time, don't expect margins on NBMs to be below 80%**"* (Jefferies desk); SIG independently — *"**floor pricing itself supports ~80% GM**, while non-NBM volumes retain upside exposure to market pricing"* (SIG · Mehdi Hosseini, Positive, PT $2,745, 2026-08-13).
- **🔴 THE CONSENSUS GAP, QUANTIFIED: the top-line guide "implies big upward revisions to current consensus forecasts (+16% / -4% / -30% y-y for FY28E / FY29E / FY30E)"** (Jefferies TMT desk · Rankie Wong, 2026-08-13, sales & trading commentary). ⚠️ **Consensus is modelling FY30 revenue ~30% BELOW the company's own framework — i.e. the Street is still underwriting a cycle-down that management says will not occur. That is the cleanest expression on this wiki of the "is memory still cyclical?" debate.**
- **⚠️ THE HONEST BULL FRAMING, from the same desk, and it is the one to carry:** *"**my sense is that peak bearishness in memory is already past us**… still that we are likely to get a **more slow-burn (and healthy) recovery, rather than a V-shaped bounce** — the ferocity of EPS revisions will inevitably slow as the pace of ASP increases start to decelerate, and **noise around risk from China supply will come and go, but will never fully go away**. Yes, objectively the stocks are dirt cheap — but **the market remains skeptical on whether the brutal cyclicality in memory is really truly behind us. So it will probably take a few more Q's of consistent execution… for the multiple expansion thesis to fully play out**"* (Jefferies desk, 2026-08-13). GS reaches the same place: NBMs *"could help mute industry cyclicality, [but] we think **this concept still needs to be proven — and it will take time to be fully reflected in the stock's multiple**"* (GS · James Schneider, Buy, 2026-08-13). ⚠️ **And the loudest investor pushback on the day: there is NO THROUGH-CYCLE MODEL — only three years.**
- **🔴 SUPPLY IS NOW SET BY TRANSITION PACE, NOT WAFER ADDS — and there is more headroom than the plan uses.** SIG: **~27% annual bit-per-wafer productivity growth from BiCS5 through BiCS11, "well above its planned mid-to-high-teens output growth"**, with **BiCS8→BiCS10 alone delivering 65% more bits per wafer**; BofA states the same roadmap as **~54% average generation-to-generation bit-per-wafer growth across BiCS5→BiCS11**. ⚠️ **BASES: ~27% PER YEAR (SIG) and ~54% PER GENERATION (BofA) are different denominators and must not be netted — a generation spans more than a year. Both logged as published.** ➜ **The strategic reading is what matters: SanDisk is choosing to withhold supply it demonstrably could deliver, which is exactly the discipline the ~80% GM claim requires — and exactly the discipline a competitor could break.**
- **TAM and mix, restated by the company: NAND >$300bn in CY26 (~3x y/y) approaching ~$500bn in CY27; data center at ~50% of industry bits in CY26 vs ~30% last year** (SIG). ⚠️ **BofA's accompanying growth rates (data center at a high-teens CAGR vs Consumer/Edge at 30%+) appear transposed against the "data center overtakes consumer in 2026" claim they support — flagged, not adopted; use SIG's bit-share figures.**
- **🔴 KV CACHE GETS SIZED AND, MORE USEFULLY, GETS A MEMORY HIERARCHY: ~1ZB persistent KV-cache installed base by 2030, ~35% of the NAND market in that year** (SIG; Jefferies logs it as *"35% of the 1.2ZB AI DC TAM by 2030E"*). SIG's net-new architectural point: **QLC sits in the colder, high-capacity data-lake tier while higher-performance TLC SSDs sit closer to the GPU for staging, checkpointing and hotter KV-cache workloads** — *"suggests that **inference-driven NAND demand may carry a RICHER MIX than a capacity-only thesis would imply**."* ➜ **Directly relevant to the QLC-vs-TLC split this theme has been tracking: the answer being proposed is "both, at different tiers," not "QLC wins."**
- **⚠️ HBF DISAPPOINTED ON TIMING, AND IT IS THE HBM-ADJACENT PART OF THIS PAGE:** **first HBF memory die TAPED OUT, first inference product samples in 2027, NO commercial shipment timeline, and explicitly NOT in revenue projections**; **[[META]] has joined the HBF Technology Consortium** (with SNDK, [[SKHYNIX]], [[GOOG]] and Tenstorrent). Jefferies desk: *"relatively **lackluster/disappointing** — clearly seems like it's still relatively far away… **lots of direct comparisons between HBF and HBM**, so management clearly framing **HBF as a substitute for HBM**, and a way to **materially reduce GPU requirements for AI inference**"* (2026-08-13). ➜ **The substitution ambition is now explicit; the timeline is not.**
- **PEER CAPITAL-RETURN BENCHMARKS, useful for the whole memory complex: Kioxia 50% of excess FCF + a ~$5bn buyback · [[MU]] targeting 100% of FCF via dividends and buybacks · [[SAMSUNG]] and [[SKHYNIX]] both at 50%** — against SanDisk's new **100% of excess FCF** (Jefferies desk, 2026-08-13).
- **✅ A CROSS-THEME LINKAGE WORTH CARRYING: NAND price strength transmits into HDD revenue faster than the data-center contract book does.** [[STX]] expert commentary the same day: **Edge IoT (~20% of Seagate revenue) competes DIRECTLY with NAND and re-prices quarter-to-quarter, producing "very healthy" price increases** (Capstone recap of an expert call, 2026-08-13). ⚠️ **Speaker identified only as "likely from Seagate" — expert-network commentary, not company guidance.**
