> **Backfill note: the expert call below is dated 2026-07-30 and reached the wiki late via /run-inbox on 08-12. The 07-30 summary was already ingested; what follows is net-new from the full transcript. ⚠️ EXPERT VIEW throughout — Hongkyo Jung, former SK Hynix executive, on a BTG Pactual-hosted call. Not a BTG house estimate; no rating or PT attaches.**

- 🔴 **A BEAR-SIDE 2027 HBM PRICE CEILING OF +20-30%, AND THE BINDING CONSTRAINT IS [[NVDA]]'s OWN MARGIN POLICY RATHER THAN SUPPLY.** Against the widely-modelled doubling (*"HBM4 plus currently around 550, will be doubled next year, like 1,200 or 1,400"*), the expert expects only *"**between 20 to 30%**… because **Nvidia, they keep like 75% their profit for [the] overall GPU system. So they do not increase the BOM, the component cost, the HBM cost very much**."* Reference point: *"Nvidia adjusted **20% increase**"* on HBM in 1Q. ⚠️ **This is the LOW END of every 2027 HBM ASP input this page carries (Redburn/UBS <40%, Susquehanna +35% blended, bulls 50-100%, SemiAnalysis "significant increase to match commodity-DRAM margins") — and it argues the supplier repricing runs into a CUSTOMER-MARGIN wall rather than a demand wall. That is a different failure mode from the one this page has been modelling, and it is testable: it predicts HBM ASP decelerates while HBM VOLUME does not.** **2028 is the hinge year** (all three players' new fabs land then), mitigated by LTA coverage on **customised** product (*"not like a commodity product where they can sell to the others"*) and by hyperscaler capex that is already committed (*"they cannot reduce now"*). (BTG Pactual-hosted expert call · Hongkyo Jung, former SK Hynix executive, 2026-07-30) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_30_btg_former_hynix_expert_30_07_26.html)
- 🔴 **THE SPOT-VS-CONTRACT GAP, QUANTIFIED — and it explains why Korean reported ASPs looked weak against the trackers in 2Q.** TrendForce expected 2Q **DRAM +55% / NAND +70%**; SK hynix reported **+30% / +50%**. The expert: *"those numbers are more for the **SPOT MARKET** prices increases. Not… the **CONTRACT** prices. The actual price that these big players apply with customers, **it is they do not follow the same pattern**."* ➜ **Practical rule for this page: marking Korean memory to TrendForce spot mismeasures by ~25pts on DRAM in a spike quarter. Spot is a leading indicator of direction, not a proxy for realised ASP.** (same call)
- **INDUSTRY CAPACITY ON A WAFER-INPUT BASIS — the cross-company map this page has lacked: [[SAMSUNG]] ~700k wpm · [[SKHYNIX]] ~600k wpm · [[MU]] ~300k wpm**, DRAM share ~35-38% / ~28%. Supply meets only **"60% to 70%"** of demand, hence *"whatever they produce, they buy… this is a supply side market. **But once supply is big enough compared to demand… they will choose better product or cheaper price**."* ⚠️ **Sits in tension with the yield-led framing elsewhere on this page (SK hynix HBM4 yield ~80-85% vs Samsung ~60%, the stated reason *"NVIDIA highly rely on SK Hynix… because SK Hynix with high yield provide high volume"* for Rubin): the expert's discriminator is VOLUME, not technology. Both retained — they imply different share paths as capacity lands in 2028.** (same call)
- **THE ROADMAP BREAKS FROM SCALING TO STACKING, WITH A DATE.** Below the 1D node *"it is practically difficult to scale down further,"* so **3D DRAM is targeted for commercialisation *"maybe in 2032 or 2033"***, and **hybrid bonding replaces TCB from HBM4E/HBM5** for speed, energy efficiency and heat dissipation. ⚠️ Expert view, not a house forecast. (same call)
- 🔴 **TWO HOUSES, ONE FACT, OPPOSITE CONCLUSIONS ON THE NAND MULTIPLE — and it converts the LTA debate from an earnings debate into a multiple debate.** On [[SNDK]]'s **two-thirds of volume at an 80% floor gross margin**: **Bernstein** says *"greater LTA coverage is **POSITIVE** for the stock's multiple and re-rating potential… reduce perceived cyclicality"* (Outperform, PT $3,000); **Jefferies**, the same day, says *"if you're signing deals at 80% gross margin, **your ASPs are not going up as much**… **THE MULTIPLE CONTRACTS**."* ➜ **Same contract structure, opposite sign on the multiple. The testable split: Bernstein is pricing reduced downside variance, Jefferies is pricing forgone upside — so the resolution comes from what spot does NEXT, not from the LTA terms themselves.** (Bernstein · Mark C. Newman / April Li / Phoebe Sun, "What we learned from our NDR with STX", 2026-08-07; Jefferies · Blayne Curtis, 2026-08-07) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bernstein_WDC_Memory_Storage_What_we_learned_from_our_NDR_with_STX.html)

## Recent signals (Aug 11, /run-inbox — PRIMARY PDFs) — THE EQUIPMENT LAYER PUTS **$69bn / $96bn** OF DRAM WFE BEHIND THE 2027-28 SUPPLY DEBATE, AND THE KOREAN DEAL WEEKEND GETS ITS FACILITY-LEVEL DETAIL
- 🔴 **DRAM WFE IS THE BIGGEST SINGLE LINE IN BERNSTEIN'S WFE RAISE, AND IT IS THE SUPPLY-SIDE ANSWER TO THIS PAGE'S SHORTAGE DEBATE: DRAM WFE to $69bn (2027E) and $96bn (2028E), from $57bn / $71bn prior — +53% and +37% y/y — with NAND to $20bn / $29bn (from $18bn / $23bn), +58% / +42%. Raising WFE growth to 75% in two years", 2026-08-11). ⚠️ **Two readings, both worth carrying. (a) BULLISH FOR THE SHORTAGE THESIS in the near term — tools ordered in 2027 are bits in 2028-29, so a DRAM WFE line this size does not relieve the 2026-27 fill-rate problem this page tracks (60-70% fill, LTA tenors going 3y→5y). (b) BEARISH FOR 2028-29 — $96bn of DRAM WFE in 2028 is the capital formation that ends shortages, and it lands exactly where the JPM "glut narrows" revision (08-09, on this page) and the 2028 down-leg calls (DRAM −8% / NAND −14%) already point. The equipment layer is the leading indicator and it is now pointing at supply.**
- ⚠️ **NAND WFE IS AGAIN FORECAST TO GROW FASTER THAN DRAM in both years (+58%/+42% vs +53%/+37%), which is the same signal Jefferies used on 08-07 to build the BEARISH LTA argument — someone is adding NAND capacity. Bernstein does not reconcile the two; both stay on the page.** **Also relevant to the China supply overhang: Bernstein has China WFE at $57bn / $73bn / $101bn for 2026-28E with memory "still expected to LEAD the surge with +88% / +81% / +62% y/y growth" — the fastest-growing application inside the fastest-revised region.** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/BERN_259278.html)
- **THE KOREAN DEAL WEEKEND, from the primary of a note this page has carried only as a desk relay (Bernstein · Mark Li / Stacy Rasgon et al., "Global Memory, NVIDIA & Broadcom: Quick thoughts on strategic partnerships", 2026-07-27; ingested 08-11). ① The [[SKHYNIX]]/[[NVDA]] LOIs cover memory AND "a 2GW VERA RUBIN DSX AI FACTORY planned to be online FOR SK TELECOM IN 2027" — dated, sized, named offtaker. The memory leg is "long-term technical developments & STABLE SUPPLY of next-gen AI memory (HBM4 & later?)" (question mark Bernstein's). ② NEW TO THE WIKI: "SK Group reportedly will ADDITIONALLY collaborate with other global tech companies for memory supply worth US$250B OVER THE NEXT 5 YEARS" — sourcing is "reportedly," not disclosure, and there are no named counterparties. ③ Industry frame: consensus memory revenue ~US$0.9T (CY26) → US$1.3T (CY27) → US$1.3T (CY28) across Samsung Memory / SK hynix / Micron / SanDisk / KIOXIA / CXMT / YMTC.** ⚠️ **The FLATNESS from '27 to '28 is the part worth attacking — it is a consensus artefact, not a Bernstein forecast, and it sits oddly against both the LTA-tenor extension and the $96bn of 2028 DRAM WFE above.**
- 🔴 **AND THE RATING LOGIC, which is a product-level discriminator this page should test: Bernstein is Outperform on [[SAMSUNG]], [[SKHYNIX]] and [[MU]] and UNDERPERFORM on [[KIOXIA]] — "we worry about the long-term threat from China in NAND… THE THREAT IS MUCH LOWER IN DRAM AS CHINA EVENTUALLY WILL FIND IT DIFFICULT TO COMPETE IN DRAM MARKET WITHOUT EUV."** ➤ **That is a LITHO-ACCESS CEILING argument, and it cuts against how the CXMT threat is measured across this wiki: UBS's 240K→292K→382K→466K wpm ramp and JPM's 16% capacity / 11% bit share by 28E both measure INSTALLED CAPACITY, which Bernstein says is not the variable that decides it. The disagreement is about the metric, not the number — and it is testable, because a DRAM node ceiling shows up in bit-per-wafer, not in wpm.** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260726_Bernstein_NVDA_Global_Memory-_NVIDIA_-_Broadcom-_Quick_thoughts_on_stra.html)

**(5) SUPPLY-SIDE ADDITIONS AND ONE REGISTER CHANGE.** [[SKHYNIX]] *"announced they would increase NAND production in China by 50%"* (SKHY **+5%**, partly on buyback anticipation), and separately **"[[SKHYNIX]] becomes [[KIOXIA]]'s largest shareholder as Toshiba trims stake"** (TMTB EOD Wrap; DIGITIMES, both 2026-08-11). ⚠️ **Both headline-level — no capacity base for the +50%, no stake percentages for the register change.** ➜ **Note the tension: a producer adding 50% NAND capacity in China while becoming the largest shareholder of a NAND competitor is a conflict, not a synergy, and it sits directly against the "NAND discipline" leg of the LTA bull case. Also on the page today: [[SNDK]] +2.6% into its 08-13 analyst day; DIGITIMES flags Samsung "zHBM" and "V10 BV-NAND" concepts (names only, no specs); and a South Korean minister "warns about CXMT capex as semiconductor profits debate heats up."**

## Recent signals (Aug 10, PM) — ⚠️ THE RUBIN ULTRA DE-SPEC RE-OPENS: MS SAYS **TIERED SKUs**, NOT A SINGLE SPEC, WITH THE DECISION STILL OPEN UNTIL END-3Q26 — AND THE PERFORMANCE COST IS LOCALISED TO **DECODING**

**⚠️⚠️ THIS PARTIALLY WALKS BACK THE "SETTLED" FRAMING THIS PAGE ADOPTED ON AUG 7.** On 08-07 UBS was read as closing the 8-Hi-vs-12-Hi question (*"HBM4 8-Hi for Rubin Ultra followed by a switch to HBM4E 8-Hi… vs our prior expectation of HBM4E 12-Hi"*). Morgan Stanley's Asia checks say the answer is **both, by SKU** (Morgan Stanley · Charlie Chan · Daniel Yen · Daisy Dai · Tiffany Yeh, 2026-08-10):
- *"We think Nvidia's **Rubin Ultra will feature several SKUs** with the **high-end version adopting HBM4e 8-Hi** and **lower-end versions using HBM4 12-Hi or 8-Hi**."* Drivers named in order: **(1) HBM capacity shortages, (2) cost pressures, (3) optimization for different workloads based on memory-capacity requirements.**
- **🔴 THE DECISION IS NOT YET MADE: "Nvidia should make final decision BEFORE END-3Q26."** ⚠️ **So the wiki should carry the 08-07 UBS spec as the leading case, not as a settled fact, until end-September.** The bit-demand and ASP work built on it (UBS's 25.1bn Gb 2027 NVDA HBM consumption, 61.5bn Gb industry, +79% blended ASP) is directionally intact under either outcome — a tiered mix sits *between* the two spec cases, not outside them.
- **🔴 NET-NEW AND THE MOST USEFUL LINE: the de-spec's cost is workload-specific.** *"Our checks suggest **HBM de-specification will have a more meaningful impact on DECODING than PRE-FILL workloads, particularly for long-context-window LLMs**."* This is the first attempt on this page to say **where** less HBM per package actually hurts — and it points at exactly the workload class (long-context agentic inference) that the [tokenmaxxing.md](tokenmaxxing.md) page has growing fastest.
- **The volume offset is restated, and extended to ASICs:** *"reducing memory content per chip **may increase overall chip shipments**, boosting volume-driven suppliers"*, and — the part that travels furthest — *"**we do not rule out major ASIC projects adopting different HBM configurations for similar reasons**."* ⚠️ **If the tiering logic propagates to TPU/Trainium/MTIA, the 2027 HBM bit forecasts built on fixed per-accelerator content are wrong in the same direction for merchant AND custom silicon.** Cross-ref [custom-asic-tpu.md](custom-asic-tpu.md), [../NVDA.md](../NVDA.md), [../MU.md](../MU.md), [../SKHYNIX.md](../SKHYNIX.md), [../SAMSUNG.md](../SAMSUNG.md).

**⚠️ THE ROTATION ARGUMENT IS NOW BEING MADE EXPLICITLY BY THE FOUNDRY ANALYST (JPM · Gokul Hariharan, [[TSM]] July sales note, 2026-08-10).** *"Recent **de-speccing trends in HBM/memory** are also likely to drive **incremental investor interest towards the AI compute complex, AWAY FROM MEMORY**."* ⚠️ Logged because it names the **flow-of-funds** consequence of the de-spec rather than the earnings consequence — and it is consistent with the desk-level sentiment read the same day: *"Feels like a **growing consensus that MEMORY is going to be hard, chop around, be in transition**, that there are easier long ideas out there… Feels both possible, sound, **but also possibly the wrong time to turn bearish** (after a big correction, and 4 'buyside' misses… but into multi-year gigawatt build-outs)"* (BofA TMT desk / Brian Fenske, 2026-08-10). **Both are sentiment observations, not forecasts — filed as positioning colour.**

## Recent signals (Aug 10) — the CXMT initiation lands in full: the DDR-vs-HBM ASP FORK is quantified (137% vs 43% CAGR), AI-server DRAM content is marked at 27x, and the AFFORDABILITY risk gets a hyperscaler number
- **The full 41-page UBS CXMT initiation (Jimmy Yu / Gaudois / Xinlei Li, Buy PT Rmb70, 2026-08-07) is now on file — the model behind the Monthly's "CXMT not a threat" call.** Supply path: 240K (end-25) → 292K → 382K → **466K wpm (end-28E)**; bits +40% CAGR to **58bn GB 2028E = c.10% of industry supply** (7% in '25); **bit growth then HALVES to 16% CAGR 2028-30E** as the no-EUV constraint bites beyond G5 (ArF multi-patterning, yields "not at par", US-tool installed-base support limits). CXMT HBM = 0.4% ('26E) → 2% ('28E) → **7% ('30E)** of its bits — HBM3 MP ramping (Feb-26), first domestic HBM3e sampling. [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260807_UBS_688825-CH_CXMT_Corporation_-Capitalising_on_the_memory_supercycle.html)
- 🔴 **THE ASP FORK, with its mechanism: "the top three DRAM suppliers have reallocated resources to support HBM, which has resulted in STRONGER PRICE HIKE MOMENTUM FOR DDR THAN HBM." UBS DDR ASP $0.40/Gb ('25) → $1.61 ('26E) → $2.24/Gb ('27E) = 137% CAGR, vs HBM $1.52 → $1.74 → $3.13 = 43%.** Blended industry DRAM ASP +238%/+45% '26/'27E. ⚠️ Basis note: these are the initiation's supply-side blended ASPs — do NOT net against the Monthly's NVDA-consumption HBM marks ($3.5-3.9/Gb HBM4/4E, +79% blended) also dated 08-07; different bases, both UBS, both stand.
- **Demand structure: DRAM content per AI server = 27x a standard server for hyperscalers (vs 2x in 2022; Gartner/UBS)** — the sharpest content-multiplier mark this page carries; server bit demand +66% 2025-27E vs +30% overall (global DRAM demand CAGR 30% '25-27E vs 14% '22-24); hyperscaler server shipments 22% CAGR '25-27E (vs 6% '22-24). Undersupply reaffirmed **until at least Q2'28**.
- ⚠️ **THE AFFORDABILITY RISK, sized: global memory semis revenue US$1.6trn+ in 2027, of which ">50% would be consumed directly or indirectly" by hyperscalers (via accelerators effectively reselling memory) — implying "YoY memory consumption could increase to over US$550bn for hyperscalers," who "will remain dependent on the capital markets to bridge the gap between capex and operating cash flow."** The buyer-financing dependence is now stated inside the bull note itself — same fault line as the Monthly's "main fundamental risk is memory spending sustainability."
- **The umbrella evidence: CXMT's blended ASP modelled BELOW industry ($1.33/2.14/2.17/Gb '26-28E) with smartphone-maker checks finding "limited price discounts" — and CXMT's 75-85% EBIT margins '26-28E (Q126 OM already 70%, disclosed) on a commodity-heavy mix are themselves the measure of how hot the DDR leg runs.** Company pages: [[SKHYNIX]] (the mix that underearns the fork), [[MU]] (the China-ban vacuum), [[SAMSUNG]] (longest the DDR leg). Localisation detail on [china-export](china-export.md).

## Recent signals (Aug 9) — ★ JPM's FULL MODEL LAYER: the HBM SHORTAGE NARROWS (glut −20/−35/−37% → −15/−14/−22%) YET HBM REVENUE GOES UP 12-13% — because the de-spec cuts BITS while ASP more than offsets; and ASIC passes NVDA as the biggest HBM bit consumer in 2027E

**Provenance note — this is the PRIMARY, and the wiki previously held only its top-line.** The headline bullets of this note (TAM +4-8%, bid-to-supply 70-80%, HBM pricing +42% y/y '27, memory value share 31%/49%, WSPM 1.9M→2.85M, LTA prepayment 20-25%) were folded into [../MU.md](../MU.md) on 08-10 and the OW-ranking/shareholder-return leg into [../SAMSUNG.md](../SAMSUNG.md). **What follows is the model layer underneath them, which was never taken** — Tables 8/9, the capacity maps and the HBM share/wallet series. (J.P. Morgan · **Jay Kwon** / Sangsik Lee / Neelay Kamath / Harlan Sur / Mio Shikanai, *"Global Memory Market — Global Memory TAM and S&D update: Stock correction is behind us but memory crisis is still ongoing"*, **2026-08-09**, 43pp)

**🔴 THE CENTRAL REVISION, AND IT CUTS BOTH WAYS.** JPM trims **HBM bit demand by 4%/10%/19%** across 2026-28E to **3,579 / 6,268 / 10,751mn (1GB-eq)** — and *still* raises HBM industry revenue by **13%/12%** in 27/28E. The reconciliation is price: HBM ASP goes to **$1.9 / $2.8 / $3.4 per Gb** (26/27/28E), **+8%/+13% above the May model** in 27/28E. **Bits down, dollars up.** The de-spec is therefore *not* a revenue event on JPM's numbers — it is a mix-and-price event.

| HBM (1GB-eq mn, US$mn) | 2026E | 2027E | 2028E | vs May-26 model |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Bit demand** | 3,579 | 6,268 | 10,751 | **−4% / −10% / −19%** |
| — of which **NVDA** | 2,101 | 2,607 | 4,854 | **−7% / −31% / −40%** ⬅ the whole cut is NVDA |
| — of which **AMD** | 235 | 634 | 999 | **+9% / +50% / +12%** |
| — of which **Others (ASIC)** | 1,243 | 3,028 | 4,898 | +1% / +11% / +14% |
| **Procurement demand** (t+4mo) | 4,451 | 7,205 | 11,449 | −7% / −20% / −18% |
| **Bit supply** | 4,152 | 6,613 | 9,350 | −1% / +5% / −1% |
| **Supply after CoWoS** | 3,802 | 6,205 | 8,960 | −1% / +6% / +2% |
| **CoWoS yield** | 92% | 94% | 96% | +1 / +1 / +3pp |
| **S-D glut ratio** | **−15%** | **−14%** | **−22%** | was −20% / −35% / −37% |
| **Industry revenue (US$mn)** | 64,287 | **145,617** | **252,075** | −1% / **+13% / +12%** |

- **⚠️ Read the glut row carefully — it is the one genuinely BEARISH-at-the-margin line in a bullish note.** JPM's HBM shortage is now **materially less severe than it modelled in May** (2027E glut −14% vs −35% prior; accumulated −13.9 weeks vs −24.2). The note does not lead with this. It remains a double-digit shortage in all three years, and JPM's framing is *"softening shortage but still a shortage"* — but anyone carrying the May-model shortage depth on this page should mark it down. **Held as JPM's mark, not adopted as the house view.**
- **🔴 ASIC OVERTAKES NVDA AS THE LARGEST HBM BIT CONSUMER IN 2027E — the single most strategically loaded number in the note.** HBM bit demand mix (chart-read, 2023→2028E): **NVDA 60% → 69% → 69% → 59% → 42% → 45%**; **ASIC 32% → 21% → 22% → 35% → 48% → 46%**; AMD 8-10% throughout. On JPM's numbers **the custom-ASIC complex consumes more HBM bits than NVIDIA from 2027**. Cross-read to [custom-asic-tpu](custom-asic-tpu.md).
- **HBM TAM path (US$mn):** 4,065 (2023) → 16,935 (2024) → 33,541 (2025) → **64,287 (2026E) → 145,617 (2027E) → 252,075 (2028E)**; y/y +317% / +98% / +92% / **+127% / +73%**.
- **HBM ASP mechanics.** Blended **+42% y/y in 2027E** — JPM calls this *"far more reasonable but shy of the market's bullish expectation of 2x y-y"* — decomposed into **30-40% like-for-like** hikes plus mix. By generation (US$/Gb): **HBM3E 1.8 / 2.3 / 2.3 · HBM4 2.3 / 2.9 / 3.3 · HBM4E 3.4 / 3.7 · HBM5 4.3** (2026-28E), i.e. **HBM4E carries a ~20% premium to HBM4 in 27E**. For 2028 JPM assumes **<10% like-for-like** with blended +22% coming from mix. Crucially: *"the current set-up points to individual suppliers' HBM performance (heat dissipation, die speed) and qualification status that determine pricing MORE SO THAN HBM S-D"* — **a supplier-differentiation pricing model, not a commodity one.**
- **The spec table behind the de-spec** (JPMe): **Rubin Ultra goes from 4 compute dies to 2**; RU HBM4E **16-Hi 64GB ×16 = 1,024GB → 12-Hi 48GB ×12 = 576GB**; a second RU step **12-Hi 48GB ×16 = 768GB → 8-Hi 32GB ×12 = 384GB**; **Rubin HBM4 12-Hi 36GB ×8 = 288GB → 8-Hi 24GB ×8 = 192GB** (two SKUs). **JPM assumes 70% of SKUs adopt the downsized specs** — an explicit, checkable modelling assumption, and the swing factor if the tiered-SKU picture on this page (MS, 08-10) resolves the other way. JPM's stated cause is the same as everyone else's: *"end-customers aiming to cope with the current shortage situation."*
- **HBM market share by sales (chart-read, 2023→2028E):** **SKH 51 → 55 → 60 → 46 → 42 → 42%**; **SEC 49 → 41 → 20 → 34 → 37 → 38%**; **MU 0 → 5 → 20 → 20 → 21 → 20%**. SKH cedes ~18pts of share from the 2025 peak; SEC's recovery to **37-38% by 27/28E** is JPM's explicit call and *"largely in-line with SEC's target to reach HBM M/S similar to its DRAM M/S (mid-to-high 30%)."*
- **Wallet share WITHIN each buyer (chart-read) — the two books look completely different.** Within **NVDA** (2023→2027E): SKH **91 → 83 → 74 → 54 → 53%**, SEC 9 → 9 → 1 → **24 → 24%**, MU 0 → 8 → **25 → 22 → 22%**. Within **ASIC**: SEC **96 → 75 → 50 → 52 → 54%** (dominant), SKH 4 → 25 → **43 → 38 → 34%**, MU 0 → 0 → 7 → **10 → 12%**. **SEC's franchise is the ASIC book; SKH's is NVIDIA.** Given the bit-mix shift above, that is the mechanism behind JPM's *"SEC > SKH in the near-term."*
- **★ The SEC–[[AVGO]] LTA gets an allocation split.** JPM: SEC's **>$200bn LTA with Broadcom** has **90-95% of contract value allocated to HBM for ASIC** — *"likely TPU and other ASIC projects including OpenAI/Anthropic."* ⚠️ The customer attribution is JPM's inference ("likely"), **not a disclosure** — logged as a broker read. Detail on [../AVGO.md](../AVGO.md), [../SAMSUNG.md](../SAMSUNG.md).
- **Supply-side arithmetic.** HBM wafers rise to **31% of DRAM capacity by 2028E** (13% 2024, 19% 2025, 23% 26E, 28% 27E) vs **<20% in 2025A**, at a **3-4x die penalty** — *"3-4 conventional DRAM wafers to produce an equivalent amount of bits for HBM."* JPM puts the combined drag on DRAM supply growth at **24%**.
- **HBM5 (late-28E) — three different thermal answers, and they are now on the record separately.** **[[SAMSUNG]]:** HPB (Heat Path Block) architecture + **2nm GAA base die for 50% faster speed vs HBM4E**, developing 16-Hi HBM5 with **both TCB and HCB** to find the performance/economics optimum. **[[SKHYNIX]]:** **iHBM** as its thermal-management answer and a likely HBM5 design; management flagged **HCB** as the potential HBM5 packaging solution on the 2Q26 call. **[[MU]]:** *"limited signals from MU considering HCB"* — local sources point instead to a **trench-cooling solution (microscopic grooves etched into the silicon die with cooling fluid circulated through the channels)**. Packaging read-through on [cowos-packaging](cowos-packaging.md).
- **🔴 FLASH MEMORY SUMMIT 2026 — HBF gets a spec, and a new tier appears between HBM and SSD.** JPM: the AI *"memory wall"* remains the primary bottleneck (longer context, KV cache, agentic workloads, plus the DRAM/HBM cost burden), driving *"accelerating interest in lower cost memory/storage tiers."* **High Bandwidth Flash (HBF): stacked NAND 8-hi/16-hi targeting up to 512GB capacity and ~0.4–3TB/s bandwidth** — with **ecosystem/software enablement the gating factor**. Also surfaced: **system-level disaggregation of prefill (HBM XPU) vs decode (HBM and/or HBF)**, and on storage **NVIDIA SCADA (GPU-direct-to-eSSD)** and **CMX (GPU→DPU→eSSD)**. Net per JPM: *"inference memory constraints should lift demand for DRAM (via CXL) and potentially more NAND for near-GPU memory and high-speed storage"* — clear positive for eSSD/HBF, modest positive for conventional DDRx/LPDDRx via **CXL pooling/offload**. Tier detail on [cxl-memory-fabric](cxl-memory-fabric.md).
- **Memory model, full revision (Table 9, US$bn):** total memory revenue **970.9 / 1,441.7 / 1,826.2** (26/27/28E), **+4% / +6% / +8%** vs May — DRAM **634.1 / 982.9 / 1,310.3** (0% / +1% / +6%), **NAND 336.8 / 458.7 / 515.9 (+12% / +17% / +14% — the largest upward revision in the note)**, HBM as above. DRAM ASP **+236% / +29% / +7%**; NAND ASP **+251% / +23% / −4%**. Memory capex **$123.0 / $172.1 / $189.1bn** (+7% / +13% / +3%), of which DRAM **$99.8 / $144.3 / $154.6bn** (+9% / **+16%** / +4%). **Memory OPM settles at a "new norm" of high-70%** (76% 2026E YTD, 78% 27E, 77% 28E).
- **Valuation frame JPM leans on:** peak market cap / memory TAM has compressed to **2.8x** (vs 2.9x 2018, 4.3x 2021, 5.0x 2025) → *"~50% upside using 27E, ~88% upside using 28E"*; on a profit base at **4x P/EBIT**, **~110% upside**. ⚠️ Mkt-cap basis is **SEC + SKH + MU only**.
- **The demand leg JPM says is underestimated:** *"Memory demand broadening out from GPU to CPU appears to have been UNDERESTIMATED BY INVESTORS (conceptually well known but actual S-D impact underlooked)"* — **AI CPU unit TAM growing at a 155% CAGR** (Vera/Rosa class), which JPM argues offsets the SOCAMM/HBM content optimisation. Flagged as *"a key source of potential upward revisions to demand."*
- **What JPM will NOT underwrite:** a 2028 supply surprise. Getting DRAM supply growth to 30% y/y in 2028E (vs its 25% base) would require *"meaningful production rotation from HBM to DRAM, an additional 70-80K wfpm of additional wafer rollout on an annual basis, AND significant technology acceleration pulling forward to 1dnm"* — *"extremely physically challenging."*

## Recent signals (Aug 7) — 🔴 THE RUBIN ULTRA DE-SPEC IS SETTLED (8-Hi, NOT 12-Hi) AND IT COMES WITH MORE UNITS, NOT FEWER; UBS CUTS THE 2027 INDUSTRY REVENUE FORECAST BY 9% *WHILE STAYING BULLISH*; AND THE FIRST CREDIBLE BEAR ON LTAs ARRIVES FROM A SELL-SIDE BULL

**🆕 (0) ADDED 2026-08-10 — THE LTA STRUCTURE FINALLY HAS A SCOPE AND A TENOR, FROM THE COMPANY, AND IT INCLUDES HBM.** The LTA argument has run on this page since July without anyone stating what an LTA actually covers. [[SKHYNIX]] told UBS on its 2Q26 NDR: **"A MEANINGFUL PORTION OF REVENUES WILL BE TIED UP TO LTAs, and >50% OF BITS SOON is a reasonable assumption. LTAs are OVER 5 YEARS, and cover DDR BUT ALSO IN SOME CASES HBM AND NAND."** ⚠️ **THIS IS THE MOST STRUCTURALLY CONSEQUENTIAL SENTENCE ADDED TO THIS PAGE IN A WEEK, AND IT ARMS BOTH SIDES OF THE DEBATE BELOW. For the UBS bull case, >50% of bits on five-year contracts is exactly the mechanism that narrows margins through the cycle and raises ROE. For the Jefferies bear in item (3), it is exactly the mechanism that "EXTENUATES A CYCLE" at ASPs softer than the open market. Neither reading is settled by the disclosure — but both were previously arguing about a contract structure whose SCOPE was unknown, and the scope is now: majority of bits, five years, HBM inside it in some cases.** ⚠️ **Note specifically what changes: this wiki had been treating LTAs as a DDR/NAND phenomenon with HBM priced annually outside them. HBM sitting inside multi-year agreements materially changes the shape of the 2027 HBM pricing reset discussed in item (1) — a repricing negotiated inside a five-year contract is a different animal from an annual reset.**
- 🔴 **AND MANAGEMENT'S OWN FRAMING OF THE DE-SPEC, which is the company-side answer to item (1) below: "Whilst NOT COMMENTING on possible de-specing, IF IT WAS TO OCCUR IT WOULD REFLECT SUPPLY SHORTAGE, NOT A CHANGE IN DEMAND."** ✅ **That is the same conclusion UBS reached from the unit math (de-spec plus MORE units = a bill of materials rationed by supply) — now stated independently by the largest supplier. Two routes, one answer.**
- ⚠️ **THE 2027 HBM PRICING RESET, AND IT POINTS DOWN ON PREMIUM: "2027 PRICING RESET IS STILL SEEKING TO BRING [HBM] PROFITABILITY IN LINE WITH DDR."** ⚠️ **Read that against the "HBM-vs-EPS paradox" this wiki carries (HBM's stable 40-60% margin sitting BELOW what conventional server DRAM earns in a spike): the largest HBM vendor is targeting HBM economics UP TOWARD commodity DRAM, not defending an HBM premium. That is a convergence thesis, and it argues the HBM-share-of-DRAM-revenue mix question matters less to earnings than the page has assumed.**
- **HBM CAPACITY AND THE VENDOR CROSSOVER, from the same note: "the capacity deployed for HBM will reach 230K WPM BY THE END OF 2026 AND 270K BY END 2027," supporting 17.2bn Gb of HBM shipment in 2026 (+37% y/y) and 21.3bn in 2027 (+24%). 🔴 **AND THE SHARE CROSSOVER: UBS forecasts SK Hynix retaining #1 in 2026 at 47% of industry bit shipments but "FALL[ING] SLIGHTLY BELOW [[SAMSUNG]] IN 2027 WITH 38% of industry bit shipments (SAMSUNG 40%; [[MU]] 22%)."** ⚠️ **A Samsung-over-SK-Hynix HBM bit-share crossover in 2027 is a material competitive call and it belongs next to the HBM4 yield gap this wiki carries (SK hynix ~80-85% vs Samsung ~60%, BTG expert 07-30) — the two are in tension and neither is retracted. Front-end DRAM capacity going to HBM: "500K/690K WPM BY THE END OF 2026/2027, OR 24%/30% OF INDUSTRY CAPACITY."**
- **Bit end-consumption on the same note's framing: DRAM bit end-consumption "GROWING 40% YoY [in 2027] VS 21% IN 2026E, and NAND FLASH 26% FROM 23%," with "ALMOST ALL OF THE INCREMENTAL WAFER CAPACITY ADDITION FOR DRAM… GOING TO HBM, and NO NEW CAPACITY EX CHINA BEING ADDED TO NAND." The named risk is affordability: "memory industry revenues are APPROACHING US$1.61TN IN 2027E."** ✅ **Consistent with the industry numbers in item (1); logged here because the NDR states the SUPPLY mechanism (HBM absorbing the increment, no ex-China NAND additions) more directly than the monthly does.** (UBS Global Research · Nicolas Gaudois / Jimmy Yoon / Luke Yoo, "SK Hynix 2Q26 NDR Key Takeaways", 2026-08-07)

**🔴 (1) THE 12-Hi QUESTION IS CLOSED, AND THE ANSWER INVERTS THE OBVIOUS READ.** Yesterday this page carried GFHK's "Rubin Ultra HBM stepped down 384GB → 192/256GB; **8-Hi design finalized, YET 12-Hi REMAINS ON LINE-UP**" as contested. UBS's checks now resolve it: **"Nvidia will likely INITIALLY USE HBM4 8-Hi FOR RUBIN ULTRA followed by A SWITCH TO HBM4E 8-Hi. This is compared to our PRIOR EXPECTATION OF RUBIN ULTRA USING HBM4E 12-Hi. WE BELIEVE THIS IS IN RESPONSE TO PERSISTENT TIGHT SUPPLY."** Content math: **initial VR300 at 384GB HBM4/GPU (8-Hi × 24Gb × 8 × 2) → 512GB HBM4E (8-Hi × 32Gb × 8 × 2), versus a prior 768GB estimate — a ~33% cut in per-GPU content at the endpoint.** DIGITIMES headlines the same direction the same day ("Nvidia tests **leaner Rubin Ultra memory designs with 2027 HBM supply in question**"). ⚠️ **Both are supply-chain relays, not company statements. But they agree with each other and with the SUPPLY-failure causal reading this page adopted on Aug 6 — and they contradict GFHK on the 12-Hi survival. Adopt the 8-Hi path; retire the 12-Hi line.**
- 🔴 **AND THE PART THAT MATTERS FOR BITS: UBS RAISED 2027 [[NVDA]] HBM end-consumption to 25.1bn Gb (was 24.0bn) DESPITE cutting content per GPU — "as GPU UNITS IMPLIED GO UP TO 12.6MIL FROM 11.0MIL."** **De-spec plus more units is the signature of a BILL-OF-MATERIALS RATIONED BY SUPPLY, not of softening demand: NVIDIA is trading memory per die for die count.** [[GOOG]] TPU HBM also raised, to **19.2bn Gb (was 17.4bn)** on unit upside. **Industry HBM end-consumption: 61.5bn Gb in 2027 (was 58.7bn), +91% y/y.** Pricing raised with it: **HBM4E ~US$3.9/Gb in '27 (+75% y/y), HBM4 US$3.5 (+60% y/y), blended industry HBM ASP +79% y/y (was +66%).** (UBS Memory Semis Monthly · Nicolas Gaudois / Timothy Arcuri et al., 2026-08-07)

**🔴 (2) UBS CUTS 2027 INDUSTRY REVENUE 9% AND TELLS YOU TO BUY THE PULLBACK — the cut is LTAs, not demand.** **"Memory stocks have PULLED BACK 36% ON AVERAGE FROM THE JUNE HIGHS. Yet, the industry is moving towards MORE SUSTAINABLE CYCLES AND HIGHER FCF RETURNS… memory stocks now discount PRE-AI THROUGH-CYCLE ROEs (eg. [[SAMSUNG]]: 13.9% vs '12-22 average 15.2%)."** **The cut: "LTAs ARE REIGNING IN SOME OF THE PRICING UPSIDE INTO THE UPCYCLE and hence, we bring down our '27E memory industry revs forecasts to US$1.61TN ('26E: US$0.94TN), OR 9% BELOW PRIOR."** ⚠️ **This is the clearest statement yet of the LTA trade-off this page has been tracking since July: "LTAs are OPM/ROE ACCRETIVE THROUGH THE CYCLE and in essence, for memory FCF and ultimately, shareholder returns" — you give up peak ASP to buy duration and returns.** **"The main fundamental risk is MEMORY SPENDING SUSTAINABILITY FOR CUSTOMERS, notably hyperscalers."** **Cycle shape, and the DRAM/NAND split is now dated: "we continue to forecast the DRAM UPCYCLE TO CONTINUE FOR LONGER THAN NAND (2Q28E VS. 4Q27E)."** Ratings: **Samsung Key Call Buy; CXMT, Kioxia, [[MU]], Nanya Tech and [[SKHYNIX]] Buy.**
- **DRAM supply/demand, restated with the gap explicit: 2027 DRAM bit DEMAND +40.1% y/y (raised from +36%) on stronger server DDR, against industry supply growth of only +20.6% — "reinforcing our view of a PROLONGED DRAM UNDERSUPPLY CYCLE THROUGH AT LEAST 2Q28."** ('26 bit demand +21%.)
- **NAND, and the contract-price range is WIDER than the market thinks: "our checks on NAND flash (ex LTA) contract pricing negotiations suggest a WIDER RANGE of QoQ increases for 3Q26E FROM +20% TO >30% QoQ, STILL AHEAD of some of the recent market expectations of high teens up. On average, we now forecast ex-LTA NAND ASPs UP +23% QoQ (WAS +30%), +12% in 4Q, and WITH LTAs, CLOSER TO +20%/+8% QoQ respectively."** ⚠️ **The ex-LTA vs with-LTA spread (+23% vs +20% in 3Q) is the LTA drag measured directly — small in the near quarter, and that is itself informative: the LTAs are not yet the binding constraint on realised price.**
- **NAND demand mix, which is where the split lives: smartphones (22% of '26E industry bits) and client/PC SSD (10%) stay WEAK; server/storage SSD (43% of total) STRONGER — bit demand growth raised to +62% y/y in '26 (was +54%) and +51% in '27 (was +45%). Total NAND bit end-demand +23% y/y '26 and +26% '27. NAND industry revenue US$301bn/US$492bn '26/'27 — "close enough to [[SNDK]]'s forecast (US$300bn/US$500bn)."** ✅ **A sell-side model and the company's own TAM now agree within ~2% — worth noting given how far apart the NAND views were in July.**
- 🔴 **CXMT INITIATED WITH A BUY, AND UBS'S CONCLUSION IS THAT THE CHINA BEAR IS OVERSTATED: "CXMT NOT A THREAT TO OVERALL DRAM SUPPLY/DEMAND. We forecast CXMT to reach 9% BIT SUPPLY SHARE IN '27 vs 7% IN '25, with wafer capacity moving to 382K WPM BY 4Q27 from 292K in 4Q26 and 240K in 4Q25."** **The mechanism for why the Street is too high is specific and reusable: "SOME OF THE MARKET EXPECTATIONS ARE PROBABLY MORE ALIGNED WITH EQUIPMENT DELIVERY VS ACTUAL INSTALLATION/VALIDATION AND ACTUAL PRODUCTION CAPACITY — AND HENCE TOO HIGH."** **Technology gap quantified: "CXMT is moving to G5 process tech LATE '26 (SUB 15 NM) vs Samsung / SK Hynix / MU moving to 1d nm (SUB 11 NM) IN 2H27. Furthermore, in part due to manufacturing equipment EXPORT CONTROL, production YIELDS FOR CXMT ARE NOT AT PAR with the top 3."** ⚠️ **Cross-file to [china-export](china-export.md): the export-control regime is being credited here with holding a competitor two nodes back, which is the bull-case version of the same policy this wiki tracks as a risk elsewhere.** (UBS Memory Semis Monthly · Gaudois / Arcuri / Yasui / Yu / Yoon / Yoo / Vella / Kinoshita / Lin / Bouvignies / Abrams, 2026-08-07)

**🔴 (3) THE FIRST CREDIBLE LTA BEAR, AND IT COMES FROM A HOUSE THAT IS BULLISH ON THE CYCLE.** Jefferies' post-earnings semis debrief turns explicitly **BEAR on memory**, and the argument is the mirror image of the LTA bull case this page has carried since July: **"the enthusiasm around LONG-TERM AGREEMENTS in memory (e.g. [[SNDK]]/[[WDC]]) is being QUESTIONED. LTAs are NOT viewed as a 'GAME CHANGER' but rather as mechanisms that 'EXTENUATE A CYCLE' RATHER THAN PREVENT IT."** 🔴 **The pricing point is the falsifiable one: "DEALS SIGNED AT 80% GROSS MARGIN IMPLY SOFTER ASPs THAN OPEN MARKET, QUESTIONING THE UPSIDE POTENTIAL from historical $300-500 EPS expectations."** **And the supply point is the one to watch: "[[LRCX]]'s strong performance and RECORD NAND REVENUE indicate that 'SOMEONE'S ADDING CAPACITY' in NAND, LIKELY THE KOREANS, EARLIER THAN PREVIOUSLY ANTICIPATED. This raises concerns about potential future OVERSUPPLY."** ⚠️ **That is a supply-response signal read off the EQUIPMENT layer rather than off company guidance — the same inference route that worked on this page in July, now pointing the other way.** **Third leg, and it connects to the de-spec above: "HIGH COST SENSITIVITY IN AI is driving discussions about DIFFERENT MEMORY TIERING (e.g. using DRAM to offset HBM, or SSDs). This implies that EVEN IN THE DATA CENTER, MEMORY PRICING HAS HIT A POINT WHERE DEMAND IS AT RISK OF DISRUPTION, pushing users to seek cheaper alternatives."** (Jefferies Semis EPS Debrief Call · Blayne Curtis, 2026-08-07, via Fernanda Watkins)
- ⚠️ **HOLD BOTH SIDES. The de-spec-as-supply-failure reading (Aug 6, restated above) and Jefferies' de-spec-as-cost-revolt reading are OBSERVATIONALLY SIMILAR — both produce lower memory content per system — and the discriminating test is the one this page already recorded: A SUPPLY-DRIVEN DE-SPEC COINCIDES WITH RISING ASPs; A COST-REVOLT DE-SPEC DOES NOT. Spot and contract are still rising (below), which favours the supply reading — but Jefferies' point is about the FORWARD elasticity, not the current print.**

**(4) The trackers: prices still rising, but the RATE OF INCREASE IS NARROWING — and Bernstein says the narrowing may already be below Street.** **"DRAM Jul contracts suggested 17% conventional DRAM price increase QoQ in 3QCY26… with 17% for PC, 16% for Server, 11% for Mobile & ~27% for Consumer (size of increase is A TOUCH AHEAD of their model). DRAM spot was fairly strong in Jul: PC DRAM spot +8.8–17% MoM while SERVER DRAM WAS MIXED at -6.7% to +3.8% MoM.** ⚠️ (server spot going negative at the low end is the first soft print in that series this page has logged). **NAND wafer spot MIXED — 512Gb FALLING 3% MoM while 1Tb wafer REBOUNDED 8% MoM.** 🔴 **"Jul contracts suggested conventional DRAM & NAND contract prices to rise by CLOSE TO 20% QoQ in 3QCY26. THE SIZE OF THE INCREASE NARROWED NOTABLY VS. 2Q, AND MAY BE A BIT BELOW THE LATEST STREET EXPECTATION. Shortage should persist into CY27 to keep prices elevated, especially for DRAM, though ROOM FOR UPSIDE IS LIKELY LIMITED, GIVEN MEMORY IS BECOMING A BURDEN FOR BOTH AI & NON-AI APPLICATIONS & PRICE CAPS SET IN SOME LTAs."** (Bernstein memory tracker via Tyler Seidman, 2026-08-07) ⚠️ **Bernstein and Jefferies are now saying the same thing from different data: the price ceiling is being set by AFFORDABILITY and by LTA caps, not by supply.**
- **WSTS confirms how concentrated the cycle still is: "Total semiconductor sales grew 135% YoY in June after rising 128% in May. YoY strength was AGAIN DOMINATED BY MEMORY with sales MORE THAN QUADRUPLING AT 379% YoY, driving ~$275B IN INCREMENTAL REVENUE YTD and with MEMORY PRICING ALONE DRIVING ~2/3 OF OVERALL INDUSTRY GROWTH since the beginning of the year. EXCLUDING MEMORY, SALES WERE STILL UP STRONGLY AT 38% YoY."** (Bernstein WSTS tracker, 2026-08-07)
- **UBS's SIA read adds the record and the units: "Global total semiconductor sales reached a RECORD $147.8B IN JUNE, up 10.8% M/M… CQ2 total semiconductor sales grew a remarkable 36.6% Q/Q, ~2,880BPS ABOVE the 10-yr seasonal average. MEMORY ROSE 64.2% Q/Q… DRAM sales reached A NEW RECORD OF $56.8B, up 7.6% M/M, driven by ASP up 8.7% M/M. NAND sales jumped 14.8% M/M, with units/ASP up 5.0%/9.3% M/M."** 🔴 **AND THE VOLUME CHECK THAT MATTERS AGAINST THE "IT'S ALL PRICE" BEAR: "Total Semiconductor UNITS rose 9.5% Q/Q in CQ2, with MEMORY UNITS UP 32.2% Q/Q, CONFIRMING THAT THIS CYCLE IS NOT PURELY AN ASP PHENOMENON but is being underpinned by GENUINE VOLUME GROWTH as well, particularly in DRAM/NAND."** Full-year: **2026E total semis $1,637.9B (+119.6% y/y), 2027E $2,397.5B (+46.4%)** vs 2025 actual $745.8B. (UBS Global Semiconductor · Arcuri, 2026-08-07)

**(5) Capital returns become a real line item, and the numbers are large.** BofA lays out the [[SAMSUNG]] execution plan behind the "50% of 2024-26 FCF after the W10tn annual dividend" framework the CFO gave on 30 July: **"(1) SPECIAL DIVIDEND PAYMENTS OF W30TN+ IN 3Q OR 4Q; (2) SHARE BUYBACKS OF W40TN+ IN 1H27; and (3) YEAR-END DIVIDENDS OF W30TN, to be paid in April 2027. Separately, SEC needs W30TN+ WORTH OF TREASURY SHARES FOR EMPLOYEE SPECIAL BONUSES."** And for **[[SKHYNIX]]: "we also expect SK Hynix to return 50% of FCF, WITH A GREATER EMPHASIS ON BUYBACKS VS CASH DIVIDENDS, SUCH AS W40TN+ VS W20TN+."** (BofA Global Memory Tech · Simon Woo et al., 2026-08-06/07) ⚠️ **These are BofA's ANALYSIS of execution paths, not announced plans — but they put a scale on the "capital returns" pressure this page logged on Aug 6 as a Reuters-sourced qualitative item.**
- **And the supply-side commitment that pairs with it: [[SKHYNIX]] to spend ~$38–39BN on Korean fab expansion — "Yongin Y2 will be the SECOND OF FOUR FABS planned at the Yongin cluster, breaking ground JULY NEXT YEAR, targeting JUNE 2029 FOR THE FIRST CLEANROOM," with the budget also including M17 (DRAM and NAND). JPM's Jay Kwon reads it as IN LINE — "consistent with the company's plan to fill its entire WSPM at Y1 by 2030 (near 400K WSPM). They need to start investing now for the next leg of WFE installation (at Y2) IN 2031."** (Bloomberg via JPM "Chips for Breakfast" · Joshua Meyers, 2026-08-07) ⚠️ **Note the dates: first cleanroom 2029, WFE installation 2031. This is NOT supply relief for the 2026-28 window under debate — it is the reason the undersupply forecast can run to 2Q28 without contradiction.** Cross-file [semicap-wfe](semicap-wfe.md).
- **The demand-side counterweight remains the loudest voice on the tape: [[SPCX]] management, on its first earnings call, "MEMORY IS NOW THE LIMITING FACTOR FOR COMPUTE, with industry output growing ~20% PER YEAR AGAINST DEMAND GROWING 200% OR MORE, a dynamic management argued should push memory pricing HIGHER rather than lower"** (Jefferies · Blayne Curtis, 2026-08-07) — the same claim this page logged on Aug 5 from a machine translation, now confirmed in an English sell-side note.
- 🔴 **NET-NEW DEMAND-SIDE BYPASS, filed here because it is the first architecture on this wiki that routes AROUND both constraints: [[QCOM]]'s High Bandwidth Compute (HBC). IR head Brett Simpson: "for HBC QCOM ALREADY HAS THE MEMORY ALLOCATION NEEDED. IT DOESN'T USE HBM — IT USES LPDDR — AND IT ALSO DOESN'T USE CoWoS," placing the accelerator die directly under the LPDDR stack with TSVs instead of a 2.5D interposer. Claims: AI250/HBC Gen 1 = 133 TB/s per card (18x the effective memory bandwidth of AI200 with LPDDR5X); AI300/HBC Gen 2 = 54x over AI200; per-watt bandwidth 6x HBM; commercial sampling MID-2027.** ⚠️ **Company claims, unverified. But if the LPDDR route scales, it is a structural demand leak from HBM into conventional DRAM at the decode stage — which is exactly where [[MU]]'s "storage headroom before core memory" mechanism and the prefill/decode disaggregation thesis both point.** (Jefferies · Conor O'Mara hosting QCOM IR, 2026-08-07)

## Recent signals (Aug 6) — 🔴 THE DE-SPEC IS RE-EXPLAINED AS A SUPPLY FAILURE (60–70% FILL), THE DOWN-LEG FINALLY GETS A DATE AND A NUMBER (2028: DRAM −8% / NAND −14%), AND A STRUCTURAL REASON EMERGES WHY DRAM IS MORE DEFENDED THAN NAND

- 🔴🔴 **THE CAUSAL CORRECTION ON THE DE-SPEC, and it inverts the market's reading (GFHK note via @jukan05, 2026-08-06).** The chain, restated: **"[[NVDA]] CUTTING MEMORY: VERA CPU SOCAMM FROM 192GB → 96GB → SELECTIVE SKUs TO 64GB BY 1Q27. RUBIN ULTRA HBM STEPPED DOWN FROM 384GB TO 192GB/256GB (HBM4/4E); 8-Hi DESIGN FINALIZED, YET 12-Hi REMAINS ON LINE-UP."** Then the why: **"Rubin's SOCAMM2 has been NERFED once again — down to 64GB. BUT IS THAT BEARISH? IF ANYTHING, IT'S THE OPPOSITE. Working off a 192GB SOCAMM2 configuration, NVIDIA HAD ASKED THE MEMORY MAKERS FOR A CORRESPONDING VOLUME OF SOCAMM2 — AND THE MEMORY MAKERS CAME BACK TELLING THEM THEY COULD ONLY SUPPLY 60–70% OF THAT VOLUME. THIS ISN'T A DEMAND PROBLEM, IT'S A SUPPLY PROBLEM. THE SPEC WAS CUT BECAUSE THEY COULDN'T GET THE SOCAMM2 SUPPLY."** ⚠️ **This matters because it flips the sign on every de-spec datapoint this theme carries. Content-per-rack falling because NVIDIA CANNOT PROCURE memory is BULLISH for memory pricing and neutral-to-bullish for NVIDIA unit output; the same fact read as demand rationing is bearish for both. THE TWO HYPOTHESES ARE DISTINGUISHABLE and the test is already available: a supply-constrained de-spec should coincide with RISING memory ASPs — which is exactly what BofA's spot data shows this week (NAND 512Gb–1Tb +5–6%, legacy 256Gb +10%+, DRAM spot +1–2%, "better than seasonal"). Note the 60–70% fill rate is consistent with the "2027 allocation round has already closed, fill rates are 60–70%" signal logged on Aug 4 — two independent arrivals at the same number. Unverified supply-chain-newsletter relay; the fill rate is the specific checkable claim.** Same source corrects a competing house: **"Contrary to what a certain research house published, RUBIN ULTRA IS NOT BEING CONSIDERED WITH 8Hi ONLY. AN HBM4E 12Hi SKU IS ALSO UNDER CONSIDERATION"** (down from an original 16Hi). Competitive context: **[[AMD]] MI450 Custom and [[GOOG]] TPU "Humufish" are ALREADY on 8-Hi** — i.e. NVIDIA is converging down to where competitors already sit, not falling behind them. Compensating lever: **"SCALE-UP EXPANDING VIA NVL576 W/ NPO OPTICS TO OFFSET REDUCED MEMORY CONTENT"** → optical content being added because memory content was forced down; cross-filed [themes/optical-cpo](optical-cpo.md), [[COHR]], [[LITE]].
- 🔴 **THE DOWN-LEG NOW HAS A DATE AND A MAGNITUDE, from a sell-side model rather than a vibe (BofA · Simon Woo / Dai Shen / Vivek Arya / Mikio Hirakawa / Matt Shin, "Global Memory Tech" weekly, 2026-08-06):** *"last-minute memory price hikes in 4Q, with industry-average **DRAM UP 8% QoQ and NAND UP 3%**, ahead of **STABLE PRICING OR MILD CORRECTIONS THROUGH 1Q–4Q27**. That said, **2027 YEAR-AVERAGE ASP SHOULD STILL BE HIGHER THAN IN 2026**, given the relatively low base in 1H26. **ASP DECLINES SHOULD EVENTUALLY EMERGE IN 2028 (DRAM −8% AND NAND −14% ASSUMED BUT NO HARD-LANDING, JUST A HEALTHY CORRECTION WARRANTING A HIGH-MARGIN PROFILE)**."* **Industry size track: GLOBAL DRAM SALES US$134BN / $573BN / $847BN / $917BN IN 2025/26/27/28.** Model inputs worth keeping: **[[SKHYNIX]]'s 2Q DRAM ASP up ONLY 30% q/q "VS 45–70% AMONG PEERS," with a +25% 3Q recovery and more meaningful HBM4 shipments; [[KIOXIA]] guiding 3Q revenue +35% q/q, implying a NEAR-20% NAND ASP HIKE, ABOVE the industry low-to-mid teens.** ⚠️ **A modelled 2028 DRAM trough of just −8% is the central quantitative claim of the whole "duration not amplitude" thesis this theme has been building. Carry it as the BASE CASE TO TEST, not as a bear case to fear — and note it falls INSIDE the LTA coverage windows ([[SNDK]] has ~2/3 of FY28 bits contracted at floor; [[MU]]'s SCAs ~40% of volume), i.e. precisely the scenario the contract structures exist to survive.**
- 🔴 **A STRUCTURAL REASON DRAM IS MORE DEFENDED THAN NAND — new, and it explains the asymmetry in BofA's own numbers ([[MU]] mgmt to Deutsche Bank · Melissa Weathers at FMS 2026, 2026-08-06):** *"when supply tightens, **CUSTOMERS REDUCE STORAGE HEADROOM BEFORE THEY REDUCE CORE MEMORY CAPACITY**, since **STORAGE CAN BE ADDED LATER VIA PLUGGABLE SSD, WHEREAS MEMORY UPGRADES REQUIRE PHYSICAL DISASSEMBLY**. This creates a **HIGHER DEGREE OF INELASTICITY IN MEMORY & AI SYSTEMS**."* ⚠️ **Read the derivative: in a tight market NAND is the first line item cut, DRAM the last. That is a mechanical explanation for BofA modelling 2028 NAND −14% versus DRAM −8%, and for the divergence visible in the same week's prints — [[SNDK]]'s bit-growth miss against DRAM spot still rising.** Same source, the tiering that sizes memory content: **"HBM is best used for the HOT PATH, SOCAMM2 MAIN MEMORY FOR KV OVERFLOW, DISAGGREGATED DDR5 POOLS FOR THE LONG TAIL,"** with memory now **"APPROACHING 50% OF SYSTEM VALUE" from ~10% three decades ago**, and the mechanism: **"POOR KV CACHE HANDLING FORCES GPUs TO RECOMPUTE PRIOR WORK RATHER THAN ADVANCE NEW WORK, WHICH INFLATES UTILIZATION METRICS WITHOUT PRODUCING REAL OUTPUT."**
- ⚠️ **THE STRUCTURAL BEAR TO THIS ENTIRE THEME, stated by a bull and worth quoting exactly (DB, same note): "we continue to monitor WHETHER MEMORY INTENSITY PER GPU WILL KEEP SCALING WITH CONTEXT-WINDOW GROWTH, OR WHETHER KV-CACHE ORCHESTRATION SOFTWARE CAN BECOME EFFICIENT ENOUGH AT EVICTION AND RECALL THAT SOME OF THIS MEMORY-CONTENT UPSIDE WILL BE ENGINEERED AWAY."** **Software-defeats-hardware is the cleanest available refutation of the memory-content thesis, and it is now on the page in a bull's words. Susquehanna's Hosseini arrives at an adjacent conclusion from the price side: as AI spend shifts training → inference, [[SNDK]] "will likely need to offer customers MORE COMPELLING ECONOMICS… TARGETED PRICING ADJUSTMENTS OF A FEW CENTS PER GB" to buy elasticity for KV-cache offload — deliberately cheaper $/GB, explicitly NOT an ASP correction (2026-08-06).**
- **HBF: the first pushback is about PRICE, not physics.** Asked on [[SNDK]]'s call whether High Bandwidth Flash carries a premium to conventional NAND, management said **"it's A LITTLE EARLY TO TALK ABOUT PRICING on some of those nodes just yet"** — and the objection is correct that HBF pricing must be judged against HBM **"since EVERY HBF ORDER IS AN ALLOCATION DECISION MADE AGAINST HBM"** (@ParadisLabs, 2026-08-05). Separately, from a direct management conversation: SanDisk was **"VERY CAGEY… WOULD NOT COMMIT TO GIVING ME A HARD NUMBER" on ENDURANCE** (only a warranty and a five-year expected life) and is **"targeting something IN THE PRICE RANGE OF HBM,"** which the reporter argues **"REDUCES THE DEMAND QUITE SUBSTANTIALLY since they aren't at parity with HBM4"** (@bubbleboi, 2026-08-05). ⚠️ **Unverified journalist/FinTwit channel work — but together they form a specific, checkable bear: HBF priced like HBM without HBM performance or a disclosed endurance spec has no obvious buyer. SanDisk's ANALYST DAY on 2026-08-13 (NYC) is billed to cover the HBF roadmap and is the resolution point.** **Competitive: [[SAMSUNG]] unveiled zNAND-O at FMS bringing HBF to EDGE AI, with QUALCOMM AND GOOGLE "ALREADY EVALUATING ADOPTION," though TrendForce says consumer commercialisation "will ultimately hinge on COST COMPETITIVENESS" (@trendforce, 2026-08-06) — the same cost objection from the other end of the market.**
- **⚠️ A POSSIBLE SUPPLY-SIDE EXPLANATION FOR THE TWIN NAND MISSES, unverified: [[KIOXIA]] "BEGAN MASS PRODUCTION of its 10th-generation 3D NAND, BiCS10, at the K2 fab in Kitakami, Iwate Prefecture, IN EARLY JULY. This could be ONE REASON BOTH KIOXIA AND SANDISK MISSED EXPECTATIONS" (@jukan05 citing Korean media, 2026-08-06).** **A node transition at the JV fab consumes wafer starts and yield-learning capacity in the ramp quarter, depressing sellable bits at BOTH partners simultaneously — which would reframe the "bit-growth miss" from demand/share into transition timing, consistent with GS attributing [[WDC]]'s exabyte shortfall the same night to "tech-transition-driven lumpiness and mix." NOT adopted; flagged for verification.**
- **SUPPLY-SIDE ADDITIONS (2028+ risk, WFE positive today): Nanya's board raised 2026 capex NT$52bn → NT$69.7bn (US$2.2bn, +34%) for Fab 5A, a programme costing up to NT$346.6bn (US$10.7bn) by 2029 — "the biggest investment by a Taiwan DRAM maker in 20 years" — bringing 10NM-CLASS PROCESSES AND EUV to a Taiwanese DRAM fab (@dnystedt, @trendforce, 2026-08-06). ⚠️ RECONCILIATION FLAG: distinct from the "US$16bn 2027-29" Nanya figure this theme carried in July — different period, company-board-approved; do not merge. Against it, the supply response is physically capped: EUV lead times are now 3–3.5 YEARS (a Winbond executive, which cut its own 2026 capex because of it — @dnystedt, 2026-08-06) and [[LRCX]]'s CFO says CLEAN-ROOM AVAILABILITY is the current binding constraint with greenfield shell taking ~3 YEARS regardless of geography (DB, 2026-08-06). Both constraints are the same length: no fast supply response exists from either direction through ~2029.**
- **CAPITAL RETURNS become the new pressure point on the Korean pair: "[[SAMSUNG]] Electronics and [[SKHYNIX]] face GROWING CALLS FROM INVESTORS wanting a greater share of excess cash via DIVIDENDS OR BUYBACKS, after the pair provided SCANT DETAIL ON CAPITAL RETURNS when reporting AI-driven record profit" (@ReutersBiz, 2026-08-06); BofA notes "Samsung's earlier dividend payments well expected." Contrast [[SNDK]], which authorised an ADDITIONAL $14BN buyback (remaining capacity $15.5BN) and repurchased 2.836mn shares for ~$4.5bn at a ~$1,587 average, and [[MU]], where @MF_Camillus argues "CAPITAL RETURN PROSPECTS" is one reason the US name deserves a premium. Cross-filed [[MU]], [[SNDK]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[SKHYNIX]].**
- **Demand-elasticity datapoint from the invoicing layer, and it is the bluntest of the window: Wistron chairman Simon Lin — AI servers are 95% of its server business and "the AI trend is so strong that EVEN HIGH MEMORY CHIP PRICES HAVEN'T SLOWED CUSTOMER SPENDING," adding he does not fear even higher memory prices because "CUSTOMERS WILL STILL BUY" (@dnystedt, 2026-08-06).** Against it, the CONSUMER end is showing resistance: Microsoft "QUIETLY STOPS RECOMMENDING 32GB OF RAM FOR WINDOWS 11" (TechRadar, 2026-08-06), [[AAPL]] raised some Mac/iPad prices effective JUNE 25 on memory cost its IR called a **"100-YEAR FLOOD,"** and Hosseini flags consumer electronics "approaching a POINT OF DEMAND RESISTANCE as consumers become less willing to absorb higher prices associated with rising BOM costs." ⚠️ **The split is the signal: datacentre demand is price-insensitive, consumer demand is not — which is why datacentre mix (SanDisk 38% of bits exiting F26 vs ~12% a year earlier) is the variable that determines whether the cycle's ASP gains stick.**
- **Also: South Korean industry experts predict [[GOOG]]'s accelerated TPU deployment "will push HBM demand CLOSE TO THAT OF [[NVDA]]" (Digitimes via @The_AI_Investor, 2026-08-05) — unattributed-expert relay, logged as directional only; and Roundhill's $DRAM ETF is now the reference point new sector ETFs are benchmarked against (@jukan05, @ParadisLabs, 2026-08-06), a positioning/flow fact.**

## Recent signals (Aug 5) — ★ THE LARGEST BUYER ON THE TAPE ARGUES MEMORY PRICES GO UP, THE PRODUCER GUIDES ASP GROWTH DOWN, AND APPLE'S CHEAP-CHINA ESCAPE ROUTE IS CLOSED

**Three things landed on 2026-08-05 that belong together, because they pull the same debate — is the price leg peaking? — in opposite directions from opposite ends of the chain.**

**1. ⚠️ ★ THE DEMAND SIDE, FROM A BUYER, ON AN EARNINGS CALL — ✅ NOW UPGRADED TO THE PRIMARY TRANSCRIPT (ingested 2026-08-05).** ⚠️ **This item was originally logged from a Chinese-language tweet with the caveat "the wiki holds no SPCX transcript, so treat the exact wording as approximate." The Bloomberg FINAL TRANSCRIPT is now on file. THE SUBSTANCE IS CONFIRMED EXACTLY — the ~20%/yr supply vs ~200%/yr demand framing and the Economics-101 conclusion are all verbatim.** Musk, actual words: ***"Then look at the rate at which logic and memory is being produced. And one was always considered a limiting factor here. LIMITING FACTOR CURRENTLY IS MEMORY. The memory output is increasing by around 20% PER YEAR. Now normally that would be a fantastically fast and amazing for any large mature industry. But ask yourself, is the demand increasing by 20% a year? No, THE DEMAND IS INCREASING BY 200% A YEAR, MAYBE HIGHER. So if you've got demand increasing much faster than supply, then Economics 101 would suggest that THE PRICE INCREASES. IT DOES NOT DECREASE."*** (SpaceX Q2 2026 earnings call, Bloomberg FINAL TRANSCRIPT, 2026-08-04) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Space_Exploration_Technologies_Corp_Earnings_Call_2026804_DN000000003115071217pdf.html). ⚠️ **This page has spent three weeks assembling the supply side's version of this argument ([[SAMSUNG]]'s LTA floors, [[SKHYNIX]]'s allocation round, [[MU]]'s coverage, [[SNDK]]'s NBMs). This is the first time a hyperscale-scale BUYER has made the same argument publicly and against its own interest — and it names a specific supply constant (~20%/yr bit growth) against a specific demand rate (~200%/yr). Weight it as the strongest demand-side corroboration on the page, and discount it for the speaker's incentive to justify capex.** 🆕 **THE CONTEXT THE RELAY STRIPPED, AND IT CUTS THE OTHER WAY — read the paragraph the quote sits inside before using it as a straight bull datapoint. Musk arrived at the memory point while arguing that INTELLIGENCE PER WATT IS RISING FAST ENOUGH TO BE DEFLATIONARY: _"the intelligence per watt is increasing rapidly, so that means the USEFULNESS PER WATT is increasing rapidly… you start seeing SMALLER AND SMALLER MODELS DO MORE AND MORE AMAZING THINGS. There's an argument that we may get to the point where EVEN AN H100 IS ABLE TO DELIVER USEFUL AI THAT'S COMPARABLE TO A HUMAN ENGINEER. Based on current trends, I think that's not an impossible outcome."_ ⚠️ **The same speaker, in the same answer, is arguing that model efficiency is improving so fast that OLD silicon stays economically useful. That is the algorithmic-efficiency counterweight to his own bit-demand extrapolation, and it is exactly the mechanism the bear case on this page needs — logged here rather than filtered out, because the relay's version presented only the half that supports higher prices.** ⚠️ **Note also the buyer's own hedge on content elasticity, from the [[NVDA]] page's 08-04 rows: [[AMD]] said the same week that customers can "MODIFY THAT MEMORY FOOTPRINT if TCO is not as significant in certain workloads." The reconciliation this wiki carries stands: HBM content per GPU is negotiable at the margin; aggregate bit demand still exceeds supply.**

**2. ⚠️ AND THE PRODUCER GUIDED THE OTHER WAY THE SAME DAY. [[SNDK]] (Q4 FY26, 08-05) disclosed that 2/3 of its June-quarter revenue growth came from pricing — implying ~mid-30s% q/q ASP growth versus [[KIOXIA]]'s +70% q/q — and guided the September quarter to "bit growth and MODEST ASP growth," against Kioxia's implied Sept ASP guide of +25-30% q/q. Management called mid-80s gross margin "a FAIR RETURN." The stock fell on peak-margin fears.** ⚠️ **So on the same day: the biggest incremental buyer says prices must rise, and the NAND price-setter guided its own realised ASP growth down and conceded the margin ceiling. Both are facts. The reconciliation candidates are (a) NAND ≠ DRAM/HBM, (b) contracted LTA pricing decouples realised ASP from marginal spot, (c) SanDisk is trading price for duration. This page should not net them out — it should track which resolves first.**

**3. ★ APPLE'S CHEAP-CHINA WORKAROUND IS BLOCKED, AND IT CUTS AGAINST THE STANDING BEAR CASE. (@jukan05 citing South Korean media, 2026-08-05):** **"[[AAPL]] recently negotiated with [[CXMT]] over supply prices for mobile DRAM, including LPDDR5X, in an effort to ease manufacturing cost pressures… BUT CXMT REJECTED ITS REQUEST FOR PRICE CUTS. CXMT INSTEAD INSISTED ON PRICES THAT WERE HIGHER THAN OR COMPARABLE TO THOSE OFFERED BY [[SAMSUNG]] AND [[SKHYNIX]]."** The Korean read-through: **"China's CXMT Plays Hardball — Strengthening Samsung and SK's DRAM Bargaining Power… with the cheap-Chinese-component workaround now blocked, Samsung Electronics and SK hynix have been RELIEVED OF THE BURDEN OF SHIPPING COMMODITY DRAM and are concentrating their production lines on HIGH-VALUE AI MEMORY SUCH AS HBM."** ⚠️ **This page carries CXMT capacity as "the new supply overhang" (Aug 3 signals). A CXMT that refuses to undercut on price is a materially different object from a CXMT that floods the market — same capacity, opposite price behaviour. Logged as evidence AGAINST the flood thesis, not as its refutation: one customer negotiation is not a pricing policy, and the sourcing is Korean media relayed by a FinTwit aggregator.**

**4. ⚠️ AND THE SHORTAGE IS NOW CONSTRAINING A FLAGSHIP LAUNCH, WITH A DOLLAR FIGURE. Culpium (Tim Culpan) exclusive, 2026-08-05: [[AAPL]] and its assemblers are "SCRAMBLING" for mobile DRAM six weeks before the iPhone Ultra / 18 / 18 Pro launch. The mechanism is NOT the logic node — the A20 Pro on [[TSM]] N2 has "good volume and yields," but it is packaged alongside DRAM via TSMC's WMCM process ("CoWoS for mobile"), so "wafers are piling up waiting for DRAM" and "TSMC IS SITTING ON AROUND $1 BILLION OF APPLE PROCESSORS AWAITING DRAM DELIVERY."** ⚠️ **The public corroboration is a TSMC disclosure the Street skipped: CFO Wendell Huang, 2026-07-16 — "inventory days increased seven days to 87 days, PRIMARILY DUE TO THE RAMP OF N2 TECHNOLOGY" (+11 days y/y, +7 q/q). On Culpan's sourcing, that inventory build is partly finished logic waiting on memory. This is the first datapoint on this page tying memory scarcity to a THIRD PARTY'S REPORTED BALANCE SHEET rather than to commentary.** Apple's response is already visible: raising prices and steering buyers from MacBook Air to MacBook Pro (Gurman). Single-source supply-chain reporting; the $1bn is not company-confirmed.

**5. Supporting price/volume prints the same session:** **Nanya (Taiwan's largest DRAM maker) July revenue +719.6% y/y to NT$43.87bn, +49.3% m/m, with Jan-Jul at NT$175.5bn (+660.9%); market estimates put DRAM prices +60% in 2Q over 1Q on FLAT bit shipments** (@dnystedt, 2026-08-05) — a clean, independent, price-not-volume confirmation from outside the big three. **TrendForce: 512Gb TLC NAND wafer spot +4.55% w/w to US$20.125 (Aug 3) — but explicitly "lacks demand support," with the spot market "lingering in gloominess amidst sluggish end consumer demand"** (@trendforce, 2026-08-05) ⚠️ **which is the same AI-strong / consumer-weak split this page already carries, and is consistent with SanDisk's "modest" forward ASP guide.** **[[SAMSUNG]] at FMS 2026 previewed zHBM (HBM stacked directly on the accelerator; targeting 8x HBM5 performance, 10x+ density, 3x energy efficiency, 50%+ lower thermal resistance) and V10 bonded NAND at >400 layers, ~58% denser than V9** (@The_AI_Investor, 2026-08-05) — long-dated concept, logged for the roadmap. **[[SKHYNIX]]'s Solidigm is pursuing a pre-IPO round ahead of a possible Nasdaq listing at a reported KRW 50tn valuation, versus the ~KRW 10tn SK Hynix agreed to pay Intel for the NAND/SSD business in 2020** (@jukan05, 2026-08-05). **Reuters: Samsung and SK Hynix are testing AMEC etch equipment for their China fabs — "the aim is NOT to expand production capacity in China, but to KEEP CHINESE SUPPLIERS IN RESERVE in case U.S. restrictions are extended to the servicing, repair, or replacement of existing Western equipment"** (@jukan05, 2026-08-05).

⚠️ **6. THE ANALOG CHAIN JUST COPIED THE MEMORY PLAYBOOK, which is the structural item of the week. [[IFX]] disclosed on its FY3Q26 print (08-05) MULTI-YEAR CAPACITY RESERVATION AGREEMENTS with leading AI customers, "concluded or in negotiation, with a cumulative revenue of a HIGH-SINGLE-DIGIT BILLION EURO amount." That is the same structure — customers pre-committing to capacity corridors rather than buying spot — now appearing OUTSIDE memory, in power semis. If the LTA/NBM architecture is generalising across the AI supply chain, the "memory is uniquely contracted" framing on this page needs to become "scarce AI inputs are getting contracted," and the read-through is to every constrained node, not just DRAM and NAND.**

## Recent signals (Aug 4, PM) — 🔴 THE 2027 ALLOCATION ROUND HAS ALREADY CLOSED, FILL RATES ARE 60-70%, AND TWO BIG BUYERS GAVE OPPOSITE MEMORY SIGNALS ON THE SAME DAY

**1) The allocation fact, which matters more than the price prints.** @The_AI_Investor (2026-08-03): **"Everyone knows 2027 memory is sold out. That's not the news. THE NEWS IS THE 2027 ALLOCATION ROUND ALREADY CLOSED, QUIETLY, IN JULY AND AUGUST… Nobody is publicizing their allocation. DEPOSITS ARE NOW UNIVERSAL — even small buyers who'll never get a 3-5 year LTA are WIRING CASH UPFRONT to hold a slot. VOLUME IS LOCKED, PRICE IS NOT: 2027 DRAM and NAND ASPs get set NEAR SHIPMENT. Anyone assuming LTAs cap the upside HAS IT BACKWARDS — THE SUPPLIERS KEPT THE OPTIONALITY. Fill rates running 60-70% of what buyers asked for. HBM and AI server now take ~70% of DRAM."** Corroborated independently by @jukan05 and @aleabitoreddit citing **DigiTimes: 2027 DRAM/HBM capacity fully booked ahead of schedule; NAND less tight but bookings expected filled by end-August; customers allocated only 60-70% of requested volumes; buyers deliberately not publicising allocations for fear of shrinking them** (2026-08-03/04). ⚠️ **Three independent accounts converge on the 60-70% fill rate — that is the most repeated hard number of the week. All are channel/FinTwit sourced, none company-confirmed.**

**2) "Volume locked, price not" sits ON one side of an unresolved contractual dispute.** ⚠️ **BofA (Simon Woo, 08-04) agrees: "ASP is NOT REALLY FIXED and is MOSTLY NEGOTIABLE EVERY QUARTER, EVEN FOR FIVE-YEAR CONTRACTS." JPM (Jay Kwon, 08-04) partly disagrees, disclosing that [[SKHYNIX]]'s LTAs with ten key customers (>half of volume) carry "VARIOUS PRICING TERMS (floor price, take-or-pay, prepayment, CEILING PRICE WITH/WITHOUT PRICE HIKE TERMS)" — so a subset DOES cap the upside. Both cannot be true across the whole book. Reconcilable version: quarterly renegotiation is the practice, ceilings exist in a minority of contracts. Left unreconciled and flagged — this is now the most consequential undisclosed contractual fact in the complex, and it is one step better characterised than on Aug 2.**

**3) 🆕 First-mover DISADVANTAGE on LTAs — the cleanest bear datapoint of the week, and it is specific.** Counterpoint's Q2 DRAM share data has **[[SKHYNIX]] only ~1 percentage point ahead of [[MU]] on revenue share**, attributed to **HBM3E prices actually FALLING through renegotiations rather than rising**, HBM4 launch delays, and — structurally — **Hynix having signed its LTAs EARLIER than competitors, so "Hynix's fixed LTA prices are reportedly LOWER than those of its rivals"** (@jukan05, 2026-08-04). ⚠️ **This inverts the LTA-as-moat framing this page has carried: signing early bought volume certainty at the cost of price. If accurate, the LTA winner is whoever signed LAST.**

**4) 🔴 TWO LARGE BUYERS, OPPOSITE SIGNALS, SAME DAY — carry both.**
- **[[SPCX]] (Q2 call, 08-04): "MEMORY IS THE MAIN LIMITING FACTOR FOR AI COMPUTE — while supply is increasing 20% ANNUALLY, demand is spiking by ~200%, which is why memory prices continue to head higher."** A buyer guiding to ~10GW of compute by end-2027 naming memory as *the* constraint is the strongest demand-side corroboration this page has.
- **[[AMD]] (Q2 call, 08-04): has "VERY GOOD VISIBILITY INTO HBM ALLOCATION FOR 2027" (LTAs signed) — but "every customer is looking at how to OPTIMIZE MEMORY FOOTPRINT… we have the OPPORTUNITY TO MODIFY MEMORY FOOTPRINT IF TCO IS NOT AS SIGNIFICANT in certain workloads… for some small/medium-size models, they may not get as much benefit from memory."**
⚠️ **The reconciliation that fits both: HBM content per accelerator is negotiable at the margin; aggregate bit demand still exceeds supply. That is precisely the 10-20% residual gap JPM models after the 8Hi migration (down from 30-40%) — a shortage market, but a less extreme one.**

**🆕 The [[AMD]] quote above is now sourced to the PRIMARY transcript, and it arrives with two things the relay could not carry — the QUESTION that produced it and the WORKLOAD-DEPENDENCY qualifier (AMD Q2 2026 earnings call, Bloomberg LIVE TRANSCRIPT, 2026-08-04).** The question was **Vivek Arya's (BofA), and its framing is the demand-side sizing this page needs: "when we look at the specs of Helios, it has, I think, ALMOST 50% MORE HBM than the competition… how much of the benefit is because of having 50% more HBM, and DOESN'T THAT EXPOSE AMD MORE TO MEMORY COST INFLATION? How are you ensuring that you're able to maintain margins… and get this memory allocation?"** — consistent with the **432GB HBM4 (12 stacks) per MI455X vs 288GB (8 stacks) on NVDA and [[GOOG]]** content already carried on [[AMD]]. Lisa Su's answer, in sequence: **(1) allocation is secured and long-dated — "we've worked with our memory partners for MULTIPLE YEARS to ensure that we have a strong ramp… there is VERY GOOD VISIBILITY INTO HBM ALLOCATION FOR WHAT WE EXPECT TO DELIVER IN 2027,"** covering both GPU HBM and general system memory across the DC business; **(2) 🆕 the benefit of the extra HBM is WORKLOAD-DEPENDENT, not architectural — "when you think about the LARGER MODEL SIZES, they really benefit from the larger memory footprint, and that goes into the total cost of ownership… so think about it as THE BENEFIT OF THE MEMORY IS WORKLOAD DEPENDENT… but there are some workloads, let's call it MEDIUM SIZED MODELS, THAT MAY NOT GET AS MUCH OF A BENEFIT. And in that case, WE WOULD ADDRESS THE MEMORY FOOTPRINT as you might expect"**; **(3)** then the de-spec option already logged above, verbatim: *"every customer is looking at how to optimize their memory footprint, and we have the opportunity, if desired, to also modify that memory footprint if the total cost of ownership is not as significant in certain workloads."* ⚠️ **Read as demand-side evidence that HBM CONTENT PER ACCELERATOR IS NEGOTIABLE AND GETS DECIDED WORKLOAD-BY-WORKLOAD: the vendor carrying ~50% MORE HBM than Nvidia is the one on record saying the extra capacity does not always pay for itself — and saying it into an inflating memory market, i.e. with a live cost incentive to trim. Two bounds on how far to take it: (a) it does NOT dispute aggregate scarcity — Su is explicit that 2027 allocation is already visible and multi-year contracted, which is a bullish supplier datapoint on volume; (b) it is NOT a margin concession — Su never answered the margin half of Arya's question, and Jean Hu's separate 2027 GM comments make no mention of memory cost. Treat it as content-per-unit risk to bit demand at the margin, stacked on the Rubin Ultra despec item ⑤ — and note the ASYMMETRY between the two: for AMD, de-speccing is a TCO CHOICE OFFERED TO CUSTOMERS; for NVDA the same move is described by both sides as SUPPLY-DRIVEN.**

**5) Rubin Ultra despec, now quantified.** Eugene Investment & Securities via @jukan05: assuming **NVDA's 2027 CoWoS allocation of 1,200k → accelerator output ~9.9M units, Rubin Ultra ~1.6M units**; a shift from **HBM4E 12Hi 384GB to HBM4 8Hi 192GB** would cut **NVDA's 2027 HBM demand ~10%, from 24.1bn Gb to 21.6bn Gb**. **TrendForce: DRAM shortage persists through 2027; NVDA weighing HBM4e 8hi / HBM4 12hi / HBM4 8hi in parallel with the spec unset.** ⚠️ **Mechanism agreed on both sides to be SUPPLY, not demand — @jukan05: "the Rubin Ultra HBM despec is a SUPPLY ISSUE. Not a demand issue." Still contradicted by the 08-03 ODM procurement checks on this page ("NO DESPEC IN MEMORY FROM NVIDIA," $7-8m/rack budgets) — primary supply-chain evidence vs Korean sell-side modelling, unresolved.**

**6) 🆕 HBF gets a published standard and a hyperscaler.** **[[SKHYNIX]] + [[SNDK]] released the first HBF specification at FMS 2026, with [[GOOG]] and Tenstorrent as key ecosystem partners** — up to **512GB on a two-stack configuration (8-Hi and 16-Hi dies)**, bandwidth grades **0.4-3.0TB/s** (JPM · Jay Kwon, 2026-08-04). Roadmap: **SanDisk prototypes 2H26, pilot line complete 2H26, commercialisation targeted 2027, Japan a candidate production site (ETNews); TrendForce sees demand inflecting 2030.** TrendForce frames HBF as **complementary to HBM, not competing** — addressing HBM's cost/capacity limits for LLM inference where "HBM delivers unmatched bandwidth [but] it is difficult for it alone to meet the massive capacity requirements." ⚠️ **The dissent is specific and from someone physically at FMS meeting SK and SanDisk execs: @bubbleboi — "my sources tell me that the MAIN CUSTOMER FOR HBF IS CHINESE (Huawei?) which makes sense since they are completely blacklisted from HBM… success will hinge on RELIABILITY and my view is it's 2 YEARS AT BEST… it's an engineering challenge that would eat up silicon area that should be spent on compute. My guess is DISAGGREGATED MEMORY WILL BE A BETTER SOLUTION." If the anchor demand is a sanctioned buyer, the addressable market is far smaller than the consortium framing implies. Single unverified source — but the most informed-sounding objection on record.** Also announced: **SKHYNIX V10 375-layer 4D NAND at FMS, mass production targeted early 2027**; **[[SAMSUNG]] debuted new 3D HBM and flash technologies** (Semiecosystem/Mark LaPedus, 2026-08-04); **[[MRVL]] announced an AI memory-infrastructure portfolio for agentic inference** at the same event.

**7) Downstream price pass-through is now visible in consumer products.** **A shortage of Apple's best-selling MacBook Air caused by memory-chip shortages is reported, with the disruption expected to ripple to [[TSM]], Quanta and Foxconn** (@dnystedt citing Bloomberg, 2026-08-04). **NVDA consumer graphics-card prices +30% in one week in China** (EDN). **[[AMD]] guided Gaming −31% y/y and a softer 2H PC market explicitly on component costs.** **MLCC prices up 30% across the board** (TrendForce). ⚠️ **This is the "memory inflation as a constraint on what gets built" thesis (MS, Jul 27) showing up in end-market units rather than in supplier margins — the first cluster of hard evidence for it.** **CXMT: HP, ASUS and Acer have started using small amounts of CXMT DRAM in laptops for NON-US markets** (Nikkei Asia), and CXMT is set to make small quantities of **LPDDR6** by end-2026 (Bloomberg) — ⚠️ but @jukan05 relays a channel view that **"CXMT's price is NOT cheaper than the top three players"**, which undercuts the substitution logic.

## Recent signals (Aug 4) — the LTA debate gets its GAME-THEORY closure argument from the buy side: breaking one costs you your ALLOCATION, and allocation is now what sets accelerator share

**★ THE DIRECT COUNTER TO THE AUG-2 "BUYERS DESCRIBE THE OPTION TO STOP PAYING" THREAD, argued from the buyer's seat** (Invest Like the Best podcast · Gavin Baker, Atreides, ~2026-08-04; [transcript archived](../../_equity_calls/Overall/2026-08-04_GavinBaker_ILTB-AI-repricing.md)). ⚠️ *Informal podcast commentary, not verified financials.*
- **The premise: memory-per-flop is the axis that dominates all others. "The more memory you put with flops for a given unit of compute, the more tokens you get out — it's the SINGLE MOST IMPORTANT THING you could do to increase token output per unit of compute. And that definitionally lowers costs, which is why demand hasn't responded at all negatively — there's been NO ELASTICITY."** (The demand-side identity underneath this page's despec-refutation rows.)
- **★ The game theory, played forward to 2027-28: four buyers matter at scale ([[AMZN]]/Trainium, [[GOOG]]/TPU, [[AMD]], [[NVDA]] "much bigger than everybody else combined"), and accelerator market shares "for the next several years are going to be determined by SUPPLY-CHAIN ALLOCATIONS and what you have pre-purchased." Therefore: "you're vaguely tempted to break one of these LTAs to get a lower price… [but] if you break your LTA and in the next two or three years FOR ANY REASON leverage shifts back to the memory guys, YOU'RE OUT OF BUSINESS. They say: fine, you broke the price agreement, we're going to break the VOLUME agreement and give the volume to your competitor. You just lost share… you might blow up your entire business and your franchise by breaking an LTA." And: "the logic almost holds even in a severe oversupply situation" — oversupply is followed by undersupply, and the memory suppliers remember.** ⚠️ Note what this does and does not answer: it is an argument about the OPTION VALUE of walking (low, because allocation is existential), not about the accounting floors — the Samsung −5%/+10-20% asymmetry (Jul 31/Aug 2 rows) still defines the payoff INSIDE the agreements; Baker's claim is that the exit door is welded shut by share math.
- **The regime change vs every prior cycle, named: [[AAPL]] — "going back three, four, five years, they're overwhelmingly the largest purchaser… they know they can do whatever they want with no consequences — even if they super screw Hynix, Micron will of course take them. THIS IS JUST DIFFERENT. You have at least four players and all the startups."** He also concedes the regime change beat him: the shift from "crushing numbers in the short term" to trading "short-term upside for durability" via LTAs is "a transition in the market that I got wrong."
- **The capital-structure extension — memory joins the GPU credit wrapper: asked what he'd do as CEO of [[SKHYNIX]]: "the exact same thing Nvidia is doing right now… go to the buyers of GPUs, Trainiums, whoever, and say I'll PARTICIPATE IN THE NVIDIA CREDIT WRAPPER" — contribute cash to the financing structure, take "a cut of the ongoing revenues," i.e. a ROYALTY ON RECURRING COMPUTE REVENUE. "I'm sure our friends at Blackstone and Apollo are suggesting some variant of this to the memory companies… it's almost a logical extension of the LTAs."** Watch against the SCA/prepayment structures already on this page — same direction of travel: spot upside converted into contracted, financeable streams.
- Cycle color from the same conversation: **"the spot price of DRAM this month" is on his list of accelerating metrics**, and "everybody is citing these LTAs — everything's in a shortage right now."

## Recent signals (Aug 3) — ★ THE DESPEC BEAR CASE IS REFUTED AT THE PROCUREMENT DESK, HBM4 COST IS RE-MARKED TO ~$31/GB WITH A TIERED STRUCTURE, and [[CXMT]] capacity is the new supply overhang

**★ ① THE MOST IMPORTANT ITEM: "NO MEMORY DESPEC FROM NVIDIA," AND THE PROOF IS A BUDGET NUMBER RATHER THAN A DENIAL (Jefferies · Arthur Lai ODM checks, via Conor O'Mara, JEF Asia morning, 2026-08-03).** **Verbatim: "Arthur's checks with ODMs in recent weeks suggest NO DESPEC IN MEMORY FROM [[NVDA]] as the PERFORMANCE OF THE AI SERVERS WOULD BE SEVERELY IMPACTED. All of the procurement managers he speaks to at the various ODMs are also PREPARING THEIR PURCHASING PLANS BASED ON A PRICE OF $7-8M PER RACK, WHICH ALSO SUGGESTS NO DESPEC. After all the concerns about despec including on racks, that sounds BULLISH FOR MEMORY."** ⚠️ **Why the second sentence is the stronger evidence: a despec shows up in the rack BOM before it shows up in a spec sheet, and the buyers are budgeting at full price. This page has carried "buyers describe the option to stop paying" (Aug 2) and the memory-content-cut argument as the central bear objection to the up-cycle's durability. This is the first CHECK-BASED rebuttal — not a company denial, not a sell-side assertion — and it comes from the layer between the memory makers and the hyperscalers. It does not resolve the pricing debate, but it removes the mechanism by which the bear case was supposed to operate.**
