Both are true and they are different arguments.
- **NEW (2026-07-26) — the tightest formulation of the structural bull case on this page, and it comes from the competitor's analyst (Nomura, same note as above): "We estimate that strong demand for agentic AI will drive a more than sevenfold increase in global memory usage (with 60%+ bit CAGR) over 2026-30F, EVEN AFTER accounting for potential adoption of memory-efficiency technologies (discounting memory usage by 4x). However, expansion of semiconductor capacity faces multiple bottlenecks (e.g. clean rooms, equipment, materials, and talents). Memory makers are expanding capacity at a 30-40% bit CAGR over 2026-30F, but that could still be insufficient."** The reason to carry this rather than another bull note: it **pre-applies a 4x haircut for memory-efficiency techniques** (quantization, KV-cache compression, MoE — the "tokenmaxxing" bear) and demand still outruns supply by a wide margin. That is a much harder claim to dismiss than an unadjusted 7x. Cross-check against BofA/Arya's independent version of the same argument: **"Kimi K3 still requires ~1.4TB of HBM per serving instance despite ultra-low API pricing,"** and **"open-weight models multiply memory demand because every customer deployment creates a new memory endpoint"** — Jevons stated as architecture rather than elasticity.
- **NEW (2026-07-26) — the memory bill is now visibly cutting NVIDIA's own product specs, which is the clearest observable evidence yet that this upcycle is being paid for out of the accelerator BOM (Jefferies Asia TMT · Conor O'Mara, citing WCCFTECH, 2026-07-26).** After the reported **RTX 50 ramp delay on memory costs** and a **SOCAMM spec cut**, NVDA is **now also reported to be cutting the memory spec on NVL72** — because **"memory is the majority of the BOM on VR200."** ⚠️ **The dispersion in sell-side HBM pricing inputs is remarkable and should be treated as an open question, not a number: rack models are running on $/GB assumptions from ~$20 (Jefferies' own Sherman, which O'Mara says is too low) to "$53 per GB in 2027" — a ~2.7x spread — with one broker at ~$9.1m per rack.** Anyone reconciling accelerator cost models across houses needs to check which $/GB is embedded before comparing. Trade-press + desk opinion, not NVDA disclosure. See [[NVDA]].
- **NEW (2026-07-26) — Apple's lobbying ask is now specific, and Micron is publicly opposed (WSJ, via Vital Knowledge):** "Apple is lobbying the White House aggressively for permission to utilize **Chinese memory chips in devices sold outside the US** while Micron is fiercely opposed to the move, warning that **China's companies could decimate the American memory industry over time just as occurred in steel and manufacturing**." Two escalations vs the 06-25 version of this story: the **ex-US carve-out** is a much narrower and therefore more grantable request than blanket approval, and **MU's opposition is on the record with an industrial-policy frame**. The KIS counter still stands (China would restrict foreign supply anyway; CXMT has shifted LPDDR→DDR for domestic datacentres), so no supply changes yet — but it lands the same weekend as the CXMT initiation and IPO, and it tells you how binding the memory bill has become at the largest customer. See [[AAPL]], [[MU]].
- **NEW (2026-07-26) — the July contract check, with the consensus gap made explicit (BofA · Simon Woo, restated in the TMT Idea Bank):** "more Tier 2 OEMs and module makers agreed to pay **15-20% higher PC DRAM contract prices for July vs June. This implies a 30-40% QoQ increase that exceeds consensus (e.g. TrendForce: up 13-18% for 3Q DRAM ASP ex-HBM)."** BofA's research team described as "**Memory bulls**" into a week with the **CXMT IPO (7/27), SK hynix (7/28), Samsung (7/29) and Kioxia (7/31)** all landing. Tape corroboration: **memory was the best-performing sub-sector last week (MU +8%, WDC +9%, STX +8%, SNDK +6%) even as the SOX sat -19% off its June high** — though note memory also carries the **largest sub-sector derating since the June 22 SOX peak at -24% share price / -36% P/E** (Jefferies), i.e. earnings are being upgraded faster than the multiple is falling. ⚠️ **Positioning reads contradict and are left unreconciled: MS Prime has HFs recovering ~50% of the capital unwound through June with the Global Memory (MSXXGMEM) L/S ratio at ~7x vs peaks >20x (re-engaged, not crowded) and 61% of MS's EU clients saying now is the time to add memory longs; JPM/Schlegel has global semis at the 98th %ile on net exposure and L/S ratios on long-term metrics; and Jefferies' own desk reports "memory our most sold sector" on Friday's flows.** The likely reconciliation is the lookback window (MS vs 2026 peaks, JPM vs history) but it is not resolved here.
- **NEW (2026-07-25) — the POLICY bear case on memory pricing, which is independent of demand and is not in any bottoms-up model on this page (Susquehanna · Mehdi Hosseini, "US Negotiating Leverage Bears Fruit", Technology Hardware weekly, 2026-07-25).** Hosseini argues the US administration's negotiating strategy is working: **Korean memory suppliers are now expected to unveil major US memory-fab investment plans** (the Korean president was in Silicon Valley for meetings and an AI summit; Tom's Hardware reports Samsung and SK Hynix set to announce large deals with leading US tech firms). The leverage rests on two points: **(1) >50% of Korean suppliers' memory revenue comes from US-based customers**; **(2) CXMT and YMTC are now a credible supply alternative** — both **barred from supplying US-based OEMs**, both on the **Pentagon's Section 1260H list**, **YMTC also on the Commerce Entity List**. So Washington holds a switch. **Net-new sizing: SIG expects CXMT and YMTC to represent 17% and 25% of DRAM and NAND supply respectively by YE28.** The reason this is near-term rather than a 2029 story: **greenfield US memory fabs cannot come online before 2029** given construction and tooling lead times, so **in the interim "CXMT & YMTC could still offer a valuable short-term remedy to a tight memory supply-demand balance; the US essentially killing two birds with one stone"** — easing the imbalance *and* damping memory-price inflation. **Read plainly: a portion of current DRAM/NAND pricing rests on an export-control regime that the same administration has an incentive to relax once the fab commitments are banked.** Cross-ref [../MU.md](../MU.md), [../SKHYNIX.md](../SKHYNIX.md), [../SAMSUNG.md](../SAMSUNG.md), [china-export.md](china-export.md).
- **NEW (2026-07-25) — a customer-side duration estimate that runs past most sell-side models: DRAM shortages until at least 2H2028 (AlphaSense expert call — Senior Hardware Engineer, Amazon, published 2026-07-24).** A current AWS hardware engineer with direct experience in AI custom silicon and cloud infrastructure calls **DRAM "the tightest supply chain constraint,"** with **shortages expected to last until at least the second half of 2028**, demand outpacing supply **despite efficiency improvements**, while **CPU supply is stable and not a major bottleneck**. Valuable because it is the *buyer* speaking, not a supplier or a broker. Note the tension with the Susquehanna bullet above: the physical shortage and the political relief valve are not mutually exclusive — they simply resolve the price at different levels.
- **NEW (2026-07-25) — pass-through is now visible at the consumer edge, and a competitor is calling a record TAM.** **Framework nearly doubled memory pricing on 32GB and 64GB Laptop 13 Pro configurations overnight**, its CEO saying that absorbing the LPCAMM2 supplier hikes would put "our ability to operate at real financial risk" (Tom's Hardware, 2026-07-23) — the sharpest consumer-facing evidence yet that contract-price increases are reaching end markets rather than being absorbed by OEMs. **Nanya Tech sees the memory market reaching a record ~US$800bn in 2026, nearly half of all semiconductor revenue** (TrendForce, 2026-07-23) — a competitor TAM claim, useful as an upper bound on how far the industry is extrapolating. Logged with scepticism from the same weekly: **Adata's chairman claims the DRAM shortage lasts another 10 years** and dismisses AI-bubble talk "until 2040 or 2050" — an outlier vendor claim, recorded for the record only. Also: **Musk says Micron is "making room" for Tesla in the years to come**, hinting at a long-term memory supply arrangement (TrendForce, 2026-07-23) — a named non-hyperscaler anchor. (All via Susquehanna · Hosseini weekly, 2026-07-25.)
- **NEW (2026-07-25) — the second-order effect the wiki should now track as one mechanism: memory-cost inflation is BIFURCATING the handset market.** UBS's handset work this week concludes that **Apple, and to a lesser extent Samsung, are weathering memory pricing pressure materially better than Chinese Android vendors** — and uses that single mechanism to (i) cut [../QCOM.md](../QCOM.md) (PT $235→$190; smartphone units now **-10% C2026 / +2% C2027, from -7% / +1%**; only mid-single-digit y/y smartphone QCT pricing growth), (ii) cut [../ARM.md](../ARM.md) (PT $470→$360, entirely on China Android handset exposure, with data-centre forecasts untouched), and (iii) quantify the [../AAPL.md](../AAPL.md) BOM risk (pre-spike a $1,000 iPhone carried **BOM ~$580 with memory ~$60, ~10% of cost**, at ~42% contribution margin; at **+300% memory, memory becomes ~30% of BOM and contribution margin falls to 24%**, with a **$100 price increase recovering only to 31%**). Corroborating datapoint: **Samsung's Galaxy Z Fold8 launched with price hikes of up to US$100** (TrendForce, 07-23). **The read for this theme: memory inflation is no longer only a supplier-margin story — it is now actively reallocating profit pools within the customer base, and the losers are concentrated in Chinese Android.** (UBS · Tim Arcuri / David Vogt via Ruple "Tech Musings", 2026-07-25.)
- **NEW (2026-07-24) — the HBM4 pin-speed bake-off, and it is the most important supplier-side datapoint of the quarter: AMD MI455X takes the bandwidth crown with 12 stacks / 432GB / 23.3 TB/s versus Rubin's 8 stacks / 288GB / 22 TB/s — near parity despite a 50% wider bus, because Nvidia runs 10.7 Gbps pin speed vs AMD's 7.6 Gbps, and Nvidia's decision to raise the target FORCED memory suppliers to rework HBM4 (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026", 2026-07-24).** The supply-chain consequence is the point, not the spec race. **The spec:** the large MI455X package fits **12 stacks of HBM4 = 432GB, "industry leading compared to Nvidia and Google shipping only 8 stacks which is 288GB per package,"** and makes MI455 the memory-bandwidth winner at **23.3 TB/s per chip, implying HBM4 pin speeds of 7.6 Gbps** (up from the 19.6 TB/s advertised at last year's Advancing AI). **But despite a 50% wider bus than Rubin, total bandwidth is "barely above Rubin at 22 TB/s"** — because at Nvidia's marketed 22 TB/s Rubin runs a **10.7 Gbps pin speed, 40% faster than MI455's**. **The mechanism, and the reason this belongs on this page:** *"Nvidia aggressively raised its targeted HBM4 pin speeds last year, well above the original JEDEC HBM4 specification. This change was precisely driven by the need to make up for the memory bandwidth deficit to AMD MI455X."* Consequences named: **"memory suppliers had to rework their HBM4 to deliver the spec Nvidia was targeting,"** which **pushed out upstream output for Rubin (now resolved)**, and **"NVIDIA will be forced to use a much higher quality bin than AMD"** — i.e. a **binning/yield split between the two HBM4 buyers**, with the tighter bin on the larger volume. **AMD and Nvidia are the only two companies shipping HBM4 this year.** Packaging enabler: AMD's base die is now closer to reticle-sized with the **HBM-facing edge at 32mm to squeeze in 3 cubes per edge, versus 29mm on the MI300X AID which could not fit 3**. ⚠️ **The 2027 pricing call, which is the bit to carry into supplier models:** SemiAnalysis expects **"a significant increase in HBM pricing in 2027 as memory suppliers move to match the margins they now earn on commodity DRAM"** — AMD "can't pass the full memory increase on to its customers" because "most of AMD's volume goes to large buyers with real negotiating leverage," so it lands as a **gross-margin drag on the MI455X package**; and **"HBM, DRAM, and NAND prices alike have increased dramatically since our baseline in mid-2025."** **The offsetting de-spec, which is the bear leg for bit demand:** AMD's original 2025 BOM estimate carried **"a staggering 138TB in DRAM across LPDDR5X and DDR5 MRDIMM"**; the current estimate has **LPDDR5X entirely removed** — *"previous roadmaps had up to 1TB of LPDDR as second tier memory for each MI455X attached on the EAM module, however this has now disappeared … another consequence of tight memory supply"* — plus **a significant DDR5 de-spec**, so **"much of the DRAM price increases are offset by aggressive de-specs."** Read for the theme: **HBM pricing power is real and moving to match commodity-DRAM margins, but the non-HBM bit content per accelerator is being engineered downward in response** — pricing up, quantum down, which is exactly the "memory-quantum / de-speccing" thread this page tracks (and the same de-spec logic as Meta's custom MI455 below). (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026", 2026-07-24; see [../AMD.md](../AMD.md), [../NVDA.md](../NVDA.md), [../MU.md](../MU.md), [../SKHYNIX.md](../SKHYNIX.md), [../SAMSUNG.md](../SAMSUNG.md); packaging leg on [cowos-packaging.md](cowos-packaging.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_24_ENC_Can_AMD_break_the_CUDA_Moat_AMD_Advancing_AI_2026_email.html)
- **NEW (2026-06-14, light) — the China mobile-DRAM datapoint the CXMT debate on this page has been missing a teardown for: a Chinese flagship is DUAL-SOURCED between CXMT and Samsung, with CXMT's G4 process inferred at ~0.3 Gib/mm², "roughly equivalent to other manufacturers' 1z" (SemiAnalysis / STEEL Team, "Is SMIC N+3's Metal Pitch Smaller than Intel 18A's?", 2026-06-14).** From SemiAnalysis's first public teardown-lab report. The **Kirin 9030 Pro carries 12GB of Samsung DRAM in two stacks of four dies, identified as the K4L2E165YD — a 12Gb LPDDR5X-9600 device on Samsung's 1a node** (the fourth generation of its 10nm-class DRAM after 1x/1y/1z), with the note that **"1a has shipped in volume since 2022, so this is current memory rather than older-node inventory."** The **16GB Pro Max variants "were found with both CXMT and Samsung packages"** — the CXMT part marked **CXDD7JEDM, two stacks of four dies, packaged in week 45 of 2025** — and X-ray CT-inferred die dimensions are "consistent with a known density of **~0.3 Gib/mm² for the CXMT G4 process, roughly equivalent to other manufacturers' 1z processes.**" Read: **CXMT is qualified inside a Chinese flagship alongside Samsung, but roughly one node-generation behind on density (1z vs 1a)** — which is a concrete, physically-measured version of the "CXMT ~20% of wafers but only ~6-7% of bits" framing already on this page, and consistent with BofA's "CXMT expanding capacity has had only a limited impact" read at the high end. (SemiAnalysis / STEEL Team, 2026-06-14; CXMT has no wiki page; the China-policy leg is on [china-export.md](china-export.md), the packaging leg on [cowos-packaging.md](cowos-packaging.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_14_ENC_Is_SMIC_N3s_Metal_Pitch_Smaller_than_Intel_18As_email.html)
- **NEW (2026-05-21, one line) — HBM verification is now approaching logic-chip complexity, and HBM4 has become a flagship IP title (SemiAnalysis "EDA Market Primer", 2026-05-21).** A design-cost read-through for the HBM supply chain: **"HBM verification now approaches logic-chip complexity as stacking and interposer routing requirements grow with each generation"** — memory makers (Samsung, SK hynix, Micron, Kioxia) run specialised DRAM/NAND/HBM tool flows, and every new protocol (PCIe Gen6, **HBM4**, UCIe) "adds verification surface area that compounds on existing workloads." On the buyer side, **a single flagship AI chip now consumes $10-15M of interface IP licensing (PCIe Gen5/Gen6, UCIe, HBM3/HBM4, 800G/1.6T Ethernet) versus $2-5M for a traditional datacenter CPU**, and **Cadence's IP portfolio "hit critical mass with HBM4, 224G SerDes, and LPDDR6"** with IP revenue +~25% in 2025. Read: the HBM generation cadence is now a real fixed-cost escalator on both the memory maker and the accelerator designer — a small structural argument for HBM pricing power. (SemiAnalysis "EDA Market Primer", 2026-05-21; off-coverage SNPS/CDNS — no wiki pages) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_05_21_ENC_EDA_Market_Primer_email.html)
- **BACKFILL — HISTORICAL, dated 2026-03-03 (ingested 2026-07-27; ~5 months old — file as demand-side context and do NOT let it supersede any 2026 Q2/Q3 memory number on this page) — the consumer/mobile demand-DESTRUCTION edge of the up-cycle, modelled component-by-component, and the earliest dated version of an argument this page only picked up in July (Bernstein · Mark C. Newman "Apple Deep Dive: Risks and Opportunities in a year of transition", 2026-03-03).** **The line item: in Bernstein's iPhone 18 Pro Max BOM build, the 12GB DRAM line goes from $28.93 to $114.00, +294%, taking memory from ~5% to ~17% of BOM (16-17% on the 512GB/1TB configs); the 256GB NAND line goes $10.58 → $28.45, +169%.** Total device cost rises **$563.80 → $694.49 (+23%) on 256GB and $574.38 → $722.95 (+26%) on 512GB**, which Bernstein blends to **"close to 24.5%"** for the Pro Max and headlines as a **potential ~25% iPhone BOM increase.** The underlying price series: **Apple mobile-DRAM module pricing $2.40/GB in 2QCY25 → $9.5/GB estimated 2QCY26 = +294%** (via +89% q/q January mobile-DRAM contract prices plus a modelled further +25% q/q), and **Apple NAND +169% over the same window** on a die-level basis. ⚠️ **BASIS NOTE — do not mix with the page's contract-price cadence: this is a 2QCY25→2QCY26 Y/Y module-price change for MOBILE DRAM at the point Bernstein thinks iPhone contracts get negotiated. It is not the Q/Q conventional-DRAM contract series in [`dram-pricing-2026`](../_meta/assumptions.md) (TrendForce +15-20% Q3 / +3-8% Q4, revised 2026-06-30), and it is not comparable to the July PC DRAM contract marks above.** **The half-offset scenario is the more useful one for a base case: reducing both DRAM and NAND increases by half puts the DRAM line at $71.47 (+147%) and total device cost +14.5%**, on flat ASPs and +5.5% units, giving **FY27 EPS of $8.81 — 15% below Bernstein and 5.3% below consensus.** **Bernstein attributes the surge to "the large AI demand for memory" — i.e. this page's supply-tightness thesis, arriving at the consumer BOM.** Two second-order legs: **lower-end Android OEMs "cannot secure enough memory," which Bernstein frames as a share-gain opening for [[AAPL]]**; and **OEMs may respond "either by securing more favorable memory pricing with existing suppliers or even TURNING TO CHINESE SUPPLIERS to offset cost inflation"** — the demand-pull behind the CXMT/YMTC design-in fight logged above. **Why carry a five-month-old note: it is the earliest dated, fully-specified version of the mechanism this page only started tracking on 07-25 via UBS (a $1,000 iPhone at BOM ~$580 / memory ~$60 ≈ 10%, going to ~30% of BOM at +300% memory). Bernstein's March arithmetic — memory 5% → ~17% of BOM on +294% — is an independent and much earlier corroboration of the same bifurcation call, and it means the profit-pool reallocation was modellable in Q1, not discovered in July.** It is the counterweight to a purely supply-side reading of the up-cycle: **at some pass-through level the marginal consumer unit does not get built** (see the -11% y/y Q2 2026 global smartphone shipments already logged above, and the QCOM/ARM cuts on 07-25). (Bernstein · Mark C. Newman "Apple Deep Dive: Risks and Opportunities in a year of transition", 2026-03-03 — **historical backfill, March-2026 estimates; every price mark here is superseded by the later 2026 series on this page**; see [../AAPL.md](../AAPL.md), [../QCOM.md](../QCOM.md), [../ARM.md](../ARM.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/BERN_252051.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-23/24) — the pricing check is BEATING while the tape sold off: DRAM contract prices +15-20% m/m for July, implying +30-40% q/q vs consensus of +13-18%; and China's record imports argue CXMT substitution is not yet biting (BofA · Simon Woo / Dai Shen / Vivek Arya, "Global Memory Tech" weekly + "Simon's tech check", 2026-07-23/24).** Weekly channel checks found **more Tier-2 OEMs and module makers agreed to pay 15-20% higher PC DRAM contract prices for July vs June — a +30-40% q/q increase that exceeds consensus** (TrendForce: +13-18% for 3Q DRAM ASP ex-HBM). Supporting: **spot +2-3% w/w**; **Korea semis exports +181% y/y in the first 20 days of July**; and the datapoint most relevant to the China-substitution bear — **China's memory imports hit a record US$32bn (+~250% y/y) despite rising domestic DRAM and NAND production**, with Chinese OEMs and hyperscalers still relying heavily on Korean chips at **both** the high and low end, "suggesting that **CXMT's expanding capacity has had only a limited impact**." **Demand anchor quantified:** BofA's Justin Post models **Alphabet capex US$200bn/300bn in 2026/27 (vs US$91bn in 2025)**, implying **Google alone needs 50%+ more memory chips in 2027 than 2026** — three mechanisms: memory's rising share of Big Tech capex; **DRAM/NAND prices under LTAs unlikely to rise much from 4Q26 or at least 1Q27, so more bits at stable ASPs**; and **external TPU sales to third parties consuming more Asian memory including HBM** (see [custom-asic-tpu](custom-asic-tpu.md), [../GOOG.md](../GOOG.md)). **MS's cycle framing is the most useful addition for this theme (Joseph Moore via MS TMT sales, 07-24):** after fresh **data-center purchasing** checks MS **defended [../MU.md](../MU.md) and [../SNDK.md](../SNDK.md), calling the selloff "a compelling entry point"** — **memory is increasingly THE bottleneck to AI builds (and agentic-CPU builds), durably so**, with **data-center memory prices +25%+ in 3Q** (a deceleration from 2Q's larger increases, "but that has been obvious"), **NAND checks inflecting recently**, and the shortage expected to **intensify in 2027 and again in 2028**. **The key structural claim: "LTAs and de-speccing could flatten the amplitude of the cycle vs inflated expectations — but increase duration, which is likely better for stocks in the long run"** — i.e. the LTA/take-or-pay build-out this theme tracks is an **amplitude-for-duration trade**, which is the mechanism behind the memory re-rating argument rather than a contradiction of it. **The hardest physical tightness datapoint of the week (@jukan05, 07-23):** after reporting **YMTC has surpassed Micron and SanDisk in NAND market share, narrowing the gap to Kioxia to just 0.4pp** and watching memory stocks sell off, he pushed back — *"NAND is still in short supply, and that shortage will persist. **NVIDIA's CMX racks are currently so constrained by NAND shortages that they cannot even be shipped fully populated.**"* **Both belong on the page: YMTC share risk is real, and so is near-term enterprise-NAND tightness.** ⚠️ **The tape went the other way for reasons unrelated to pricing:** Asian memory was very weak on 07-23 with the **KOSPI -5.7% on no memory-specific newsflow**, which Rothschild's desk attributes to the US session "or even the Anthropic datapoint" — an unverified TickerTrends read that **Anthropic ARR sequential growth has slowed significantly** (Rothschild & Co · Adley, 07-24; see [../ANTHROPIC.md](../ANTHROPIC.md)). **Worth recording as a mechanism for this theme: the memory complex now appears to trade off frontier-lab ARR perception, not DRAM pricing** — a positioning vulnerability precisely when the fundamental checks are beating. Also logged: **BofA's Arya arguing open-source AI is *bullish* for memory** — closed models amortise demand across shared HBM pools in a few data centres, whereas **open models replicate model weights into a separate HBM pool with every self-hosted deployment** (via @jukan05, 07-22); **LTAs are spreading beyond memory into passives** (Samsung Electro-Mechanics MLCC agreement with an unnamed "large global company," @jukan05 07-23); and **BofA expects Samsung's 07-30 call to carry a massive buyback, earlier dividends and more bullish 2H/2027 guidance** (see [../SAMSUNG.md](../SAMSUNG.md)).
- **NEW (2026-07-21) — HEADLINE: Rubin Ultra may launch at 384GB HBM4E (not 1TB) as CoWoS-L + HBM supply constrain the 16-stack design; HBF emerging as a long-term HBM complement (Arete Research "Real-Time Views #68", 2026-07-21).** Arete (Nam Hyung Kim) reads the SemiAnalysis (June 30) report that NVIDIA may have **cancelled the four-reticle Rubin Ultra design** on CoWoS-L packaging challenges: if so, the originally-anticipated **1TB HBM config (16× 64GB HBM4E stacks) becomes impractical**, likely reverting to **eight 48GB HBM4E stacks (12-Hi) = 384GB total** — still a **+33% step over Rubin's expected 288GB** while **substantially easing industry-wide HBM supply constraints**. Rationale: HBM is a *lower-margin* business than conventional DRAM (so suppliers had little incentive to over-invest for a 1TB roadmap), and the industry has never mass-produced 16-Hi HBM4E at the scale a 1TB Rubin Ultra would need; 12-Hi lowers complexity, improves yields and system cost. Corroborated by **Samsung sampling 12-Hi (48GB) HBM4E instead of 16-Hi (64GB)**. **Read for the memory makers:** a demand-quantum reset lower on the top-bin part but a *relief* on the supply crunch — bullish for HBM makers' margin/yield mix, mild negative for the "1TB = massive HBM bit pull" bull. Separately, **High Bandwidth Flash (HBF)** is emerging as a promising **long-term complement to HBM** (not a replacement): SanDisk disclosed its HBF IP (TransForce, June 22) — SanDisk + SK Hynix are working to standardise HBF, roadmap **512GB (16 stacked dies) → ~1.5TB gen-3**; a tiered hierarchy (HBM = latency-sensitive active compute, HBF = static read-intensive model weights, CMS SSD = write-intensive KV-cache) — but significant endurance (SLC PE-cycle), controller, thermal, packaging + ecosystem hurdles remain before commercialisation, and SLC-HBF will cost several × enterprise QLC SSDs. The Samsung P5 + SK Hynix M17 greenfield NAND builds (below) are what create the die headroom for HBF long-term. (Arete Research "Real-Time Views #68", 2026-07-21; see [../MU.md](../MU.md), [../SKHYNIX.md](../SKHYNIX.md), [../SAMSUNG.md](../SAMSUNG.md), [../SNDK.md](../SNDK.md), [../WDC.md](../WDC.md), [../KIOXIA.md](../KIOXIA.md); CoWoS-L angle on [cowos-packaging.md](cowos-packaging.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Semis_Hardware_Real_Time_Views_July_26_Edition_1.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-21) — Meta's custom cut-down AMD MI450 carries HALF the HBM stacks + 8-Hi (not 12-Hi) HBM4 — a de-speccing datapoint on the top-bin demand quantum (SemiAnalysis "Meta's Infrastructure Team Needs A Culture Reset", 2026-07-21).** SemiAnalysis reports Meta commissioned a **custom cut-down MI450 — half the compute silicon per package and half the number of HBM stacks** vs the full MI450X, with **HBM4 downgraded to 8-Hi (vs 12-Hi in the standard SKU)**. Context: the standard **AMD MI450 is "the most advanced GPU in the market" (2nm, hybrid bonding, 12 stacks of HBM, the largest CoWoS reticle) → highest compute + memory density per chip** — Meta "choosing to cut this engineering marvel in half blunts the proposition completely" (the custom part has significant compute + HBM deficiency, uninteresting to Meta's own teams or external customers). Read for the HBM makers: another **de-speccing / demand-quantum nuance** — a marquee custom accelerator carrying half the HBM stacks and 8-Hi (vs 12-Hi) trims per-unit HBM bit pull, echoing the NVDA-LPDDR-halving and Rubin-Ultra-384GB-reset threads already on this page (mild negative for the "content-doubling" HBM bull, though supply-relieving). (SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-21; see [../AMD.md](../AMD.md), [../META.md](../META.md); the Meta-infra-dysfunction leg on [tokenmaxxing.md](tokenmaxxing.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_21_ENC_Metas_Infrastructure_Team_Needs_A_Culture_Reset_email.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-21) — NAND greenfields resume: SK Hynix M17 (~KRW80tn/$53bn, ~150k wpm NAND by '30-31) + Samsung P5 (~70-80k wpm) — but net capacity growth still <10% by '30 as process migrations eat additions → structurally tight NAND (Arete "Real-Time Views #68", 2026-07-21).** After years of NAND-greenfield avoidance (ex-Kioxia), improving profitability + expected prolonged tight supply are driving long-term capacity expansion; with process migrations now generating limited bit growth + poor returns, **greenfields become the primary avenue to preserve share**. Arete estimates **SK Hynix M17 could add ~150k wpm NAND by '30-31** (facility also supports DRAM; includes Solidigm) and **Samsung P5 ~70-80k wpm** over the same period — the second major Korean NAND greenfield after Samsung's P5. But **continued effective-wafer loss from process migrations offsets much of it → global net NAND wafer capacity rises <10% by '30 from ~1.2m wspm** — i.e. NAND stays structurally tight. Greenfield NAND capital intensity **~$2.7bn per 10k wafer starts/month** (vs <$2.0bn for DRAM). Constructive long-term NAND read. (Arete "Real-Time Views #68", 2026-07-21; see [../SKHYNIX.md](../SKHYNIX.md), [../SAMSUNG.md](../SAMSUNG.md), [../MU.md](../MU.md), [../SNDK.md](../SNDK.md), [../WDC.md](../WDC.md), [../KIOXIA.md](../KIOXIA.md))
- **NEW (2026-07-20) — MS/Joe Moore "US memory selloff = entry point": DC memory prices +≥25% like-for-like 2Q→3Q; memory revenues "now over $200bn this quarter vs $46bn" a year ago; HBM4 complexity to absorb capacity with content doubling into Rubin Ultra; NAND capex "unbelievably benign" (Morgan Stanley · Joseph Moore, 2026-07-20).** Moore reads the US memory selloff as an entry point, marking data-center memory prices **"up at least 25% on a like-for-like basis from 2q to 3q,"** and citing SIA that **DRAM pricing was +70% q/q in 1q and over +40% in 2q**, with **memory revenues "now over $200bn this quarter vs $46bn this time last year."** Nuances: **NVDA cut its main LPDDR5 rack content (de-speccing)** (corroborates the "NVDA halved LPDDR5/rack" thread already on this page), **"HBM4 complexity will absorb capacity, content doubling next year with Rubin Ultra,"** and **NAND capex is "unbelievably benign"** (supply-discipline positive). Ratings: [../MU.md](../MU.md) OW PT $1,200; [../SNDK.md](../SNDK.md) OW PT $1,750. (Morgan Stanley · Joseph Moore "US memory selloff = entry point", 2026-07-20) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/MS_on_Memory.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-20) — Bernstein/Rasgon WFE-per-GW build: each incremental GW ≈ 46-50K WSPM split DRAM 53% / NAND 20% / HBM 16% / logic 11%; [../AMAT.md](../AMAT.md) the highest DRAM/HBM exposure (Bernstein · Stacy Rasgon, 2026-07-20).** Bernstein sizes the wafer-fab-equipment pull per gigawatt of AI compute: **each incremental GW ≈ 46-50K wafer-starts-per-month, split DRAM 53% / NAND 20% / HBM 16% / logic 11%** — memory (DRAM+HBM+NAND) ~89% of the incremental wafer starts. Equipment cost **per 10K WSPM = $3.4B logic, $1.4B HBM/DRAM, $1.3B NAND**, and **[../AMAT.md](../AMAT.md) has the highest DRAM/HBM equipment exposure** among the WFE names. Quantifies the memory-dominant wafer/equipment intensity behind the super-cycle. (Bernstein · Stacy Rasgon "WFE / AI GW note", 2026-07-20; cross-ref [semicap-wfe.md](semicap-wfe.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bernstein_on_WFE.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-20 / 2026-07-15) — MS/Lee Simpson: DRAM investment "strengthened materially," improving litho intensity "especially in DRAM"; Korean/Micron capex hikes largely already in ASML's backlog (Morgan Stanley · Lee Simpson, ASML note 2026-07-20 + "Didier ASML post-2Q" call 2026-07-15).** MS's ASML work reads the DRAM-capex leg for the litho supply governor: **DRAM investment has "strengthened materially," improving litho intensity "especially in DRAM,"** and the **SK Hynix / Samsung / Micron capex hikes are described as largely already sitting in ASML's (undisclosed) backlog** — i.e. the memory-capacity race is already booked at the litho bottleneck. Corroborates the ASML-2Q26-roadshow "DRAM now ~50% of ASML demand / EUV layers keep growing" bullet on this page. (Morgan Stanley · Lee Simpson, 2026-07-20 / 2026-07-15; see [semicap-wfe.md](semicap-wfe.md), [../ASML.md](../ASML.md)) [note](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/ASML_20260720_0400.html) · [call](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_15_didier_asml_post2q.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-20) — BofA "Big Tech setups lightning round": DRAM price +60% in the last 3 months; HBM "not up that much" — the input-cost-inflation debate (BofA, 2026-07-20).** BofA's lightning-round setup note marks **DRAM price +60% in the last 3 months** but flags that **HBM is "not up that much"** — framing the input-cost-inflation debate (conventional DRAM ASP now inflating the HBM BOM faster than HBM's own price, a margin-mix wrinkle for the HBM makers). (BofA "Big Tech setups lightning round", 2026-07-20) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_20_bofa_big_tech_setups_lightning_round.html)
- **NEW (2026-06-25) — Cantor/AMAT "Master Class": the DRAM technology roadmap from the equipment side — 4F2 then 3D DRAM lift AMAT's DRAM SAM +10% then +30%; $10B incremental over 5 years; CMOS-Bonded Array (hybrid bonding) a core inflection (Cantor · C.J. Muse "AMAT Master Class: DRAM & Advanced Packaging", 2026-06-25).** Applied's DRAM Master Class laid out **5 emerging DRAM inflections**: (1) more EUV with 6F2 (more etch), (2) advanced wiring/transistors (new Epi steps, more copper interconnect favoring PVD/CMP, FinFET peripheral logic), (3) transition to **CMOS-Bonded Array (memory + logic built separately + hybrid bonding)**, (4) transition to **4F2** (vertical cell transistors), and (5) eventually **3D DRAM (+30% to AMAT's SAM, led by etch/deposition)** — with AMAT's SAM up **~10% moving 6F2→4F2** and a **$10B incremental growth opportunity over 5 years ($6B periphery-transistor scaling + $4B advanced capacitors)**. Equipment-supplier confirmation of the leading-edge DRAM roadmap (hybrid-bonded CMOS-array → 4F2 → 3D DRAM) that underwrites bit-supply growth and rising litho/etch intensity in memory — corroborates the DRAM-node-migration leg of this page's supply thesis (the WFE/AMAT-rating detail on [semicap-wfe.md](semicap-wfe.md); the HBM-AP leg on [cowos-packaging.md](cowos-packaging.md)). (Cantor · C.J. Muse, AMAT OW/PT $650, 2026-06-25; see [../AMAT.md](../AMAT.md), [../MU.md](../MU.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_25_FW_CantorAMAT_Todays_Master_Class_Focus_on_DRAM_and_Advanced_Packaging_email.html)
- **NEW (2026-06-23) — Google Principal Architect (expert call): HBM is a "huge cost driver" and "tight by design" on the triopoly; each new GPU generation demands more (Google · Arthur Khessin, Office of the CTO, expert call, 2026-06-23).** In a hyperscaler-perspective memory-cycle call, Google's Arthur Khessin called **HBM "a huge cost driver within the compute stack" due to the triopoly (SK Hynix / Samsung / Micron)** and one of the few components that stays **tight "by design" as each new GPU generation continuously demands more** — one of his top physical bottlenecks (alongside power/grid gear, skilled labor, and EML lasers) for AI build-out through 2027-28. Component-cost inflation per MW is rising (HBM + mandatory liquid cooling the biggest drivers) inside a **$30-40B / 1GW campus of which >50% (~$20B) is compute**. A clean hyperscaler-side confirmation of sustained HBM pricing power / tightness feeding the memory makers. (Google · Arthur Khessin expert call, 2026-06-23; see [../SKHYNIX.md](../SKHYNIX.md), [../SAMSUNG.md](../SAMSUNG.md), [../MU.md](../MU.md); DC-capex/power leg on [ai-datacenter-power.md](ai-datacenter-power.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_23_Call_Google_-_Principal_Architect_Office_of_the_CTO_Arthur_Khessin_Memory_Cycle_2306_email.html)
- **NEW (2026-06-17, backfilled) — Bernstein "The CPU Renaissance? $223bn TAM": the HBM markup inside NVDA/ASIC packages (60s-70s% GM) is at risk if hyperscalers source HBM direct — the report's main model risk (Bernstein, 2026-06-17).** Bernstein flags that HBM carries a **60s-70s% gross-margin markup inside NVDA/ASIC packages**, and that **hyperscalers may increasingly source HBM directly (Alchip/MediaTek already offer this)**, which would **shrink the accelerator value base** — the note's main model risk. A bear nuance on where HBM value accrues (memory makers vs the accelerator designers' markup); the server-CPU-TAM leg is on [custom-asic-tpu.md](custom-asic-tpu.md). (Bernstein "The CPU Renaissance? $223bn TAM", 2026-06-17) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bernstein_on_CPU.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-20) — architectural substitution risk rises with HBM price:** QCOM’s HBC proposal places an accelerator directly beneath stacked LPDDR via TSVs and claims **6× bandwidth/W vs HBM** plus **4–8× better decode performance/W and TCO**; Citrini cautions the **~133 TB/s** figure is aggregate effective bandwidth in memory-bound operations, not sustained application bandwidth. Near-term HBM pricing remains bullish for suppliers, but higher prices strengthen the incentive to design systems that require **fewer HBM bytes/token**. Roadmap: AI200 LPDDR in 2026, AI250 HBC Gen1 in 2027, AI300 HBC Gen2 in 2028 (Citrini Semis, 2026-07-20; see [../QCOM.md](../QCOM.md)).
- **NEW (2026-07-19) — Kimi K3 "DeepSeek moment" drove a semis/memory selloff on the "efficient open models = less memory" fear — but the read is net-POSITIVE for DRAM/NAND (FundaAI "Deep|LLM", 2026-07-19; UBS · Tim Arcuri / Robert Ruple, 2026-07-18; Global SEMIS Daily · Beavington, 2026-07-19).** Moonshot's open-source **Kimi K3** (first Chinese model competitive at the frontier in coding) triggered a sharp AI-value-chain unwind this week (Semis/SOX **−9.9% 5D**, SK Hynix ordinary **−18.7% 5D** on a post-ADR-listing technical breakdown, Tech/S5INFT −3.78%). The bear framing: cheaper, more-efficient open models "meaningfully reduce memory and networking demand." **The fundamental read cuts the other way.** FundaAI's teardown: K3 compressed its KV cache only **~75% (vs DeepSeek V4's ~90%)** and is a **very large 2.8T-parameter MoE** (16 of 896 experts/token, KDA + Gated MLA + MXFP4 weights) that Moonshot recommends deploying on a **≥64-accelerator high-bandwidth supernode** — i.e. **heavier model weights + wider Expert Parallelism = still plenty of HBM, scale-up networking and GPUs**; K3's low token efficiency also makes it **more expensive per unit of intelligence than GPT-5.6 Sol** (Beavington: ~75% more expensive per unit of equivalent intelligence). NAND-based KV-cache offload (the DeepSeek-V4-inflection thesis, late-Apr) is now near-universal (Anthropic/OpenAI/Kimi), a NAND-demand tailwind. **UBS/Arcuri's conclusion (Private Software & AI conf): token-/value-maxing is NOT a headwind for semis** — strong AI demand + open-source adoption is ultimately positive (Jevons), and the correction "points more towards a technical/systematic de-risking event driven by positioning" than a fundamentals deterioration (see [tokenmaxxing.md](tokenmaxxing.md), [../MU.md](../MU.md), [../SKHYNIX.md](../SKHYNIX.md)).
- **NEW (2026-07-19) — Korea + US probe into memory-interface-IC price collusion (Renesas/Rambus/Montage) — Bernstein calls it "likely manageable" (Bernstein · David Dai, 2026-07-19).** A South Korea investigation (reported 07-15), following a separate earlier **US DOJ** probe (Rambus disclosed a grand-jury subpoena), is examining whether the three memory-interface vendors **coordinated pricing of DDR5 registered clock drivers (RCDs) and data buffers**. Bernstein's read: **memory-interface GM is not unusually high**, so the antitrust risk is likely manageable (Renesas 6723.JP Outperform, PT ¥6,300). Relevant to the DRAM-module/DDR5 supply chain this page tracks (RCD/data-buffer content rises with DDR5/SOCAMM attach) and a modest overhang for the interface-IC names, not the DRAM makers themselves. (Bernstein · David Dai, 2026-07-19)
- **NEW (2026-07-17) — Expert sizes the wafer bill: ~350,000 300mm wafer starts per GW of AI compute, of which HBM is 60-70% — but no HBM-style price spike in base wafers (AlphaSense expert call — ex-Broadcom/Intel/Huawei/TI CTO, 2026-07-17).** A 300mm-wafer-supply expert (25+ yrs, now CTO of a mid-size fabless) frames the memory pull on the raw-wafer chain: **AI compute drives ~350,000 300mm wafer starts per GW, with HBM memory accounting for 60-70% of that wafer demand** — i.e. the memory content, not logic, is the dominant wafer consumer per GW. He sees continued **300mm-wafer tightness** on AI demand but **not a dramatic HBM-like price spike**, because base-wafer suppliers stay disciplined (3-5yr LTAs, capacity added only against multi-year commitments; a new 300mm fab takes 3-5yrs and $2.8-3.9bn). He ranks **HBM + advanced packaging as the primary bottlenecks for AI compute scaling, with wafer supply a secondary constraint**. Quantifies why HBM's ~3x wafer displacement (this page's core mechanic) shows up all the way upstream in silicon-wafer demand. (AlphaSense expert, 2026-07-17; see [../MU.md](../MU.md), [../SKHYNIX.md](../SKHYNIX.md), [semicap-wfe.md](semicap-wfe.md))
- **NEW (2026-07-17) — ASML pushes back on the "DRAM litho intensity declines" myth: 6F²→4F² is slower than anyone expected, EUV layer count keeps GROWING, and DRAM is now ~50% of ASML demand (ASML mgmt "2Q26 London Roadshow", 2026-07-17).** ASML directly rebuts the bear view that DRAM litho intensity falls as the roadmap advances: the **6F²→4F² transition is slower than anyone anticipated** (customers still running 6F², with more advanced litho/EUV letting them extend 6F²), and through and after the 4F² transition ASML expects the **number of EUV layers to keep growing**, with advanced-immersion layers rising too. **3D DRAM keeps slipping** — "on the horizon" for ~10 years now: was 2025, then 2030, now **2035–2037** — so customers will try to stretch 4F² across 3–5 nodes (ASML sees a possible future of DRAM in 3D integration). Demand/pricing: DRAM/HBM pricing is high → a clear need for more capacity, with DRAM customers driving capacity as fast as possible (fab projects pulled in, mega-fabs added, some requests to expedite ASML equipment). **DRAM is now ~50% of ASML demand (was ~30%)**, and EUV return-intensity for DRAM continues to improve. Reinforces the EUV-scarcity supply governor on DRAM bit growth. (see [../SKHYNIX.md](../SKHYNIX.md), [../SAMSUNG.md](../SAMSUNG.md), [semicap-wfe.md](semicap-wfe.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_17_asml_2q26_london_roadshow_virtual_group_meeting.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-14) — Mirae: memory backdrop "exceptionally tight"; DRAM DDR5/DDR4 spot up 40+ straight days; big-tech cloud backlog $2.1tr (Mirae Asset · Young-gun Kim "SK Hynix — Take a broader view", 2026-07-14).** In its SK Hynix note Mirae argues industry supply/demand stays exceptionally tight: **16Gb DDR5 & DDR4 spot prices have risen 40+ consecutive trading days, nearing prior highs** even with the CXMT-listing overhang; the demand pool keeps building — combined **big-tech + neocloud order backlog $2.1tr (+24% QoQ)**, the 2-yr-recognition portion **$656bn (+38% QoQ)** (Google backlog +93% / Amazon +49% QoQ in 1Q26) — "makes higher [hyperscaler] capex all but inevitable." For the memory makers: SK Hynix **LTAs cover ~50% of revenue** and 2027 OP guided to **W389tr (+46% YoY)** as **HBM drives overall pricing higher** and LTA/HBM-committed capacity crowds out conventional DRAM. Corroborates the multi-year-shortage / pricing-durability thesis. (Mirae Asset · Young-gun Kim, 2026-07-14; see [../SKHYNIX.md](../SKHYNIX.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260714_SK_Hynix_000660_KS_Buy.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-20) — NAND demand melt-up: NVDA Rubin "CMX" carries 576 SSDs / 9,600 TB; NAND demand for CMX ~35M TB (2026) → >100M TB (2027); Samsung-NVDA expand into NAND as V10 ships (@jukan05, 2026-07-20).** Two NAND-specific reads from @jukan05: (1) **Nvidia's Rubin CMX** module is equipped with **576 SSDs = 9,600 TB total storage**, with industry estimates of NAND demand for CMX **surging from ~35M TB this year to >100M TB next year** — a direct sizing of the AI-server NAND-content melt-up (read-through [../SNDK.md](../SNDK.md), [../KIOXIA.md](../KIOXIA.md), [../WDC.md](../WDC.md), [../STX.md](../STX.md)); (2) **Samsung is expanding its Nvidia alliance into NAND flash supply** (building on existing DRAM/HBM/foundry ties) as **V10 (10th-gen V-NAND) shipments begin** — leveraging Samsung's NAND scale (see [../SAMSUNG.md](../SAMSUNG.md)). (@jukan05, 2026-07-20)
- **NEW (2026-07-20) — Memory Big-3 scrap in-house CXL controller development (@jukan05, 2026-07-20).** The three largest memory makers (Samsung / SK Hynix / Micron) have reportedly **all halted in-house development of CXL controller chips** — a technology they had been investing in for CXL memory-expansion modules. Read: not necessarily bearish CXL itself (controller supply likely shifts to merchant vendors), but a notable strategic retreat given the Big-3's DRAM-capacity is fully absorbed by HBM/DDR5 in the current shortage. (@jukan05, 2026-07-20; see [cxl-memory-fabric.md](cxl-memory-fabric.md))
- **NEW (2026-07-19/20) — Meritz/channel checks: 3Q DRAM ASP +21% QoQ (vs TrendForce 13-18%); server DRAM (64GB) spot prices rising sharply since mid-July; Middle-East sovereign AI buyers begin long-term memory procurement talks with Korean makers (@jukan05 citing Meritz, 2026-07-19/20).** Fresh corroboration of the DRAM-tightness cadence: Meritz's channel check puts **3Q conventional DRAM ASP +21% QoQ** — more optimistic than TrendForce's 13-18% (8-13% incl. HBM) and in line with BofA's raised +21% mark. **Server DRAM (64GB) spot prices have risen sharply since mid-July** (spot re-opening a gap above contract). And a fresh demand vector: **Middle-Eastern sovereign AI investors (incl. Saudi Arabia) have begun discussing mid-to-long-term memory-procurement plans with Korean memory makers** — a new sovereign-demand leg on top of hyperscaler LTAs. (@jukan05 citing Meritz Securities, 2026-07-19/20; see [../MU.md](../MU.md), [../SAMSUNG.md](../SAMSUNG.md), [../SKHYNIX.md](../SKHYNIX.md))
- **NEW (2026-07-20) — Kimi-K3 "DeepSeek-moment" memory-stock selloff, but read as memory-intensive → memory "first to find its feet" (Rothschild/Redburn, 2026-07-20; tape via KOSPI).** The Moonshot Kimi K3 launch (China frontier model) triggered a fresh AI-capex-durability scare across memory: **KOSPI −4.7% overnight (07-20), SK Hynix/Samsung off similarly, Kioxia −10% in Japan.** But Rothschild/Redburn's read is that **"memory will be the first to find its feet"** because **Kimi K3 is extremely memory-intensive, if compute-efficient** — i.e. cheaper/efficient models still need large memory footprints (KV-cache/context), a Jevons-style positive for the DRAM/NAND complex even as GPU-demand durability is questioned. Sits against the broader semis wobble (SOX −20%+ off highs, MS MOMO −40%). (Rothschild/Redburn · Rowan Adley, 2026-07-20; the model/token angle on [tokenmaxxing.md](tokenmaxxing.md))
- **NEW (2026-07-14) — MS/Moore NVDA-NDR recap: shortage "persistent for several years," but NVDA trades "peak cycle for duration" — a mild dampener on peak memory EPS (Morgan Stanley · Joe Moore "NVDA NDR recap", 2026-07-14).** In the Q&A recap of NVIDIA's NDR (the same event behind the 07-10 MS "NVDA NDR highlights" note already in Sources), Jensen framed the memory shortage as **multi-year / "persistent for several years"** but said NVIDIA won't be bottlenecked — if token growth is 10× and memory grows ~20%, NVIDIA gets more efficient in its memory use (compute/networking/storage offsets), so DRAM won't stop it making its numbers. Moore's read for the memory complex: this **trims the peak earnings power** the Street modelled (less "sense of crisis than three months ago") but makes the up-cycle feel **more durable — "trading peak cycle for duration."** Also: HBM4 price renegotiation ~a year out (post purchase-agreement roll-off) is the key memory-margin swing; Jensen said **"your Micron stock will be fine"**; MS models NVDA GM to **73% next year** (from mid-70s) partly on the 4× memory markup. A duration-bullish / peak-bearish nuance on the memory-EPS debate. (Morgan Stanley · Joe Moore, 2026-07-14; see [../MU.md](../MU.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_14_joe_moore_14_jul_26.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-13) — @zephyr_z9 ground-up 2030 DRAM S&D model: wafers 2.0M→4.8M wpm still leaves a ~25% general-DRAM deficit in 2030; HBM market $1.5-1.8T (@zephyr_z9, 2026-07-13).** Built as an answer to Chairman Chey's "customers want 4-6× supply, wafers only ~2× by 2030" (Bloomberg TV, 06-10, below). **Supply:** Big-4 end-26 ≈2.04M wpm (Samsung 720k / SK 590k / MU 375k / CXMT 350k — Big-3 within UBS/TF ranges; CXMT wafer share ~17% rhymes with the SK roadshow's "CXMT ~20% of wafers, 6-7% of bits") → **4.8M wpm 2030 base / 5.68M China-unconstrained** (Samsung 1.40-1.45M · SK 1.18M = 2× · MU 775-780k · CXMT 950k-1.45M · YMTC 200k · Swaysure+JHICC 260k). **HBM wafer penalty ~2.7× (HBM3E) → 4× (HBM4E), worse for HBM5** (die 35-45% larger + TSV/stacking net yield; bus 2048→4096-bit) — the *direction* is opposite the house engine (3.5×→3.1× on density gains): if he's right, the engine's 2028 "mechanical turn" (HBM wafers flattening ~695k → adds go to DDR) is optimistic, and ~70% of Big-3 wafers serve HBM by 2030. **Demand 157.5 EB (=1,260 bn Gb) in 2030:** agentic CPUs 75 EB (30M CPUs at 1:1 CPU:GPU × 2.5 TB DDR6 — 3.7× the house 28E GB/CPU of 683 GB; 48% of total demand and the weakest-sourced line), general cloud 25 EB (25M svr × 1 TB), HBM 37.5 EB (15M × 1TB HBM4e + 15M × 1.5TB HBM5 — units *conservative* vs UBS's 27.6M accels already in 27E; content 2-3× BofA's 464 GB/accel CY30), consumer 20 EB (starved, "loses the allocation fight" — the demand-destruction thesis extended). Sits +21% above ASML's own 2030 forecast (130 EB, 26% CAGR). **Result: 91 EB supply vs 120 EB ex-HBM demand = ~−25% deficit in 2030**; his China-max case (+860k wpm of D1b) closes it to ≈−1 EB but ~7-10 EB of Chinese HBM demand absorbs the increment. **Pricing:** DRAM ASP $1.5-2.0/Gb in 2030 (brackets house 28E $1.62/Gb), HBM $5-6/Gb (= TrendForce/Avril's "needed by '27" level arriving 3 years later); **DRAM TAM $2.6-3.3T with HBM alone $1.5-1.8T = ~10× BofA's $168bn CY30** (bits 2.7× × price 3.5×). Read: the anti-Bernstein — 28E ≈0 becomes a pause, not the top; implied WFE (~700k wpm adds/yr 27-30) ≈2.3× the house 27→28 pace. Verify next: the 75 EB agentic-CPU line and the HBM4E/5 penalty direction (die-size specs are checkable). His supply math is *generous* (clean node densities D1a-d 0.32-0.70 Gb/mm² at 70-95% yields ≈21-30k Gb/wafer vs ~16k engine blended) — the deficit is demand-driven. (@zephyr_z9, 2026-07-13; China fab granularity → [china-export.md](china-export.md))
- **NEW (2026-07-12) — BofA "super-cycle reconfirmed" (memory indicator record 183); Nanya print says DDR4 tighter than DDR5 and legacy DRAM more profitable than HBM; CXMT low-impact (BofA · Simon Woo; MS · Charlie Chan; JPM · Jay Kwon, 2026-07-12).** BofA's memory indicator (spot/billings/Korea-exports composite) hit a **record 183 in May** (vs 2017/2024 peaks 120-130), DRAM & NAND global billings **+300%+ YoY**, and BofA **raised its 3Q DRAM ASP assumption to +21% QoQ (from +17%)** — many new quarterly contracts already show 20%+ hikes. Nanya's 2Q print (rev +684% YoY, GM 79.5%, ASP +60%+ QoQ) drove **three-broker upgrades to price targets** (MS OW NT$580, JPM OW NT$710, BofA Buy NT$660) and three net-new reads for the DRAM complex: the **upcycle "may extend to 2028,"** **DDR4/legacy pricing momentum is stronger than DDR5** (severe DDR3/4 shortage), and — strikingly — **legacy DRAM is more profitable than HBM** (BofA). On the bear side, **CXMT's IPO share-subscription is set 07-16** (2Q guide ~US$9.5bn = high-single-digit % of global DRAM share despite ~mid-teen % of capacity), but BofA sees **low CXMT impact** — high-end DRAM (10Ghz+ LPDDR5, HBM, SOCAMM, GDDR7) stays mostly ex-China on quality + US controls. Rebuts the Samsung-correction "downturn" fear directly. (see [MU.md](../MU.md), [SAMSUNG.md](../SAMSUNG.md), [SKHYNIX.md](../SKHYNIX.md))
- **NEW (2026-07-12) — JPM server-CPU supercycle pulls DIMM/SOCAMM: NVDA Vera + Google Axion + agentic-AI servers tighten general-server memory; SK Hynix/Samsung named beneficiaries (JPMorgan "JPM proprietary server CPU shipment model", Hung/Hariharan, 2026-07-12).** JPM's new server-CPU model — total server-CPU shipments **26mn units (2025) → 68mn (2028), 38% CAGR**, agentic-AI CPUs at **155% CAGR** as the breakout vector — has a direct memory read-through: the super server-CPU cycle (more CPUs, higher core counts, rising attach) **drives DIMM/SOCAMM demand, with DRAM vendors SK Hynix and Samsung Electronics named beneficiaries**. The NVDA Vera CPU / Google Axion ramp + agentic orchestration servers are the pull, corroborating the "AI-CPU demand tightening LPDDR/SOCAMM" thread already on this page (JPM's Nanya OW upgrade thesis). Nomura's read: LTA pricing for semi wafers sold to memory customers **could potentially double** — semi wafers are only **<1% of the memory wafer price today vs 3-5% two years ago** — i.e. semi-inflation / upstream cost pressure now reaching the raw-wafer layer for memory makers, a sign the upcycle is underway. (Nomura · Donnie Teng, 2026-07-12; event 2026-07-09)
- **NEW (2026-07-10) — Nanya Jun-26: rev +68% q/q / +684% y/y to $2.61B (ATH), GM 79.5%, ASP +>60% q/q / +>500% y/y, bit shipments flat — legacy-DRAM ASP-driven tightness (@SKundojjala, 2026-07-10).** Nanya's June-quarter revenue hit an **all-time-high $2.61B (+68% q/q, +684% y/y)** at **79.5% GM**, on **ASP +>60% q/q / +>500% y/y with bit shipments flat** — pure price, no volume, a clean read that the tightness has spread into **legacy DRAM** (Nanya is DDR4/legacy-heavy). - **NEW (2026-07-10) — MS/NVDA NDR: memory shortage multi-year & supply "basically static" vs 10x/yr token growth; NVDA halved LPDDR5 content/rack; Groq SRAM as the offset (Morgan Stanley "NVDA NDR highlights", Joseph Moore, 2026-07-10).** From NVIDIA's NDR, the memory shortage "looks to last for several years," and with the industry requiring ~**10x token growth each year while memory supply is "basically static,"** something has to give — NVIDIA **cut the LPDDR5 content per rack in half** to meet rack demand (corroborating MS/Moore's 06-22 read below), and management flagged the importance of **Groq technology, which uses SRAM that "used to be much more expensive than DRAM,"** as an offset. Compute/networking/memory are inter-related — you can use two to optimize a shortage of the third. MS notes this commentary "could dampen enthusiasm for memory stocks," while stressing the offsets are only happening because of a multi-year shortage (see [../NVDA.md](../NVDA.md)). - **NEW (2026-06-10 interview; ingested 2026-07-10) — SK Chairman Chey: capacity to double in 5yr (customers want 4-6x); supply tight until AI "settles"; HBM4e 12-layer; "memory-as-a-service" (Bloomberg TV — Chairman Chey Tae-won, 2026-06-10).** In a Bloomberg TV interview around the SK Hynix Nasdaq ADR listing, SK Group Chairman Chey Tae-won said SK Hynix will **double total wafer capacity within 5 years** — "typically that's unrealistic and probably over-supply... but my customer said that's not enough. We need more... they want us to quadruple, five times, six times." He expects supply tightness to persist **until "human society has some settlement with the AI,"** driven by KV-cache/agentic memory demand (a user running "tens or hundreds" of AI agents, each generating KV-cache that must be stored). HBM4e shown at **12 layers**. He floats **"memory-as-a-service"** — delivering tailored, fixed memory-systems solutions per customer/application rather than just selling chips — as a future model (the token-cost/dollar-per-token angle is on [tokenmaxxing.md](tokenmaxxing.md)). (see [../SKHYNIX.md](../SKHYNIX.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_10_ceo_hynix_interview_10jun26.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-09) — Bernstein quantifies LTA effectiveness: the ~$3.33bn deposit secured industry-wide is <1% (~0.6%) of the multi-year revenue LTAs must protect; a "secure" 20% deposit implies ~$1tn upfront (nobody has it) (Bernstein "Memory LTA effectiveness", 2026-07-09).** Bernstein's top-down check on how protective the new memory LTAs really are: against the multi-year (3-5yr, ~4yr avg) revenue the LTAs are meant to protect, only **~$3.33bn of deposits are secured industry-wide — <1% (~0.6%) of the protected value** (more may come from Samsung/SK Hynix, but "how much more to make it meaningful?"); a genuinely secure **20% deposit would require ~$1tn paid upfront today**, which no one has on hand. The **Micron–Anthropic deal** is a supply guarantee in exchange for an equity stake (**vendor financing**) — it "did not reduce the risk for investors." LTA protection is also **time-dependent**: as remaining performance obligations fall, the customer walk-away barrier drops, so a low-enough market price lets customers exit. On coverage ceiling: SanDisk's CEO says customer-customers (PC/smartphone) are transactional and won't sign LTAs, and China prefers local suppliers (whose share is rising) — so the **LTA coverage ceiling is only ~50-67%**, with one-third to one-half of the market always uncovered. A skeptical, quantitative counterpoint to the "LTAs have real teeth" bull case in the Bernstein "New Memory LTAs part 2" bullet directly below (see [../MU.md](../MU.md), [../SNDK.md](../SNDK.md)). [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_09_bernstein_on_lta_s.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-06) — SK Hynix ADS roadshow: LTAs shift memory from cyclical to contracted; >50% of 2028-30 capacity expansion under LTA; total wafer capacity ~2x by 2030 but demand bigger; margin-focused (not floor-price like Micron) (SK Hynix ADS roadshow, 2026-07-06).** On the pre-listing roadshow, SK Hynix framed the structural change: the new **3-5yr LTAs (vs old 1-yr "gentleman's agreements" that were unenforceable) carry penalty provisions** when either party breaks, giving mid-term demand visibility that de-risks capex and lowers business volatility → supports a higher P/E. **Unlike Micron's floor-price emphasis, SK Hynix says its focus is margin, not the pricing arrangement**, and prioritizes volume over price in LTAs (past turbulence came from demand/supply mismatch + long-forecast error). **"Way over 50%" of 2028-30 capacity expansion will be LTA-related** (major customers = >50% of revenue); LTA signing expected before year-end. **Total wafer capacity to ~double by 2030, but customer demand for 2030 is "a lot bigger" than potential supply.** HBM's **annual-contract structure yields more stable margin** than DRAM's quarterly/monthly pricing, so a higher HBM mix lowers volatility. **ASIC share of HBM is rising but "the majority still goes into GPU."** On China: impact "fairly small/minimal" — **CXMT has ~20% of DRAM wafer capacity but only ~6-7% of bits** (very low yield → poor quality; fine for low-end consumer, not AI infra), with Chinese expansion gated by inability to source Western advanced tools (YMTC precedent). (see [../SKHYNIX.md](../SKHYNIX.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_06_sk_hynix_roadshow.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-09) — Bernstein "New Memory LTAs part 2": case studies show the LTAs have "real teeth" — three structural differences vs Microchip PSP / Hemlock; "meaningful but not infinite" protection (Bernstein · Mark Newman, 2026-07-09).** Newman's part-2 note (delivering the deep-dive teased in the 07-08 tracker bullet below) benchmarks the New Memory LTAs (SNDK/MU) against two *failed* legacy structures — **Microchip's Preferred Supply Program** (12-mo rolling NCNR backlog, no cash commitment, relaxed to 6mo Aug-2023 then discontinued Feb-2024; collapsed into a COVID-era analog glut, MCHP net sales −42% Q3 FY25 vs Q3 FY24) and **Hemlock's polysilicon take-or-pay** (legally bulletproof but commercially uncollectible — a ~$793M SolarWorld judgment left largely unpaid against an insolvent buyer). Three structural differences make the new contracts durable: (1) **upfront, back-end-weighted financial commitment** (cash deposits / letters of credit / third-party guarantees, coverage ratio of remaining collateral to remaining obligations rising toward **75-100% in the outer years**, precisely when downturn risk is highest); (2) **better counterparty quality** (investment-grade, revenue-diversified hyperscalers vs thinly-capitalized/diffuse solar and analog customer bases); (3) **structural AI demand** (high-end memory sold out through 2026 into 2027) vs Microchip's transient shortage-driven double-ordering. Contract-collateral sizing: **SanDisk backs an estimated ~$69B of remaining performance obligations with >$11B of guarantees** (mostly on escrow-like third-party balance sheets), while **Micron backs its obligations with $18B of cash deposits on its own balance sheet + $4B of letters of credit** (consistent with the Jefferies $22bn total-commitments figure already on this page). Verdict: the LTAs provide "**meaningful but not infinite**" downside protection — significantly more than the legacy cases analyzed, but not a floor at any price. SNDK PT reiterated $3,000 (Bernstein · Mark Newman, "New Memory LTAs part 2", 2026-07-09; see [../SNDK.md](../SNDK.md), [../MCHP.md](../MCHP.md)).
- **NEW (2026-07-09) — WSTS May: memory +321% YoY; memory to >50% of CSP hardware capex; memory TAM to $1.3trn 2027 (JPM · Gokul Hariharan, "Asian Semis / May WSTS", 2026-07-09).** Overall semis +118% YoY in May (highest since 1994); **memory +321% YoY, memory ASP +211% YoY**. JPM forecasts **memory price growth +220–250% YoY for 2026E**, relatively stable 2027 on rising LTA mix; **memory's share of total CSP hardware capex to exceed 50% this year, up from mid-teens** at the start of the AI cycle; **HBM ASP +10% YoY like-for-like / +30% blended in 2027**; **memory TAM expands to ~US$1.3trn in 2027, from ~US$200bn+/US$900bn+ in 2025/26** (JPM · Gokul Hariharan, 2026-07-09).

Deck also flags GPU-pricing durability (H100 AI-cloud +13%/+21% trailing 3m/6m; A100 +7%/+5%) as evidence installed-base economics hold (NVIDIA "NDR deck", 2026-07; see [../NVDA.md](../NVDA.md)). A demand-side read on the DRAM-EUV-intensity thread this page tracks (Bernstein 1d High-NA, TrendForce EUV-second-constraint). ([MS, 2026-07-07](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/ASML_20260707_0400.html); see [semicap-wfe.md](semicap-wfe.md), [../ASML.md](../ASML.md))
- **NEW (2026-07-06) — Bernstein: DRAM to adopt High-NA EUV *first*, in 2027 (1d node); DRAM litho intensity ~20% (1z) → ~30% (1d) (David Dai "ASML: High-NA EUV cost deep dive", 2026-07-06).** Bernstein's High-NA cost deep-dive lands a memory-side conclusion: **DRAM will likely adopt High-NA EUV before logic — in 2027 at the 1d node** — because DRAM's smaller die uses only 1 mask (a single AA exposure, no stitching), making the High-NA cost far easier to justify than for reticle-size logic die (which need 2 masks / AB exposure at ~23% lower throughput). Best-guess sequence: **SK Hynix + Samsung DRAM in 2027 (1d)**, ahead of logic (Intel 2028 / A14, TSMC ~2030). The read-through for the memory super-cycle: **DRAM litho intensity rises from ~20% (1z/1Y) to ~30% (1d)** as EUV layer counts climb (3-4 exposures at 1a → 6-7 at 1c) and High-NA is inserted at 1d — i.e. the leading-edge DRAM node-migration that underwrites the bit-supply-growth ceiling is getting *more* litho-intensive, reinforcing the EUV-scarcity governor on DRAM supply that this page's bull case leans on (Bernstein "ASML: High-NA EUV cost deep dive", 2026-07-06). _(Full ASML/litho-side detail on [semicap-wfe.md](semicap-wfe.md).)_
