(2) **Samsung preliminary Q2 2026** came in ahead of consensus — sales **KRW 171T** (vs KRW 169.2T) / operating income **KRW 89.4T** (vs KRW 84.2T) (via Vital Knowledge, 2026-07-06; see [SAMSUNG.md](../SAMSUNG.md)). (3) **SK Hynix Nasdaq listing (ticker SKHY, ~Jul 10)** — a fresh capital raise on top of the KRX line (see [SKHYNIX.md](../SKHYNIX.md)). (4) **Jefferies (Jeff Kim, 2026-07-06) raised its SK Hynix TP to KRW 4.2m (~+92% upside)** on the AI memory up-cycle. Net: memory names remain resilient on improving DRAM pricing.
- **NEW (2026-07-03) — UBS raises 2H26 contract-pricing forecasts again; DRAM undersupplied to at least 2Q28, memory-industry revenue $992bn/$1,763bn 2026/27 (UBS · Nicolas Gaudois, "Memory Semis Monthly — July '26", 2026-07-03).** UBS lifts base-line **DRAM** contract pricing to **+32% QoQ in 3Q26 (from +17%)** and **+18% in 4Q26 (from +12%)**, after +67% in 2Q26 (realized ASP varies by vendor on LTA/HBM mix); **NAND** to **+30% 3Q26 (from +17%)** / +12% 4Q26, after +67% in 2Q. Structural call: DRAM **undersupplied until at least 2Q28** — 2027 bit demand +36.2% vs supply +19.3%, so the sufficiency ratio *deteriorates* to **-13.6%** (from -8.1% in 2026E), "unprecedented over the last 30 years"; NAND upcycle runs to at least 4Q27. Memory-industry revenue reaches **$992bn (2026) / $1,763bn (2027)**. HBM demand tweaked up to **33.1bn Gb 2026 (+90% YoY) / 58.7bn Gb 2027 (+77%)**, ≈8.5m/11.0m NVDA GPU-equivalent (consistent with UBS's raised CoWoS estimate). LTA nuance: **Micron's SCAs use a floor+ceiling**; **Korean makers fix ~60-70% of volumes over 5-yr contracts** (Samsung completing more revised DDR5 LTAs by 3Q, target 50-70% of volumes; SK Hynix focused on large hyperscalers for DDR5+NAND); **"Meta not an issue"** for HBM (recent press on Meta possibly selling compute externally read as monetization of older assets ahead of Blackwell/Rubin/MTIA rollout → no impact on HBM procurement). UBS stays positive on the group with **Samsung Key Call Buy (PT KRW550k)** and Buy on **SK Hynix (PT KRW3.20m, from KRW3.00m)**, **Micron (PT US$1,625)**, **Kioxia (PT ¥132,000)** and **Nanya Tech (PT NT$495)** — the memory industry set to generate ~US$1.2tn of FCF in 2027E, which UBS expects to drive a step-up in shareholder returns. Main risk = **hyperscaler affordability** (they must keep tapping capital markets to fund the capex — links to [hyperscaler-capex.md](hyperscaler-capex.md)). _(A raise vs UBS's own prior; runs hotter than even TrendForce's revised-up base case below.)_ Same day, **JPM upgraded Nanya to OW (PT NT$230→NT$710, FY26-28E EPS +48-118%)** on a "**all DRAM moves together during an upcycle**" thesis — DDR4 S-D now following DDR5, sufficiency ratios "extremely low," NVDA Vera CPU tightening LPDDR/SOCAMM — an independent read-through for the whole DRAM complex (JPM · Jay Kwon, 2026-07-03). Korean memory names **rebounded hard** the same session (KOSPI +5.7%, SK Hynix +10%/+18% off lows, Samsung +8.0%; Jefferies · Conor O'Mara), reversing the 07-01/02 Meta-Compute selloff. ⚠️ **Basis note (2026-07-28, Memory_supply_demand v4 rebuild audit):** the two UBS sufficiency figures in this bullet are on **different bases** — the **-8.1% (2026) is inventory-ADJUSTED** (a +29.2bn Gb restock sits inside it), while the **-13.6% (2027) is raw END-demand**; on UBS's own inventory-adjusted S/D row the 2027 figure improves to **-1.2%**, because a **-67.2bn Gb inventory DRAWDOWN** closes the gap (UBS Global DRAM model 260703_Client.xlsx, "DRAM Model" r34/r38). Cite one basis or the other — quoting -8.1% → -13.6% as a deterioration path mixes them.
- **NEW (2026-07-02) — MS prefers DRAM over NAND; HBM4E capacity squeeze + EUV cap are the DRAM-tightness levers; 20-30% DRAM hikes in 3Q26 (Morgan Stanley "NAND Industry Outlook: Diverging Trends", 2026-07-02).** MS (Duan Liu / Shawn Kim et al.) stays bullish on the memory cycle but **tactically prefers DRAM over NAND** on "**better LTA terms, higher demand visibility, supply discipline capped by EUV and a potential HBM4E capacity squeeze**." Concrete pricing: MS estimates **>20-30% DRAM price hikes in 3Q26** (enough to keep the YoY rate of change *accelerating*), with **3Q26 server-grade DRAM contract tracking +20% QoQ** and **3Q26 TLC eSSD +30% QoQ** (consumer-grade NAND up only slightly). **Samsung Electronics is the Asia Tech team's Top Pick in DRAM** (market leadership + shareholder returns; TP KRW381,000, OW) and **SK hynix OW** (TP KRW2,600,000); both are "key beneficiaries to AI compute and agentic AI." The bifurcation call: server demand stays strong on LTA downside protection, but **consumer NAND pricing may hit a ceiling** — actual order cuts appeared after 2Q26 price hikes as suppliers shift capacity to AI. Scenario test: significant NAND shortages persist into 2027; 2028 could see **-5% shortage if 60% YoY AI-NAND growth** with base-case greenfield expansion, or oversupply risk if both China restrictions *and* supply discipline ease. In NAND, MS prefers suppliers over module makers (Kioxia the Japan Top Pick, LTAs covering >50% of CY27 shipments; Macronix the GC Top Pick); OW Micron/SK hynix/SanDisk (Morgan Stanley "NAND Industry Outlook: Diverging Trends", 2026-07-02). _(Off-coverage NAND-name PT changes — Longsys, SIMO, Phison, Fadu — folded into [outros-asia.md](outros-asia.md).)_
- **NEW (2026-07-02) — ECTC 2026: HBM4E doubles I/O while raising speed; custom HBM (Marvell) moves interface logic off the accelerator (SemiAnalysis "EMIB-T Roadmap / ECTC 2026", 2026-07-02).** SemiAnalysis's ECTC roundup details the HBM-packaging squeeze driving the memory-intensity story: **HBM4E doubles the I/O pin count vs HBM3 while pushing data rates to 12 Gb/s+**, adding power rails (VDDQ/VDDQL) that steal routing area from signals — Samsung estimates HBM4E could need **2× the interposer layers vs HBM3E (5× vs HBM2)** and **power consumption +86% vs HBM3E**. Thermal is the emerging wall: at **16-hi HBM thermal resistance is still acceptable, but 20-hi/24-hi need new approaches** — Samsung's hybrid copper bonding (HCB) cuts stack-level thermal resistance **~19-29%** vs thermal-compression bonding. **Marvell's custom HBM** is the structural workaround: a custom base die (advanced logic process) **moves the memory controller/interface logic off the accelerator**, cutting host-ASIC HBM-PHY footprint **~60%**, shortening interposer channel length **6.5mm → 1.5mm**, and enabling **expansion interfaces** (fan-out to LPDDR or a second HBM layer) — relevant to Nvidia Feynman (announced custom HBM at GTC; ~16% of Rubin die area is HBM logic/PHYs today) and AMD MI450/MI500 LPDDR support. Samsung also showed a **TSV-free "Vertical Cu post Stack"** for mobile that cuts DRAM-stack power 41% and raises data rate 8.6→11.8 Gb/s, with read-through to future AI-accelerator HBM and server-CPU SOCAMM modules (SemiAnalysis "EMIB-T Roadmap / ECTC 2026", 2026-07-02). _(See [cowos-packaging.md](cowos-packaging.md) for the packaging-side detail; [custom-asic-tpu.md](custom-asic-tpu.md) for Marvell's base-die design-service angle.)_
- **BofA rebuts the Meta memory-cut fear + de-risks CXMT/Korea overhangs (BofA · Simon Woo/Dai Shen/Vivek Arya, Global Memory weekly, 2026-07-02).** Three takeaways after the 07-02 Meta-Compute memory selloff: (1) **Meta will NOT cut memory orders** — chipmakers and supply-chain contacts (NAND controller IC, substrate) say Meta's long-term memory/component orders are getting *stronger* (more HBM/LPDDR5/eSSD for its AI DCs); the Street's "Meta rents out overbuilt capacity → excess memory" speculation is "groundless" (Meta sharing DCs externally as a CSP ≠ excess capacity, it's business diversification using *more* advanced memory). (2) **CXMT's DRAM shipment to Apple likely low** on quality/patent issues — caps the China-oversupply bear. (3) **Korea's new memory cluster won't have a major impact** near-term (multi-decade build). Also: **Samsung's 2Q OP likely to miss slightly on a special employee bonus** (one-off cost, not demand). A direct fundamental counter to the bear leg driving the 07-02 memory tape (memory −11% intraday on 07-01, KOSPI −7.9% on 07-02). (BofA Global Memory Tech, 2026-07-02; see also [SAMSUNG.md](../SAMSUNG.md), [MU.md](../MU.md), [hyperscaler-capex.md](hyperscaler-capex.md))
- **Korea AI/semi investment plan revised sharply UP — $3.1T through 2040, doubling Korean DRAM capacity in 5 years (JPMorgan/Jay Kwon via Joshua Meyers, 2026-06-30).** Samsung Group and SK Group jointly announced **KRW4,755t (~US$3.1t)** in long-term capex through 2040 for semiconductors, AI datacenters, and robotics — a **MAJOR revision up (~2.3-3.5x) from the figure first reported just 24-48 hours earlier** (2026-06-29/30: Samsung ₩2,100tn/~$880B combined-plan estimate + SK Hynix ₩1,100tn; see also [SAMSUNG.md](../SAMSUNG.md) Changelog, which logs a ₩4,800tn/$3.1T FundaAI figure the same day — directionally consistent, headline KRW total differs slightly by source, both dwarf the prior day's number). The plan implies **doubling installed Korean DRAM capacity within 5 years**, pulling forward the Yongin fab ramp by **7-12 years**; memory executives cited say supply is only **~50% of demand and worsening**; JPM sees a **US$12-20t cumulative revenue opportunity** for Korean memory makers over the plan's life. Allocation: **60-70% of the plan to front-end wafer spend, 20-30% to infrastructure/cleanroom** (JPMorgan/Jay Kwon via Joshua Meyers, 2026-06-30). This is the single largest capacity-commitment datapoint on this page to date and pulls the "cleanroom-constrained through 2028" bottleneck framing (TrendForce/Avril, 2026-06-23, above) toward a materially earlier resolution *if* executed at pace — though the plan being multi-decade (to 2040) means near-term (2026-27) supply tightness is unaffected.
- **HBM4/HBM4E primer — the architectural breakpoint & the 3-supplier state (Atlas Peak Research "HBM4 and HBM4E: The Memory Wall Becomes the System Architecture Problem," 2026-04-03).** JEDEC finalized HBM4 in April 2025: HBM4 is the first generation to **double the interface to 2048-bit** (32 channels, 2 pseudo-channels/channel) rather than lean on pin-speed, and HBM4E extends the same 2048-bit base to 16 Gb/s. Value capture is moving **upstream/sideways into the logic base die, advanced packaging and substrate materials (ABF ~100% share)**, not only DRAM bits — HBM4/HBM4E are "memory-plus-logic-plus-packaging systems," so supply stays structurally tight because the bottleneck is a chain (TSV DRAM → base-die foundry → interposer/CoWoS → substrate → thermals/qual), not one fab step. Generational step:

  | Gen | Pin rate | BW/stack | Interface | Max cap/stack |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | HBM3E | 9.6 Gb/s | 1.229 TB/s | 1024-bit | 36 GB |
  | HBM4 (JEDEC base) | 8.0 Gb/s | 2.048 TB/s | 2048-bit | 64 GB |
  | HBM4E | 16.0 Gb/s | 4.096 TB/s | 2048-bit | 64 GB+ |

  3-supplier manufacturing state (as of 2026-04-03): **SK hynix** — 12-Hi HBM4 samples Mar-2025, dev complete Sep-2025, HBM4E ~2027, NVIDIA Vera Rubin (GTC-2026 custom cHBM "stream DQ"); **Samsung** — commercial HBM4 shipments early 2026, MP confirmed Mar-2026 (GTC) on a **4 nm in-house logic base die**, HBM4E ~2027, NVIDIA Vera Rubin + AMD MI455X, custom-HBM samples 2027; **Micron** — HBM4 samples Jun-2025, HVM for Vera Rubin Mar-2026 (36 GB 12-Hi), HBM4E ~2028, in-house CMOS base die + custom w/ TSMC, HBM customer base now 6. Base die: TSMC N12 (HBM4, for customers) / N3P (custom HBM4E); the base die is where HBM shifts from standardized memory toward customized near-memory subsystems (Micron: custom HBM4E carries higher GM than standard) (Atlas Peak Research, 2026-04-03; see [../MU.md](../MU.md), [../SKHYNIX.md](../SKHYNIX.md), [../SAMSUNG.md](../SAMSUNG.md)). [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/HBM4_and_HBM4E__The_Memory_Wall_Becomes_the_System_Architecture_Problem_Atlas_Peak_Research.html)
- **HBM sold out CY26; pricing into 2027-28.** Micron's entire CY26 HBM supply, HBM4 included, is committed on price and volume; HBM TAM ~$35B (2025) → ~$100B by 2028 (~40% CAGR), pulled in two years vs prior ([MU page](../MU.md), Q1 FY26, 2025-12-17). HBM blended ASP +~30% for next year (Samsung/Hynix) = ~10% like-for-like + mix from HBM4, but HBM4 ramp pushed to 2H so more of the blended ASP effect spills into 2027; HBM price negotiations only begin July after the June quarter (JPM/Jay, [memory-China call](../_equity_calls/Semis/2026-05-28_JPM_memory-China.md), 2026-05-28).
- **DRAM/NAND pricing still ripping.** 2Q26 DRAM ASP +50-60% q/q, NAND +75-100% q/q; 3Q26 DRAM +~20% q/q (memory ests, briefing 2026-05-29, [MU](../_briefings/by-ticker/MU.md)). Micron Q2 FY26 already printed DRAM pricing "mid-sixties %" q/q and NAND "high-seventies %", 75% gross margin, with guidance to ~81% GM next quarter ([MU page](../MU.md), 2026-03-18). JPM models full-year server-grade DRAM +~25%, with 3Q DRAM/NAND +~60% q/q and upside to 20% if LTAs aren't signed in 2Q (JPM/Jay, 2026-05-28).
- **TrendForce revises its (previously most-conservative) DRAM forecast *up* (TrendForce, 2026-06-30).** 3Q26 PC DRAM contract growth lifted to **+15-20% QoQ** (from the +8-13% Avril flagged 2026-06-23), 4Q26 to **+3-8%** (from +0-5%), and **3Q26 server DRAM +13-18% QoQ** — i.e. even the bearish desk now models pricing above its own late-June base case; TrendForce reiterates DRAM shortage / elevated hardware prices **through 2028** on **cleanroom-limited** wafer capacity as the binding bottleneck (TrendForce via @jukan05 / @trendforce, 2026-06-30). _(Supersedes the 06-23 TrendForce +8-13% Q3 / +3-8% Q4 base case cited below under 'DRAM price cadence.')_
- **LTA structure — customers proposed the 2028+ deals first; SK Hynix keeps a floor with no ceiling (KIS via @jukan05, 2026-06-29).** With commodity-DRAM operating margins already ~80% / gross margins >90%, prices can't rise endlessly on supply/demand alone — yet it was **customers, not suppliers, who first proposed 3-5yr LTAs covering 2028+**, knowing new-fab output only contributes from 2028 (a shortage-runs-long signal). Structural detail: **SK Hynix's LTA has a price floor with no ceiling, whereas Samsung/Micron structures differ** — the first time in memory history suppliers have locked a contract structure that sustains high margins over an extended period (KIS via @jukan05, 2026-06-29).
- **Jefferies/KB (Jeff Kim, 2026-07-01): DRAM +308% / NAND +256% in 2026, shortage 2+ years, memory's AI-capex share 14%→50% by 2027.** KB maintains **BUY on Samsung Electronics, TP KRW550,000**; memory shortage seen persisting **2+ years**; **DRAM prices forecast +308% in 2026, NAND +256% in 2026**, with further upside in 2027; memory's share of AI capex seen rising from **14% to 50% by 2027**; agentic AI **triples** memory content in data centers, physical AI boosts memory content **5x** (Jefferies/KB "Jeff Kim", 2026-07-01).
- **Micron: 16 SCAs signed (20% DRAM / one-third NAND volume), $22bn commitments, FY27 capex above mid-$40bn (Jefferies · Conor O'Mara, Micron IR call w/ Satya Kumar, 2026-06-30).** Micron has signed **16 Strategic Capacity Agreements (SCAs)**, covering **20% of DRAM and one-third of NAND volumes** (~25% of total company sales today, targeting up to **~50% eventually**); **$22bn total financial commitments** ($18bn cash deposits + $4bn letters of credit). **FY27 capex guided above mid-$40bn**, up sharply from **~$27bn FY26**. CY26 bit growth: **DRAM low-20s% (incl. HBM), NAND ~20%**; demand seen exceeding supply **through CY27-CY28** (Jefferies/Conor O'Mara, Micron IR call, 2026-06-30).
- **Estimate-raise wave hits the bottleneck sector — SNDK, STX, WDC (BofA/Wamsi Mohan, 2026-07-01).** BofA raises **SNDK FY2027 EPS +24% to $233.07**, **FY2028 EPS +14.5% to $254.24**, on June-quarter **bit growth +13% q/q, ASP growth +35% q/q**. Grouped in the same bottleneck-sector estimate-raise wave (HDD, not NAND, but pulled along by the same supply-tightness dynamic): **STX FY2027 EPS +9.5% to $26.88, FY2028 EPS +42% to $50.75**; **WDC FY2027 EPS +13.5% to $18.72, FY2028 EPS +48% to $32.89** (BofA/Wamsi Mohan, 2026-07-01).
- **Spot-price cross-check: DRAM up a 3rd straight month (+8x y/y), NAND down a 3rd straight month but still +5x y/y (JPM Data Center Watch/Joseph Cardoso et al, 2026-07-01).** DRAM spot prices rose for a **third straight month in June, now +8x y/y**; NAND spot prices fell for a **third consecutive month** (modest declines) after a strong 1Q26 run, but still **+5x y/y** (JPMorgan "Data Center Watch"/Joseph Cardoso et al, 2026-07-01). Corroborated by Jefferies Tech (Jeffrey Favuzza, citing Trendforce, 2026-07-01): this week's DRAM spot saw **upward momentum in LPDDR4/DDR3**, broader market sideways; NAND suppliers adjusting quotations to optimize inventory but **buyers resisting**; **512Gb TLC wafer spot prices continuing to decline** (Jefferies Tech/Jeffrey Favuzza citing Trendforce, 2026-07-01).
- **Apple-Micron pricing dispute + US price-fixing class action (JPMorgan/Joshua Meyers citing Jay Kwon, 2026-06-30).** The Apple-Micron pricing dispute is "crystallizing market anxiety" around unsustainable DRAM profit-sharing dynamics; a **US consumer class-action lawsuit** has been filed against the three major DRAM makers (Micron/Samsung/SK Hynix) alleging **price-fixing** — no government investigation yet, but flagged given the early-2000s DRAM cartel precedent (JPMorgan/Joshua Meyers citing Jay Kwon, 2026-06-30). Separately, Culpium (Tim Culpan, 2026-07-01) frames memory as having become **Apple's single-largest cost item** amid "unprecedented cost expansion" — his term, **"chipflation"** (Culpium/Tim Culpan, 2026-07-01).
- **Korea memory names sell off despite strong SOX/Nasdaq overnight — NPS rebalancing + export-data dip + unconfirmed "efficiency breakthrough" rumor (Jefferies Silk Road/Cristina Titu, 2026-07-01).** Samsung Electronics shares **-4.3% to -5.6%** and SK Hynix **-2% to -3.4%** in Korea trading, attributed to **NPS (National Pension Service) rebalancing**, a dip in **June DRAM export data**, and an **unconfirmed "memory efficiency breakthrough" rumor**; foreigners sold Korea equities for **nine straight sessions**. JPM's Andrew Tyler frames this as a **"hyperscaler curse"** — the Korea capex-beneficiary basket (ex-memory) is **up 5%** even as the memory names themselves sold off (Jefferies Silk Road/Cristina Titu, 2026-07-01; JPM/Andrew Tyler, 2026-07-01).
- **Capstone expert call — former Samsung EVP, now photonics-startup CEO (2026-06-30): 2023 downturn was the sharpest ever; LTAs "essentially unenforceable" is the skeptical counterpoint.** The 2023 memory downturn was the **"sharpest and most dramatic decline ever"** (pricing halved, revenue cut by half); HBM became the bottleneck post-2024 compute shortage, driving **10-15% undersupply** and "skyrocketing" prices/profitability. Skeptical counterpoint for the bear case above: this expert describes Long-Term Agreements (LTAs) as **"essentially unenforceable"** if market conditions turn — flagging that a customer like Nvidia could in principle use LTAs to induce oversupply (Capstone expert call, former Samsung EVP now photonics-startup CEO, 2026-06-30).
- **CXMT-Tencent $3bn DRAM deal; Apple reportedly lobbying for CXMT access (Reuters via Morgan Stanley sales note, 2026-06-30).** CXMT (Chinese chipmaker) inked a **$3bn, three-year DRAM supply deal with Tencent** for servers; Apple is reportedly **lobbying for CXMT chip access** amid memory supply constraints (Reuters via Morgan Stanley sales note, 2026-06-30) — adds a concrete, large-dollar data point to the China-ramp thread tracked above (Stratechery, 2026-06-23).
- **MU, SNDK flagged as preferred names on tightening inventory; no broad restocking cycle yet (Morgan Stanley/Joseph Moore, Semiconductor Inventory Tracker, 2026-07-01).** MU and SNDK flagged as **preferred memory names** on tightening supply/inventory; overall semi supply-chain inventory still **33 days above historical median** — i.e. **no broad restocking cycle yet**, a data point cutting against the more euphoric pricing narratives above (Morgan Stanley/Joseph Moore, "Semiconductor Inventory Tracker", 2026-07-01).
- **HDD pulled into the same tractor beam.** With NAND tight, Seagate/WDC are running ~90% sequential incremental gross margins and, unlike NAND, managing price up "more on a steady basis" to give predictability — and no longer giving back generation-change discounts in a supply-constrained market (Samik/JPM, [memory-HDD preview](../_equity_calls/Semis/2026-06-12_Samik_memory-HDD-preview.md), 2026-06-12). Memory cost pass-through is now a visible gross-margin headwind across the hardware complex (Calix, Motorola, Flex — same call).
- **Demand fuel beyond HBM: CPU + KV-cache.** NVIDIA Vera standalone CPU ~500k units this year, ~3M in 2027; AI-CPU demand could lift total server DRAM bit demand >15-20%, and Vera/in-house CPUs use higher-content LPDDR5X SO-DIMM/SoCAM, draining low-power DRAM supply (JPM/Jay, 2026-05-28; mechanism corroborated [Arcuri/Micron](../_equity_calls/Semis/2026-05-15_Arcuri-Micron_memory.md), 2026-05-15). Agentic inference grows context ~30x/yr, inflating the KV-cache across the full memory hierarchy — HBM → expansion DRAM (SoCAM/DDR) → a brand-new NAND-flash context tier (245TB SSDs) (Sathya Kumar/Micron via UBS, 2026-05-15). The low-power-DRAM squeeze is now acute: SoCAM/LPDDR5X CPU demand may exceed the smartphone DRAM market next year, and Samsung/Hynix say they can fill their fabs "even if we don't supply any volume to smartphone customers" — leaving CXMT to backfill Chinese smartphone LPDDR (JPM/Jay, 2026-05-28). The DRAM shortage now reads worse than the CPU shortage: **NVIDIA halved the LPDDR5 content in Vera Rubin racks** and servers are shipping "significantly nerfed" on DRAM — a measure of pent-up demand that snaps back (NVIDIA re-doubles memory) once supply loosens (MS/Moore, [MS memory & semicap](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_22_ms_on_memory_and_semicap_22_jun_26.html), 2026-06-22).
- **China supply ramp accelerating — the "partial quick fix" (Stratechery, 2026-06-23).** Citing the WSJ memory-crunch overview, Stratechery flags China as the closest thing to a quick fix for the shortage, though at best partial: **CXMT** (China's top DRAM maker) is seeking a **~$4B Shanghai IPO**, is building new fabs, and reported **Q1 2026 revenue +700% y/y** (products still trail the big three); **Yangtze Memory** (NAND) is building three new factories that would **more than double its capacity by end-2027**. Because DRAM (unlike logic) is fungible, OEMs can route Chinese chips to Asia-bound product and big-three chips to the West — **HP and other top PC makers are in talks to use CXMT memory in products bound for Asia**, and Apple's Tim Cook said "everything needs to be on the table" on China policy. Thompson's structural take: by under-building during their oligopoly, the big three (Samsung/SK Hynix/Micron) created the opening for Chinese competition that gets better simply by winning this volume (Stratechery, 2026-06-23).
- **The Asia-trip + TrendForce update: HBM pricing is *too low* and the allocation priority has flipped (Conor + Avril, 2026-06-23).** Two independent 2026-06-23 sources converge on the same structural read: HBM has been *under*-priced vs conventional DRAM, so makers are quietly de-prioritizing it. **General-server DRAM is now allocation #1, HBM #2 — a reversal of the last four years** (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23). The price math: server DDR5 already sits **>$2/Gb** while **HBM3E is only ~$1.3–2/Gb** (the $1.3 a 2025-fixed price, the $2 a recent upside order) and **HBM4 is currently ~$2.05/Gb** — below conventional, despite HBM4 being ~3x the die size of conventional DRAM; TrendForce's analyst ("Avril") argues HBM4 **must reach ~$5/Gb in 2027** (her conservative number — implied "should be six or seven" but discounted for the small buyer base) just to incentivize makers to keep reserving HBM wafer allocation (StoneX/TrendForce "Memory expert call — Avril", 2026-06-23). Conor's supply-chain checks frame it from NVIDIA's angle: **NVDA HBM pricing ~$17–18/GB (2026) → $30–32/GB (2027), +70–80%**, with everyone else (AMD, Broadcom, AWS) priced *higher* since NVIDIA's volume earns the lowest price; HBM4 LTAs not even finalized two weeks ago despite Samsung already shipping, with the expected increase "**90–100% vs last year's LTA**" (Redburn, 2026-06-23; StoneX/TrendForce, 2026-06-23). The 2027 industry HBM supply forecast was **revised down 60B → 50B Gb-equivalent — not on demand weakness but because conventional DRAM is more profitable**, so makers optimize mix away from HBM (there is a TSV/backend "die penalty" on HBM) (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23).
- **HBM supply +~60% in 2026, but 2027 hinges on Rubin Ultra density (Avril, 2026-06-23).** HBM supply growth is **~60% in 2026 with Rubin density *flat*** (moderate demand); **2027 growth is a question mark pending NVIDIA's Rubin Ultra density decision** — Rubin Ultra is "supposed to double density" but NVIDIA may cut it if HBM supply is short. The reserved incremental HBM wafer capacity for next year is only +60K. End-2026 HBM wafer capacity: **Samsung 180K, SK Hynix 200K (dominant), Micron 100K (risk of dropping to 80K if HBM4 qualification issues persist)** — and Samsung's HBM4 progress raises the possibility it **overtakes SK Hynix as the #1 HBM supplier in 2027** (StoneX/TrendForce "Memory expert call — Avril", 2026-06-23). Micron's weak HBM4 is paradoxically a *near-term positive*: it lets Micron reserve more wafer for higher-return conventional DRAM, helping its staggering +42% bit-supply growth (StoneX/TrendForce, 2026-06-23).
- **DRAM tight through 2028; clean-room space is the binding constraint, EUV the 2027 add-on (Avril, 2026-06-23).** TrendForce frames the supply ceiling explicitly: **through end-2028 the biggest limitation is clean-room space, not equipment or demand** — even a greenfield announced today yields no output before **H2 2029**. 2027 layers on a *second* constraint: **EUV/advanced-litho availability, as DRAM makers now compete with TSMC and foundries for tools** (corroborated by Conor's separate EUV-bottleneck read). DRAM exiting-capacity (K wafers/mo): 2025 Samsung 740 / SK Hynix 610 / Micron 365 / Nanya 65 (mostly legacy DDR4) / CXMT 300; by end-2027 Samsung +45 → ~785, SK Hynix reaches **710 (narrowing the gap with Samsung)**, Micron 440. Key fab cadence: Samsung **P5L phase-1 not ready until mid-2028** (P5L phase-2 just announced, earliest output ~2030); SK Hynix **Yongin FAB1 (200–250K) ready end-2027 but chip-out only 2028** on the 2-quarter wafer cycle; Micron Idaho 1&2 (~50–60K each, longer US lead times) + the PSMC fab acquisition (~40K, Micron-equipment transition, output H2-2027) (StoneX/TrendForce "Memory expert call — Avril", 2026-06-23).
- **LTAs — TrendForce vs Conor, two framings of the same instrument (both sources, 2026-06-23).** TrendForce: LTA discussions began Q2 2026 (first wave US CSPs + NVIDIA + Apple), terms typically **3 years (2026–28)** with a **floor price** and in some cases a **ceiling defined as ≤15% QoQ increase from Q3 2026**; **~20–30% of discussions done, expected 60–70% by end-2026**; prepayments/down-payments as a commitment mechanism (forfeited if the customer walks), and LTAs are **primarily allocation guarantees, not price-fixing**. HBM LTAs are moving from annual to **at-least-every-two-quarters renegotiation** because 2026 HBM was negotiated in 2025 at too-low levels (StoneX/TrendForce "Memory expert call — Avril", 2026-06-23). Conor's sharper buy-side framing: **LTAs are call options** — fixed volume + floor price + no cap = a call-option payoff that is worth a lot in the up-cycle and **zero in the down-cycle when hyperscalers break the contract** (as they always have); 5-year volume contracts, **1-year prepaid** per his checks (Edison's expert said 2-year), escrow unclear; max operating cash hyperscalers will bear for memory is ~30% (currently only mid-teens-to-20%), so no pushback yet — "they don't like the prices but they're in no position to push back" (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23).
- **DRAM price cadence — the bull/bear range across three desks (both sources, 2026-06-23).** Conor's experts: **+20–25% QoQ in both Q3 and Q4** (Sherman/Redburn says +30%/+40%; Edison heard +40–50%) vs buy-side expectations of only ~20% Q3 / 5–10% Q4 — "whichever one is right, memory prices keep going up" for at least six months; the first downturn signal would be **large DRAM capacity additions in 2H27** (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23). TrendForce is the conservative outlier — published **+8–13% Q3 / +3–8% Q4** vs "market chatter" of +30–40% which Avril calls neither likely nor healthy (DRAM already rose +45–50% in Q4'25, +100% in Q1, +60% in Q2) — but her "gut feeling" is **Q3 upside >20%, Q4 >10–15%**; Apple already agreed to a **>20% mobile-DRAM increase in Q3**; **Micron flagged as the price outlier** (capacity-constrained → pushes hardest for highest ASPs; if it succeeds Korean makers follow). The 2027 wildcard is **LPDDR5 becoming the tightest product** as NVIDIA Vera/next-gen CPU and smartphones (Apple) compete for it — potential for QoQ acceleration to resume (StoneX/TrendForce "Memory expert call — Avril", 2026-06-23). Customer pushback is split: large US CSPs/NVIDIA/Apple "are not complaining about price — their concern is allocation"; Asian consumer brands (PC OEMs, smartphone makers) push back but lack pricing leverage (StoneX/TrendForce, 2026-06-23).
- **BofA's "AI 2030" — memory the key constraint, MU PO $500→$950 on a sum-of-parts (BofA, 2026-05-13).** Now 6 weeks old and superseded on the headline PT (June desks at $1,625–1,750), but BofA adds two pieces of durable framing the June notes don't. (1) **The SOTP construction:** MU $950 = **$710/sh from traditional cyclical DRAM/NAND (3.1x CY27E P/B, high-end of MU's 0.8–3.1x historical range)** + **$190/sh from the HBM business (27x CY27E PE, in-line with AI-compute peers)** — the explicit split between commodity-cyclical and HBM-secular value, useful against the bear's "it's all one commodity" framing. (2) **The "structural higher floor" thesis:** memory *capacity and bandwidth* are now the binding inference bottleneck (vs compute historically) because compute scales once per model while **memory bandwidth scales per user, per query, per agent**, and context windows are expanding faster than compression (NVFP4 KV-quant) can offset. BofA sizes **HBM TAM $35B (CY25) → $168B (CY30), +37% CAGR**, HBM content/accelerator **187GB → 464GB (~20% CAGR)**, with HBM staying ~14–20% of accelerator spend; Vera Rubin uses **288GB HBM4/accelerator**. DRAM sufficiency ratio stays >110% (no material oversupply) through CY28; supply elasticity structurally lower as MU's >$25B FY26 capex goes mostly to *cleanroom construction* (meaningful capacity only CY28+) (BofA "AI 2030", 2026-05-13). [BofA "AI 2030" (2026-05-13)](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Vivek_State_of_the_union.html)
- **NAND/SSD read-through from the cache-hit re-pricing (FundaAI, 2026-06-24).** Light read-through, not the subject: FundaAI argues DeepSeek V4's targeted Cache-Hit price cut (to as low as ~¥0.025/Mtok) is backed by **KV cache moving to SSD at scale** (DSA compresses KV cache to 10% of V3.2; DualPath solves the SSD→GPU read-bandwidth bottleneck), pushing agent-scenario cache-hit rates to 95%+ — "a positive for SSDs, and **NAND demand could benefit materially**" in large-scale agent workloads (Jevons: cheaper per-token storage → retain history longer/wider → total usage expands). On the binding constraint, FundaAI's compute-bottleneck section lists **HBM/DRAM/KV-cache/CXL as what "determine inference throughput and long-context carrying capacity,"** with multi-agent fan-out pushing total KV-cache pressure "against the HBM and memory walls" and lifting the value of CXL memory pools (FundaAI "Deep|LLM 26H1 Update Part 2", 2026-06-24). [FundaAI "Deep|LLM 26H1 Update Part 2" (2026-06-24)](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/LLM_Part_2_funda.html)
- **Enterprise sourcing pain — the demand signal from the buyer side (MS expert, 2026-04-10).** A Morgan Stanley hardware-sourcing expert call (enterprise buyer, not a CSP) framed the shortage from the bottom of the queue: enterprise customers wanting DRAM/NAND are "bottom of the list" behind HBM/AI/CSP allocation. **Server prices ran +100% (up to +300%) vs Q4-2025**; storage finished-good cost +25% to +150%; lead times blew out from 4-6 weeks to 4-42 weeks. A purchase order is "like a deli ticket" — it secures queue position, not price; some OEMs invoke force-majeure to re-price after the PO. Suppliers are "sold out for 26 component-wise" with 2027 "going fast"; the buyer hopes for normalization to "15-20% q/q" increases in H1-2027 but treats 2027 as a "big unknown" — "once prices go up, they don't come down." Confirms the tightness narrative is visible all the way down to the enterprise end-buyer, with cost pass-through not yet rippling up the SaaS stack (MS "memory sourcing expert", 2026-04-10).
- **SemiAnalysis "Memory Mania" — the capacity build-out, quantified across 4 years (SemiAnalysis, 2026-02-06).** HBM wafer capacity roughly triples in two years: **~123kwspm (end-2023) → ~331kwspm (end-2025, +2.7x) → ~473kwspm (end-2026E) → ~668kwspm (end-2027E)** (SemiAnalysis "Memory Mania", 2026-02-06, p.16-17). As a share of total DRAM wafer capacity, HBM goes from **<5% (2022) → ~20% (end-2025) → ~35% (end-2027E)** (p.17), with HBM demand crossing **>10% of total DRAM demand by end-2027** (p.21). SemiAnalysis frames the DRAM supply deficit at **~7% in 2026/2027**, with the HBM-specific shortfall widening from **~5% (2026) to ~9% (2027)** — flagged here as approximate given a rendering artifact in the source merging two pages at that point; verify against the underlying chart if pulling the exact figure (p.14-15). The report's "once-in-four-decades shortage" framing rests on three compounding drivers: **HBM capacity reallocation away from commodity DRAM, cleanroom-capacity underinvestment in 2025-2026, and structural node-migration/yield complexity** (p.15-16, 23-24) — the same triangulation (capacity + cleanroom + node-yield) that the June 2026 expert calls on this page arrive at independently, now with an earlier, dated primary source. On end-demand mix: **PC/mobile is only ~30% of total DRAM end demand, with servers ~40%+ and rising** (p.21, 27).
- **HBM4/Rubin supplier-share forecast — SK Hynix dominant, Micron shut out in year one (SemiAnalysis, 2026-02-06).** For HBM4 on Rubin (R200-class), SemiAnalysis's Feb-2026 supplier-share forecast for the first 12 months was **SK Hynix ~60%, Samsung ~30%, Micron ~0%** (p.29) — a materially more bearish Micron HBM4 starting position than some of the June expert-call reads elsewhere on this page (e.g. TrendForce's Avril has Micron's HBM4 qualification issues as a live 2026 risk, consistent directionally). Useful as the earliest on-disk marker for how the HBM4/Rubin share fight was expected to open.
- **Pricing trajectory — DDR5/LPDDR5 contract pricing already inflecting hard by 1Q26 (SemiAnalysis, 2026-02-06).** DDR5 contract pricing moved **+70% QoQ in 1Q26**, LPDDR5 **+35% QoQ**, reaching **~$14/GB (DDR5) and ~$11.5/GB (LPDDR5)**; on a YoY basis (vs 1Q25) that's **+638% DDR5, +369% LPDDR5**, with SemiAnalysis forecasting a further **~2x pricing increase from 4Q25 to 4Q26** (p.25). This is the earliest datapoint on disk for the pricing melt-up this page otherwise tracks starting ~April 2026 — the trajectory was already visible in February.
- **Divergence from the capacity-race narrative: OpenAI's ASIC runs 8-Hi HBM4, not 12/16-Hi (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall", 2025-08-11).** Not every accelerator design is chasing maximum HBM stack height. OpenAI's ASIC uses **8-Hi HBM4** rather than the 12-Hi/16-Hi configurations more common elsewhere — a deliberate **bandwidth-over-capacity tradeoff** enabled by high-rank expert parallelism in its model architecture (p.19-20). Worth flagging as a live counter-thread to this page's capacity-build-out framing: not every buyer is bidding for more HBM capacity per accelerator — some are optimizing for bandwidth density instead, which if it spreads would blunt one leg of the HBM-shortage bull case (fewer GB/accelerator demanded even as unit shipments grow).
- **China-domestic-HBM wildcard: CXMT ramping HBM2 8-Hi, Huawei's memory affiliates still entity-listed (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall", 2025-08-11).** CXMT reached **HBM2 8-Hi mass production in H1 2025**, with TSV capacity guided to match Micron's by year-end (p.10-12) — a slower, lower-tier ramp than the CXMT-IPO/CXMT-sold-out datapoints tracked elsewhere on this page (which concern conventional DRAM, not HBM). Huawei's **XMC + SJSemi HBM-affiliated entities remain under US entity-list restrictions** (p.10-12). No direct wiki ticker currently captures this; flagging as a watch-item wildcard on the domestic-China side of the shortage debate, distinct from and behind the CXMT-conventional-DRAM threat already covered above.
- **HBM packaging/technical architecture layer — complements the supply/demand numbers above (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall", 2025-08-11).** MR-MUF (SK Hynix's mass-reflow molded-underfill) vs NCF/TCB (non-conductive-film thermo-compression bonding, Samsung/Micron) packaging approaches are compared across the three memory makers (p.6-9) — the process-technology backdrop to the MR-MUF-vs-hybrid-bonding debate tracked on [cowos-packaging.md](cowos-packaging.md). On the base-die supply chain: **TSMC fabricates HBM4 base-die wafers (N12/N3) for Nvidia/Broadcom/hyperscaler designers**, since the memory makers themselves mostly lack leading-edge logic capability (p.25-26, 40-42) — full mechanics of this on [cowos-packaging.md](cowos-packaging.md). SemiAnalysis's framing on who actually captures HBM economics: **Nvidia is "arguably the most profitable HBM vendor in the world"** by capturing GPU-level margin on top of HBM content it resells inside the accelerator (p.42-43) — a useful counter to any read of HBM profit pools that stops at the memory maker.

## Sizing & sequential growth (the numbers)

**Capstone models & dashboards (one click):** [Memória — resumo executivo](../_dashboards/memoria-resumo.html) — a planilha-síntese em uma página: KPIs do ciclo, demanda por aplicação e **oferta por empresa em Gb (incl. HBM por fornecedor, UBS Fig 3×Fig 8 — Samsung ultrapassa a Hynix em bits de HBM em 27E, 24,2 vs 23,0 bn Gb)**, suficiência DRAM/NAND (UBS) vs **house (o flip de 2027: +2,6% house vs −1,2% UBS)**, canibalização de wafer HBM, destruição de demanda de smartphone (contrafactual editável), P&L 27E → P/E por cenário, builds cross-checks — com download do xlsx vivo (dropdown House/UBS/Soft). · [Memória — superciclo (dashboard interativo)](../_dashboards/memoria-capstone.html) — 6 abas com builds editáveis (CPU/HBM/smartphone), engine de capacidade e cenários. Fonte-mãe: bíblia consolidada `Memória Capstone Biblia.xlsx` + pipeline em `P:\Felipe Monteiro\Memória\_build\` (UBS Memory Semis 03-jul-26 · Bernstein/Mercury/Gartner · builds Capstone; regenerável via rebuild.bat).

Memory capex is the dollar engine of the super-cycle: after a flat 2022-24 (~$55-61B), it re-accelerates to **$101B (2026E) → $123B (2028E)** as DRAM/HBM lead — memory back to **~46% of total semi capex** (vs ~32% at the 2023 bottom). Micron's own capex echoes it: ~$14B FY25 → $20B → **>$25B FY26**.

## Key debates
- 🆕 ⚠️🔴🔴 **NEW BEAR BRANCH — DENSITY PEAK: THE FIRST ARGUMENT ON THIS PAGE THAT HBM CONTENT *PER GPU* HAS TOPPED OUT, AND THE REASON IT SITS IN DEBATES RATHER THAN IN STATE-OF-PLAY IS THAT ITS LOAD-BEARING PREMISE IS EXPLICITLY UNCONFIRMED.** **DAMNANG (independent Substack author — NOT a broker and NOT a research house), "HBM Density Peak: Why AI Value Is Moving From Memory to Connectivity", 2026-08-20:** Rubin Ultra's HBM configuration is *"being reviewed downward, from HBM [12-]Hi toward HBM4 8-Hi,"* with **supply constraint named as the direct cause** — but the author's actual claim is that supply alone does not make the choice *work*: it works because **a wider scale-up domain plus software tiering absorb the loss of per-GPU memory capacity AT THE SYSTEM LEVEL**, and the direction of travel is therefore **optics + memory disaggregation** rather than more layers per stack. ⚠️⚠️ **THE AUTHOR'S OWN DISCLAIMER MUST TRAVEL WITH THIS EVERY TIME IT IS QUOTED, INCLUDING OFF THIS PAGE: *"The 8-Hi configuration for Rubin Ultra is UNCONFIRMED information based on industry checks, and the final specification may change."* This is an unconfirmed industry check published on an independent Substack. It is NOT a spec change, NOT a company disclosure and NOT a broker forecast — do not quote it as an established Rubin Ultra specification.** ⚠️ **He also declines to carry a 4-Hi variant some of his sources mention, on the grounds that *"there is no verified basis for it."* Note that omission as a discipline signal, not as evidence against 4-Hi.**
  - ✅ **WHERE IT SITS AGAINST THE FOUR PRIOR SIGHTINGS THIS PAGE ALREADY CARRIES — AND IT TAKES MORGAN STANLEY'S SIDE OF AN INTERNAL CONTRADICTION.** The 08-07 block reads UBS as having *settled* the question (8-Hi, not 12-Hi); the 08-10 PM block has MS saying **tiered SKUs with the decision open until end-3Q26**; 08-17 logs a third venue. DAMNANG is the **fourth independent sighting and it lands on "not decided"**: his field report is that **major memory makers are running DAILY MEETINGS with the customer on the basis of HBM4 8-Hi as the MAIN Rubin Ultra configuration and are entering spec decisions on that footing.** He also relays, as third-party reporting rather than his own work, **TrendForce (2026-08-04) confirming evaluation of multiple configurations with the final spec unset, The Information on prototype testing of 192GB and 256GB configurations, and SemiAnalysis's read that the main SKU moves to HBM4 8-Hi 192GB with compute performance held and memory bandwidth slightly HIGHER.** ➜ **Net for the page: the 08-07 "settled" framing stays walked back. The leading case is 8-Hi; the decision is still open; nobody outside NVIDIA has the spec.**
  - 🔴 **THE MECHANISM, WHICH IS THE PART THAT SURVIVES EVEN IF THE SPEC DOES NOT — AND IT IS A COST-PER-INCREMENTAL-GB ARGUMENT, NOT A DEMAND ARGUMENT.** Three reasons named: **(1) SUPPLY — DRAM likely stays tight through 2027 and HBM4E still carries schedule uncertainty in qualification at the performance level NVIDIA is asking for; twelve-layer stacking costs more in throughput and yield than eight. (2) SYSTEM STRUCTURE — on Rubin Ultra *"bandwidth and data movement are becoming [a] larger constraint than memory density,"* i.e. improving how data moves contributes more to performance than adding capacity. (3) PACKAGE-LEVEL COMPOUNDING — adding stacks around the GPU raises package area and interposer-edge I/O, while raising the layer count inside the same stack raises stacking complexity, yield loss, thermal load and package complexity: *"the higher HBM density goes, the more the system has to pay for each additional unit of capacity."*** ➜ **Read this directly against the bull case below: the die-displacement/trade-ratio argument says HBM makes DRAM scarce, and the density-peak argument says the SYSTEM stops buying the marginal GB before physics stops supplying it. Those are not the same claim and they can both be true — the second caps CONTENT PER GPU while the first keeps BITS PER WAFER scarce.** ⚠️ **And the author is explicit that HBM does not lose its role: local HBM stays the fastest tier at ~21-22TB/s per his earlier field checks vs NVLink 6 at roughly 3.6TB/s of GPU-to-GPU bandwidth per GPU, so *"another GPU's HBM does [not] replace local HBM"* — what changes is how much of the weights and KV cache must sit in ONE GPU's HBM. This is a TIERING argument, not a substitution argument.**
  - ⚠️ **THE REVENUE OFFSET, CARRIED AS THE AUTHOR'S OWN SENSITIVITY AND EXPLICITLY NOT AS A FORECAST.** *"Under the current supply constraint… the per-GPU bit decline cannot be applied directly as a decline in HBM market revenue. Lowering stack height raises the number of stacks that can be built from the same DRAM and stacking capacity, and NVIDIA can absorb that into additional GPU shipments."* His sensitivity (sourced to his own 2026-08-10 piece): a stacking-time effect **[the multiplier is not legible in the extracted text and has NOT been reconstructed]** times a **1.084x stack-yield improvement under a 98% per-core-die yield assumption**, giving an **EFFECTIVE OUTPUT CEILING OF ABOUT 1.63x**; assuming all of that volume is absorbed into additional NVIDIA GPU shipments, **total HBM revenue lands at ~81.5% of plan if 8-Hi is priced at 50% of the planned HBM4E, and ~98% at 60%.** ⚠️⚠️ **HIS OWN LABEL: *"These are sensitivities to stack count, not shipment forecasts."* ✅ Directionally the same shape as the model layer this page already carries from JPM (08-09): the de-spec cuts BITS while ASP more than offsets, so HBM revenue goes UP 12-13% even as the glut narrows. DAMNANG's version is a price-of-8-Hi sensitivity around ~100% of plan rather than a revenue increase — do NOT net his 81.5%/98%-of-plan against JPM's +12-13%, they are different bases (plan-relative vs revision-relative).**
  - 🔴 **AND THE COMPETITIVE-VARIABLE SWITCH, WHICH IS THE PART WITH THE LONGEST READ-THROUGH FOR THE DE-COMMODITIZATION DEBATE BELOW.** *"In the regime where 12-Hi and 16-Hi stack height was itself the axis of differentiation, high-stack qualification and stacking technology carried a large premium. If the life of 8-Hi extends, YIELD, CAPACITY, SUPPLY STABILITY and BASE DIE FUNCTIONALITY carry more weight."* ➜ **This page's existing de-commoditization vector is HBM4E customer-supplied CUSTOM LOGIC BASE DIE (ex-Micron VP sceptical on warranty/yield/liability, 2026-06-10; Redburn corroborating the direction, 2026-06-17). Density peak points the SAME way through a different door: if height stops differentiating, base-die function and supply reliability are what is left to differentiate on — which favours whoever is furthest along on custom base die and disadvantages a stacking-technology lead. [[SKHYNIX]] expanding custom base die AND a CXL memory tier together is the author's own example. Cross-ref [cxl-memory-fabric.md](cxl-memory-fabric.md) and [optical-cpo.md](optical-cpo.md), where the connectivity half of his thesis is filed.**
- 🆕 ✅ **THE DIE-DISPLACEMENT TERM GETS A THIRD, INDEPENDENT HOUSE — AND IT TIGHTENS RATHER THAN LOOSENS: the HBM silicon TRADE RATIO is 3:1 TODAY, EXPANDING TO 3.5:1–4:1 WITH NEXT-GEN HBM ARCHITECTURE, which BMO reads as "further pressure on supply"** (**BMO Capital Markets · Harsh Kumar / Wei Mok, CFA — "[[MU]]: Initiating at Outperform; Well Positioned in Memory Upcycle, SCA Offers Visibility", 2026-08-20**). ✅ **CORROBORATION, NOT NET-NEW MECHANISM: this page already carries 3:1 at HBM3E → ~4:1 by HBM4E from [[MU]] MANAGEMENT itself (08-15) and "HBM's ~3:1 die displacement" in the bull case from JPM (2026-05-28). BMO's range is the same term from an initiating third house, and it is the supply-side counterweight to the density-peak argument above — fewer layers per stack ease the STACKING bottleneck while the trade ratio keeps tightening effective DRAM supply. ATTRIBUTE THE RATIO TO BMO, NOT TO MICRON.**
  - ⚠️ **AND BMO'S TECHNOLOGY CALL, WHICH PUTS IT ON THE OPPOSITE SIDE OF THIS PAGE'S NEWEST HBM-LEADERSHIP DEBATE: BMO believes [[MU]] has CLOSED THE TECHNOLOGY GAP and LEADS IN HBM4** — it entered HBM in a less competitive position, caught up via HBM3/HBM3E, and today has *"best-in-class technology"* reflected in recent market-share gains and customer adoption; BMO also reports **extreme shortages across ALL THREE of HBM, DRAM and NAND**. ⚠️ **Set squarely against the 08-18 block on this page, where TWO houses put [[SAMSUNG]] #1 in HBM in 2027, ending the [[SKHYNIX]] leadership this theme carried for two years. Three different houses now name three different HBM4 leaders. Nothing is superseded — the disagreement is the datapoint, and it is a cleaner statement of how unsettled HBM4 leadership is than any single house's ranking.**
  - 🔴 **BMO'S NAND LEG IS A STRUCTURAL CLAIM, NOT A CYCLICAL ONE: AI datacentres have shifted from HDD to NAND-based storage over the past year — on performance, lower latency and efficiency, and as a REPLACEMENT FILLING SHORTAGES FROM TRADITIONAL MEMORY — and BMO believes this shift becomes STRUCTURAL once AI DCs recognise the benefits.** ⚠️ **Read against the NAND↔HDD substitution math already in these debates (Bernstein ex-WDC expert, 2026-05-04): the TCO crossover sits at ~1.5-3x $/GB and the gap blew out to ~15-25x, which made the swing BACK toward HDD "somewhat inevitable." BMO is arguing the opposite direction on the same substitution — that scarcity-driven adoption converts into permanent design-in. The two are a genuine, dated disagreement about whether a shortage-forced substitution sticks; both stay on the page.**
- 🆕 ⚠️ **STALE-BUT-DURABLE FRAMEWORK, LOGGED FOR THE HIERARCHY ARGUMENT AND NOT FOR ITS MARKS — ~3.7 MONTHS STALE AT INGEST.** **China Renaissance, "AI 101 Series: AI inference is reshaping the future of memory", 2026-04-30 (107pp)** argues that **AI INFERENCE CREATES A NEW DATA ACCESS LAYER BETWEEN HBM/DRAM AND TRADITIONAL STORAGE, FUNDAMENTALLY RESHAPING THE MEMORY HIERARCHY**; that **Flash storage is transitioning from CAPACITY-DRIVEN to PERFORMANCE-DRIVEN**; and that **high-IOPS Flash and HBF (High Bandwidth Flash) can deliver 5-10x ASP UPLIFT and higher margins.** ➜ **WHY A FOUR-MONTH-OLD NOTE STILL EARNS A LINE: it dates the "new tier between HBM and storage" argument to APRIL — four months before this page acquired it from JPM at FMS (HBF spec: stacked NAND 8-hi/16-hi, up to 512GB at ~0.4-3TB/s, 08-09) and from FUNDA's G1-G4 coordinate system (08-11, filed on [cxl-memory-fabric.md](cxl-memory-fabric.md)). And the 5-10x ASP UPLIFT is the term the later sources do NOT carry — it is the pricing leg the whole HBF/high-IOPS tier thesis needs, and no other source on this page supplies one.** ⚠️⚠️ **ITS PRICE TARGETS ARE HISTORICAL AND ALREADY SUPERSEDED — DO NOT LET THEM WALK BACK ANYTHING: [[KIOXIA]] (285A JP) Buy TP JPY55,400 (raised from JPY30,300, +83%) and [[SNDK]] Buy TP US$1,452 (raised from US$698, +108%), against [../SNDK.md](../SNDK.md) already carrying Citi US$2,100 and Bernstein US$3,000. KIOXIA was the note's top pick. Also named, none with a wiki page: Phison (8299 TT) TP TWD2,540 · FADU (440110 KS) TP KRW103,800 · Macronix (2337 TT) TP TWD251. Single stale house estimate — the FRAMEWORK is adopted as a dated view, the numbers are not adopted as canonical marks.**
- 🆕 ⚠️✅ **THE "CHINESE DRAM BREAKS INCUMBENT MEMORY PROFITS" BEAR CASE GETS TESTED CLAIM-BY-CLAIM BY AN INDEPENDENT AUTHOR — AND HE ENDS UP REFUTING EVERY ONE OF THEM. ⚠️ SOURCE IS ~25 DAYS STALE (dated 2026-07-27, swept late); THE PRICE MARKS ARE HISTORICAL AND THE FRAMEWORK IS THE VALUE.** (**DAMNANG, independent Substack author — NOT a broker — "After CXMT's Listing, Do the Memory Three Have Further to Fall?", 2026-07-27.** Full claim-by-claim teardown, including the process-gap and supply-timing legs, is filed on [china-export.md](china-export.md); what follows is only the part that bears on incumbent memory PROFITS.) **The worry, in his own one sentence: *"that `CXMT` will break DRAM prices and shrink the profits of Samsung Electronics, SK hynix and Micron."*** His verdict on the two claims that matter most here: **(1) the "sells 30% below the incumbents" figure is a MIX ARTEFACT** — it compares company-wide ASPs while roughly two-thirds of `CXMT` revenue is low-priced mobile memory; **like-for-like the gap is 5-10% per `CXMT`'s own IPO prospectus, and in SERVER products THE SIGN FLIPS** (July 2026: a 64GB DDR5 server module built with `CXMT` chips sold for RMB18,999 in Chinese online retail vs RMB18,595 for the same specification on [[SAMSUNG]]/[[SKHYNIX]] chips — **`CXMT` 2.2% HIGHER**; in the contract market `CXMT` was reported to have quoted ABOVE Samsung's supply price of US$1,240 per module). **(2) It has no room to sell cheap: at its current technology level `CXMT` spends MORE THAN 30% MORE than the incumbents to produce the same amount of memory, its chips are ~40% LARGER in area, and its yield falls short of the 85-90% the industry treats as standard.** ➜ **The relevance to this page is narrow and important: the memory-three profit pool is not being competed away on price by a producer whose cost base is 30%+ worse and who is currently PRICING ABOVE the incumbents in the one segment that carries the margin.** ⚠️ **All of it is one independent author's compilation of public sources — his own disclaimer notes the figures "may carry errors from those sources or from the compilation" and that he "may hold positions in the companies mentioned."**
  - 🔴 **AND ONE NUMBER FROM IT DESERVES TO SIT NEXT TO THE TRADE RATIO ABOVE, BECAUSE IT IS THE SAME SCARCITY MECHANISM DERIVED A COMPLETELY DIFFERENT WAY: *"In 2026 HBM consumes 22% of global DRAM wafer capacity while producing only 9% of the memory capacity. That gap is the underlying reason ordinary DRAM is short today."*** ⚠️⚠️ **DO NOT CHAIN OR NET THIS AGAINST THE 3:1 → 3.5:1-4:1 TRADE RATIO. The 22%/9% is an industry-wide, single-year split of WAFER capacity versus BIT capacity; the trade ratio is a PER-DIE displacement multiple. Same family of claim, different bases and different units — a supply model that multiplies them is double-counting.** ➜ **He applies the identical arithmetic to `CXMT`'s own HBM push and gets the counter-intuitive result this wiki already holds from JPM (08-09, filed on [china-export.md](china-export.md)): stacking consumes roughly three times the wafer area of ordinary DDR5 per GB, so as `CXMT` converts DRAM lines to HBM, ORDINARY DRAM PRICES COME UNDER UPWARD PRESSURE, NOT DOWNWARD. Chinese HBM ambition is DRAM-bullish on this reasoning.**
  - **HIS PER-NAME READ ON THE MEMORY THREE, CARRIED AS ONE AUTHOR'S VIEW AND WITH ITS ~25-DAY-OLD MULTIPLES LABELLED AS SUCH.** **[[SKHYNIX]] — most INSULATED** (earnings concentrated in HBM, which `CXMT` cannot enter) but its earnings ride on the capex plans of a handful of AI-accelerator customers: *"What moves SK hynix shares is that, not `CXMT`."* **[[SAMSUNG]] — hit FIRST AND HARDEST** if Chinese procurement directives widen to memory, because it sells the largest share of its output to Chinese smartphone and PC makers and because what `CXMT` can push outside China is commodity mobile/PC DRAM rather than server product — against which its forward multiple was **the lowest of the three at 4.32x**, so much of the risk was already priced. **[[MU]] — least left to lose in China**: mainland China revenue fell from ~1/8 of the total to the 7% range after the 2023 purchase ban and it exited the Chinese server-memory business in October 2025; it competes with `CXMT` outside China, but if the US moves to exclude Chinese memory from data centres and government procurement *"that competition never arrives in North America"* — on **6.42x, the highest multiple of the three**. **His conclusion on the complex: *"the recent memory correction is more likely to END after the `CXMT` [listing] than to continue"*** — because the market has already priced much of the slowing-earnings-growth concern, and for the decline to keep going you need either memory earnings to turn down or AI demand to reverse outright, and *"neither price nor volume shows any sign of coming down."* ⚠️⚠️ **THE MULTIPLES (4.32x / 4.69x / 6.42x, and `CXMT` itself at ~30x this year's expected earnings on its opening price) ARE 2026-07-27 MARKS AND ARE STALE BY ~25 DAYS. They are logged as the relative-valuation ORDERING he argued from, not as current marks.**

- **New bear branch — efficiency/substitution:** the cycle can remain tight while accelerator architectures reduce HBM intensity per token. The observable test is whether QCOM can validate its 4–8× decode/TCO claim at application level and secure LPDDR allocation; until then HBC is an execution-dependent architecture, not a change to the canonical HBM supply/pricing ledger (Citrini Semis, 2026-07-20).
- **Bull / where the money is — duration, not direction.** The cleanest bull is UBS Arcuri on MU: PT $535→$1,625, "path to >$100 EPS," C27/28/29E $155/$167/$117, LTAs locking ~30% of industry DDR bits and ~$400bn FCF C27-29E ([MU briefing](../_briefings/by-ticker/MU.md), 2026-05-26/31 — flagged "most controversial UBS note recently"). Supporting voices: DB (Weathers, Buy) "DRAM tightness extends to 2028+" (2026-06-17); GS (Schneider) "ongoing tightness through CY27, significant upside to Street" (2026-06-09); Citi 8x C27 EPS from 5x (2026-05-19); Susquehanna $1,750 (2026-05-30). Mechanism: HBM's ~3:1 die displacement + node-driven bit decline + multi-year cleanroom/EUV lead times keep supply short while LTAs/SCAs convert the equity into an earnings-durability story (JPM, 2026-05-28). Suppliers are now optimizing for **durability over sequential momentum** — most will be at ~80% gross margin by year-end and are "securing the durability of earnings rather than seeking additional sequential upside" (JPM/Jay, 2026-05-28). GS (Junie Yoon) makes the cleanest "longevity is underestimated" case: DRAM names at 5-6x earnings disbelieve durability, but a 9x earnings multiple is "conservative" vs semis peers with similar margins/moats at high-teens-to-20x — and the bear-case sensitivity (DRAM ASP **−30% in 2027 then −30%/−50% in 2028 still leaves DRAM GM ~40%**) is the downside protection that justifies an earnings (not P/B) multiple; lower crash-risk than past cycles because hyperscaler demand visibility lets suppliers spend capex efficiently rather than overbuild (GS "memory", 2026-06-08). UBS Arcuri's incremental tell into the Micron print: the Street is fixated on DDR, but **HBM pricing is "what is really surprising to the upside,"** and the **LTAs (not pricing) are the re-rate catalyst** — investor demand is "rabid" (40+ call requests/day, "panicked about missing these moves," 4 death threats after the PT-to-$1,600 note went viral) (UBS "Arcuri call", 2026-06-15).
- **Supply discipline is the load-bearing assumption.** Capex has *not* yet ramped meaningfully because it takes 12-18 months to source tools and find fab space; the industry mis-forecast a mid-26/early-27 *down*-cycle and is only now turning the crank (Micron ex-VP, [procurement call](../_equity_calls/Semis/2026-06-10_Micron-exVP_procurement.md), 2026-06-10). The 2028 governor is EUV: ASML's ~90 tools by end-CY27 are insufficient once Micron/Samsung/Hynix each add ≥100k wafers/month — Samsung/Hynix need ~20-25 tools each, Micron ~10-15, TSMC already ~40 (JPM/Jay, 2026-05-28). DRAM↔HBM mix is fungible overnight (no equipment change), so makers keep starving commodity DRAM to feed HBM — the "die penalty" that sustains the shortage (JPM, 2026-05-28; ex-VP, 2026-06-10). UBS (Arcuri) reframes the EUV governor more benignly — ASML isn't a physical bottleneck until ~**110 EUV slots (~$225B WFE)**, beyond which it must expand floor space, and it's already getting the same 8-10-quarter visibility as US toolmakers, so supply "is not going to be the limitation"; UBS raised 2028 WFE to $250B and floats 2029 into the $300B range, with memory not entering sustained oversupply until **~mid-2028 at the earliest** (UBS "Arcuri call", 2026-06-15). MS semicap (Shane) sizes WFE at **$149B (2026) → $191B (2027, +28%) → mid-teens growth (2028)**, flags 2026 DRAM WFE revisions "even more aggressive" (~+60% to ~$50B), but underscores remarkable **NAND capital discipline** — a NAND-WFE "resurrection" is a 2027 story, not a 2026 pull-in (MS "memory & semicap", 2026-06-22).
- **Bear / what could break it — the commodity snap-back.** The sharpest bear on disk is the **ex-Micron VP of Procurement** (2026-06-10): the market is pricing peak earnings as sustainable — "a highly catastrophic assumption." HBM differentiation is *temporary* (once a 2nd/3rd vendor qualifies at each customer "it already has become a commodity"); Micron's cost structure is worse than Samsung's and ~level with SK Hynix; and "your $900 stock right now will become a $50 stock again," possibly normalizing "within this calendar year or early next" as Micron/SK/Samsung bring disruptive new-fab capacity online ~a year out. He frames the AI buildout as a multi-layer bull-whip ("beer game") removed from end demand that can't amortize ~$6-700B of infra spend. The XP GPU specialist independently calls HBM "comoditizado" — once multiple vendors are NVIDIA-qualified the timing advantage decays to a commodity ([XP/GPU-memory](../_equity_calls/Semis/2026-05-27_XP_GPU-memory.md), 2026-05-27).
- **Anthropic implied to be building an in-house ASIC (Avril, 2026-06-23).** TrendForce reads the Anthropic–Micron deal — **server DRAM + enterprise SSD in 2027 plus HBM in 2028** — as direct evidence that **Anthropic is developing its own in-house ASIC** (you only need HBM if you have your own accelerator); Anthropic is pursuing the **same combined deal with SK Hynix and Samsung** (StoneX/TrendForce "Memory expert call — Avril", 2026-06-23). On the China supply side, both sources play down the near-term threat: **CXMT is sold out, with its +20K end-2026 increase mostly an HBM *attempt*** that won't ramp in volume (technology too old; output only good for low-grade consumer LPDDR5/DDR5), and Conor hears a "good chunk" of CXMT capacity is earmarked for **HBM inside China** for the domestic AI ramp — not a US oversupply threat (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23; StoneX/TrendForce, 2026-06-23). NAND: global wafer capacity *declines 3% in 2026* as Samsung/SK Hynix convert NAND to the more-lucrative DRAM (+6% 2027, +11% 2028); **YMTC is the more concerning Chinese supplier** (uses domestic tools to expand, strong in consumer-grade QLC) but its **QLC is only ~13% of output**, capping it to the consumer segment vs enterprise-SSD-critical Kioxia/Solidigm (StoneX/TrendForce, 2026-06-23).
- **Second-source qualification & China.** Customers force-qualify Chinese DRAM (CXMT) on cheap LPDDR Micron/Samsung/Hynix won't supply at current ASPs — "once they're in, they're in," a lasting share leak at the low end (ex-VP, 2026-06-10). CXMT filed its IPO at ~50% net margin, ASP +~80-85% q/q, but can't hit server or Apple grade; bottleneck is cleanroom + equipment, not policy — and US discussion of banning all equipment sales to China could cap it (JPM/Jay, 2026-05-28). HBM4E adds a wildcard: customer-supplied custom logic base die would *de*-commoditize HBM — but the ex-VP doubts it scales on warranty/yield/liability grounds (2026-06-10). Redburn's bus tour corroborates the *de*-commoditization vector: HBM is moving to **increasing base-die customization** to deliver a system-level (not commodity-chip) solution — "new agreements bind the financial and technological fortunes of the AI supply chain more closely together," suppressing double-ordering and supporting through-cycle margins (Redburn, 2026-06-17).
- **DRAM > NAND on the supply-risk axis (MS/Bernstein).** The buy-side preference has swung toward NAND/SanDisk, but MS argues that's backwards on a 2-3yr view: fundamentals are **"10 out of 10 for DRAM, ~8/10 for NAND,"** and some NAND **2028 LTA lock-in prices are *down* vs 2027** (materially so for enterprise TLC) — softened by built-in escalators — whereas DRAM long-term negotiations "go very well"; MS prefers Micron/DRAM and reads DRAM as the primary AI bottleneck (MS "memory & semicap", 2026-06-22). The NAND↔HDD substitution math (Bernstein expert, ex-WDC NAND VP): the TCO crossover where NAND takes HDD share sits at **~1.5-3x $/GB** (vs the prior ~5x); the gap blew out to ~15-25x in the run-up (vs ~4-5x in Aug-2025), so the swing back toward HDD is "somewhat inevitable" as builders re-tier away from the AI "gold rush" all-flash sloppiness. NAND's peakiness is structural: AI needs the **latest NAND node** (only ~30-35% of capacity), and the under-invested NAND base can't lift that fast — but the new LTAs are only ~25% financially guaranteed (e.g. SanDisk's $11B on $42B), so a −50% market price translates to ~−25% effective ASP, and "tearing up" means renegotiating for more years, not walking (Bernstein "HDD/NAND", 2026-05-04).
- **New structural risk to the HBM *market size* (Redburn): W2W bonding + optically-networked DRAM.** Redburn's BESI work surfaces two technologies that could shrink the HBM TAM itself, not just commoditize it. **Wafer-to-Wafer (W2W) hybrid bonding** (imec/EVG showed 200nm W2W at sub-40nm overlay) could bond DRAM *pairs* before dicing/KGD-sort, halving the per-stack bond count — though yield penalties on ~95% leading-edge DRAM yields limit it to ~2-high. More radically, **optically-networked commodity DRAM** (a large LPDDR pool reached via optics, 3-4x HBM pin speed) could "reduce the size of the HBM market altogether" — but ~300ns light↔electrical conversion latency keeps HBM on the critical path, so Redburn judges it a long-dated revolution, with HBM staying for low-latency delivery and optical DRAM as a prefetch tier (Redburn "BESI", 2026-06-15). Net: another reason the bull case rests on HBM *durability* assumptions.
- **CPU/server-DRAM demand leg quantified (UBS).** UBS's server-CPU TAM work underwrites the DRAM bit-demand uplift: server-CPU units 23M (2025) → **63-70M by 2030 (30% CAGR)** on agentic AI, with AI-CPU (head-node + standalone) climbing 3M → 33M units; standalone CPUs outside the rack go 0 → 13M — each pulling LPDDR5X SO-DIMM/SoCAM content. AI-CPU ASPs rise on core count ($2,420 → $3,769) (UBS "CPU TAM", 2026-05-21). Consistent with JPM's "AI-CPU could lift server DRAM bit demand >15-20%."
- **Timeline / inflection.** Near-term catalyst is Micron's F3Q26 print (June 24): Street rev ~$34.7B vs guide $33.5B, FY27/28 EPS pegged ~$111/$116 ([MU briefing](../_briefings/by-ticker/MU.md), 2026-06-12). The structural inflection both camps point to is **~mid-2027 to 2028**, when new fabs land — the bull says EUV scarcity caps that supply; the bear says it breaks the price. UBS converges on this: sustained memory overcapacity "not until the middle of 2028 at the earliest," so WFE wouldn't roll until ~2029 (UBS "Arcuri call", 2026-06-15). The cleanest single read across the complex now (MS): DRAM fundamentals 10/10, NAND 8/10, with the buy-side so crowded ("every hand in the room owns memory") that dips get bought — and Apple's Tim Cook calling memory pricing a "100-year flood" (WSJ) is the demand-side exclamation point (MS "memory & semicap", 2026-06-22).

- **SK Hynix's record US listing + HBM 2027 price-doubling (2026-07-10).** SK Hynix priced its Nasdaq ADR at **$149, raising ~US$26.5bn — the largest-ever first-time share sale by a foreign issuer on US markets** (past Alibaba), 7x oversubscribed with anchor allocations cut on demand, ~3% ADR premium; proceeds earmarked for DRAM/HBM capacity + ASML EUV (Reuters/Bloomberg/FT via Twitter corpus; DIGITIMES, 2026-07-10). DIGITIMES: **HBM prices set to more than double in 2027** as Rubin drives demand and supply deals tighten — but "HBM customers will wait until 2028 for the capacity" the raise funds. Competitive watch: Bloomberg profiled **China's CXMT** building a top-to-bottom memory supply chain to challenge SK Hynix/Samsung/Micron (structural low-end threat, long-dated). See [SKHYNIX](../SKHYNIX.md), [MU](../MU.md).

## Who's exposed (companies)
| Ticker | Angle | Read |
|---|---|---|
| [MU](../MU.md) | Pure-play — only US-listed scaled DRAM/NAND maker; #2-3 in HBM, credible 3rd HBM4 source given Samsung's product issues | Maximum leverage to the cycle. Q2 FY26 blowout (rev $23.9B, 75% GM); Q3 guide ~81% GM, EPS $19.15; CY26 HBM sold out; near-unanimous sell-side bull (PTs to $1,625-1,750). Bull = duration to 2028+; bear (own ex-VP) = commodity snap-back to a fraction of the price. BofA's sum-of-parts ($950 PO, since superseded) frames the value split explicitly: **$710/sh cyclical DRAM/NAND (3.1x P/B) + $190/sh HBM (27x PE)** — the secular/cyclical decomposition for the durability debate (BofA "AI 2030", 2026-05-13). |
| [NVDA](../NVDA.md) | HBM *consumer* — largest HBM4 buyer (Rubin/Vera); memory is a cost line, not a revenue line | Net positive but two-edged: memory shortage is a supply governor on Rubin, and HBM4E roughly doubles the memory BOM — GM mid-70s defensible into FY28 per Arcuri, possibly aided by HBM consignment. Vera standalone CPU ($200B TAM claim) is itself a *driver* of the DRAM/SO-DIMM demand surge (JPM, 2026-05-28; [NVDA page](../NVDA.md)). |

Also in prose: **SK Hynix** — HBM leader, reinforced by the NVIDIA multiyear tech partnership; the relative-performance long vs Samsung, lifted by Avera listing + HBM4 progress + passive/single-stock-ETF flows (JPM/Jay, 2026-05-28; Digitimes via [MU briefing](../_briefings/by-ticker/MU.md), 2026-06-09). **Samsung** — laggard: HBM4 qualified but volume not ramped, product still trails on power/speed; catalysts are HBM4 volume start, AMD chiplet wins, and shareholder-return policy (JPM, 2026-05-28; ex-VP, 2026-06-10). **Kioxia** — NAND pure-play re-rating on LTAs (P/E 3-5x → 7-8x, some bulls to 10-11x), June-quarter price +60-65% q/q, dividend/overhang the watch items (JPM/Mio, 2026-05-28). **SNDK (SanDisk)** — NAND beneficiary; led the LTA disclosure that set the framework, SSD TAM expectations raised to ~500 EB (~50-60% CAGR) vs mid-400s prior (JPM/Jay, 2026-05-28); appears in the memory-supercycle est-raise basket alongside MU/Samsung/SK Hynix (briefing 2026-05-29).

| Company | Margin trajectory | HBM position / ramp | DRAM/NAND ASP | Sold-out / LTAs | Capex / supply discipline | Bits vs price |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| [MU](../MU.md) | GM 39%→75%, Q3 guide ~81% ↑ | #2-3; HBM4 shipping, share=DRAM ↑ | DRAM +mid-60s%, NAND +high-70s% q/q ↑ | CY26 sold out incl HBM4; SCAs signed ↑ | Capex $14B→>$25B FY26 ↑ | Bits supply-capped ~20%; price-led → |
| [SKHYNIX](../SKHYNIX.md) | OP margin 41%→72% ↑ | HBM #1, ~56% share (off ~90%); HBM4 mass-prod first ↓ | "Significant" DRAM/NAND increases; HBM ASP +40-50% YoY ↑ | Sold out since 2023; 2026 locked, tight to 2027 ↑ | Capex up "significantly"; M15X/Yongin/Indiana ↑ | NAND bits −10% q/q; volume-prioritized → |
| [SAMSUNG](../SAMSUNG.md) | OP margin 6%→43% ↑ | HBM laggard ~20%; HBM4 mass-prod Feb-26, >3x y/y ↑ | DRAM ASP +low-90s%, NAND +high-80s% q/q ↑ | HBM4 "fully booked"; NAND tight ↑ | Capex "substantial" 2026 increase ↑ | DRAM bits +low-teens; conv. DRAM led → |
| [SNDK](../SNDK.md) | GM 26%→78%, Q4 guide 79-81% ↑ | NAND-only (no HBM); HBF samples 2H26 — | NAND price-led; CY26 DC growth mid-70s% ↑ | 5 NBMs, $42B backlog, >1/3 FY27 bits ↑ | Capex held ~4% of rev → | Bits flat y/y, −high-teens q/q; price/mix ↓ |
| [KIOXIA](../KIOXIA.md) | OP margin guide ~74% ↑ | NAND-only (no HBM); BiCS 332L summer-26 — | ASP +100%+ q/q, highest among peers ↑ | CY26 sold out; multi-year LTAs into 2027 ↑ | Capex ~¥470bn/yr FY26-28 (disciplined) ↑ | Bits −10% q/q on maintenance; price-led ↓ |

**Read:** Every member is riding the same melt-up — margins inflecting vertically, books sold out, and beats running on price while bits stay flat-to-down (the classic late-cycle tell that fuels the bear's commodity snap-back). The split is positional: SK Hynix leads HBM but is ceding share off ~90%, Samsung is the HBM catch-up swing on 1c-yield, MU is the pure-play with maximum leverage, and the two NAND-only names (SNDK, Kioxia) are pure ASP/LTA stories with no HBM and no DRAM offset on the turn.

_Source: member-company earnings calls; see each company page for quarter-by-quarter detail._

## Changelog
- **2026-08-25 (/run-inbox 23h — 4 current sources + a 7-piece DAMNANG historical backfill). ONE new dated block at the head of the page, 5 Sources entries. NOTHING SUPERSEDED — no ASP, bit, share, capex, PT or sufficiency mark on this page was replaced or edited.**
  🔴🔴 **THE HEADLINE IS A THREE-WAY OPEN ITEM, DELIBERATELY NOT COLLAPSED.** The 21h /wiki-ingest run recorded [[SAMSUNG]] IR as **denying** de-spec, via a one-clause BofA-hosted relay (*"still no HBM de-spec for high-end GPUs"*). The Tier-1 primary of the same day — a **BTG-hosted Samsung IR call** — has the DS spokesperson saying something materially different: customers **are** reportedly considering lowering HBM stack layers and commodity density, the driver is **supply constraint, not weak demand**, fulfilment is **60-70% across products and 50-60% for HBM**, unmet 2026 demand is **pushed into 2027**, and S/D is **tighter in 2027, 2028 and beyond**. **That is not a denial; it is "it is happening, and here is why it is bullish."** The page now carries **all three positions in one table — relayed denial / third-party active evaluation with a 1-1.5-month deadline / primary supply-driven explanation — with the standing rule that the primary outranks the relay where they conflict, but neither is deleted.** ⚠️ The most likely reconciliation (different questions asked) is flagged **as an inference, not as a resolution**.
  ⚠️ **THE UBS +90% (Samsung blended) vs JPM +42% (industry $/Gb) 2027 HBM ASP CONTRADICTION REMAINS OPEN.** What changed is its shape: Samsung management's disclosure that **the 2026 HBM contract was struck at end-2025, before the commodity-DRAM spike, and is being re-cut for 2027 "at least [to] a similar level of the commodity DRAM"** makes part of the 48pp gap a **BASE-YEAR** question rather than only a mix question — which is checkable. UBS's own **$1.47/Gb ([[SKHYNIX]]) vs $1.55/Gb ([[SAMSUNG]]) 2Q26 DRAM ASP** mark is now on the page as the correct like-for-like basis anchor. **No resolution declared. Re-routed to reconciliation with the new framing.**
  **Also newly logged and open:** (a) an **in-document inconsistency in the Samsung IR call itself** on its own 2027 HBM share target (mid-to-high-30s vs mid-to-high-20s in two answers by the same speaker) — material because two houses put Samsung #1 in HBM in 2027; (b) **Mizuho contradicting its own 08-16 AI Server Call** — Feynman at 768GB HBM4e/16-Hi then, "significant Feynman de-specing (50%)" and a 4-8Hi cap eight days later, same lead analyst; (c) a **publisher correction** — the "SemiAnalysis: Memory De-Spec, WFE, 800V" file is a **Mizuho** note written up from a Mizuho-hosted dinner with SemiAnalysis' Doug O'Laughlin, and its PTs and its ~20-30% shipment-uplift estimate are Mizuho's own.
  **Genuinely new datapoints:** UBS's flat "**Nvidia has moved to HBM4 8-Hi from HBM4E 12-Hi for VR/R300**… not affecting overall demand (more SiPs, less content per SiP)" and "**HBM could remain undersupplied into 2030 or over**"; **LTAs contract VOLUME with no pricing floor/ceiling** (SKH), matching Samsung's "ASP negotiable quarterly even under LTAs" — so this page's "LTAs smoothen the cyclicality" line is now marked **half true**; the **three-maker open-capacity pattern** ([[SAMSUNG]] 30-40% held back to capture price, [[SKHYNIX]] ~half un-LTA'd, [[MU]] ~60% of bits exposed to upside); **[[SKHYNIX]] 2027 capex W61tn +30% y/y, Yongin 1 tooling from Feb-2027, 6F² to 0a then 4F², M17 MP 1H29**; **[[MU]] F27E capex ~$50bn +67% skewed to construction with first greenfield bits small in 2028E**; **HBF at ~$1/GB and 3Tbps vs HBM4E ~$30/GB and 1.6-2Tbps** (⚠️ a 30x gap = tiering, not substitution); **[[GOOG]] TPUv8 HBM3e scaled to 8Hi**; **"3D HBM"/zHBM already shown to customers and drawing interest**; and the **[[AMAT]] equipment LTA as Samsung's downside "safety net"** behind the shells-not-tools capex pattern.

That independence from every HBM-centric model here (UBS +40% 2027E DRAM bit consumption, the HBM-absorbs-24%/30%-of-capacity figure, JPM's own 08-09 HBM S&D) is what makes it worth more than another shortage number.**
  🔴 **NET-NEW ODM-LEVEL COMPONENT RADAR: RED = DRAM, eMMC, SSD, NAND, with SERVER CPU "getting into that segment"; YELLOW = analog IC, MLCC and tantalum ("SP") capacitors.** ✅ **The yellow tier CORROBORATES rather than adds — this page already had all three top MLCC makers raising prices, MLCC +30% across the board, and SEMCO/LG Innotek bullish with large prepayments (BofA · Woo 07-31; TrendForce). The NET-NEW element is the RANKING: memory a full tier above passives, and server CPU joining it — a component this page had been treating as a demand OFFSET (JPM's AI CPU unit TAM +155% CAGR, 08-09) rather than as a constraint.**
  ✅🔴 **A MECHANISM CORROBORATED AND THEN GENERALISED, WHICH IS THE MOST TRADABLE ITEM IN THE BLOCK: JPM's "similar to HBM versus D4 — HBM demand is so strong, which segment benefited the most? DDR4" restates causality this page ALREADY HOLDS from UBS's CXMT initiation (08-07: reallocation to HBM producing "stronger price hike momentum for DDR than HBM"; DDR ASP 137% CAGR vs HBM 43%) and from Bernstein (07-20: "remarkable that price has gone up so much in conventional, but not in HBM"). What is NEW is the general law — the scarcity rent accrues to the DISPLACED product — and the forward application: the next displaced product is the GENERAL-PURPOSE SERVER and its whole BOM. A dated, scoreable prediction.**
  ⚠️🔴 **A DEBATE REOPENED AND LOGGED WITH BOTH SIDES INTACT: JPM hears "more and more spec adjustment in server side… and even for some non-server side" and warns of a "mini inventory adjustment sometime next year" driven by UNIT rationing. This page carries the opposite from the ODM desk — Jefferies' Arthur Lai checks (08-03) with "NO DESPEC IN MEMORY FROM NVIDIA" at $7-8m/rack budgets, and ODMs calling a memory-module cut "unfeasible for AI server" (08-04). NEITHER IS WITHDRAWN. They are reconcilable — NVIDIA's AI-server spec holding while general-server and non-server specs get cut is exactly what the crowding-out mechanism predicts — but they are not the same claim. Note what the "mini inventory adjustment" is: the first bear scenario on this page sourced to unit rationing rather than to capacity arriving (TrendForce +4.0% 2028) or demand cracking.**
  🔴 **NET-NEW PASS-THROUGH EVIDENCE: three rounds of price hikes — 1st commodity (memory, MLCC, some PC semis) → 2nd substrate and foundry → 3rd, and unprecedented, the ODMs themselves negotiating price-ups with customers ("that has never happened before"). The most price-taking tier in the chain obtaining increases is evidence the cost is reaching the end buyer rather than being absorbed mid-chain — bullish for memory-price durability and bearish for hyperscaler capex efficiency simultaneously.** ✅ **Timing corroborates the existing consensus: no relief before 2027 ("probably not 2027"), consistent with the three independent routes to C2028 in the 08-18 block.**
