  ⚠️🔴 **TRENDFORCE CONTRADICTS ITSELF ON DRAM IN TWELVE DAYS, AND BOTH VERSIONS NOW SIT ON THIS PAGE.** 07-30 (already here): DRAM sufficiency gap *"~-1% to -2% in 2026, **widening in 2027**"*. 08-11 (FMS, via FUNDA): the gap *"bottoms out and begins to **narrow** in 2027"*, positive by 2028. **Same house, opposite sign on the single most important date in this theme. Two qualifiers recorded: (i) the newer version is a third-party RELAY of a conference slide with no stated basis, not a TrendForce publication; (ii) the NAND leg did NOT flip — it is the same call as 07-30 with numbers attached. So TrendForce is internally consistent on NAND and self-contradictory on DRAM, which makes the NAND figures the more credible half and the ones that land on [[SNDK]]/[[KIOXIA]]/[[STX]].**
  ⚠️ **AND IT COLLIDES WITH THE UBS SERIES ALREADY HERE, LOGGED SIDE BY SIDE:** UBS (07-03) has DRAM *"undersupplied until at least 2Q28"* with 2027 sufficiency **−13.6%** raw / **−1.2%** inventory-adjusted, and NAND at **−1.0%/−9.3%/−0.7%** for 25/26/27E against the house's **+2.6%** 2027 NAND. **On DRAM 2028 the two houses are on opposite sides of zero; on NAND 2027 they are close (TrendForce +0.4%, UBS −0.7%, house +2.6%) — i.e. NAND-at-balance-in-2027 is now near-consensus and the fight is about 2028. 🔴 BASIS GUARD ADDED: TrendForce's figures come with NO stated basis while this page quotes sufficiency on three (raw end-demand, inventory-adjusted, house units). Not netted.**
  🔴 **THE BULL POLE IS THE RELAY DISSENTING FROM ITS OWN SLIDE, and its LTA mechanic is the most transferable idea in the run.** FUNDA: the ASP-growth flattening is *"a process of structural normalization"*, driven by (1) consumer price sensitivity — smartphones cannot absorb more, PC OEMs are MORE tolerant on a higher baseline ASP plus inelastic education/commercial demand — and (2) a rising LTA share *"with locked-in prices typically lower than short-term contract prices,"* so *"as the LTA ratio increases, overall ASP growth naturally decelerates"* while vendors keep upfront payments and penalty protection. **Expects memory prices to hold at elevated levels through 2027 "rather than peaking and collapsing."** ➜ **This makes decelerating BLENDED ASP a mix artefact rather than a pricing-power signal — mechanically consistent with the two contract structures documented here on 08-15 ([[MU]] SCA ceiling vs [[SNDK]] NBM floor). Anyone reading blended-ASP deceleration as the down-leg must strip LTA share out first. ⚠️ Note the elasticity ranking is now CONTESTED: MS (07-27) models a larger percentage shortfall in PCs than smartphones, FUNDA argues PC OEMs are the more tolerant buyer.**
  🔴 **NET-NEW SUPPLY MECHANISM THIS PAGE DID NOT HAVE: the semiconductor EQUIPMENT shortage as an independent binding constraint on the pace of capacity expansion (FUNDA, first flagged in its 2026-04-01 LTA report).** ➜ **Read against the $69bn/$96bn of 2027-28 DRAM WFE already here (Bernstein 08-11): if the tool layer is itself rationed, a WFE number is an ORDER number, not a capacity number.   🔴 **THE FIRST CREDIBLE LTA BEAR, from a house bullish on the cycle — Jefferies' post-earnings debrief turns explicitly BEAR on memory: LTAs "EXTENUATE A CYCLE RATHER THAN PREVENT IT"; "DEALS SIGNED AT 80% GROSS MARGIN IMPLY SOFTER ASPs THAN OPEN MARKET, QUESTIONING THE UPSIDE POTENTIAL from historical $300-500 EPS expectations"; and — read off the equipment layer — "[[LRCX]]'s RECORD NAND REVENUE indicates SOMEONE'S ADDING CAPACITY in NAND, LIKELY THE KOREANS, EARLIER THAN PREVIOUSLY ANTICIPATED." Third leg: "HIGH COST SENSITIVITY IN AI is driving DIFFERENT MEMORY TIERING… EVEN IN THE DATA CENTER, MEMORY PRICING HAS HIT A POINT WHERE DEMAND IS AT RISK OF DISRUPTION."** ⚠️ **Logged alongside, NOT netted against, the supply-failure reading — the two are observationally similar and the discriminating test stays the one already on this page (a supply-driven de-spec coincides with RISING ASPs).**
  ⚠️ **THE TRACKERS SAY THE RATE OF INCREASE IS NARROWING AND MAY NOW BE BELOW STREET (Bernstein, 08-07): July contracts imply 3QCY26 conventional DRAM +17% q/q (PC 17% / Server 16% / Mobile 11% / Consumer ~27%) and DRAM+NAND "close to 20% QoQ," but "THE SIZE OF THE INCREASE NARROWED NOTABLY VS. 2Q, AND MAY BE A BIT BELOW THE LATEST STREET EXPECTATION… ROOM FOR UPSIDE IS LIKELY LIMITED, GIVEN MEMORY IS BECOMING A BURDEN FOR BOTH AI & NON-AI APPLICATIONS & PRICE CAPS SET IN SOME LTAs." First soft print in the series: SERVER DRAM SPOT MIXED at −6.7% to +3.8% m/m; NAND 512Gb wafer spot −3% m/m (1Tb +8%).** ✅ **Counterweight on the same data: UBS's SIA read confirms the cycle is NOT purely ASP — "total semiconductor UNITS rose 9.5% Q/Q in CQ2, with MEMORY UNITS UP 32.2% Q/Q." June was a record $147.8B (+10.8% m/m), CQ2 +36.6% q/q, memory +64.2% q/q, DRAM a record $56.8B. WSTS: memory sales +379% y/y in June, ~2/3 of all industry growth YTD from memory PRICING alone; ex-memory still +38% y/y.**

If contracting is generalising across scarce AI inputs, this page's "memory is uniquely contracted" framing needs to widen to "scarce AI inputs are getting contracted" — with read-through to every constrained node rather than to DRAM and NAND alone. A large consumer buyer hoarding ahead of expected further tightening — recorded as behavioural evidence, not as a memory volume number.** **(5) MECHANISM ADDED, and it upgrades a qualitative argument on this page to a measured one: KV cache lives in HBM and spills to server DRAM; on a B300 node serving Kimi K3, HBM holds only ~3.25M tokens of KV cache after weights and above concurrency 8 the cache thrashes (hit rates <10% vs 95% theoretical), while 219.91 GB/rank of CPU DRAM offload adds 15.76M tokens (4.85x total) with a "huge" performance gain; on B200 the same workload is "much worse and nearly infeasible." - **2026-07-31 (wiki-ingest) — the LTA mechanics were disclosed and they invert the standard reading of high contract coverage. New section: `## Recent signals (Jul 31)`.** **★ The core finding: "LTA does not fix memory chip price… lot of room to RAISE contract price every quarter but there is LIMIT FOR PRICE CUT." [[SAMSUNG]] disclosed 60-70% LTA coverage of memory sales with ASYMMETRIC terms — a price-cut FLOOR of sub-5% q/q against increases broadly open at 10-20%+ — on mostly 5-YEAR ROLLING tenors where a 6th year can be added as the 1st completes. [[KIOXIA]] is "on track toward 50% LTA VOLUME COVERAGE FOR CY28"** (BofA · Woo/Shen/Arya, 2026-07-31; Kioxia Jun-Q). ⚠️ **The implication, which is easy to get backwards: these are DOWNSIDE COLLARS WITH UNCAPPED UPSIDE, not price locks. High LTA coverage is BULLISH for realised ASP stability; spot prints become a progressively WEAKER signal of realised pricing as coverage rises; and the usual "LTAs lock in today's prices before the cycle rolls" bear argument does not apply on these terms.** **Pricing evidence, with volume restraint as the deliberate mechanism: Kioxia Jun-Q ASP +70% Q/Q LIKE-FOR-LIKE on low-single-digit bit growth (OPM 75%); Samsung DRAM OPM ~80% / NAND OPM ~70% in 2Q with DRAM ASP guided +23% q/q in 3Q and +8% in 4Q after +91%/+45% in 1Q/2Q; 2Q OP W89.5tn with quarterly OP heading through W100tn+ (annual W400tn+) in 3Q/4Q26.** ⚠️ **A CEILING is also disclosed, and it is strategic rather than market-imposed: Samsung guides ASP flat-to-slightly-down through 2027-28 under LTAs and "chipmakers' strategic control (e.g., decent price cut per AI server content increase)" — trading price for content, with annual OP still rising to W500tn+ on volume and yield. A share/volume strategy, not price maximisation.** ⚠️ **DRAM and NAND DIVERGED in the July contract print: DRAM +low-teens % m/m = 30-50% q/q, FLAT for NAND.** Spot drivers into August: **restocking, Tier-2 OEMs resuming Sept/4Q launches despite cost, and China domestic smartphone shipments bottoming at 17MN UNITS IN JUNE vs a normal 25-30mn.** China supply: **"minimal impact… China-branded memory not commonly traded in the spot market."** Duration: **Samsung sees an ongoing DRAM shortage "EVEN WITH new capacity expansion through 2027-2028"; Kioxia guides CY27 "demand anticipated to exceed supply"; BofA sees tight supply and elevated pricing "at least through 2027."** **★ Best available proof of pricing power, because it appears in the buyers' own numbers: AMZN raised FY26 capex ~$200bn → ~$220bn with the increment attributed EXPLICITLY to memory costs and named "inflated prices… like memory and HARD DRIVES AND SSDs"; META attributed its raise to component/memory pricing; AAPL said memory accounts for >100% of its Sept-Q GM compression and expects to pay "even higher" in September, with UBS modelling DRAM/NAND ASPs +~30% q/q and AAPL Dec-Q GM at 45.9%.** ⚠️ **THE MAIN RISK ADDED: the demand mix is NARROW. Kioxia says agentic AI and server NAND are driving, but SMARTPHONE NAND DEMAND IS FLAT vs CY25 and PC NAND A SLIGHT DECLINE on higher BOM costs — the cycle is carried entirely by datacenter, with consumer contributing nothing and being actively rationed by price. MediaTek expects the handset market to DECLINE 15% in 2026.** WFE consequence with a timing caveat: **LRCX guided above expectations with 2027 likely a fourth straight year of WFE outgrowth and DRAM the fastest-growing segment in FY26 — but NAND WFE is DOWN 1H'26 vs 1H'25 in dollars, with the SLC/TLC-for-AI-memory-stack demand teed up for '28-'30.** Broadening evidence: **SEMCO and LG Innotek show the SAME contract structure — 2Q OP more than doubling y/y, stronger LTA orders, LARGE PREPAYMENTS funding fabs, price increases through 2H and 2027; SEMCO annual OP W2tn in 2026 → W5tn+ in 2027; all three top MLCC makers have raised prices.** Capital return: **KIOXIA ¥800bn buyback (5.5% of shares) + 3-for-1 split; Samsung buyback "when, not if" on 50% of FCF.** ⚠️ **Positioning logged so moves are not misread: KOSPI +18% with SKHYNIX +30% limit up and Samsung +27% on the Situational Awareness clearing; Korea l/s ratio retraced to 2.8x from 6x but STILL +4.5z above the 2018 average; Korean retail piling into INVERSE leveraged products; Korean ETF AUM ~W500tn = 30%+ of KOSPI trading value.** Cross-filed [[SAMSUNG]], [[KIOXIA]], [[MU]], [[SKHYNIX]], [[SNDK]], [[STX]], [[AAPL]], [[AMZN]], [[LRCX]].

- **2026-07-28 (b) — /run-inbox, three sources. One layered State-of-play bullet + three Sources entries. Nothing superseded; the existing 07-28 Jefferies relay bullet was ENRICHED, NOT DUPLICATED.** **(1) Fubon (Sherman Shang / Daniel Yang, 2026-07-28) — the primary text behind the US$31-32/GB HBM4 figure the Jefferies relay had already put on this page. What is net-new is the rest of the comparison: US$17-18/GB for HBM3e in 2026 (so NVDA's step is ~+80%, NOT a doubling), and US$35-36/GB for "other GPU and custom-ASIC projects… meaning the HBM cost will double in 2027" — i.e. THE DOUBLING IS THE INDUSTRY/NON-NVDA STATEMENT AND MUST NOT BE APPLIED TO NVDA'S OWN BOM. Two consequences logged: an explicit ~US$4/GB NVDA procurement advantage over every other accelerator program (the first quantification of an advantage the merchant-vs-custom debate has argued qualitatively), and Fubon's Rubin ASP of US$78-80K at a HELD 75-80% chip GM — meaning the accelerator ASP inflation being read as "AI inflation" is substantially an HBM pass-through, not margin expansion.** **(2) Micron IR call · Satish (2026-07-27) — two structural mechanisms new to the page. FIRST, the cleanest primary-source answer to "is memory still a commodity": HBM has run on ANNUAL VOLUME-AND-PRICE CONTRACTS FOR THREE YEARS with "no market where there's HBM on a spot market or quarterly market type business," PO-driven. The implication is logged explicitly — the DRAM spot/contract series this page tracks is not a read on HBM economics, and the second-derivative pricing debate on [macro-cycle](macro-cycle.md) does not mechanically transmit to HBM. SECOND, HBM is named by Micron as a CAUSE of the industry bit shortage ("new products such as H[B]M consuming capacity"), alongside greenfield build times and lower bit growth per node — HBM is endogenous to the commodity DRAM squeeze, not just a beneficiary. Also: two Micron white papers argue more HBM4 content or bandwidth raises token throughput (cross-filed [tokenmaxxing](tokenmaxxing.md)).** **(3) MS webcast · Sean (2026-07-28) — the HBM-vs-commodity-DRAM SUPPLY SUBSTITUTION, stated as a specific driver of TrendForce's DRAM bit-growth raise from 25% to 31% this year: "Hynix probably HBM3E, where no HBM4 will actually reduce the amount of total HBM and more DRAM will be produced as a result." ⚠️ THIS CHANGES HOW THE SK HYNIX HBM4 RAMP DELAY SHOULD BE READ ON THIS PAGE. It had been logged only as an HBM-side timing miss (the Q2 revenue shortfall Jefferies attributed to "the delay in the ramp of HBM4"). It is also bearish COMMODITY DRAM pricing via supply, because HBM4 consumes more wafer per delivered bit than HBM3E — so wafers not going into HBM4 come back out as conventional DRAM bits. Double-edged, and the second edge was missing.** ⚠️ Sean's transcript renders HBM as "HPM" throughout. Cross-filed [[SKHYNIX]], [[MU]], [[NVDA]], [macro-cycle](macro-cycle.md), [tokenmaxxing](tokenmaxxing.md), [china-export](china-export.md), [cowos-packaging](cowos-packaging.md).
- **2026-07-28** — **/wiki-ingest. 5 State-of-play bullets, driven by the [[SKHYNIX]] Q2 2026 print and Jefferies' “HAS MEMORY PEAKED?” sector note published the same morning.** **The single most actionable number added: memory price hikes are tracking 15-20% versus the 25-30% expected** — the shortage is real but the pricing power embedded in 2027 numbers is not fully materialising, which is harder to see than a demand miss because it does not show up in bit-growth data. Jefferies also puts **memory prices peaking ~1H27** and states that **2027 consensus requires ~US$2tn of US AI capex to be achievable**, with **HBM4 costs jumping to US$31-32/GB**. **SK Hynix delivered the cycle's first headline miss and it was a TIMING miss: OP ₩60.54tn vs ₩64.2tn and revenue ₩79.3tn vs ₩83.9tn, attributed specifically to the HBM4 ramp delay** — though local brokers were already at ~₩60tn, margins held (GM 83% / OM 76% / EBITDA 81%), HBM4 shipments did begin in Q2, and **1cnm HBM4E samples are already with a major customer**. DRAM ASP was still **+30% q/q**. **Two framings changed on this page.** (1) **The NVDA anchor is now being read as a pricing constraint** — “Hynix has been less aggressive than [[SAMSUNG]] on pricing given its commitments to [[NVDA]] on HBM” — so the supplier-differentiation premium and the pricing discipline question are the same fact viewed from opposite sides; paired with **~80% capex growth to the high-₩40tn range and no buyback despite large net cash**, the bear compresses to peak-margin cash funding capacity into a forecast price peak. (2) **Management's demand framing got BROADER, not narrower** — demand expanding “as AI evolves into agentic form” across HBM/server DRAM/eSSD, with efficiency gains expected to *expand* usage (the direct rebuttal to the Kimi-K3 read already on this page), and **PC/mobile softness explicitly a volume-allocation problem rather than weak end-demand.** Tape logged: **KOSPI -10.7%, sell sidecar, the year's eighth circuit breaker, Samsung -12%, SK Hynix -13%**, on **CXMT's +466% IPO debut** plus a Fed-hike scare; **[[MU]] fell harder than Hynix itself** (-8.9% intraday, -4% after hours). Cross-filed [[SKHYNIX]], [[MU]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[TER]], [china-export](china-export.md).

The resolution is whether eSSD $/GB falls faster than nearline $/TB rises.** **Second useful item is a timing analogue this page did not have: MS uses "memory pricing did not begin its sharp upward move until SEPTEMBER OF LAST YEAR" as the lead indicator for when HDD contract negotiations turn aggressive — i.e. an explicit ~1-year lag from the memory inflection to the storage tier.** Demand frame carried for sizing the sub-HBM data build-out: **300-400EB HDD supply shortfall EVERY year through CY28, CSPs requesting visibility through 2032, CY27-28 pricing toward $25-30/TB, and spot EBs at a 30-40%+ premium but only ~15-20% of nearline EB shipments** — the last figure being the cap on how much spot can flow into blended pricing. STX/WDC ratings, PTs and estimates deliberately NOT folded here. **(2) BACKFILL — Bernstein/Newman "Apple Deep Dive" (2026-03-03), five months old, filed as dated demand-side context.** **The content is the consumer/mobile DEMAND-DESTRUCTION edge of this up-cycle, modelled line-by-line: the iPhone 18 Pro Max 12GB DRAM line goes $28.93 → $114.00 (+294%), memory moves from ~5% to ~17% of BOM, NAND $10.58 → $28.45 (+169%), total device cost +23%/+26% on the 256GB/512GB configs (blended "close to 24.5%") for a headline ~25% iPhone BOM increase; the half-offset scenario puts DRAM at $71.47 (+147%), device cost +14.5% and FY27 EPS at $8.81 (-15% vs Bernstein, -5.3% vs consensus).** Underlying series: **Apple mobile-DRAM module $2.40/GB 2QCY25 → $9.5/GB 2QCY26E**, cause attributed by Bernstein to **"the large AI demand for memory."** ⚠️ **BASIS FLAG, to stop anyone mixing it with the page's contract cadence: this is a 2QCY25→2QCY26 Y/Y MOBILE-DRAM module change at Bernstein's assumed iPhone contract-negotiation point — NOT the Q/Q conventional-DRAM contract series in [`dram-pricing-2026`](../_meta/assumptions.md) (TrendForce +15-20% Q3 / +3-8% Q4, revised 06-30) and NOT comparable to the July PC DRAM contract marks.** **The reason it earns space despite the age: it is the earliest fully-specified version of the profit-pool-reallocation mechanism this page only began tracking on 07-25 via UBS (memory ~10% → ~30% of a $1,000 iPhone's BOM at +300%). Bernstein's March build — 5% → ~17% on +294% — is independent, earlier corroboration, which means the handset bifurcation was modellable in Q1 rather than discovered in July.** Two second-order legs added: **lower-end Android OEMs "cannot secure enough memory"** (an [[AAPL]] share-gain opening, consistent with the -11% y/y Q2-2026 smartphone shipment figure already on the page and the 07-25 QCOM/ARM cuts), and **OEMs potentially "turning to Chinese suppliers to offset cost inflation"** — the demand-pull under the CXMT/YMTC design-in fight. **Explicitly NOT routed to [optical-cpo.md](optical-cpo.md): verified the note contains zero mentions of CPO, optical or HBM — the keyword router's proposal was a false positive.** Cross-filed [[AAPL]], [[WDC]], [[SNDK]], [[KIOXIA]], [[MU]].

Nothing superseded.** **The most valuable addition is a cross-check, not a new forecast: Nomura initiated CXMT at Buy (TP CNY116) forecasting its global DRAM share going ~10% → ~18% by end-2028F — which independently corroborates Susquehanna's 17%-of-DRAM-supply-by-YE28 figure logged 07-25, from a house that is LONG the stock rather than bearish the incumbents.** Two bears agreeing is weak; a bull and a bear agreeing on the supply number is much stronger, and that is why it is carried. Supporting: **CXMT bit expansion 40-45% CAGR 2026-30F; 63%/74% sales/NP CAGRs 2026-28F; TP off 20x 2028F EPS of CNY5.8, explicitly benchmarked at "2x the valuation of… Micron… which has traded at ~10x over the past five years"**; gated by **the MATCH Act**, equipment **localisation ~40%**, and a worst case where **Shanghai (30-40% of total capacity by 2028F) is pushed out.** CXMT listed 7/27 at a debut BofA framed as **15-20x FY27 P/E → ~RMB5T (bull 30x → ~RMB9T)** — US$700bn-1.3tn on ~10% share, i.e. **the threat is cost of capital, not technology**; the BofA/Arya counter ("largely confined to commodity DRAM… not a credible near-term competitor in HBM3E/HBM4") is retained alongside, because they are different arguments. **Second: the strongest structural demand statement now on the page also comes from the competitor's analyst — "more than a sevenfold increase in global memory usage (60%+ bit CAGR) over 2026-30F, EVEN AFTER… discounting memory usage by 4x" for efficiency techniques, against industry bit expansion of only 30-40% CAGR ("could still be insufficient").** A pre-applied 4x haircut and demand still outruns supply is a much harder claim to dismiss than an unadjusted 7x. **Third — and this is the first OBSERVABLE evidence that the upcycle is being paid for out of the accelerator BOM: NVDA is reported to be cutting the memory spec on NVL72, after an RTX 50 ramp delay and a SOCAMM spec cut, because "memory is the majority of the BOM on VR200."** ⚠️ **Sell-side HBM $/GB inputs span ~$20 to $53/GB for 2027 — a ~2.7x spread — with one broker at ~$9.1m per rack. Check which $/GB is embedded before comparing any two accelerator cost models.** **Fourth: Apple's ask is now a specific ex-US carve-out and MU is publicly opposed** (WSJ), which changes no supply yet but shows how binding the memory bill is at the largest customer. **Fifth: July PC DRAM contract +15-20% m/m → +30-40% q/q vs TrendForce +13-18%** — and the tape is paying (memory the best sub-sector last week: MU +8%, WDC +9%, STX +8%, SNDK +6%) even though memory also carries **the largest derating since the June 22 SOX peak, -24% price / -36% P/E.** ⚠️ Positioning left unreconciled: **MS Prime has the Global Memory L/S at ~7x vs >20x peaks (re-engaged, not crowded) with 61% of EU clients saying add; JPM has global semis at the 98th %ile on long-term metrics; Jefferies' desk says "memory our most sold sector."** Cross-filed [[MU]], [[SKHYNIX]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[SNDK]], [[AAPL]], [[NVDA]], [china-export](china-export.md).

Fourth bullet consolidates, as a single tracked mechanism, that **memory-cost inflation is bifurcating the handset market**: UBS uses Apple/Samsung resilience vs Chinese Android weakness to cut QCOM ($235→$190, units -10%/+2% C26/C27 from -7%/+1%) and ARM ($470→$360, handsets only — DC forecasts untouched), and to quantify Apple's BOM risk (**$1,000 iPhone: BOM ~$580, memory ~$60 ≈ 10%, ~42% contribution margin → at +300% memory, ~30% of BOM and 24% margin; a $100 price rise recovers only to 31%**). Corroborated by **Samsung's Z Fold8 launching with up to US$100 of price increases**. Net: memory inflation is now visibly reallocating profit pools within the customer base.
- 2026-07-24 (b) — /run-inbox SemiAnalysis batch (theme-dossier pass). **Added three State-of-play bullets + a Sources entry; all additive, no prior number superseded.** (1) **S10 "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat?" (2026-07-24) — the headline and the most important supplier-side datapoint of the quarter:** the **HBM4 pin-speed bake-off** — **MI455X 12 stacks / 432GB / 23.3 TB/s at 7.6 Gbps** versus **Rubin and Google at 8 stacks / 288GB, Rubin 22 TB/s at 10.7 Gbps (40% faster)** — near parity despite AMD's 50% wider bus. The mechanism matters more than the spec: **Nvidia raised its HBM4 pin target "well above the original JEDEC HBM4 specification… precisely driven by the need to make up for the memory bandwidth deficit to AMD MI455X," which FORCED memory suppliers to rework their HBM4**, pushed out Rubin upstream output (since resolved), and means **Nvidia must use "a much higher quality bin than AMD"** — a binning/yield split across the only two HBM4 buyers shipping this year. Packaging enabler: AMD's base-die HBM-facing edge at **32mm to fit 3 cubes per edge vs 29mm on the MI300X AID**. ⚠️ **2027 pricing:** a **significant HBM price increase expected "as memory suppliers move to match the margins they now earn on commodity DRAM,"** only partly passable by AMD (large buyers with real negotiating leverage) → **MI455X package gross-margin drag**; HBM/DRAM/NAND "increased dramatically since our baseline in mid-2025." **The offsetting de-spec (bear leg for bit demand):** the original 2025 BOM carried **138TB of DRAM across LPDDR5X and DDR5 MRDIMM**; **LPDDR5X has now been removed entirely** (the planned up-to-1TB per-accelerator second memory tier on the EAM module is gone) with a significant DDR5 de-spec, so **"much of the DRAM price increases are offset by aggressive de-specs"** — pricing up, quantum down, the same logic as Meta's custom MI455. (2) **S5 SMIC/Kirin teardown (2026-06-14), light** — the physically-measured China mobile-DRAM datapoint the CXMT debate on this page lacked: **Kirin 9030 Pro on Samsung 12GB LPDDR5X-9600 (K4L2E165YD, 12Gb, Samsung 1a node)**, **16GB Pro Max variants found with BOTH CXMT and Samsung packages**, and **CXMT's G4 process inferred at ~0.3 Gib/mm², "roughly equivalent to other manufacturers' 1z"** — i.e. dual-sourced inside a Chinese flagship but about one node-generation behind on density, corroborating the existing "CXMT ~20% of wafers, ~6-7% of bits" and BofA "limited impact at the high end" framing. (3) **S4 "EDA Market Primer" (2026-05-21), one line** — **"HBM verification now approaches logic-chip complexity as stacking and interposer routing requirements grow"**; a flagship AI chip consumes **$10-15M of interface IP** (incl. HBM PHY) vs $2-5M for a traditional DC CPU; Cadence IP titles now include **HBM4**. **DEDUP — verified already on the page, nothing re-added:** S9 "Meta's Infrastructure Team Needs A Culture Reset" 07-21 (Meta's custom cut-down MI450/MI455 with **half the compute silicon, HBM cut from 12 stacks to 6, and 8-Hi instead of 12-Hi HBM4**) — present in the 2026-07-21 bullet; only the canonical SemiAnalysis email slug was added to Sources.

(1) **Kimi K3 "DeepSeek moment" memory debate** (FundaAI "Deep|LLM" 07-19 · UBS/Arcuri via Ruple 07-18 · Beavington 07-19): Moonshot's open-source K3 triggered a semis/memory selloff (SOX −9.9% 5D, SK Hynix ord. −18.7% 5D post-ADR technical break) on the "efficient open models = less memory" fear, but the fundamental read is net-POSITIVE — K3 compressed KV cache only ~75% (vs DeepSeek V4 ~90%), is a 2.8T-param MoE needing ≥64-accelerator supernodes (heavier weights + wider EP = more HBM/scale-up/GPU), NAND-based KV-cache offload now near-universal, and UBS/Arcuri concludes token-/value-maxing is NOT a semis headwind (Jevons; the correction "a technical/systematic de-risking event driven by positioning"). Cross-linked to [tokenmaxxing.md](tokenmaxxing.md). (2) **Memory-interface-IC price-collusion probe** (Bernstein · David Dai, 07-19): Korea (07-15) + prior US-DOJ investigation into Renesas/Rambus/Montage over DDR5 RCD + data-buffer pricing; Bernstein "likely manageable" (interface GM not unusually high; Renesas OP ¥6,300) — an overhang for the interface-IC names, not the DRAM makers.
